## I / 第一章
===Original===

On Thursday, the 9th of July, 186‒, Jean Bertaud and his son, well known at Orcival as living by poaching and marauding, rose at three o’clock in the morning, just at daybreak, to go fishing.
Taking their tackle, they descended the charming pathway, shaded by acacias, which you see from the station at Evry, and which leads from the burg of Orcival to the Seine.
They made their way to their boat, moored as usual some fifty yards above the wire bridge, across a field adjoining Valfeuillu, the imposing estate of the Count de Trémorel.
Having reached the riverbank, they laid down their tackle, and Jean jumped into the boat to bail out the water in the bottom.
While he was skilfully using the scoop, he perceived that one of the oar-pins of the old craft, worn by the oar, was on the point of breaking.
“Philippe,” cried he, to his son, who was occupied in unravelling a net, “bring me a bit of wood to make a new oar-pin.”
“All right,” answered Philippe.
There was no tree in the field. The young man bent his steps toward the park of Valfeuillu, a few rods distant; and, neglectful of Article 391 of the Penal Code, jumped across the wide ditch which surrounds M. de Trémorel’s domain. He thought he would cut off a branch of one of the old willows, which at this place touch the water with their drooping branches.
He had scarcely drawn his knife from his pocket, while looking about him with the poacher’s unquiet glance, when he uttered a low cry, “Father! Here! Father!”
“What’s the matter?” responded the old marauder, without pausing from his work.
“Father, come here!” continued Philippe. “In Heaven’s name, come here, quick!”
Jean knew by the tone of his son’s voice that something unusual had happened. He threw down his scoop, and, anxiety quickening him, in three leaps was in the park. He also stood still, horror-struck, before the spectacle which had terrified Philippe.
On the bank of the river, among the stumps and flags, was stretched a woman’s body. Her long, dishevelled locks lay among the water-shrubs; her dress﻿—of gray silk﻿—was soiled with mire and blood. All the upper part of the body lay in shallow water, and her face had sunk in the mud.
“A murder!” muttered Philippe, whose voice trembled.
“That’s certain,” responded Jean, in an indifferent tone. “But who can this woman be? Really one would say, the countess.”
“We’ll see,” said the young man.

===Chinese===

186-年7月9日，星期四，在奥尔西瓦尔以偷猎和偷鸡摸狗闻名的让·贝尔托（Jean Bertaud）和他儿子，清晨三点刚破晓时分就起了床，准备去捕鱼。
他们拿上渔具，走下那条掩映在洋槐树荫下的迷人小径；从埃夫里（Evry）火车站就能望见这条小径，它从奥尔西瓦尔镇一直通向塞纳河。
他们穿过一片紧邻特雷莫雷尔伯爵（Count de Trémorel）气派庄园——瓦尔菲伊（Valfeuillu）的田地，走向他们的小船；船像往常一样系在铁索桥上方约50码处。
来到河岸边，他们放下渔具，让跳进船里，舀出积在船底的水。
他正熟练地用舀水勺舀水时，发觉这只旧船上的一根被船桨磨损的桨桩快要折断了。
“菲利普（Philippe），”他冲正在解开渔网的儿子喊道，“给我拿块木头来做根新桨桩。”
“好嘞，”菲利普回答道。
田里没有树。年轻人便迈步走向几杖外的瓦尔菲伊庄园公园；他把《刑法典》第391条抛诸脑后，一步跳过了环绕特雷莫雷尔先生产业的宽阔壕沟。他本打算从一棵老柳树上砍下一截枝条，那里的老柳树下垂的枝条正好触及水面。
他一边带着偷猎者警惕不安的目光打量四周，一边刚从口袋里掏出小刀，就失声发出一声低呼：“父亲！快来！父亲！”
“怎么啦？”老偷猎者头也不抬地继续干活，随口应道。
“父亲，快过来！”菲利普继续喊道，“看在老天爷的份上，快来，快！”
让从儿子的声音里听出出事了。他扔下水勺，在焦急催促下，三步并作两步跳进了庄园公园。在吓坏了菲利普的景象面前，他也呆立在原地，吓得魂飞魄散。
在河岸边，树桩和菖蒲丛之间，横卧着一具女尸。她披散的长发浸在水生灌木丛中；她的裙子——灰绸缎料——沾满了污泥和鲜血。尸体上半身浸在浅水中，面部已陷入泥淖。
“是谋杀！”菲利普喃喃说道，声音不住发抖。
“那是一定的，”让冷淡地答道，“可这女人是谁？真像是伯爵夫人。”
“我们瞧瞧去，”年轻人说。

===Original===

He stepped toward the body; his father caught him by the arm.
“What would you do, fool?” said he. “You ought never to touch the body of a murdered person without legal authority.”
“You think so?”
“Certainly. There are penalties for it.”
“Then, come along and let’s inform the Mayor.”
“Why? as if people hereabouts were not against us enough already! Who knows that they would not accuse *us*﻿—”
“But, father﻿—”
“If we go and inform Monsieur Courtois, he will ask us how and why we came to be in Monsieur de Trémorel’s park to find this out. What is it to you, that the countess has been killed? They’ll find her body without you. Come, let’s go away.”
But Philippe did not budge. Hanging his head, his chin resting upon his palm, he reflected.
“We must make this known,” said he, firmly. “We are not savages; we will tell Monsieur Courtois that in passing along by the park in our boat, we perceived the body.”
Old Jean resisted at first; then, seeing that his son would, if need be, go without him, yielded.
They re-crossed the ditch, and leaving their fishing-tackle in the field, directed their steps hastily toward the mayor’s house.
Orcival, situated a mile or more from Corbeil, on the right bank of the Seine, is one of the most charming villages in the environs of Paris, despite the infernal etymology of its name. The gay and thoughtless Parisian, who, on Sunday, wanders about the fields, more destructive than the rook, has not yet discovered this smiling country. The distressing odor of the frying from coffee-gardens does not there stifle the perfume of the honeysuckles. The refrains of bargemen, the brazen voices of boat-horns, have never awakened echoes there. Lazily situated on the gentle slopes of a bank washed by the Seine, the houses of Orcival are white, and there are delicious shades, and a bell-tower which is the pride of the place. On all sides vast pleasure domains, kept up at great cost, surround it. From the upper part, the weathercocks of twenty châteaux may be seen.

===Chinese===

他朝尸体迈了一步；父亲一把抓住了他的胳膊。
“你要干什么，蠢货？”老让说，“没有官府许可，绝不能碰被谋杀者的尸体。”
“你觉得该这样？”
“当然。乱碰可是要受罚的。”
“那咱们走，去报告镇长。”
“干嘛去？难道这周围的人针对咱们还不够吗？谁知道他们会不会指控是我们干的——”
“可是，父亲——”
“要是咱们跑去告诉库尔图瓦先生（Monsieur Courtois），他肯定会盘问咱们为什么、凭什么跑到特雷莫雷尔先生的庄园里发现这事的。伯爵夫人被杀关你什么事？没有你，他们也能找到尸体。走吧，咱们离开这儿。”
但菲利普寸步未挪。他垂着头，下巴抵在手掌上，陷入了沉思。
“咱们必须去报信，”他坚定地说，“咱们又不是野人；咱们就跟库尔图瓦先生说，咱们划船经过庄园公园时看到了尸体。”
老让起初还反对；但看到儿子大有哪怕撇下他也自己去的架势，只好妥协了。
他们重新跳过壕沟，把渔具留在那片田里，匆匆朝镇长家走去。
奥尔西瓦尔坐落在塞纳河右岸，距离科尔贝伊（Corbeil）约一英里多路，尽管其地名源自阴森的词源，却是巴黎周边最迷人的村镇之一。那些在星期天四处闲逛、破坏力堪比秃鼻乌鸦的轻浮巴黎人，尚未发现这片风景宜人的宝地。露天咖啡馆油炸食物的刺鼻气味，还没有在这里压过忍冬花的芳香。驳船水手的号子声、汽笛的喧嚣，也从未在此激起过回声。奥尔西瓦尔的房屋懒洋洋地依偎在塞纳河水冲刷的平缓斜坡上，白墙掩映，绿荫怡人，一座钟楼更是当地引以为豪的景致。四周是一座座花费巨资维护的宏大游憩庄园。从高处极目远眺，能看到20座城堡的风向标。

===Original===

On the right is the forest of Mauprévoir, and the pretty country-house of the Countess de la Brèche; opposite, on the other side of the river, is Mousseaux and Petit-Bourg, the ancient domain of Aguado, now the property of a famous coach-maker; on the left, those beautiful copses belong to the Count de Trémorel, that large park is d’Etiolles, and in the distance beyond is Corbeil; that vast building, whose roofs are higher than the oaks, is the Darblay mill.
The mayor of Orcival occupies a handsome, pleasant mansion, at the upper end of the village. Formerly a manufacturer of dry goods, M. Courtois entered business without a penny, and after thirty years of absorbing toil, he retired with four round millions of francs.
Then he proposed to live tranquilly with his wife and children, passing the winter at Paris and the summer at his country-house.
But all of a sudden he was observed to be disturbed and agitated. Ambition stirred his heart. He took vigorous measures to be forced to accept the mayoralty of Orcival. And he accepted it, quite in self-defence, as he will himself tell you. This office was at once his happiness and his despair; apparent despair, interior and real happiness.
It quite befits him, with clouded brow, to rail at the cares of power; he appears yet better when, his waist encircled with the gold-laced scarf, he goes in triumph at the head of the municipal body.
Everybody was sound asleep at the mayor’s when the two Bertauds rapped the heavy knocker of the door. After a moment, a servant, half asleep, appeared at one of the ground-floor windows.
“What’s the matter, you rascals?” asked he, growling.
Jean did not think it best to revenge an insult which his reputation in the village too well justified.
“We want to speak to Monsieur the Mayor,” he answered. “There is terrible need of it. Go call him, Monsieur Baptiste; he won’t blame you.”
“I’d like to see anybody blame me,” snapped out Baptiste.
It took ten minutes of talking and explaining to persuade the servant. Finally, the Bertauds were admitted to a little man, fat and red, very much annoyed at being dragged from his bed so early. It was M. Courtois.
They had decided that Philippe should speak.
“Monsieur Mayor,” he said, “we have come to announce to you a great misfortune. A crime has been committed at Monsieur de Trémorel’s.”
M.

===Chinese===

右侧是莫普雷瓦尔（Mauprévoir）森林，以及拉布雷什伯爵夫人（Countess de la Brèche）漂亮别致的乡村别墅；对岸是穆索（Mousseaux）与珀蒂堡（Petit-Bourg），那是阿瓜多（Aguado）从前的产业，如今属于一位著名的马车制造商；左侧那片美丽的杂木林属于特雷莫雷尔伯爵，那座宽阔的公园是埃蒂奥勒（d’Etiolles）庄园，更远处则是科尔贝伊；那座屋顶高过橡树的庞大建筑，是达尔布莱（Darblay）磨坊。
奥尔西瓦尔镇长在村子上方拥有一栋气派体面的宅邸。库尔图瓦先生早年经营布匹生意，白手起家，分文皆无，历经30年辛劳打拼，退休时坐拥整整400万法郎巨资。
随后他打算同妻儿安度平静岁月，冬居巴黎，夏住乡间别墅。
然而突然之间，人们发觉他心神不宁、躁动不安。野心撩拨着他的心弦。他使尽浑身解数，促成大家“硬逼”他接下奥尔西瓦尔镇长的职位。而他最终接受了，正如他自己逢人便说的那样，完全是出于“勉为其难”。这个职位既是他的苦恼，也是他的幸福；表面上是苦恼，内心深处却是真真切切的得意与欢喜。
他极擅长锁紧双眉，抱怨政务操劳之苦；而当他腰系金线缎带，得意洋洋地走在市政官员队伍前头时，整个人显得格外神气。
贝尔托父子敲响沉重的门环时，镇长家里所有人都还沉睡未醒。片刻之后，一名睡眼惺忪的男仆出现在一楼的一扇窗前。
“大清早吵什么，你们这两个无赖？”他没好气地喝问。
让觉得没必要为这句辱骂动怒，毕竟他们在村里的名声确实太招人非议了。
“我们要见镇长先生，”让答道，“十万火急。去叫他吧，巴蒂斯特先生（Monsieur Baptiste），他绝不会怪你的。”
“我倒要看看谁敢怪我，”巴蒂斯特没好气地顶了回去。
他们费了整整十分钟解释劝说，男仆才勉强应允。最终，贝尔托父子被领到一个身材矮胖、满面通红的男人面前；大清早被从被窝里拽起来，这人显然满腹怨气。此人正是库尔图瓦先生。
他们事先商定好由菲利普开口。
“镇长先生，”他说，“我们是来向您报告一件天大的祸事的。特雷莫雷尔先生家里发生了一起罪案。”

===Original===

Courtois was a friend of the count’s; he became whiter than his shirt at this sudden news.
“My God!” stammered he, unable to control his emotion, “what do you say﻿—a crime!”
“Yes; we have just discovered a body; and as sure as you are here, I believe it to be that of the countess.”
The worthy man raised his arms heavenward, with a wandering air.
“But where, when?”
“Just now, at the foot of the park, as we were going to take up our nets.”
“It is horrible!” exclaimed the good M. Courtois; “what a calamity! So worthy a lady! But it is not possible﻿—you must be mistaken; I should have been informed﻿—”
“We saw it distinctly, Monsieur Mayor.”
“Such a crime in my village! Well, you have done wisely to come here. I will dress at once, and will hasten off﻿—no, wait.” He reflected a moment, then called:
“Baptiste!”
The valet was not far off. With ear and eye alternately pressed against the keyhole, he heard and looked with all his might. At the sound of his master’s voice he had only to stretch out his hand and open the door.
“Monsieur called me?”
“Run to the justice of the peace,” said the mayor. “There is not a moment to lose. A crime has been committed﻿—perhaps a murder﻿—you must go quickly. And you,” addressing the poachers, “await me here while I slip on my coat.”
The justice of the peace at Orcival, M. Plantat﻿—“Papa Plantat,” as he was called﻿—was formerly an attorney at Melun. At fifty, Mr. Plantat, whose career had been one of unbroken prosperity, lost in the same month, his wife, whom he adored, and his two sons, charming youths, one eighteen, the other twenty-two years old. These successive losses crushed a man whom thirty years of happiness left without defence against misfortune. For a long time his reason was despaired of. Even the sight of a client, coming to trouble his grief, to recount stupid tales of self-interest, exasperated him. It was not surprising that he sold out his professional effects and goodwill at half price. He wished to establish himself at his ease in his grief, with the certainty of not being disturbed in its indulgence.
But the intensity of his mourning diminished, and the ills of idleness came. The justiceship of the peace at Orcival was vacant, and M. Plantat applied for and obtained it. Once installed in this office, he suffered less from ennui.

===Chinese===

库尔图瓦是伯爵的朋友；听到这突如其来的噩耗，他的脸色瞬间变得比衬衫还要惨白。
“天哪！”他结结巴巴地说，无法克制内心的震颤，“你说什么——罪案！”
“是的；我们刚刚发现了一具尸体；就像您此刻站在这里一样千真万确，我相信那就是伯爵夫人。”
这位体面的绅士茫然无措地向天举起双手。
“可是在哪儿，什么时候的事？”
“就在刚才，在庄园公园边上，我们正要去收网的时候。”
“太可怕了！”善良的库尔图瓦先生失声喊道，“真是天降横祸！多么端庄贤淑的夫人啊！但这绝不可能——你们一定是弄错了；要是出事我早该接到消息——”
“我们看得真真切切，镇长先生。”
“在我的村子里竟然发生这种大案！好吧，你们跑来报告是对的。我马上去穿衣服，立刻赶过去——不，等等。”他思忖了片刻，随即大喊：
“巴蒂斯特！”
男仆其实就在不远处。他把耳朵和眼睛轮流贴在门锁孔上，正使尽全力偷听窥视。听到主人的召唤，他只需一抬手就推开了门。
“先生叫我？”
“快跑去找治安法官，”镇长吩咐道，“一刻也不能耽误。出大案了——很可能是谋杀——你得动作麻利点。至于你们，”他转向那两个偷猎者，“在这儿等着，我去套上外衣。”
奥尔西瓦尔的治安法官普朗塔先生（M. Plantat）——人称“普朗塔老爹”——从前在默伦（Melun）当过代理人。50岁那年，仕途一帆风顺的普朗塔先生，在同一个月内接连痛失所爱：他挚爱的妻子，以及两个正值青春年华的儿子，一个18岁，另一个22岁。这一连串的打击彻底击垮了他，30年的顺遂幸福让他在灾难面前毫无招架之力。有很长一段时间，旁人都以为他神智失常了。甚至只要有当事人前来打扰他的悲痛、喋喋不休地讲那些鸡毛蒜皮的利益纠纷，都会让他大发雷霆。因此，他以半价贱卖了自己的事务所和执业权也就不足为奇了。他只想安安静静地沉浸在自己的哀伤之中，确信不会再有人来打扰他品尝痛苦。
但随着哀伤的烈度逐渐消退，无所事事的百无聊赖接踵而至。适逢奥尔西瓦尔的治安法官职位空缺，普朗塔先生便申请并获得了该职。一走马上任，他的苦闷确实减轻了许多。

===Original===

This man, who saw his life drawing to an end, undertook to interest himself in the thousand diverse cases which came before him. He applied to these all the forces of a superior intelligence, the resources of a mind admirably fitted to separate the false from the true among the lies he was forced to hear. He persisted, besides, in living alone, despite the urging of M. Courtois; pretending that society fatigued him, and that an unhappy man is a bore in company.
Misfortune, which modifies characters, for good or bad, had made him, apparently, a great egotist. He declared that he was only interested in the affairs of life as a critic tired of its active scenes. He loved to make a parade of his profound indifference for everything, swearing that a rain of fire descending upon Paris, would not even make him turn his head. To move him seemed impossible. “What’s that to me?” was his invariable exclamation.
Such was the man who, a quarter of an hour after Baptiste’s departure, entered the mayor’s house.
M. Plantat was tall, thin, and nervous. His physiognomy was not striking. His hair was short, his restless eyes seemed always to be seeking something, his very long nose was narrow and sharp. After his affliction, his mouth, formerly well shaped, became deformed; his lower lip had sunk, and gave him a deceptive look of simplicity.
“They tell me,” said he, at the threshold, “that Madame de Trémorel has been murdered.”
“These men here, at least, pretend so,” answered the mayor, who had just reappeared.
M. Courtois was no longer the same man. He had had time to make his toilet a little. His face attempted to express a haughty coldness. He had been reproaching himself for having been wanting in dignity, in showing his grief before the Bertauds. “Nothing ought to agitate a man in my position,” said he to himself. And, being terribly agitated, he forced himself to be calm, cold, and impassible.
M. Plantat was so naturally.
“This is a very sad event,” said he, in a tone which he forced himself to make perfectly disinterested; “but after all, how does it concern us? We must, however, hurry and ascertain whether it is true. I have sent for the brigadier, and he will join us.”
“Let us go,” said M. Courtois; “I have my scarf in my pocket.”
They hastened off. Philippe and his father went first, the young man eager and impatient, the old one sombre and thoughtful.

===Chinese===

这个自知余生无多的人，开始对自己经手的那上千起形形色色的案子产生了兴趣。他将超群的智慧倾注其中，凭借敏锐过人的心智，从不得不听的无数谎言中抽丝剥茧，辨别真伪。此外，尽管库尔图瓦先生屡屡相劝，他仍坚持离群索居，声称社交令他疲惫，而一个失意之人混在人群中也只会惹人嫌厌。
苦难改变了人的性情，或善或恶，而在外人看来，苦难让他变成了一个极其自私冷漠的人。他声称自己对人世间的事不过像一个厌倦了名利场的看客那样冷眼旁观。他喜欢炫耀自己对一切的漠不关心，甚至发誓哪怕烈火之雨降临巴黎，也绝不会让他多转一下脖子。要想打动他似乎比登天还难。“那关我什么事？”是他挂在嘴边的口头禅。
巴蒂斯特离开一刻钟后，走进镇长家的正是这么一个人。
普朗塔先生身材瘦长，神经质。他的相貌并不出众。头发剪得很短，那双焦躁不安的眼睛仿佛总在搜寻着什么，鼻梁狭长而尖削。遭此横祸之后，他原本端正的嘴唇变了形；下唇微微耷拉着，给人一种颇具欺骗性的木讷之感。
“他们告诉我，”他一迈进门槛就说，“特雷莫雷尔夫人遇害了。”
“至少这屋里的两个人是这么咬定的，”刚刚重新露面的镇长回答道。
库尔图瓦先生此刻已经判若两人。他趁机把自己略作打理。脸上一副强作镇定的傲慢神情。他暗自懊恼自己刚才在贝尔托父子面前流露出悲痛，有失体统。“身居我这样的地位，任何事都不应让我失态，”他暗自思忖。尽管内心掀起了惊涛骇浪，他却竭力装出一副沉着、冷漠、波澜不惊的模样。
而普朗塔先生则生来如此。
“这确实是一桩极悲惨的变故，”他用一种强装得完全事不关己的口吻说道，“但归根结底，又跟我们有什么相干？不过，我们还是得赶紧去查证是否属实。我已经派人去叫宪兵班长，他会在路上跟我们会合。”
“我们走吧，”库尔图瓦先生说，“我已经把缎带揣在口袋里了。”
他们匆匆出发了。菲利普和他的父亲走在前头，年轻人焦急迫切，老头则阴郁深沉、心事重重。

===Original===

The mayor, at each step, made some exclamation.
“I can’t understand it,” muttered he; “a murder in my commune! a commune where, in the memory of men, no crime has been committed!”
And he directed a suspicious glance toward the two Bertauds. The road which led toward the château of M. de Trémorel was an unpleasant one, shut in by walls a dozen feet high. On one side is the park of the Marchioness de Lanascol; on the other the spacious garden of Saint Jouan. The going and coming had taken time; it was nearly eight o’clock when the mayor, the justice, and their guides stopped before the gate of M. de Trémorel.
The mayor rang. The bell was very large; only a small gravelled court of five or six yards separated the gate from the house; nevertheless no one appeared.
The mayor rang more vigorously, then with all his strength; but in vain.
Before the gate of Mme. de Lanascol’s château, nearly opposite, a groom was standing, occupied in cleaning and polishing a bridle-bit. “It’s of no use to ring, gentlemen,” said this man; “there’s nobody in the château.”
“How! nobody?” asked the mayor, surprised.
“I mean,” said the groom, “that there is no one there but the master and mistress. The servants all went away last evening by the 8:40 train to Paris, to the wedding of the old cook, Madame Denis. They ought to return this morning by the first train. I was invited myself﻿—”
“Great God!” interrupted M. Courtois, “then the count and countess remained alone last night?”
“Entirely alone, Monsieur Mayor.”
“It is horrible!”
M. Plantat seemed to grow impatient during this dialogue. “Come,” said he, “we cannot stay forever at the gate. The gendarmes do not come; let us send for the locksmith.” Philippe was about to hasten off, when, at the end of the road, singing and laughing were heard. Five persons, three women and two men, soon appeared.
“Ah, there are the people of the château,” cried the groom, whom this morning visit seemed to annoy, “they ought to have a key.”
The domestics, seeing the group about the gate, became silent and hastened their steps. One of them began to run ahead of the others; it was the count’s valet de chambre.
“These gentlemen perhaps wish to speak to Monsieur the Count?” asked he, having bowed to M. Plantat.
“We have rung five times, as hard as we could,” said the mayor.
“It is surprising,” said the valet de chambre, “the count sleeps very lightly.

===Chinese===

镇长每走一步都要发出一声惊叹。
“我简直无法理解，”他咕哝着，“在我的公社里发生谋杀！在一个人们记事以来从未发生过任何罪案的公社里！”
说着，他怀疑地瞥了贝尔托父子一眼。通往特雷莫雷尔先生城堡的道路并不好走，两侧被12英尺高的围墙夹在中间。一边是拉纳斯科尔侯爵夫人（Marchioness de Lanascol）的公园；另一边是圣茹昂（Saint Jouan）宽阔的花园。这一来一回耽搁了不少时间；当镇长、法官以及带路的人停在特雷莫雷尔先生的大门前时，时间已经将近8点了。
镇长拉响了门铃。门铃非常大；门与房屋之间只隔着一个五六码见方的碎石铺成的小院；然而，却没有人出来应门。
镇长更用力地拉铃，接着使出了吃奶的力气；但依旧毫无回音。
在路对面的拉纳斯科尔夫人城堡大门前，站着一名马夫，正忙着擦拭打磨一副马嚼子。“拉铃也没用，先生们，”那人说道，“城堡里一个人都没有。”
“怎么！没人？”镇长惊奇地问。
“我的意思是，”马夫说，“里面除了主人和太太，谁都不在。仆人们昨晚全坐8点40分的火车去巴黎了，去参加老厨娘德尼夫人（Madame Denis）的婚礼。他们该搭今早头班火车赶回来。我本人也收到了请帖呢——”
“老天爷啊！”库尔图瓦先生打断了他，“那么说，昨晚就只有伯爵和伯爵夫人两个人留下了？”
“就他们俩，镇长先生。”
“太可怕了！”
在这番对话中，普朗塔先生似乎显得有些不耐烦。“行了，”他说，“我们不能老在大门口耗着。宪兵还没来；咱们打发人去找个锁匠吧。”菲利普正准备飞奔而去，就在这时，路尽头传来了歌声和欢笑声。很快，五个人影出现了，三女两男。
“啊，城堡里的人回来了，”马夫嚷道，这场晨间造访似乎打扰了他的兴致，“他们身上该有钥匙。”
仆人们看到聚在大门口的一群人，顿时止住了嬉闹，加快了脚步。其中一人快步跑在前头；那是伯爵的贴身男仆。
“几位先生想必是要见伯爵先生吧？”他向普朗塔先生鞠了一躬后问道。
“我们已经使劲拉了五次铃了，”镇长说。
“真奇怪，”贴身男仆说，“伯爵睡觉一向很惊醒的。

===Original===

Perhaps he has gone out.”
“Horror!” cried Philippe. “Both of them have been murdered!” These words shocked the servants, whose gayety announced a reasonable number of healths drunk to the happiness of the newly wedded pair. M. Courtois seemed to be studying the attitude of old Bertaud.
“A murder!” muttered the valet de chambre. “It was for money then; it must have been known﻿—”
“What?” asked the mayor.
“Monsieur the Count received a very large sum yesterday morning.”
“Large! yes,” added a chambermaid. “He had a large package of bank-bills. Madame even said to Monsieur that she should not shut her eyes the whole night, with this immense sum in the house.”
There was a silence; each one looked at the others with a frightened air. M. Courtois reflected.
“At what hour did you leave the château last evening?” asked he of the servants.
“At eight o’clock; we had dinner early.”
“You went away all together?”
“Yes, sir.”
“You did not leave each other?”
“Not a minute.”
“And you returned all together?”
The servants exchanged a significant look.
“All,” responded a chambermaid﻿—“that is to say, no. One left us on reaching the Lyons station at Paris; it was Guespin.”
“Yes, sir; he went away, saying that he would rejoin us at Wepler’s, in the Batignolles, where the wedding took place.” The mayor nudged the justice with his elbow, as if to attract his attention, and continued to question the chambermaid.
“And this Guespin, as you call him﻿—did you see him again?”
“No, sir. I asked several times during the evening in vain, what had become of him; his absence seemed to me suspicious.” Evidently the chambermaid tried to show superior perspicacity. A little more, and she would have talked of presentiments.
“Has this Guespin been long in the house?”
“Since spring.”
“What were his duties?”
“He was sent from Paris by the house of the ‘Skilful Gardener,’ to take care of the rare flowers in Madame’s conservatory.”
“And did he know of this money?”
The domestics again exchanged significant glances.
“Yes,” they answered in chorus, “we had talked a great deal about it among ourselves.”
The chambermaid added: “He even said to me, ‘To think that Monsieur the Count has enough money in his cabinet to make all our fortunes.’ ”
“What kind of a man is this?”
This question absolutely extinguished the talkativeness of the servants.

===Chinese===

也许他出门了。”
“太可怕了！”菲利普喊道，“他们两个都被谋杀了！”这句话惊呆了仆人们，他们脸上的喜色表明刚才在婚礼上为新婚夫妇的幸福喝了不少喜酒。库尔图瓦先生似乎在暗中观察老贝尔托的举止神情。
“谋杀！”贴身男仆喃喃道，“那准是为了图财；这事肯定走漏了风声——”
“什么事？”镇长追问道。
“伯爵先生昨天上午刚收到了一大笔钱。”
“一大笔！可不是嘛，”一个女仆插嘴道，“好大一包钞票呢。夫人甚至对先生说，屋里放着这么大一笔巨款，她整夜都合不上眼。”
四周顿时一片死寂；众人面面相觑，脸上满是惊恐。库尔图瓦先生沉吟着。
“你们昨晚是几点离开城堡的？”他盘问仆人们。
“8点钟；我们早早就吃了晚饭。”
“你们是一起走的吗？”
“是的，先生。”
“中间没有分开过？”
“一分钟都没分开。”
“回来也是全都在一起？”
仆人们交换了一个意味深长的眼神。
“都在，”一个女仆回答道，“——也就是说，也不是全在。到巴黎里昂火车站时，有一个人离开了我们；是盖斯潘（Guespin）。”
“是的，先生；他走的时候说，会在巴蒂尼奥勒（Batignolles）举行婚礼的韦普勒（Wepler）餐馆跟我们会合。”镇长用手肘轻轻碰了碰法官，示意他注意，随后继续盘问那个女仆。
“这个盖斯潘，照你们这么叫他——你们后来又见到他了吗？”
“没有，先生。整个晚上我都白费口舌问了好几次他去哪儿了；他的缺席让我觉得十分可疑。”显然，女仆极力想展现自己非凡的敏锐洞察力。再多说一句，她恐怕就要扯到预感上去了。
“这个盖斯潘在府里干了多久了？”
“从今年春天开始。”
“他负责什么差事？”
“他是巴黎‘能工巧匠花木行’派来的，专门照料夫人温室里的名贵花卉。”
“他也知道这笔钱的事吗？”
仆人们再次交换了意味深长的眼色。
“知道，”他们异口同声地回答，“我们在私底下议论过好多次呢。”
女仆又补充道：“他甚至还对我说过：‘想想看，伯爵先生书房里的钱，足够让我们所有人都发家致富了。’”
“他是个什么样的人？”
这个问题彻底让这帮多嘴多舌的仆人闭上了嘴。

===Original===

No one dared to speak, perceiving that the least word might serve as the basis of a terrible accusation. But the groom of the house opposite, who burned to mix himself up in the affair, had none of these scruples. “Guespin,” answered he, “is a good fellow. Lord, what jolly things he knows! He knows everything you can imagine. It appears he has been rich in times past, and if he wished﻿—But *dame*! he loves to have his work all finished, and go off on sprees. He’s a crack billiard-player, I can tell you.”
Papa Plantat, while listening in an apparently absentminded way to these depositions, or rather these scandals, carefully examined the wall and the gate. He now turned, and interrupting the groom:
“Enough of this,” said he, to the great scandal of M. Courtois. “Before pursuing this interrogatory, let us ascertain the crime, if crime there is; for it is not proved. Let whoever has the key, open the gate.”
The valet de chambre had the key; he opened the gate, and all entered the little court. The gendarmes had just arrived. The mayor told the brigadier to follow him, and placed two men at the gate, ordering them not to permit anyone to enter or go out, unless by his orders. Then the valet de chambre opened the door of the house.

===Chinese===

谁也不敢吭声了，大家都意识到哪怕最微不足道的一句话，都可能成为可怕指控的根据。但对面人家的马夫一心想掺和进这桩案子里，可没有这些顾忌。“盖斯潘嘛，”他回答道，“是个挺不错的小伙子。天哪，他肚子里好玩的事儿多着呢！你想得出的名堂他全懂。听说他以前也阔过，要是他愿意的话——可是，哎呀！他这人就喜欢把活儿干完然后四处鬼混。我跟你们说，他可是个台球高手。”
普朗塔老爹看似漫不经心地听着这些证词，或者不如说是风凉话，同时仔细检查着院墙和大门。这时他转过身来，打断了马夫的话：
“听够了，”他说，这让库尔图瓦先生大为愕然，“在继续盘问之前，咱们先弄清楚到底有没有发生命案；毕竟现在还没证实呢。谁拿着钥匙，把门打开。”
贴身男仆身上有钥匙；他打开了大门，众人鱼贯走入碎石铺成的小院。宪兵们刚好赶到。镇长吩咐宪兵班长跟紧自己，并在门口派驻了两名宪兵，严令未经他的许可，任何人不得进出。随后，贴身男仆打开了宅邸的大门。

## II / 第二章
===Original===

If there had been no crime, at least something extraordinary had taken place at the château; the impassible justice might have been convinced of it, as soon as he had stepped into the vestibule. The glass door leading to the garden was wide open, and three of the panes were shattered into a thousand pieces. The carpeting of waxed canvas between the doors had been torn up, and on the white marble slabs large drops of blood were visible. At the foot of the staircase was a stain larger than the rest, and upon the lowest step a splash hideous to behold.
Unfitted for such spectacles, or for the mission he had now to perform, M. Courtois became faint. Luckily, he borrowed from the idea of his official importance, an energy foreign to his character. The more difficult the preliminary examination of this affair seemed, the more determined he was to carry it on with dignity.
“Conduct us to the place where you saw the body,” said he to Bertaud. But Papa Plantat intervened.
“It would be wiser, I think,” he objected, “and more methodical, to begin by going through the house.”
“Perhaps﻿—yes﻿—true, that’s my own view,” said the mayor, grasping at the other’s counsel, as a drowning man clings to a plank. And he made all retire excepting the brigadier and the valet de chambre, the latter remaining to serve as guide. “Gendarmes,” cried he to the men guarding the gate, “see to it that no one goes out; prevent anybody from entering the house, and above all, let no one go into the garden.”
Then they ascended the staircase. Drops of blood were sprinkled all along the stairs. There was also blood on the baluster, and M. Courtois perceived, with horror, that his hands were stained.
When they had reached the first landing-stage, the mayor said to the valet de chambre:
“Tell me, my friend, did your master and mistress occupy the same chamber?”
“Yes, sir.”
“And where is their chamber?”
“There, sir.”
As he spoke, the valet de chambre staggered back terrified, and pointed to a door, the upper panel of which betrayed the imprint of a bloody hand. Drops of perspiration overspread the poor mayor’s forehead. He too was terrified, and could hardly keep on his feet. Alas, authority brings with it terrible obligations! The brigadier, an old soldier of the Crimea, visibly moved, hesitated.
M.

===Chinese===

哪怕还没确定是否发生了命案，城堡里也至少发生了非同寻常的事；冷若冰霜的治安法官一迈进门厅，便能对此深信不疑。通往花园的玻璃门大敞着，其中三块玻璃碎成了千万片。两道门之间铺着的涂蜡油布地毯被撕裂开来，白色大理石板上清晰可见大滴大滴的鲜血。楼梯脚下的血迹比别处更大，而最底下一级台阶上的一摊溅血更是触目惊心。
库尔图瓦先生平日里哪见过这等场面，更无力承担眼下需要履行的职责，顿时感到一阵眩晕虚脱。好在，他从对自己官方身份的看重中汲取了一种本不属于他性格的力量。对这桩案子的初步勘验显得越是棘手，他便越下定决心要体面威严地进行下去。
“带我们去你看到尸体的地方，”他对贝尔托说。但普朗塔老爹出面干预了。
“我认为，从搜查屋里开始会更明智、也更有条理一些，”他提出了异议。
“也许——对——没错，我也是这么想的，”镇长说道，紧紧抓住对方的建议，犹如落水者抓住了救命木板。他把除了宪兵班长和贴身男仆之外的所有人都打发退了出去，留着后者充当向导。“宪兵们，”他朝守门的卫兵喊道，“看好大门，任何人不得出去；阻止任何人进入宅邸，最重要的是，绝不能让人进花园。”
随后他们登上了楼梯。整段楼梯上都星星点点地洒着血滴。栏杆扶手上也有血迹，库尔图瓦先生惊恐地发觉自己的双手也沾上了血渍。
当他们来到第一处楼梯平台时，镇长对贴身男仆说：
“告诉我，我的朋友，你的男主人和女主人是住同一个房间吗？”
“是的，先生。”
“他们的房间在哪儿？”
“就在那儿，先生。”
贴身男仆一边说着，一边惊骇地向后踉跄了几步，用手指着一扇门，门的上半部嵌板上赫然印着一个清晰的血手印。大滴的冷汗从可怜的镇长额头上涔涔滚落。他也吓坏了，双腿几乎站立不稳。唉，权力总是伴随着可怕的重负！宪兵班长曾是一名克里米亚老兵，此刻也显出明显的动容，犹豫不决。

===Original===

Plantat alone, as tranquil as if he were in his garden, retained his coolness, and looked around upon the others.
“We must decide,” said he.
He entered the room; the rest followed.
There was nothing unusual in the apartment; it was a boudoir hung in blue satin, furnished with a couch and four armchairs, covered also with blue satin. One of the chairs was overturned.
They passed on to the bedchamber.
A frightful disorder appeared in this room. There was not an article of furniture, not an ornament, which did not betray that a terrible, enraged and merciless struggle had taken place between the assassins and their victims. In the middle of the chamber a small table was overturned, and all about it were scattered lumps of sugar, vermilion cups, and pieces of porcelain.
“Ah!” said the valet de chambre, “Monsieur and Madame were taking tea when the wretches came in!”
The mantel ornaments had been thrown upon the floor; the clock, in falling, had stopped at twenty minutes past three. Near the clock were the lamps; the globes were in pieces, the oil had been spilled.
The canopy of the bed had been torn down, and covered the bed. Someone must have clutched desperately at the draperies. All the furniture was overturned. The coverings of the chairs had been hacked by strokes of a knife, and in places the stuffing protruded. The secretary had been broken open; the writing-slide, dislocated, hung by its hinges; the drawers were open and empty, and everywhere, blood﻿—blood upon the carpet, the furniture, the curtains﻿—above all, upon the bed-curtains.
“Poor wretches!” stammered the mayor. “They were murdered here.”
Everyone for a moment was appalled. But meanwhile, the justice of the peace devoted himself to a minute scrutiny, taking notes upon his tablets, and looking into every corner. When he had finished:
“Come,” said he, “let us go into the other rooms.”
Everywhere there was the same disorder. A band of furious maniacs, or criminals seized with a frenzy, had certainly passed the night in the house.
The count’s library, especially, had been turned topsy-turvy. The assassins had not taken the trouble to force the locks; they had gone to work with a hatchet. Surely they were confident of not being overheard; for they must have struck tremendous blows to make the massive oaken bureau fly in pieces.
Neither parlor nor smoking-room had been respected.

===Chinese===

唯独普朗塔神色自若，就像在自家花园里闲逛一样，依旧保持着冷静，环顾着其他人。
“我们必须做出决断，”他说。
他迈步进了房间；其他人紧随其后。
小起居室里倒没有什么异样；这是一间挂着蓝色缎面挂毯的闺房，摆着一张睡榻和四张同样蒙着蓝色缎面的扶手椅。其中一张扶手椅被掀翻在地。
他们接着走向卧室。
这间屋子里呈现出一派骇人的凌乱。没有一件家具，没有一件摆设，不在昭示着凶手与受害者之间曾发生过一场怎样惨烈、狂暴而残酷的殊死搏斗。卧室中央的一张小桌子被掀翻了，四周散落着方糖、朱砂红瓷杯和瓷器碎片。
“啊！”贴身男仆惊呼道，“恶徒闯进来的时候，先生和太太正在喝茶！”
壁炉台上的摆件全被扫落到了地板上；座钟在摔落时停在了3点20分。座钟旁原本放着台灯；灯罩全碎了，灯油洒了一地。
床帐被硬生生扯了下来，罩在床上。显然有人曾绝望地死死拽住床幔。所有的家具都被掀翻在地。椅套被刀刃乱砍撕裂，几处露出了填充物。写字台被暴力砸开；折叠书写板脱了臼，只挂在一侧的铰链上；抽屉全被拉开且空空如也，到处都是血——地毯上、家具上、窗帘上全都是血——尤其是床幔上，血迹斑斑。
“可怜的人！”镇长结结巴巴地说道，“他们就是在这儿被杀害的。”
所有人一时间都被吓呆了。然而与此同时，治安法官却全神贯注于细致入微的搜查，在小本子上做着记录，查看着每一个角落。等他搜查完毕：
“走吧，”他说，“咱们去别的房间瞧瞧。”
到处都是同样的凌乱。毫无疑问，昨夜有一伙发狂的疯子，或是陷入歇斯底里狂暴状态的罪犯，在这座宅邸里肆虐了一整晚。
尤其是伯爵的书房，被翻了个底朝天。凶手甚至懒得去撬锁，而是直接抡起斧头劈砍。他们笃定不会被人听见；因为要想把厚实的橡木写字台砸成碎片，非得使出惊天动地的力道不可。
无论是客厅还是吸烟室，无一幸免。

===Original===

Couches, chairs, canopies were cut and torn as if they had been lunged at with swords. Two spare chambers for guests were all in confusion.
They then ascended to the second story.
There, in the first room which they penetrated, they found, beside a trunk which had been assaulted, but which was not opened, a hatchet for splitting wood which the valet de chambre recognized as belonging to the house.
“Do you understand now?” said the mayor to M. Plantat. “The assassins were in force, that’s clear. The murder accomplished, they scattered through the château, seeking everywhere the money they knew they would find here. One of them was engaged in breaking open this trunk, when the others, below, found the money; they called him; he hastened down, and thinking all further search useless, he left the hatchet here.”
“I see it,” said the brigadier, “just as if I had been here.”
The ground-floor, which they next visited, had been respected. Only, after the crime had been committed, and the money secured, the murderers had felt the necessity of refreshing themselves. They found the remains of their supper in the dining-room. They had eaten up all the cold meats left in the cupboard. On the table, beside eight empty bottles of wine and liqueurs, were ranged five glasses.
“There were five of them,” said the mayor.
By force of will, M. Courtois had recovered his self-possession.
“Before going to view the bodies,” said he, “I will send word to the procureur of Corbeil. In an hour, we will have a judge of instruction, who will finish our painful task.”
A gendarme was instructed to harness the count’s buggy, and to hasten to the procureur. Then the mayor and the justice, followed by the brigadier, the valet de chambre, and the two Bertauds, took their way toward the river.
The park of Valfeuillu was very wide from right to left. From the house to the Seine it was almost two hundred steps. Before the house was a grassy lawn, interspersed with flowerbeds. Two paths led across the lawn to the riverbank.
But the murderers had not followed the paths. Making a shortcut, they had gone straight across the lawn. Their traces were perfectly visible. The grass was trampled and stamped down as if a heavy load had been dragged over it. In the midst of the lawn they perceived something red; M. Plantat went and picked it up.

===Chinese===

长榻、椅子、天篷被砍得稀烂，布满窟窿，就好像曾有人用利剑在上面疯狂乱刺一样。两间留宿客人的客房也全被翻得乱七八糟。
随后他们登上了三楼。
在那里，他们走进的第一间房里，在一只遭受过暴力劈砍但未被劈开的箱子旁，发现了一把劈柴用的短斧，贴身男仆认出那是宅邸里原本就有的工具。
“你现在明白了吗？”镇长对普朗塔先生说，“凶手人手充足，这是明摆着的。行凶之后，他们分散在城堡各处，到处搜寻他们明知藏在这里的巨款。其中一人正在砸这个箱子，楼下其他人找到了钱；他们一喊，他就匆忙赶下楼去，觉得没必要再搜下去了，斧头就落在了这里。”
“我全看明白了，”宪兵班长说，“就跟我亲眼瞧见了一样。”
他们随后巡视的一楼倒是完好无损。只是在作案得手并拿走钱财后，凶手们觉得有必要饱餐补充体力。他们在餐厅里发现了这帮人吃剩的宵夜残羹。橱柜里留下的所有冷盘熟食全被一扫而空。餐桌上，除了8个空的葡萄酒和烈酒瓶外，还整整齐齐地摆着5只酒杯。
“他们一共有五个人，”镇长断言道。
凭着强大的意志力，库尔图瓦先生重新找回了镇定自若的神态。
“在去看尸体之前，”他说，“我要派人去通知科尔贝伊（Corbeil）的检察官。一个小时内，预审法官就会赶到，接手我们这桩痛苦的差事。”
一名宪兵奉命给伯爵的双轮轻便马车套上马，飞奔去向检察官报信。随后，镇长和法官在班长、贴身男仆以及贝尔托父子的跟随下，向河边走去。
瓦尔菲伊庄园公园左右极其宽阔。从宅邸到塞纳河足有近200步的距离。宅邸前面是一片绿草如茵的草坪，其间点缀着花坛。两条小径横穿草坪通往河岸。
但凶手并没有走小径。他们图近抄了近道，径直穿过了草坪。他们的痕迹一目了然。草地被践踏踩实，就像曾经拖拽过重物一样。在草坪中央，他们发现了一件红色的东西；普朗塔先生走上前去把它捡了起来。

===Original===

It was a slipper, which the valet de chambre recognized as the count’s. Farther on, they found a white silk handkerchief, which the valet declared he had often seen around the count’s neck. This handkerchief was stained with blood.
At last they arrived at the riverbank, under the willows from which Philippe had intended to cut off a branch; there they saw the body. The sand at this place was much indented by feet seeking a firm support. Everything indicated that here had been the supreme struggle.
M. Courtois understood all the importance of these traces.
“Let no one advance,” said he, and, followed by the justice of the peace, he approached the corpse. Although the face could not be distinguished, both recognized the countess. Both had seen her in this gray robe, adorned with blue trimmings.
Now, how came she there?
The mayor thought that having succeeded in escaping from the hands of the murderers, she had fled wildly. They had pursued her, had caught up with her there, and she had fallen to rise no more. This version explained the traces of the struggle. It must have been the count’s body that they had dragged across the lawn.
M. Courtois talked excitedly, trying to impose his ideas on the justice. But M. Plantat hardly listened; you might have thought him a hundred leagues from Valfeuillu; he only responded by monosyllables﻿—yes, no, perhaps. And the worthy mayor gave himself great pains; he went and came, measured steps, minutely scrutinized the ground.
There was not at this place more than a foot of water. A mud-bank, upon which grew some clumps of flags and some water-lilies, descended by a gentle decline from the bank to the middle of the river. The water was very clear, and there was no current; the slippery and slimy mire could be distinctly seen.
M. Courtois had gone thus far in his investigations, when he was struck by a sudden idea.
“Bertaud,” said he, “come here.”
The old poacher obeyed.
“You say that you saw the body from your boat?”
“Yes, Monsieur Mayor.”
“Where is your boat?”
“There, hauled up to that field.”
“Well, lead us to it.”
It was clear to all that this order had a great effect upon the man. He trembled and turned pale under his rough skin, tanned as it was by sun and storm. He was even seen to cast a menacing look toward his son.
“Let us go,” said he at last.

===Chinese===

那是一只拖鞋，贴身男仆认出那是伯爵的。再往前走，他们找到了一条白丝绸手帕，男仆声称常常看到伯爵围在脖子上。这条手帕上沾满了鲜血。
终于，他们来到了河岸边，就在菲利普原本打算砍下一截树枝的那几棵柳树下；在那里，他们看到了尸体。此处的沙滩上布满了想要寻求牢固支点的深深脚印。一切迹象都表明，这里曾发生过最后的殊死搏斗。
库尔图瓦先生深知这些痕迹的重要性。
“谁都别往前走，”他说着，在治安法官的陪同下走近了尸体。虽然面部难以辨认，但两人都认出这就是伯爵夫人。他们俩都曾见过她穿这件带有蓝色饰边的灰色丝绸长裙。
可是，她怎么会到这儿来的呢？
镇长认为，她设法从凶手手中逃脱后，拼命狂奔奔逃至此。凶手一路穷追不舍，在这里追上了她，而她就此倒下，再也没能站起来。这种推测解释了打斗的痕迹。被拖过草坪的，想必一定是伯爵的尸体。
库尔图瓦先生情绪激动地侃侃而谈，试图把自己的想法灌输给法官。但普朗塔先生几乎充耳不闻；你甚至会以为他的心思远在100里格之外的别处；他只是用单音节词应付着——是、不是、也许。而这位可敬的镇长却不辞辛劳，来回走动，丈量步伐，巨细靡遗地勘验地面。
在这个地方，水深不过一英尺。一片生长着几丛菖蒲和睡莲的泥滩，从岸边平缓地延伸至河心。河水清澈见底，没有水流；湿滑泥泞的淤泥清晰可见。
库尔图瓦先生的调查正进行到这里，脑海中突然灵光一闪。
“贝尔托，”他说，“过来。”
老偷猎者依言走上前去。
“你说你是从小船上看到尸体的？”
“是的，镇长先生。”
“你的船在哪儿？”
“就在那儿，拖在那片田边上。”
“好吧，带我们去看看。”
所有人都看出这个命令对这老头产生了极大的震动。在他那张饱经风吹日晒、粗糙黝黑的脸皮下，他浑身发颤，脸色变得煞白。人们甚至看到他朝儿子投去了一记充满怨毒与威胁的目光。
“走吧，”他终于开口道。

===Original===

They were returning to the house when the valet proposed to pass over the ditch. “That will be the quickest way,” said he, “I will go for a ladder which we will put across.”
He went off, and quickly reappeared with his improvised footbridge. But at the moment he was adjusting it, the mayor cried out to him:
“Stop!”
The imprints left by the Bertauds on both sides of the ditch had just caught his eye.
“What is this?” said he; “evidently someone has crossed here, and not long ago; for the traces of the steps are quite fresh.”
After an examination of some minutes he ordered that the ladder should be placed farther off. When they had reached the boat, he said to Jean, “Is this the boat with which you went to take up your nets this morning?”
“Yes.”
“Then,” resumed M. Courtois, “what implements did you use? your cast net is perfectly dry; this boat-hook and these oars have not been wet for twenty-four hours.”
The distress of the father and son became more and more evident.
“Do you persist in what you say, Bertaud?” said the mayor.
“Certainly.”
“And you, Philippe?”
“Monsieur,” stammered the young man, “we have told the truth.”
“Really!” said M. Courtois, in an ironical tone. “Then you will explain to the proper authorities how it was that you could see anything from a boat which you had not entered. It will be proved to you, also, that the body is in a position where it is impossible to see it from the middle of the river. Then you will still have to tell what these footprints on the grass are, which go from your boat to the place where the ditch has been crossed several times and by several persons.”
The two Bertauds hung their heads.
“Brigadier,” ordered the mayor, “arrest these two men in the name of the law, and prevent all communication between them.”
Philippe seemed to be ill. As for old Jean, he contented himself with shrugging his shoulders and saying to his son:
“Well, you would have it so, wouldn’t you?”
While the brigadier led the two poachers away, and shut them up separately, and under the guard of his men, the justice and the mayor returned to the park. “With all this,” muttered M. Courtois, “no traces of the count.”
They proceeded to take up the body of the countess.

===Chinese===

他们正要返回宅邸，这时男仆提议跨过壕沟。“那是最近的路，”他说，“我去拿架梯子横搭在上面。”
他快步走开，很快拿着充当临时便桥的梯子折返回来。但就在他正要架设梯子的一刹那，镇长冲他大喊：
“住手！”
壕沟两侧贝尔托父子留下的脚印刚刚落入了他的眼帘。
“这是什么？”他说，“显然有人从这儿跨过去过，而且时间并不久；因为脚印痕迹十分新鲜。”
勘验了几分钟后，他命令把梯子架在更远的地方。当他们走到小船跟前时，他对老让说：“这就是你今早去收网用的那只船吗？”
“是的。”
“那么，”库尔图瓦先生追问道，“你用了什么渔具？你的撒网完全是干透的；这根篙子和这副船桨起码有24小时没沾过一滴水了。”
父子俩的窘迫与慌乱变得越来越明显。
“贝尔托，你还坚持你刚才的说法吗？”镇长质问道。
“当然。”
“你呢，菲利普？”
“先生，”年轻人结结巴巴地说，“我们说的是真话。”
“真话吗！”库尔图瓦先生用讥讽的口吻冷笑一声，“那你就得向有关官府解释清楚，你怎么可能坐在一只根本没下水的小船里看清任何东西。他们还会向你证明，尸体所在的位置从河中央是根本不可能看得到的。另外，你还得解释清楚草地上这些脚印是怎么回事，它们从你的船一直延伸到壕沟被好几个人来回跨过好几次的地方。”
贝尔托父子俩低下了头。
“班长，”镇长命令道，“以法律的名义逮捕这两个人，严禁他们之间有任何交谈串供。”
菲利普仿佛浑身脱力病倒了一般。至于老让，他只是耸了耸肩，对儿子咕哝道：
“哼，你非要来报信，这下满意了吧？”
当宪兵班长押解着两名偷猎者离开，在部下的看管下将他们分别单独关押时，法官和镇长返回了公园。“忙活了半天，”库尔图瓦先生喃喃自语，“还是不见伯爵的踪影。”
他们接着着手起运伯爵夫人的遗体。

===Original===

The mayor sent for two planks, which, with a thousand precautions, they placed on the ground, being able thus to move the countess without effacing the imprints necessary for the legal examination. Alas! it was indeed she who had been the beautiful, the charming Countess de Trémorel! Here were her smiling face, her lovely, speaking eyes, her fine, sensitive mouth.
There remained nothing of her former self. The face was unrecognizable, so soiled and wounded was it. Her clothes were in tatters. Surely a furious frenzy had moved the monsters who had slain the poor lady! She had received more than twenty knife-wounds, and must have been struck with a stick, or rather with a hammer; she had been dragged by her feet and by her hair!
In her left hand she grasped a strip of common cloth, torn, doubtless, from the clothes of one of the assassins. The mayor, in viewing the spectacle, felt his legs fail him, and supported himself on the arm of the impassible Plantat.
“Let us carry her to the house,” said the justice, “and then we will search for the count.”
The valet and brigadier (who had now returned) called on the domestics for assistance. The women rushed into the garden. There was then a terrible concert of cries, lamentations, and imprecations.
“The wretches! So noble a mistress! So good a lady!”
M. and Mme. de Trémorel, one could see, were adored by their people.
The countess had just been laid upon the billiard-table, on the ground-floor, when the judge of instruction and a physician were announced.
“At last!” sighed the worthy mayor; and in a lower tone he added, “the finest medals have their reverse.”
For the first time in his life, he seriously cursed his ambition, and regretted being the most important personage in Orcival.

===Chinese===

镇长派人找来两块木板，大家万分小心地平铺在地上，这样既能抬移伯爵夫人，又不会破坏法医检验所必需的痕迹。唉！这确实就是曾经美丽迷人的特雷莫雷尔伯爵夫人！这里曾经有过她巧笑倩兮的娇容、顾盼生辉的双眸、精致秀气的朱唇。
然而昔日的风采已荡然无存。她的面容已无法辨认，沾满了污垢，伤痕累累。身上的衣衫被撕成了碎片。杀害这位可怜夫人的恶魔们必定是处于一种狂暴的歇斯底里之中！她身上挨了20多处刀伤，而且肯定还被人用棍棒、甚至更可能是铁锤重击过；她曾被人拖着双脚和头发在地上拖行！
在她的左手里，紧紧攥着一截粗布布条，毫无疑问是从某位凶手的衣服上硬扯下来的。镇长目睹这惨绝人寰的景象，感到双腿发软，不得不倚靠在冷峻不动的普朗塔手臂上。
“先把她抬进屋里吧，”治安法官说，“然后再搜寻伯爵。”
男仆和已经折返的班长招呼其他仆人前来帮忙。仆役女人们哭喊着冲进花园。现场顿时爆发出一片可怕的号哭、哀悼与咒骂声。
“这群天杀的恶棍！多么高贵的女主人！多么仁慈的太太啊！”
任谁都能看得出来，特雷莫雷尔夫妇深受府里下人的爱戴。
伯爵夫人的遗体刚被安放在一楼的台球桌上，外面便通报预审法官和一名法医到了。
“总算来了！”这位可敬的镇长长舒了一口气；随后他又压低声音补充道，“再耀眼的勋章也有其阴暗的背面。”
平生第一次，他由衷地咒骂起自己的野心，后悔当上了奥尔西瓦尔头一号显赫的人物。

## III / 第三章
===Original===

The judge of instruction of the tribunal at Corbeil, was M. Antoine Domini, a remarkable man, since called to higher functions. He was forty years of age, of a prepossessing person, and endowed with a very expressive, but too grave physiognomy. In him seemed typified the somewhat stiff solemnity of the magistracy. Penetrated with the dignity of his office, he sacrificed his life to it, rejecting the most simple distractions, and the most innocent pleasures.
He lived alone, seldom showing himself abroad; rarely received his friends, not wishing, as he said, that the weaknesses of the man should derogate from the sacred character of the judge. This latter reason had deterred him from marrying, though he felt the need of a domestic sphere.
Always and everywhere he was the magistrate﻿—that is, the representative, even to fanaticism, of what he thought the most august institution on the earth. Naturally gay, he would double-lock himself in when he wished to laugh. He was witty; but if a bright sally escaped him, you may be sure he repented of it. Body and soul he gave to his vocation; and no one could bring more conscientiousness to the discharge of what he thought to be his duty. He was also inflexible. It was monstrous, in his eyes, to discuss an article of the code. The law spoke; it was enough; he shut his eyes, covered his ears, and obeyed.
From the day when a legal investigation commenced, he did not sleep, and he employed every means to discover the truth. Yet he was not regarded as a good judge of instruction; to contend by tricks with a prisoner was repugnant to him; to lay a snare for a rogue he thought debasing; in short, he was obstinate﻿—obstinate to foolishness, sometimes to absurdity; even to denying the existence of the sun at midday.
The mayor and Papa Plantat hastened to meet M. Domini. He bowed to them gravely, as if he had not known them, and presenting to them a man of some sixty years who accompanied him:
“Messieurs,” said he, “this is Doctor Gendron.”
Papa Plantat shook hands with the doctor; the mayor smiled graciously at him, for Dr. Gendron was well-known in those parts; he was even celebrated, despite the nearness of Paris. Loving his art and exercising it with a passionate energy, he yet owed his renown less to his science than his manners.

===Chinese===

科尔贝伊（Corbeil）法庭的预审法官是安托万·多米尼先生（M. Antoine Domini），这是一位非凡的人物，后来升任了更高的司法职务。他年届40岁，相貌堂堂，面容极富表现力，却未免过于严肃。在他身上，似乎凝聚了司法官界特有的那种刻板而庄重的威严。他深谙自己职务的尊严，并为之奉献了一生，舍弃了最微不足道的心灵消遣与最无害的世俗享乐。
他离群索居，深居简出；极少招待朋友，用他自己的话说，是不希望凡夫俗子的软弱削弱法官神圣不可侵犯的特质。后一个原因使他至今未娶，尽管他内心深处也渴望拥有一方温馨的家庭天地。
无论何时何地，他都恪尽司法官的本色——也就是说，他是自己心目中世间最神圣制度近乎狂热的代表。他本性其实爽朗风趣，但若是想纵声大笑，总要把房门反锁两道才肯开怀。他机智敏锐，可一旦偶尔失口说出俏皮话，你尽可确信他事后定会懊悔不已。他将全副身心倾注于自己的天职之中；在履行自认的职责时，没人比他更恪守良知。与此同时，他也是个铁面无私之人。在他眼里，哪怕对法典的某一条款提出半点异议，都是大逆不道的狂妄。法律既已发话，便无可置疑；他闭上双眼，捂住耳朵，唯有绝对服从。
从某项司法调查开启的那一天起，他就彻夜难眠，穷尽一切手段去探求真相。然而，他却并不被公认为一名高明的预审法官；同犯人玩弄机巧心计令他深恶痛绝；给恶棍设下圈套在他看来无异于自甘堕落；简而言之，他固执己见——固执得近乎愚顽，有时甚至荒唐到了哪怕正午时分也敢矢口否认太阳存在的地步。
镇长和普朗塔老爹连忙迎上前去拜会多米尼先生。多米尼庄重地向他们躬身致意，神情冷淡得宛如彼此素不相识一般；他向两人引荐了同来的一位年约60岁的长者：
“两位先生，”他说，“这位是让德龙医生（Doctor Gendron）。”
普朗塔老爹同医生握了握手；镇长则向他报以亲切的微笑，因为让德龙医生在这一带颇有名望；尽管邻近巴黎，他依然声名远扬。他热爱医道，行医时怀着满腔热忱，然而他的名气在很大程度上并非源于他的医术，而是来自于他的脾气秉性。

===Original===

People said: “He is an original;” they admired his affectation of independence, of scepticism, and rudeness. He made his visits from five to nine in the morning﻿—all the worse for those for whom these hours were inconvenient. After nine o’clock the doctor was not to be had. The doctor was working for himself, the doctor was in his laboratory, the doctor was inspecting his cellar. It was rumored that he sought for secrets of practical chemistry, to augment still more his twenty thousand livres of income. And he did not deny it; for in truth he was engaged on poisons, and was perfecting an invention by which could be discovered traces of all the alkaloids which up to that time had escaped analysis. If his friends reproached him, even jokingly, on sending away sick people in the afternoon, he grew red with rage.
“*Parbleu!*” he answered, “I find you superb! I am a doctor four hours in the day. I am paid by hardly a quarter of my patients﻿—that’s three hours I give daily to humanity, which I despise. Let each of you do as much, and we shall see.”
The mayor conducted the newcomers into the drawing-room, where he installed himself to write down the results of his examination.
“What a misfortune for my town, this crime!” said he to M. Domini. “What shame! Orcival has lost its reputation.”
“I know nothing of the affair,” returned the judge. “The gendarme who went for me knew little about it.”
M. Courtois recounted at length what his investigation had discovered, not forgetting the minutest detail, dwelling especially on the excellent precautions which he had had the sagacity to take. He told how the conduct of the Bertauds had at first awakened his suspicions; how he had detected them, at least in a pointblank lie; how, finally, he had determined to arrest them. He spoke standing, his head thrown back, with wordy emphasis. The pleasure of speaking partially rewarded him for his recent distress.
“And now,” he concluded, “I have just ordered the most exact search, so that doubtless we shall find the count’s body. Five men, detailed by me, and all the people of the house, are searching the park. If their efforts are not crowned with success, I have here some fishermen who will drag the river.”
M. Domini held his tongue, only nodding his head from time to time, as a sign of approbation.

===Chinese===

人们常说：“他是个怪人；”大家对他那种标榜独立、愤世嫉俗和生硬无礼的作风津津乐道。他只在早晨5点到9点之间出诊——若是谁觉得这段时间不方便，那就只能自认倒霉。过了9点，休想再请动医生半步。医生要为自己干活了，医生要钻进实验室，医生要去巡视自家的地窖。坊间风传他在钻研实用化学的秘方，好让自己那每年20000锂的进项更上层楼。对此他也从不否认；因为事实上，他正潜心研究毒物学，并且正在完善一项重大发明，以便检测出迄今为止逃过一切化学分析的所有生物碱痕迹。若是朋友们哪怕出于玩笑责备他不该在下午把病人拒之门外，他便会气得满脸通红。
“见鬼！”他反唇相讥道，“瞧你们说得多轻巧！我一天当4个小时的医生，而我的病人里连四分之一给得起诊费的都没有——这意味着我每天无偿把3个小时奉献给了我所鄙视的人类。要是你们每个人也能做到这一步，咱们再来理论！”
镇长将新来的两位引进了客厅，他在那儿安置了一张桌子，用来记录勘查的结果。
“在我的镇子里发生这样的罪案，真是奇耻大祸啊！”他对多米尼先生说，“何等蒙羞！奥尔西瓦尔的清誉全毁了。”
“我对案情一无所知，”法官回答道，“前去通报的宪兵也说不清原委。”
库尔图瓦先生巨细靡遗地讲述了自己的勘验发现，唯恐遗漏最琐碎的细节，尤其不厌其烦地大谈特谈自己凭借过人睿智采取的种种防范措施。他讲到贝尔托父子的行径起初如何引起了他的怀疑；他又是如何当场戳穿了他们彻头彻尾的谎言；最终，他又如何断然决定将他们缉拿归案。他站着侃侃而谈，仰着头，辞藻浮夸。倾诉的快感在很大程度上抚平了他不久前遭受的惊吓。
“而眼下，”他总结道，“我刚刚下令展开最严密的搜寻，因此毫无疑问，我们很快就能找到伯爵的遗体。由我抽调的5名男子，加上宅邸里的所有仆役，正在搜查公园。若是他们的努力未能奏效，我还在这儿安排了几个渔民下河打捞。”
多米尼先生一言不发，只是不时微微点头，以示赞许。

===Original===

He was studying, weighing the details told him, building up in his mind a plan of proceeding.
“You have acted wisely,” said he, at last. “The misfortune is a great one, but I agree with you that we are on the track of the criminals. These poachers, or the gardener who has disappeared, have something, perhaps, to do with this abominable crime.”
Already, for some minutes, M. Plantat had rather awkwardly concealed some signs of impatience.
“The misfortune is,” said he, “that if Guespin is guilty, he will not be such a fool as to show himself here.”
“Oh, we’ll find him,” returned M. Domini. “Before leaving Corbeil, I sent a despatch to the prefecture of police at Paris, to ask for a police agent, who will doubtless be here shortly.”
“While waiting,” proposed the mayor, “perhaps you would like to see the scene of the crime?”
M. Domini made a motion as if to rise; then sat down again.
“In fact, no,” said he; “we will see nothing till the agent arrives. But I must have some information concerning the Count and Countess de Trémorel.”
The worthy mayor again triumphed.
“Oh, I can give it to you,” answered he quickly, “better than anybody. Ever since their advent here, I may say, I have been one of their best friends. Ah, sir, what charming people! excellent, and affable, and devoted﻿—”
And at the remembrance of all his friends’ good qualities, M. Courtois choked in his utterance.
“The Count de Trémorel,” he resumed, “was a man of thirty-four years, handsome, witty to the tips of his nails. He had sometimes, however, periods of melancholy, during which he did not wish to see anybody; but he was ordinarily so affable, so polite, so obliging; he knew so well how to be noble without haughtiness, that everybody here esteemed and loved him.”
“And the countess?” asked the judge of instruction.
“An angel, Monsieur, an angel on earth! Poor lady! You will soon see her remains, and surely you would not guess that she has been the queen of the country, by reason of her beauty.”
“Were they rich?”
“Yes; they must have had, together, more than a hundred thousand francs income﻿—oh, yes, much more; for within five or six months the count, who had not the bucolic tastes of poor Sauvresy, sold some lands to buy consols.”
“Have they been married long?”
M. Courtois scratched his head; it was his appeal to memory.

===Chinese===

他暗自思忖、权衡着听到的每一个细节，在脑海中勾勒出一步步的办案步骤。
“您处置得很有分寸，”他终于开口说道，“祸事固然惨重，但我赞同您的看法，我们已经摸到了罪犯的蛛丝马迹。这几个偷猎者，或是那个不知所踪的园丁，多半与这起人神共愤的罪案脱不了干系。”
有那么几分钟，普朗塔先生已经颇为别扭地流露出几分按捺不住的不耐烦。
“糟糕的是，”他说，“如果盖斯潘真有罪，他绝不会蠢到再在这里抛头露面。”
“哦，我们自会找到他的，”多米尼先生接话道，“在离开科尔贝伊之前，我已经给巴黎警察厅发了加急公文，请求派遣一名刑警前来协助，想必不久便会赶到。”
“趁这当口，”镇长提议道，“您也许想先去瞧瞧案发现场？”
多米尼先生刚要起身，接着又坐了回去。
“其实大可不必，”他说，“在刑警赶到之前，我们什么也别去动。不过，我必须了解一些关于特雷莫雷尔伯爵夫妇的情况。”
可敬的镇长再次找到了大显身手的机会。
“哎呀，这事我能告诉您，”他急切地接茬道，“没人比我知道得更清楚了。自从他们搬到这儿起，可以说，我就是他们最要好的挚友之一。啊，先生，多么迷人的一对壁人啊！品格高尚，和蔼可亲，待人赤诚——”
一想起友人们生前的种种美德，库尔图瓦先生不禁哽咽失声。
“特雷莫雷尔伯爵，”他接着说，“年方34岁，一表人才，聪慧绝顶。不过他有时也会陷入莫名的忧郁，在那期间谁也不愿见；但他平时总是那么和气，那么彬彬有礼，乐于助人；他是如此懂得如何在保持贵族气派的同时毫无傲慢之气，以至于这里的每一个人都敬重他、爱戴他。”
“伯爵夫人呢？”预审法官问。
“一位天使，先生，真真是一位降落凡间的天使！可怜的夫人！等会儿您就能见到她的遗容了，您做梦也不会想到，她曾凭着倾国倾城的美貌艳冠全郡。”
“他们富有吗？”
“富有；他们合在一起的年收入肯定超过10万法郎——噢，还不止呢；因为就在五六个月前，伯爵因为没有可怜的索夫勒齐（Sauvresy）那种寄情田园的雅兴，变卖了几处地产来购置公债。”
“他们成婚很久了吗？”
库尔图瓦先生挠了挠头；这是他在努力调动记忆。

===Original===

“Faith,” he answered, “it was in September of last year; just six months ago. I married them myself. Poor Sauvresy had been dead a year.”
The judge of instruction looked up from his notes with a surprised air.
“Who is this Sauvresy,” he inquired, “of whom you speak?”
Papa Plantat, who was furiously biting his nails in a corner, apparently a stranger to what was passing, rose abruptly.
“Monsieur Sauvresy,” said he, “was the first husband of Madame de Trémorel. My friend Courtois has omitted this fact.”
“Oh!” said the mayor, in a wounded tone, “it seems to me that under present circumstances﻿—”
“Pardon me,” interrupted the judge. “It is a detail such as may well become valuable, though apparently foreign to the case, and at the first view, insignificant.”
“Hum!” grunted Papa Plantat. “Insignificant﻿—foreign to it!”
His tone was so singular, his air so strange, that M. Domini was struck by it.
“Do you share,” he asked, “the opinion of the mayor regarding the Trémorels?”
Plantat shrugged his shoulders.
“I haven’t any opinions,” he answered: “I live alone﻿—see nobody; don’t disturb myself about anything. But﻿—”
“It seems to me,” said M. Courtois, “that nobody should be better acquainted with people who were my friends than I myself.”
“Then, you are telling the story clumsily,” said M. Plantat, dryly.
The judge of instruction pressed him to explain himself. So M. Plantat, without more ado, to the great scandal of the mayor, who was thus put into the background, proceeded to dilate upon the main features of the count’s and countess’s biography.
“The Countess de Trémorel, née Bertha Lechaillu, was the daughter of a poor village schoolmaster. At eighteen, her beauty was famous for three leagues around, but as she only had for dowry her great blue eyes and blond ringlets, but few serious lovers presented themselves. Already Bertha, by advice of her family, had resigned herself to take a place as a governess﻿—a sad position for so beautiful a maid﻿—when the heir of one of the richest domains in the neighborhood happened to see her, and fell in love with her.
“Clement Sauvresy was just thirty; he had no longer any family, and possessed nearly a hundred thousand livres income from lands absolutely free of incumbrance. Clearly, he had the best right in the world to choose a wife to his taste. He did not hesitate.

===Chinese===

“说实在的，”他回答道，“那是去年9月的事；刚好6个月前。还是我亲自为他们主持的婚礼呢。可怜的索夫勒齐当时刚去世一年。”
预审法官从卷宗笔记上抬起头，神情颇为诧异。
“您提到的这位索夫勒齐，”他探询道，“究竟是何许人也？”
一直在角落里狠狠咬着指甲、看似对眼前一切漠不关心的普朗塔老爹，冷不丁霍然站起身来。
“索夫勒齐先生，”他说，“是特雷莫雷尔夫人的前夫。我的老友库尔图瓦把这关键的一笔给漏掉了。”
“哎！”镇长受了委屈似地咕哝道，“在我看来，在眼下这种节骨眼上——”
“请原谅我打断一下，”法官说，“正是这样的细节往往极具价值，尽管表面看来似乎游离于案情之外，初看微不足道。”
“哼！”普朗塔老爹冷笑了一声，“微不足道——无关紧要！”
他的口吻如此奇特，神态又如此古怪，令多米尼先生心头猛地一震。
“关于特雷莫雷尔夫妇，”法官问，“您也持镇长同样的看法吗？”
普朗塔耸了耸肩。
“我没有任何看法，”他回答道，“我孤身一人独居——不见任何人；从不为任何事操闲心。不过——”
“在我看来，”库尔图瓦先生插嘴道，“对于我自己的至交密友，没人能比我更清楚内情了。”
“那么，你讲得可真够蹩脚的，”普朗塔先生冷冰冰地顶了一句。
预审法官极力敦促他把话讲明白。于是，普朗塔先生毫不客气地撇开被晾在一旁、大感难堪的镇长，径直展开详述伯爵与伯爵夫人的生平往事。
“特雷莫雷尔伯爵夫人，闺名贝尔塔·勒夏伊（Bertha Lechaillu），是一个贫苦乡村教师的女儿。18岁时，她的美貌在方圆3里格内无人不知、无人不晓，但由于她除了一双湛蓝的大眼睛和一头金色的鬈发之外别无嫁妆，几乎没有正经的求婚者上门提亲。在家庭的劝说下，贝尔塔甚至已经认命，准备去富贵人家当家庭教师——对这样一位美丽的姑娘来说，那真是一份凄苦的差事——就在这时，附近最显赫庄园的继承人恰巧见到了她，并对她一见钟情。
“克莱芒·索夫勒齐（Clement Sauvresy）年方30岁；他孑然一身，已无近亲，名下毫无抵押负担的田产每年能带来近10万锂的丰厚收益。显然，他是世上最有资格按自己的心意挑选妻子的男子。他毫不犹豫。

===Original===

He asked for Bertha’s hand, won it, and, a month after, wedded her at midday, to the great scandal of the neighboring aristocracy, who went about saying: ‘What folly! what good is there in being rich, if it is not to double one’s fortune by a good marriage!’
“Nearly a month before the marriage, Sauvresy set the laborers to work at Valfeuillu, and in no long time had spent, in repairs and furniture, a trifle of thirty thousand crowns. The newly married pair chose this beautiful spot in which to spend their honeymoon. They were so well-contented there that they established themselves permanently at Valfeuillu, to the great satisfaction of the neighborhood.
“Bertha was one of those persons, it seemed, who are born especially to marry millionaires. Without awkwardness or embarrassment, she passed easily from the humble schoolroom, where she had assisted her father, to the splendid drawing-room of Valfeuillu. And when she did the honors of her château to all the neighboring aristocracy, it seemed as though she had never done anything else. She knew how to remain simple, approachable, modest, all the while that she took the tone of the highest society. She was beloved.”
“But it appears to me,” interrupted the mayor, “that I said the same thing, and it was really not worth while﻿—”
A gesture from M. Domini closed his mouth, and M. Plantat continued:
“Sauvresy was also liked, for he was one of those golden hearts which know not how to suspect evil. He was one of those men with a robust faith, with obstinate illusions, whom doubts never disturb. He was one of those who thoroughly confide in the sincerity of their friends, in the love of their mistresses. This new domestic household ought to be happy; it was so. Bertha adored her husband﻿—that frank man, who, before speaking to her a word of love, offered her his hand. Sauvresy professed for his wife a worship which few thought foolish. They lived in great style at Valfeuillu. They received a great deal. When autumn came all the numerous spare chambers were filled. The turnouts were magnificent.
“Sauvresy had been married two years, when one evening he brought from Paris one of his old and intimate friends, a college comrade of whom he had often spoken, Count Hector de Trémorel. The count intended to remain but a short time at Valfeuillu; but weeks passed and then months, and he still remained. It was not surprising.

===Chinese===

他登门向贝尔塔求婚，赢得了芳心，一个月后，在大正午举行了婚礼；这在邻近的贵族圈子里激起了轩然大波，大家议论纷纷：‘真是疯了！要是有钱不能通过门当户对的好姻缘让财富翻上一番，有钱还有什么用！’
“大婚前将近一个月，索夫勒齐就张罗工人在瓦尔菲伊大兴土木，没过多久，光是修缮房屋和添置家具就花掉了整整30000埃居的巨款。新婚夫妇选中了这片风景绝胜之地度蜜月。他们对这里流连忘返，索性在瓦尔菲伊长久定居下来，这让四邻乡里大感欢欣。
“贝尔塔似乎正是那种天生就注定要嫁给百万富翁的女子。她毫无拘谨局促之态，游刃有余地从协助父亲的简陋乡间校舍，跨入了瓦尔菲伊的金碧辉煌的客厅。每当她以女主人的身份招待周边所有的贵胄名流时，那份从容大度仿佛她生来就浸润其间。她懂得如何在保持最高贵社交风度的同时，依然待人真诚、亲切、谦逊。人人都爱戴她。”
“可依我看，”镇长忍不住插话道，“我刚才说的也是这个意思嘛，实在没必要——”
多米尼先生做了一个手势制止了他，普朗塔先生继续往下说：
“索夫勒齐同样深受爱戴，因为他怀有一颗赤金般的仁厚之心，从不知恶意揣度他人。他是那种怀有坚定信念、执着于美好幻象、从未被怀疑动摇过的人。他全心全意信任朋友的赤诚，信任情人的爱意。这样一对崭新的家庭理应幸福美满；事实也确实如此。贝尔塔深爱着她的丈夫——那个在她耳边吐露半句甜言蜜语之前，就先向她奉上婚约的坦荡君子。索夫勒齐对妻子敬若神明，几乎没人觉得这有什么荒唐。他们在瓦尔菲伊的生活排场极大，宾客盈门。每到秋季，那诸多闲置的客房总是住得满满当当。车马仪仗极尽奢华。
“索夫勒齐婚后两年的一天傍晚，他从巴黎带回了一位昔日的挚友，那是他常挂在嘴边的大学同窗，埃克托·德·特雷莫雷尔伯爵。伯爵原本只打算在瓦尔菲伊盘桓几日；然而几周过去了，几个月过去了，他依然流连忘返。这也并不奇怪。

===Original===

Hector had passed a very stormy youth, full of debauchery, of clubs, of gambling, and of amours. He had thrown to the winds of his caprices an immense fortune; the relatively calm life of Valfeuillu was a relief. At first people said to him, ‘You will soon have enough of the country.’ He smiled, but said nothing. It was then thought, and rightly, perhaps, that having become poor, he cared little to display his ruin before those who had obscured his splendor. He absented himself rarely, and then only to go to Corbeil, almost always on foot. There he frequented the Belle Image hotel, the best in the town, and met, as if by chance, a young lady from Paris. They spent the afternoon together, and separated when the last train left.”
“*Peste!*” growled the mayor, “for a man who lives alone, who sees nobody, who would not for the world have anything to do with other people’s business, it seems to me our dear Monsieur Plantat is pretty well informed.”
Evidently M. Courtois was jealous. How was it that he, the first personage in the place, had been absolutely ignorant of these meetings? His ill-humor was increasing, when Dr. Gendron answered:
“Pah! all Corbeil prated about that at the time.”
M. Plantat made a movement with his lips as if to say, “I know other things besides.” He went on, however, with his story.
“The visit of Count Hector made no change in the habits at the château. Monsieur and Madame Sauvresy had a brother; that was all. Sauvresy at this time made several journeys to Paris, where, as everybody knew, he was engaged in arranging his friend’s affairs.
“This charming existence lasted a year. Happiness seemed to be fixed forever beneath the delightful shades of Valfeuillu. But alas! one evening on returning from the hunt, Sauvresy became so ill that he was forced to take to his bed. A doctor was called; inflammation of the chest had set in. Sauvresy was young, vigorous as an oak; his state did not at first cause anxiety. A fortnight afterward, in fact, he was up and about. But he was imprudent and had a relapse. He again nearly recovered; a week afterward there was another relapse, and this time so serious, that a fatal end of his illness was foreseen. During this long sickness, the love of Bertha and the affection of Trémorel for Sauvresy were tenderly shown.

===Chinese===

埃克托早年度过了一段放浪形骸的狂暴岁月，充斥着纵欲、俱乐部、豪赌和风流韵事。他把一笔庞大的家产在随心所欲的挥霍中消耗殆尽；瓦尔菲伊相对宁静的田园生活对他来说是一种慰藉。起初人们对他说：‘你很快就会过腻乡下日子的。’他只是一笑置之，沉默不语。于是大家心想，或许正如人们所料，他既然已经沦为赤贫，自然不愿在那些见识过他昔日荣华的人面前自曝落魄。他极少离开庄园，即便外出也只是去科尔贝伊，而且几乎总是步行前往。在镇上，他常去最好的‘好肖像’旅店（Belle Image hotel），并在那里仿佛不期而遇般地同一位来自巴黎的少妇幽会。他们整个下午都呆在一起，直到末班火车开动才依依惜别。”
“见鬼！”镇长嘟囔着，“对于一个深居简出、不见外人、天下塌下来也不愿插手旁人闲事的隐士来说，我们亲爱的普朗塔先生打探起消息来可真够灵通的啊。”
显而易见，库尔图瓦先生吃醋了。怎么可能连他这个全镇头号大人物都对这些秘密私会一无所知呢？正当他的恼怒逐渐升级时，让德龙医生插了一句：
“呸！那档子事当年在科尔贝伊早就传得沸沸扬扬了。”
普朗塔先生嘴角微微一撇，那副神情仿佛在说：“我知道的秘密可远不止这些。”不过，他还是接着往下讲述。
“埃克托伯爵的到来并未打乱城堡里的生活日常。无非是索夫勒齐夫妇多了一位兄弟而已。在那段日子里，索夫勒齐好几次前往巴黎，人人都知道，他是去帮朋友料理收拾残局。
“这种惬意快活的日子持续了一年。幸福似乎在这片瓦尔菲伊令人沉醉的绿荫下永久扎下了根。然而天有不测风云！一天傍晚打猎归来，索夫勒齐突发恶疾，不得不卧床不起。府里请来了大夫；诊断为严重的肺部发炎。索夫勒齐年轻力壮，结实得如同一株橡树；他的病情起初并未引起人们的忧虑。事实上，两周之后，他已经能够下床走动了。但他一时疏忽大意，病情骤然复发。他再次勉强好转；可一周之后病情再度恶化，这一次势头极其凶猛，以至于人们已隐隐预感到了不祥的结局。在这漫长的病榻岁月里，贝尔塔对索夫勒齐的深情与特雷莫雷尔对他的挚爱，展现得淋漓尽致。

===Original===

Never was an invalid tended with such solicitude﻿—surrounded with so many proofs of the purest devotion. His wife and his friend were always at his couch, night and day. He had hours of suffering, but never a second of weariness. He repeated to all who went to see him, that he had come to bless his illness. He said to himself, ‘If I had not fallen ill, I should never have known how much I was beloved.’ ”
“He said the same thing to me,” interrupted the mayor, “more than a hundred times. He also said so to Madame Courtois, to Laurence, my eldest daughter﻿—”
“Naturally,” continued M. Plantat. “But Sauvresy’s distemper was one against which the science of the most skilful physicians and the most constant care contend in vain.
“He said that he did not suffer much, but he faded perceptibly, and was no more than the shadow of his former self. At last, one night, toward two or three o’clock, he died in the arms of his wife and his friend. Up to the last moment, he had preserved the full force of his faculties. Less than an hour before expiring, he wished everyone to be awakened, and that all the servants of the castle should be summoned. When they were all gathered about the bedside, he took his wife’s hand, placed it in that of the Count de Trémorel, and made them swear to marry each other when he was no more. Bertha and Hector began to protest, but he insisted in such a manner as to compel assent, praying and adjuring them, and declaring that their refusal would embitter his last moments. This idea of the marriage between his widow and his friend seems, besides, to have singularly possessed his thoughts toward the close of his life. In the preamble of his will, dictated the night before his death, to M. Bury, notary of Orcival, he says formally that their union is his dearest wish, certain as he is of their happiness, and knowing well that his memory will be piously kept.”
“Had Monsieur and Madame Sauvresy no children?” asked the judge of instruction.
“No,” answered the mayor.
M. Plantat continued:
“The grief of the count and the young widow was intense. M. de Trémorel, especially, seemed absolutely desperate, and acted like a madman. The countess shut herself up, forbidding even those whom she loved best from entering her chamber﻿—even Madame Courtois. When the count and Madame Bertha reappeared, they were scarcely to be recognized, so much had both changed.

===Chinese===

世上从未有哪个病人受到过如此无微不至的呵护——被如此纯粹而深沉的奉献包围着。他的妻子和他的朋友日夜守候在他的病榻旁。他饱受病痛的折磨，却从未有过一分一秒的厌倦。他对每一个前来探望的人反复说，他甚至感谢这场疾病。他暗自思忖：‘要是我没病倒，我永远也不会知道自己竟然被如此深沉地爱着。’ ”
“这话他也对我讲过，”镇长打断道，“讲了足有100多次呢。他还对库尔图瓦夫人讲过，对我大女儿洛朗斯（Laurence）讲过——”
“这是自然的，”普朗塔先生接着说道，“然而索夫勒齐所患的沉疴，纵使最精湛的名医良药与最无微不至的照料，也终究无力回天。
“他说自己并不感到多么剧痛，但他的生命却在肉眼可见地枯萎凋零，渐渐化作昔日健魄的一具空壳。终于，在一个夜里，大约2、3点钟的时候，他在妻子与挚友的臂弯中溘然长逝。直到生命的最后一刻，他始终保持着清晰的神智。在咽气前不到一个小时，他坚持要叫醒所有人，召集城堡里的所有仆役到场。当下人们全聚在病榻周围时，他拉起妻子的手，交到了特雷莫雷尔伯爵的手中，强令他们发誓在他离世后结为夫妻。贝尔塔和埃克托起初百般抗拒，但他执拗得不容拒绝，苦苦哀求、赌咒发誓，宣称他们的拒绝会让他在九泉之下死不瞑目。此外，让遗孀与挚友喜结良缘的念头，在他生命的弥留之际似乎完全占据了他的心智。在临终前夜口授给奥尔西瓦尔公证人比里先生（M. Bury）的遗嘱序言中，他郑重写道，他们的结合是他平生最恳切的心愿，他深信他们会获得幸福，也深信自己的记忆会被他们虔诚供奉。”
“索夫勒齐夫妇没有留下子嗣吗？”预审法官问。
“没有，”镇长回答。
普朗塔先生继续往下说：
“伯爵和年轻未亡人的悲痛是撕心裂肺的。尤其是特雷莫雷尔先生，整个人宛如彻底崩溃的狂人。伯爵夫人闭门不出，甚至连她平日里最亲近的人也严禁踏入她的卧室一步——就连库尔图瓦夫人也被挡在门外。当伯爵和贝尔塔夫人终于再度露面时，人们几乎认不出他们了，两人的容貌发生了极大的改变。

===Original===

Monsieur Hector seemed to have grown twenty years older. Would they keep the oath made at the deathbed of Sauvresy, of which everyone was apprised? This was asked with all the more curiosity, because their profound sorrow for a man who well merited it, was admired.”
The judge of instruction stopped M. Plantat with a motion of his hand.
“Do you know,” asked he, “whether the rendezvous at the Hotel Belle Image had ceased?”
“I suppose so, sir; I think so.”
“I am almost sure of it,” said Dr. Gendron. “I have often heard it said﻿—they know everything at Corbeil﻿—that there was a heated explanation between M. de Trémorel and the pretty Parisian lady. After this quarrel, they were no longer seen at the Belle Image.”
The old justice of the peace smiled.
“Melun is not at the end of the world,” said he, “and there are hotels at Melun. With a good horse, one is soon at Fontainebleau, at Versailles, even at Paris. Madame de Trémorel might have been jealous; her husband had some first-rate trotters in his stables.”
Did M. Plantat give an absolutely disinterested opinion, or did he make an insinuation? The judge of instruction looked at him attentively, to reassure himself, but his visage expressed nothing but a profound serenity. He told the story as he would any other, no matter what.
“Please go on, Monsieur,” resumed M. Domini.
“Alas!” said M. Plantat, “nothing here below is eternal, not even grief. I know it better than anybody. Soon, to the tears of the first days, to violent despair, there succeeded, in the count and Madame Bertha, a reasonable sadness, then a soft melancholy. And in one year after Sauvresy’s death Monsieur de Trémorel espoused his widow.”
During this long narrative the mayor had several times exhibited marks of impatience. At the end, being able to hold in no longer, he exclaimed:
“There, those are surely exact details; but I question whether they have advanced us a step in this grave matter which occupies us all﻿—to find the murderers of the count and countess.”
M. Plantat, at these words, bent on the judge of instruction his clear and deep look, as if to search his conscience to the bottom.
“These details were indispensable,” returned M. Domini, “and they are very clear.

===Chinese===

埃克托先生仿佛一下子苍老了20岁。他们会不会遵守在索夫勒齐临终前许下的、早已传得尽人皆知的誓言呢？人们怀着格外好奇的心情探寻着，因为大家对他们为那位值得哀悼之人的深切悲痛钦佩不已。”
预审法官抬手制止了普朗塔先生。
“您知道吗，”他问道，“‘好肖像’旅店的幽会从那以后停止了吗？”
“我想是的，先生；我以为是这样。”
“我几乎可以肯定，”让德龙医生开口说，“我常听人提起——在科尔贝伊什么事都瞒不过人——特雷莫雷尔先生同那位巴黎俏佳人之间发生过一场激烈的争吵。吵翻之后，人们就再也没在‘好肖像’旅店见过他们的身影了。”
老治安法官嘴角泛起一丝笑意。
“默伦（Melun）又不是天涯海角，”他说，“而且默伦镇上到处是旅店。骑上一匹好马，很快就能赶到枫丹白露、凡尔赛，甚至直奔巴黎。特雷莫雷尔夫人完全有理由吃醋起疑；她丈夫的马厩里可养着好几匹一等一的快马呢。”
普朗塔先生说这番话究竟是出于绝对客观的判断，还是意有所指的影射？预审法官审视地凝视着他，试图求证，但法官的脸上除了深不可测的坦然与宁静之外，看不出任何波澜。他讲起这段过往来，语气平淡得就跟讲述旁人的任何琐事毫无二致。
“请继续讲下去，先生，”多米尼先生重新开口道。
“唉！”普朗塔先生感叹道，“人世间没有任何东西是永恒的，哪怕悲伤也不例外。对此我比任何人体会都要深切。不久，最初几天的号啕大哭与绝望狂暴，在伯爵和贝尔塔夫人心中逐渐演变成了合乎情理的哀思，随后又化作了淡淡的忧伤。索夫勒齐去世整整一年后，特雷莫雷尔先生迎娶了他的未亡人。”
在这段漫长的叙述中，镇长好几次流露出按捺不住的不耐烦。最后，他终于再也按捺不住，大声嚷嚷起来：
“行啦，这些细节确实翔实确凿；但我怀疑这到底能不能让我们在当前压倒一切的大事上前进半步——那就是揪出杀害伯爵和伯爵夫人的凶手。”
普朗塔先生闻言，将清澈而深邃的目光投向预审法官，仿佛要将他的五脏六腑都看穿一般。
“这些细节至关重要，”多米尼先生回应道，“而且条理极为清晰。

===Original===

Those rendezvous at the hotel struck me; one knows not to what extremities jealousy might lead a woman﻿—”
He stopped abruptly, seeking, no doubt, some connection between the pretty Parisian and the murderers; then resumed:
“Now that I know the Trémorels as if I had lived with them intimately, let us proceed to the actual facts.”
The brilliant eye of M. Plantat immediately grew dim; he opened his lips as if to speak; but kept his peace. The doctor alone, who had not ceased to study the old justice of the peace, remarked the sudden change of his features.
“It only remains,” said M. Domini, “to know how the new couple lived.”
M. Courtois thought it due to his dignity to anticipate M. Plantat.
“You ask how the new couple lived,” said he hastily; “they lived in perfect concord; nobody knows better about it than I, who was most intimate with them. The memory of poor Sauvresy was a bond of happiness between them; if they liked me so well, it was because I often talked of him. Never a cloud, never a cross word. Hector﻿—I called him so, familiarly, this poor, dear count﻿—gave his wife the tender attentions of a lover; those delicate cares, which I fear most married people soon dispense with.”
“And the countess?” asked M. Plantat, in a tone too marked not to be ironical.
“Bertha?” replied the worthy mayor﻿—“she permitted me to call her thus, paternally﻿—I have cited her many and many a time as an example and model, to Madame Courtois. She was worthy of Hector and of Sauvresy, the two most worthy men I have ever met!”
Then, perceiving that his enthusiasm somewhat surprised his hearers, he added, more softly:
“I have my reasons for expressing myself thus; and I do not hesitate to do so before men whose profession and character will justify my discretion. Sauvresy, when living, did me a great service﻿—when I was forced to take the mayoralty. As for Hector, I knew well that he had departed﻿—from the dissipations of his youth, and thought I discerned that he was not indifferent to my eldest daughter, Laurence; and I dreamed of a marriage all the more proper, as, if the Count Hector had a great name, I would give to my daughter a dowry large enough to gild any escutcheon. Only events modified my projects.”
The mayor would have gone on singing the praises of the Trémorels, and his own family, if the judge of instruction had not interposed.

===Chinese===

旅店里的那些幽会引起了我的高度警觉；天知道女人的嫉妒心会把人逼到何等丧心病狂的地步——”
他猛地刹住话头，毫无疑问是在脑海中探寻着那位巴黎俏佳人与凶手之间是否存在某种牵连；随后他接着说：
“既然我现在对特雷莫雷尔夫妇的了解就跟曾经同他们朝夕相处一般透彻，那我们就回到案情本身的事实上来吧。”
普朗塔先生方才还炯炯有神的双眼瞬间黯淡了下去；他微微启唇仿佛想说什么，却终究保持了沉默。唯独始终不动声色观察着老治安法官的医生，敏锐地捕捉到了他神情的微妙突变。
“现在只剩下一个问题，”多米尼先生说，“就是这对新婚夫妇平日里的夫妻感情究竟如何。”
库尔图瓦先生认为有必要维护自己的尊严，于是抢在普朗塔先生前头开了口。
“您问他们夫妻俩过得怎么样，”他急急忙忙地抢白道，“他们过得琴瑟和鸣，和睦得无以复加；关于这一点，没人比我这个同他们最贴心的密友更有发言权了。对可怜的索夫勒齐的怀念是维系他们幸福的纽带；他们之所以待我这般亲厚，正是因为我常常同他们提起故人。从没有过半点阴霾，没有过一句拌嘴。埃克托——我习惯亲昵地这么唤他，这位可怜可敬的伯爵——对待妻子体贴入微，就像热恋中的情人一样；那种无微不至的呵护，恐怕大多数夫妻成婚没多久就抛到九霄云外了。”
“伯爵夫人呢？”普朗塔先生问道，语调里的讽刺意味明显得不加掩饰。
“贝尔塔吗？”这位可敬的镇长答道，“——她允许我像长辈一样慈爱地直呼其名——我曾无数次把她当作典范与楷模，拿去敲打库尔图瓦夫人。她完全配得上埃克托和索夫勒齐，这两个我平生所遇最值得敬重的正人君子！”
随后，察觉到自己的满腔热忱让在座的听众略感诧异，他放缓了语调，压低嗓门补充道：
“我这么说是有我的缘由的；在诸位面前我也毫不避讳，诸位的操守与声誉足以让我畅所欲言。索夫勒齐在世时，曾帮过我一个大忙——就在我勉为其难接下镇长重担的时候。至于埃克托，我深知他早已改邪归正——告别了年少荒唐的放浪生活，而且我隐隐察觉出他对我的长女洛朗斯（Laurence）并非全无意向；我曾盘算过一门再合适不过的亲事，毕竟埃克托伯爵家世显赫，而我能给女儿陪送足够让任何贵族纹章都熠熠生辉的丰厚嫁妆。只可惜后来的变故打乱了我的全盘计划。”
要是预审法官不出面打断，镇长还会滔滔不绝地继续夸耀特雷莫雷尔夫妇以及他自己的体面门第。

===Original===

“Here I am fixed,” he commenced, “now, it seems to me﻿—”
He was interrupted by a loud noise in the vestibule. It seemed like a struggle, and cries and shouts reached the drawing-room. Everybody rose.
“I know what it is,” said the mayor, “only too well. They have just found the body of the Count de Trémorel.”

===Chinese===

“我现在心里有数了，”他开口说道，“眼下，在我看来——”
门厅里骤然响起的一阵巨大喧哗声打断了他的话头。听上去像是一场扭打，哭喊声与叫嚷声直穿透进客厅。在座的所有人霍然起身。
“我知道是怎么回事了，”镇长说，“唉，我再清楚不过了。他们刚刚找到了特雷莫雷尔伯爵的遗体。”

## IV / 第四章
===Original===

The mayor was mistaken. The drawing-room door opened suddenly, and a man of slender form, who was struggling furiously, and with an energy which would not have been suspected, appeared, held on one side by a gendarme, and on the other by a domestic.
The struggle had already lasted long, and his clothes were in great disorder. His new coat was torn, his cravat floated in strips, the button of his collar had been wrenched off, and his open shirt left his breast bare. In the vestibule and court were heard the frantic cries of the servants and the curious crowd﻿—of whom there were more than a hundred, whom the news of the crime had collected about the gate, and who burned to hear, and above all to see.
This enraged crowd cried:
“It is he! Death to the assassin! It is Guespin! See him!”
And the wretch, inspired by an immense fright, continued to struggle.
“Help!” shouted he hoarsely. “Leave me alone. I am innocent!”
He had posted himself against the drawing-room door, and they could not force him forward.
“Push him,” ordered the mayor, “push him.”
It was easier to command than to execute. Terror lent to Guespin enormous force. But it occurred to the doctor to open the second wing of the door; the support failed the wretch, and he fell, or rather rolled at the foot of the table at which the judge of instruction was seated. He was straightway on his feet again, and his eyes sought a chance to escape. Seeing none﻿—for the windows and doors were crowded with the lookers-on﻿—he fell into a chair. The fellow appeared the image of terror, wrought up to paroxysm. On his livid face, black and blue, were visible the marks of the blows he had received in the struggle; his white lips trembled, and he moved his jaws as if he sought a little saliva for his burning tongue; his staring eyes were bloodshot, and expressed the wildest distress; his body was bent with convulsive spasms. So terrible was this spectacle, that the mayor thought it might be an example of great moral force. He turned toward the crowd, and pointing to Guespin, said in a tragic tone:
“See what crime is!”
The others exchanged surprised looks.
“If he is guilty,” muttered M. Plantat, “why on earth has he returned?”
It was with difficulty that the crowd was kept back; the brigadier was forced to call in the aid of his men.

===Chinese===

镇长料错了。客厅的门猛地被撞开，一个身形瘦削的男子正疯狂地拼命挣扎，爆发出一股任谁也意想不到的蛮力；他一边被一名宪兵死死按住，另一边被一名男仆紧紧扭住。
这场扭打显然已经持续了很久，他的衣衫已被扯得破烂不堪。崭新的外套被撕破了，领带碎成了布条飘在胸前，领扣被硬生生扯掉，敞开的衬衫露出了胸膛。在门厅和院子里，仆人们和围观群众的歇斯底里的怒吼声此起彼伏——闻讯赶来的100多人聚集在大门周围，大家迫不及待地想打听，更迫不及待地想亲眼瞧瞧。
愤怒的人群高喊着：
“就是他！打死这个杀人犯！是盖斯潘（Guespin）！快看他！”
而这个陷入极度惊恐的可怜虫，依然在垂死挣扎。
“救命啊！”他沙哑地嘶喊着，“放开我！我是无辜的！”
他死死抵住客厅的门框，他们怎么也无法把他推向前半步。
“推他进去，”镇长命令道，“快推他进去。”
下令容易动手难。恐惧赋予了盖斯潘惊人的力气。但医生急中生智，推开了另一扇对开的门；恶徒顿时失去了支撑，一下子跌倒，或者不如说是滚到了预审法官就座的桌子脚下。他立刻又一跃而起，目光四下搜寻逃跑的机会。眼见无路可逃——因为窗前和门口都挤满了看热闹的人——他颓然瘫倒在一张椅子里。这个家伙此刻简直就是恐惧达到顶点的化身。在他那张青一块紫一块的惨白面孔上，扭打中挨揍的伤痕清晰可见；发白的嘴唇不住地颤抖，上下颌不住地动弹，仿佛想为干渴灼热的舌头寻觅一点唾沫；那双瞪圆的眼睛布满血丝，流露出极度的绝望与痛苦；他的身躯因痉挛而抽搐佝偻。眼前的景象是如此骇人，以至于镇长认为这恰好是一个展现强大道德震撼力的契机。他转向人群，指着盖斯潘，用悲壮的语调宣布：
“看看犯罪的下场吧！”
其余几人则面面相觑，大感惊愕。
“要是他真有罪，”普朗塔先生喃喃自语道，“他到底哪根筋搭错，竟然还会跑回来？”
好不容易才把人群挡在外面；宪兵班长不得不叫来部下协助弹压。

===Original===

Then he returned and placed himself beside Guespin, thinking it not prudent to leave him alone with unarmed men.
But the man was little to be feared. The reaction came; his overexcited energy became exhausted, his strained muscles flaccid, and his prostration resembled the agony of brain fever. Meanwhile the brigadier recounted what had happened.
“Some of the servants of the château and the neighboring houses were chatting near the gate, about the crime, and the disappearance of Guespin last night, when all of a sudden, someone perceived him at a distance, staggering, and singing boisterously, as if he were drunk.”
“Was he really drunk?” asked M. Domini.
“Very,” returned the brigadier.
“Then we owe it to the wine that we have caught him, and thus all will be explained.”
“On perceiving this wretch,” pursued the gendarme, who seemed not to have the shadow of a doubt of Guespin’s guilt, “François, the count’s valet de chambre, and Baptiste, the mayor’s servant, who were there, hastened to meet him, and seized him. He was so tipsy that he thought they were fooling with him. When he saw my men, he was undeceived. Just then one of the women cried out, ‘Brigand, it was you who have this night assassinated the count and the countess!’ He immediately became paler than death, and remained motionless and dumb. Then he began to struggle so violently that he nearly escaped. Ah! he’s strong, the rogue, although he does not look like it.”
“And he said nothing?” said Plantat.
“Not a word; his teeth were so tightly shut with rage that I’m sure he couldn’t say ‘bread.’ But we’ve got him. I’ve searched him, and this is what I have found in his pockets: a handkerchief, a pruning-knife, two small keys, a scrap of paper covered with figures, and an address of the establishment of Vulcan’s Forges. But that’s not all﻿—”
The brigadier took a step, and eyed his auditors mysteriously; he was preparing his effect.
“That’s not all. While they were bringing him along in the courtyard, he tried to get rid of his wallet. Happily I had my eyes open, and saw the dodge. I picked up the wallet, which he had thrown among the flowers near the door; here it is. In it are a one-hundred-franc note, three napoleons, and seven francs in change. Yesterday the rascal hadn’t a sou﻿—”
“How do you know that?” asked M. Domini.
“*Dame!

===Chinese===

随后班长折返回来，守在盖斯潘身旁，深知把这样一个家伙单独留给手无寸铁的文职人员绝非明智之举。
但这人其实已不足为惧。狂躁退去后的虚脱随之而来；他过度透支的精力消耗殆尽，紧绷的肌肉瘫软下来，整个人萎靡得仿佛正饱受脑膜炎垂死挣扎的折磨。与此同时，班长讲述了事情的经过。
“城堡里的一些下人和附近人家的仆人正聚在大门口，议论着这桩凶案以及昨夜盖斯潘的失踪，就在这时，突然有人瞧见他远远地走来，摇摇晃晃，扯着嗓子大声唱歌，活像个烂醉如泥的酒鬼。”
“他真喝醉了吗？”多米尼先生问。
“醉得厉害，”班长回答道。
“这么说，多亏了黄汤下肚，我们才能抓到他，这下一切都能水落石出了。”
“一看到这个恶徒，”宪兵接着汇报道，言语间似乎对盖斯潘的罪责没有半点怀疑，“当时在场的伯爵贴身男仆弗朗索瓦（François）和镇长家的仆人巴蒂斯特（Baptiste）立刻迎上前去，一把扭住了他。他醉得晕头转向，还以为别人在跟他开玩笑。等他看到我的部下时，才如梦初醒。就在那时，一个女人尖叫道：‘强盗，就是你昨晚残杀了伯爵和伯爵夫人！’他的脸色刷地一下变得比死人还要惨白，整个人呆若木鸡，一句话也说不出来。接着他就拼命挣扎反抗，差点儿就让他挣脱跑了。哼！这无赖力气可大得很呢，虽然看着不像。”
“他就什么都没说？”普朗塔问。
“半个字都没吐；他满腔怒火，牙关咬得死紧，我敢说他连句‘行行好’都挤不出来。不过我们总算把他逮住了。我搜了他的身，这是从他口袋里翻出来的东西：一条手帕、一把修枝刀、两把小钥匙、一张写满数字的纸条，还有一张‘火神锻造坊’商号的地址名片。不过，这还不是全部——”
班长上前迈了一步，神神秘秘地打量着听众；他正在酝酿语出惊人的效果。
“这还不是全部。当他们把他押进院子时，他试图把随身带的皮夹子扔掉。好在我眼疾手快，一眼看穿了他的把戏。我捡起了那个被他扔在门边花丛里的皮夹子；就是这个。里面有一张100法郎的纸币、三枚拿破仑金币，还有7法郎零钱。而昨天，这无赖身上连一个子儿都没有——”
“你怎么知道的？”多米尼先生问。
“哎呀！

===Original===

* Monsieur Judge, he borrowed of the valet François (who told me of it) twenty-five francs, pretending that it was to pay his share of the wedding expenses.”
“Tell François to come here,” said the judge of instruction. “Now, sir,” he continued, when the valet presented himself, “do you know whether Guespin had any money yesterday?”
“He had so little, Monsieur,” answered François promptly, “that he asked me to lend him twenty-five francs during the day, saying that otherwise he could not go to the wedding, not having enough even to pay his railway fare.”
“But he might have some savings﻿—a hundred-franc note, for instance, which he didn’t like to change.”
François shook his head with an incredulous smile.
“Guespin isn’t the man to have savings,” said he; “Women and cards exhaust all his wages. No longer ago than last week, the keeper of the Café du Commerce came here and made a row on account of what he owed him, and threatened to go to the count about it.”
Perceiving the effect of what he said, the valet, as if to correct himself, hastened to add:
“I have no ill-will toward Guespin; before today I’ve always considered him a clever fellow, though he was too much of a practical joker; he was, perhaps, a little proud, considering his bringing up﻿—”
“You may go,” said the judge, cutting the disquisition of M. François short; the valet retired.
During this colloquy, Guespin had little by little come to himself. The judge of instruction, Plantat, and the mayor narrowly watched the play of his countenance, which he had not the coolness to compose, while the doctor held his pulse and counted its beating.
“Remorse, and fear of punishment,” muttered the mayor.
“Innocence, and the impossibility of proving it,” responded Plantat in a low tone.
M. Domini heard both these exclamations, but did not appear to take notice of them. His opinion was not formed, and he did not wish that anyone should be able to foretell, by any word of his, what it would be.
“Are you better, my friend?” asked Dr. Gendron, of Guespin.
The poor fellow made an affirmative sign. Then, having looked around with the anxious glance of a man who calculates a precipice over which he has fallen, he passed his hand across his eyes and stammered:
“Something to drink!”
A glass of water was brought, and he drank it at a draught, with an expression of intense satisfaction. Then he got upon his feet.

===Chinese===

法官先生，他管男仆弗朗索瓦借了25法郎（是弗朗索瓦亲口告诉我的），借口说是拿来分摊婚礼的开销。”
“叫弗朗索瓦进来，”预审法官吩咐道。“现在，这位先生，”当男仆来到面前时，法官继续问道，“你知道盖斯潘昨天手头有没有钱吗？”
“他穷得叮当响，先生，”弗朗索瓦毫不迟疑地答道，“以至于白天的时候他跑来找我借25法郎，说要是借不到他就去不成婚礼了，连坐火车的车票钱都掏不出来。”
“但他或许有些积蓄——比方说一张100法郎的纸币，平时舍不得破开。”
弗朗索瓦带着怀疑的冷笑摇了摇头。
