Mme. Petit brought in the dishes, and came in thrice as often as was necessary, her ears wide open, leaving the door ajar as often as she dared. Poor woman! she had prepared an excellent dinner, and nobody paid any attention to it.
M. Lecoq was fond of titbits; yet, when Louis placed on the table a dish of superb grapes﻿—quite out of season﻿—his mouth did not so much as expand into a smile. Dr. Gendron would have been puzzled to say what he had eaten. The dinner was nearly over, when M. Plantat began to be annoyed by the constraint which the presence of the servants put upon the party. He called to the cook:
“You will give us our coffee in the library, and may then retire, as well as Louis.”
“But these gentlemen do not know their rooms,” insisted Mme. Petit, whose eavesdropping projects were checked by this order. “They will, perhaps, need something.”
“I will show them their rooms,” said M. Plantat, dryly. “And if they need anything, I shall be here.”
They went into the library. M. Plantat brought out a box of cigars and passed them round:
“It will be healthful to smoke a little before retiring.”
M. Lecoq lit an aromatic weed, and remarked:
“You two may go to bed if you like; I am condemned, I see, to a sleepless night. But before I go to writing, I wish to ask you a few things, Monsieur Plantat.”
M. Plantat bowed in token of assent.
“We must resume our conversation,” continued the detective, “and compare our inferences. All our lights are not too much to throw a little daylight upon this affair, which is one of the darkest I have ever met with. The situation is dangerous, and time presses. On our acuteness depends the fate of several innocent persons, upon whom rest very serious charges.

===Chinese===

尽管围坐在餐桌旁时气氛不再像先前那般沉闷，三人却仿佛达成了某种心照不宣的默契，对今晚折磨所有人心智的那桩惨案避而不谈。直到端上咖啡、珀蒂夫人奉命带上房门退下之后，普朗塔先生才将胳膊肘撑在桌上，开口打破了沉默：
“好啦，勒科克先生，”他说，“我们现在洗耳恭听。”
让德龙医生把椅子拉得离桌子更近了些；刑警没有回答，而是从口袋里掏出一个雪茄盒，毕恭毕敬地递向两位同伴。得到婉拒后，他慢条斯理地点燃了一支雪茄，微笑着吐出一团轻烟，说道：
“在下准备就绪了。”
他的语气、神态与举止彻底判若两人。他的脸上不再挂着白日里那种造作的傻笑，不再洋溢着小零售布商那副谄媚畏缩的奴才相；取而代之的是一张坚毅沉着、炯炯有神的面容，透着一股不怒自威的冷酷与锐利。在座的二人暗自惊叹眼前这不可思议的脱胎换骨，勒科克先生接着说道：
“坦率地说，二位先生，自打接下这桩案子以来，在下可从未经历过如此惊心动魄的时刻。好几位身负重嫌的无辜之人的身家性命与前途命运，全系于我们此刻的洞察力之上。

===Original===

We have a theory: but Monsieur Domini also has one, and his, let us confess, is based upon material facts, while ours rests upon very disputable sensations and logic.”
“We have more than sensations,” responded M. Plantat.
“I agree with you,” said the doctor, “but we must prove it.”
“And I *will* prove it, parbleu,” cried M. Lecoq, eagerly. “The affair is complicated and difficult﻿—so much the better. Eh! If it were simple, I would go back to Paris instanter, and tomorrow I would send you one of my men. I leave easy riddles to infants. What I want is the inexplicable enigmas, so as to unravel it; a struggle, to show my strength; obstacles, to conquer them.”
M. Plantat and the doctor looked steadily at the speaker. He was as if transfigured. It was the same yellow-haired and whiskered man, in a long overcoat: yet the voice, the physiognomy, the very features, had changed. His eyes shone with the fire of his enthusiasm, his voice was metallic and vibrating, his imperious gesture affirmed the audacity and energy of his resolution.
“If you think, my friends,” pursued he, “that they don’t manufacture detectives like me at so much a year, you are right. When I was twenty years old, I took service with an astronomer, as his calculator, after a long course of study. He gave me my breakfasts and seventy francs a month; by means of which I dressed well, and covered I know not how many square feet with figures daily.”
M. Lecoq puffed vigorously at his cigar a moment, casting a curious glance at M. Plantat. Then he resumed:
“Well, you may imagine that I wasn’t the happiest of men. I forgot to mention that I had two little vices: I loved the women, and I loved play. All are not perfect. My salary seemed too small, and while I added up my columns of figures, I was looking about for a way to make a rapid fortune. There is, indeed, but one means; to appropriate somebody else’s money, shrewdly enough not to be found out. I thought about it day and night. My mind was fertile in expedients, and I formed a hundred projects, each more practicable than the others. I should frighten you if I were to tell you half of what I imagined in those days. If many thieves of my calibre existed, you’d have to blot the word ‘property’ out of the dictionary. Precautions, as well as safes, would be useless. Happily for men of property, criminals are idiots.”
“What is he coming to?”

===Chinese===

我们心头自有一套推论；然而多米尼先生同样有一套推论，而且平心而论，他的结论建立在看得见摸得着的客观实物之上，而我们的推论却建立在极具争议的感觉与逻辑推理之上。”
“我们掌握的远不止是感觉，”普朗塔先生回应道。
“我赞同普朗塔的看法，”医生说，“但我们必须拿出真凭实据来。”
“而在下必定会拿出真凭实据来，见鬼！”勒科克先生激动地喊道，“案情越是错综复杂、棘手难办——越是正中在下的下怀！哼！倘若案情当真一目了然，在下大可立刻打道回巴黎，明早随便派来我手下的一个小卒子便能应付。在下向来把简单的谜题留给小毛孩。在下所渴望的，正是那些匪夷所思的离奇谜团，好让我亲手拆解抽丝剥茧；我渴望一场恶斗，好彰显我的筋骨蛮力；我渴望千难万险，好将其彻底踏碎征服。”
普朗塔先生与医生目不转睛地凝视着眼前侃侃而谈的男子。他整个人宛如脱胎换骨变了一具皮囊。尽管依然是那个头戴黄色假发、留着假胡须、身穿褪色长大衣的矮个子；然而嗓音、神态乃至五官的线条，却发生了翻天覆地的巨变。他的双眼中燃烧着狂热的雄心火焰，嗓音带着金属般的震颤共鸣，专横有力的手势无不昭示着他破釜沉舟的决断与魄力。
“二位朋友，若是诸位以为像在下这般的身手是靠每年领几百两碎银子就能批发培养出来的，那可就大错特错了。20岁那年，在经历了一场漫长严苛的苦学之后，在下受雇于一位天文学家，给他当测算小工。他每天管我一顿早饭，外加每月70法郎的微薄薪饷；凭着这笔进项，我穿戴得体，每天在数不清的方尺白纸上填满密密麻麻的演算数字。”
勒科克先生狠狠吸了一口雪茄，别有深意地瞥了普朗塔先生一眼。随后他接着说道：
“诸位尽可以想象，那时的在下可算不上天底下最快活的汉子。在下忘了交待，我身上有两个微不足道的小毛病：一好女色，二好博彩。人非圣贤孰能无过。薪水实在少得可怜，每当我在账册上一列列累加数字时，脑子里全在琢磨着如何才能一步登天暴富发家。事实上，世间唯有一条捷径：那就是把旁人的不义之财巧取豪夺过来，而且手段高明到绝不露出半点马脚。我日思夜想，夜不能寐。我的脑子里充斥着形形色色的机巧诡计，脑海中盘旋着100个一个比一个具备实操可行性的惊天计划。若是在下把当年臆想出的勾当如实吐露一半，定会把二位吓得魂飞魄散。世间要是多几个在下这般成色的江洋大盗，法兰西字典里恐怕非得把‘财产’这两个字彻底抠掉不可。所有的防范措施、所有的钢铁保险柜，全都会化作一堆废铁。万幸的是，对于有产者而言，天底下的罪犯大体全是一群不折不扣的笨蛋蠢猪。”
“他究竟要扯到哪儿去？”

===Original===

thought the doctor.
“One day, I became afraid of my own thoughts. I had just been inventing a little arrangement by which a man could rob any banker whatever of 200,000 francs without any more danger or difficulty than I raise this cup. So I said to myself, ‘Well, my boy, if this goes on a little longer, a moment will come when, from the idea, you will naturally proceed to the practice.’ Having, however, been born an honest lad﻿—a mere chance﻿—and being determined to use the talents which nature had given me, eight days afterward I bid my astronomer good morning, and went to the prefecture. My fear of being a burglar drove me into the police.”
“And you are satisfied with the exchange?” asked Dr. Gendron.
“I’ faith, Doctor, my first regret is yet to come. I am happy, because I am free to exercise my peculiar faculties with usefulness to my race. Existence has an enormous attraction for me, because I have still a passion which overrides all others﻿—curiosity.”
The detective smiled, and continued:
“There are people who have a mania for the theatre. It is like my own mania. Only, I can’t understand how people can take pleasure in the wretched display of fictions, which are to real life what a tallow dip is to the sun. It seems to me monstrous that people can be interested in sentiments which, though well represented, are fictitious. What! can you laugh at the witticisms of a comedian, whom you know to be the struggling father of a family? Can you pity the sad fate of the poor actress who poisons herself, when you know that on going out you will meet her on the boulevards? It’s pitiable!”
“Let’s shut up the theatres,” suggested Dr. Gendron.
“I am more difficult to please than the public,” returned M. Lecoq. “I must have veritable comedies, or real dramas. My theatre is﻿—society. My actors laugh honestly, or weep with genuine tears. A crime is committed﻿—that is the prologue; I reach the scene, the first act begins. I seize at a glance the minutest shades of the scenery. Then I try to penetrate the motives, I group the characters, I link the episodes to the central fact, I bind in a bundle all the circumstances. The action soon reaches the crisis, the thread of my inductions conducts me to the guilty person; I divine him, arrest him, deliver him up.

===Chinese===

让德龙医生暗自思忖。
“终于有一天，在下被自己心头翻涌的念头吓出了一身冷汗。我当时刚刚发明了一套极其精巧的小机关，凭着它，任何人都能神不知鬼不觉从任何一家银行金库里卷走200,000法郎巨资，其轻而易举的程度绝不下于我现在端起这只茶杯。于是我暗自告诫自己：‘好小子，要是再这么任由欲念放任下去，早晚有一天，你必定会顺理成章将这些念头付诸实践。’然而，由于在下生来本性纯良——纯属运气使然——而且下定决心要把上苍赐予我的这副过人天赋用在正道上，整整8天之后，我向天文学家道了声别，径直迈进了巴黎警察厅的大门。正是对自己沦为江洋大盗的恐惧，硬生生把我逼成了王牌刑警。”
“那么，你对这场人生交易感到心满意足吗？”让德龙医生问。
“说实在的，医生，在下平生至今还从未品尝过半点懊悔。在下活得快活得很，因为我能随心所欲施展我这套独门绝技，为我的人类同胞排忧解难。这红尘俗世对在下有着无可抗拒的致命吸引力，因为在我心头，始终燃烧着一股压倒一切其他欲望的狂热执念——那就是无穷无尽的探求欲与好奇心。”
大侦探微微一笑，接着说道：
“世上有些人是个戏痴。这种癖好同在下的执念大同小异。唯独在下实在无法理解，人们怎么可能从舞台上那些粗制滥造的虚构编造中品尝到乐趣呢，那些破烂戏码比起真实的人间万象，简直就像一根劣质牛油蜡烛同天上的红日争辉。在在下看来，人们竟然会对那些表演得再逼真也不过是逢场作戏的虚情假意产生共鸣，简直匪夷所思。什么！难道你们真能对着一个明知在台下为养家糊口焦头烂额的喜剧滑稽演员的俏皮话放声大笑吗？难道你们真能对着一个喝假毒药自尽、明知一散场就能在大街上撞见她闲逛的女戏子的悲惨命运掬一把同情之泪吗？真是荒诞可笑！”
“不如把戏院全关门大吉算了，”让德龙医生打趣道。
“在下比起普通看客来要挑剔严苛得多，”勒科克先生回应道，“在下必须亲历真刀真枪的真实喜剧，抑或是真实的人间惨剧。在下的剧场——正是这茫茫人海的社会舞台。在下的戏子要么放浪形骸真情大笑，要么痛哭流涕洒下痛彻心扉的滚烫热泪。一桩命案降临——那是大戏的序幕；在下赶赴案发现场，第一幕大戏正式拉开帷幕。在下一眼就能洞悉布景之中最细枝末节的微妙阴影。随后在下便千方百计探寻动机，为角色排兵布阵，将零散的枝节插曲严丝合缝铆接在核心主干上，把所有的线索捆扎成一副坚不可摧的罗网。剧情很快迎来高潮冲突，在下的归纳演绎链条指引着我步步逼近真凶；我识破他，擒获他，将他押上审判台。

===Original===

Then comes the great scene; the accused struggles, tries tricks, splits straws; but the judge, armed with the arms I have forged for him, overwhelms the wretch; he does not confess, but he is confounded. And how many secondary personages, accomplices, friends, enemies, witnesses are grouped about the principal criminal! Some are terrible, frightful, gloomy﻿—others grotesque. And you know not what the ludicrous in the horrible is. My last scene is the court of assize. The prosecutor speaks, but it is I who furnished his ideas; his phrases are embroideries set around the canvas of my report. The president submits his questions to the jury; what emotion! The fate of my drama is being decided. The jury, perhaps, answers, ‘Not guilty;’ very well, my piece was bad, I am hissed. If ‘Guilty,’ on the contrary, the piece was good, I am applauded, and victorious. The next day I can go and see my hero, and slapping him on the shoulder, say to him, ‘You have lost, old fellow, I am too much for you!’ ”
Was M. Lecoq in earnest now, or was he playing a part? What was the object of this autobiography? Without appearing to notice the surprise of his companions, he lit a fresh cigar; then, whether designedly or not, instead of replacing the lamp with which he lit it on the table, he put it on one corner of the mantel. Thus M. Plantat’s face was in full view, while that of M. Lecoq remained in shadow.
“I ought to confess,” he continued, “without false modesty, that I have rarely been hissed. Like every man I have my Achilles heel. I have conquered the demon of play, but I have not triumphed over my passion for woman.”
He sighed heavily, with the resigned gesture of a man who has chosen his path. “It’s this way. There is a woman, before whom I am but an idiot. Yes, I the detective, the terror of thieves and murderers, who have divulged the combinations of all the sharpers of all the nations, who for ten years have swum amid vice and crime; who wash the dirty linen of all the corruptions, who have measured the depths of human infamy; I who know all, who have seen and heard all; I, Lecoq, am before her, more simple and credulous than an infant. She deceives me﻿—I see it﻿—and she proves that I have seen wrongly. She lies﻿—I know it, I prove it to her﻿—and I believe her.

===Chinese===

接着便迎来了最惊心动魄的大场面；被告垂死挣扎，玩弄花招，诡辩抵赖；然而法官手握在下亲自替他锻造的致命重锤，将恶徒当头砸得粉身碎骨；他纵使咬死不招，也早已阵脚大乱溃不成军。而在核心重犯身旁，又围绕着多少形形色色的次要配角、同谋共犯、狐朋狗友、仇敌冤家与各路证人啊！有的凶神恶煞、阴森凄厉——有的滑稽荒诞、丑态百出。而诸位根本无法想象在骇人听闻的罪恶之中往往交织着怎样荒唐透顶的滑稽闹剧。在下的终幕大戏是重罪巡回法庭。公诉人当庭慷慨陈词，可他的全副论点全是由在下亲手呈递给他的；他那些华丽辞藻不过是在在下勘查笔录的粗胚画布上绣出的锦缎花边而已。法官大人向陪审团提交有罪与否的决断；何等令人窒息的心跳时刻！在下的大作究竟成败几何正系于一瞬。陪审团若是裁定‘无罪’；好吧，算在下的戏本砸了锅，在下自当甘受倒彩嘘声。反之，若是裁定‘罪名成立’，那就证明大作精妙绝伦，在下理该赢得满堂喝彩与无上凯旋。翌日清晨，在下便能大步流星走进牢房去探望在下的戏中主角，拍着他的肩膀得意洋洋地奚落道：‘老伙计，你认栽吧，你小子道行差得远呢！’ ”
此时此刻的勒科克先生究竟是吐露肺腑真言，还是依然在逢场作戏？他突然抛出这番自传究竟意欲何为？刑警仿佛丝毫没有察觉到两位同伴脸上的错愕，悠闲地点燃了第二支雪茄；随后，不知是有心还是无意，他没有把引火的油灯放回桌上，而是顺手搁在了壁炉台的一角。如此一来，普朗塔先生的面容被灯火照得纤毫毕现，而勒科克先生自己则深陷在浓重的阴影之中。
“在下不得不毫无虚伪谦逊地招认，”他接着说道，“在下平生极少遭遇倒彩。但人非草木孰能无过，在下身上同样有一处阿喀琉斯之踵。在下虽已彻底战胜了嗜赌成性的恶魔，却终究未能参透情关，未能战胜对女人的狂热沉沦。”
他长长叹了一口气，带着一个甘愿认命之人的无奈手势。“事情正是如此。世间有这么一个女子，在这个女人面前，在下简直退化成了一个不折不扣的大傻瓜。是的，在下堂堂大侦探，令全法兰西神偷巨寇闻风丧胆的克星，曾亲手拆解过万国所有老千骗局的高手，十年来在罪恶与泥潭中搏杀游刃有余的硬汉；亲手清洗过世间所有腐朽污秽脏衣裳、丈量过人类所有卑鄙无耻极限的老手；在下这个无所不知、阅尽沧桑的勒科克，站在她面前，却比襁褓中的婴儿还要天真稚嫩、轻信愚昧。她欺瞒我——我明明看在眼里——她却能巧舌如簧证明是我看花了眼。她撒谎——我心里雪亮，甚至当场拿铁证砸在她脸上——到头来我却依然情不自禁相信她的鬼话。

===Original===

It is because this is one of those passions,” he added, in a low, mournful tone, “that age, far from extinguishing, only fans, and to which the consciousness of shame and powerlessness adds fire. One loves, and the certainty that he cannot be loved in return is one of those griefs which you must have felt to know its depth. In a moment of reason, one sees and judges himself; he says, no, it’s impossible, she is almost a child, I almost an old man. He says this﻿—but always, in the heart, more potent than reason, than will, than experience, a ray of hope remains, and he says to himself, ‘who knows﻿—perhaps!’ He awaits, what﻿—a miracle? There are none, nowadays. No matter, he hopes on.”
M. Lecoq stopped, as if his emotion prevented his going on. M. Plantat had continued to smoke mechanically, puffing the smoke out at regular intervals; but his face seemed troubled, his glance was unsteady, his hands trembled. He got up, took the lamp from the mantel and replaced it on the table, and sat down again. The significance of this scene at last struck Dr. Gendron.
In short, M. Lecoq, without departing widely from the truth, had just attempted one of the most daring experiments of his repertoire, and he judged it useless to go further. He knew now what he wished to know. After a moment’s silence, he shuddered as though awaking from a dream, and pulling out his watch, said:
“*Par le Dieu!* How I chat on, while time flies!”
“And Guespin is in prison,” remarked the doctor.
“We will have him out,” answered the detective, “if, indeed, he is innocent; for this time I have mastered the mystery, my romance, if you wish, and without any gap. There is, however, one fact of the utmost importance, that I by myself cannot explain.”
“What?” asked M. Plantat.
“Is it possible that Monsieur de Trémorel had a very great interest in finding something﻿—a deed, a letter, a paper of some sort﻿—something of a small size, secreted in his own house?”
“Yes﻿—that is possible,” returned the justice of the peace.
“But I must know for certain.”
M. Plantat reflected a moment.
“Well then,” he went on, “I am sure, perfectly sure, that if Madame de Trémorel had died suddenly, the count would have ransacked the house to find a certain paper, which he knew to be in his wife’s possession, and which I myself have had in my hands.”
“Then,” said M. Lecoq, “there’s the drama complete.

===Chinese===

全因为这正是那种狂热执念中的一种，”他放低了嗓音，语气凄凉地补充道，“那种不仅不会随着岁月的流逝而枯竭、反倒会在时光中越烧越旺的烈火，而内心的羞耻感与无能为力更为其火上浇油。一个人苦苦深爱着对方，却深知自己绝无可能获得同等的回报，这种绝望的苦涩唯有亲身体验过的人才能懂得其痛彻心扉的深沉。在偶有的清醒理智关头，一个人打量审视着自己，他对自己说：不，这绝无可能，她几乎还是个不谙世事的娇女，而我已是个半截入土的老朽。他嘴上虽然这般告诫自己——然而在心底深处，总有一道压倒理智、压倒意志、压倒毕生阅历的虚妄希望之光顽固不灭，他暗自嘀咕：‘谁知道呢——兴许有奇迹呢！’他痴痴守候着，守候着什么呢——守候着奇迹降临吗？可在这个年头，世间早已没有奇迹了。然而无论如何，他依然在痴心妄想着。”
勒科克先生戛然而止，仿佛心头翻涌的情感哽咽得让他无法自持。普朗塔先生始终在一旁机械地抽着烟斗，一口接一口极有节奏地吐出青烟；然而他的脸色已然大变，目光游移不定，双手微微颤抖。他霍然起身，从壁炉台上端回油灯放回到餐桌正中，随后重新落座。这场戏码的深邃杀机，直到此刻才猛然击中了让德龙医生。
简而言之，勒科克先生在并未完全脱离事实的前提下，刚刚当场施展了他平生压箱底绝活中最凶险大胆的一次心理试探，而他认定没必要再进一步逼问下去了。他想要摸清的底细如今已尽在掌握。一阵死寂过后，他猛地一激灵仿佛大梦初醒一般，掏出怀表，说道：
“天哪！瞧在下这嘴碎的，工夫全耽搁了！”
“而盖斯潘眼下还在大牢里蹲着呢，”医生提醒道。
“我们自会把他捞出来的，”大侦探回答道，“只要他当真是无辜的；因为这回在下已经彻底勘破了整桩谜案，诸位若是不嫌弃，尽可以称其为在下心中的一出传奇大戏，而且全剧毫无破绽裂痕。然而在眼下，唯独存在着一项至关重要的核心事实，光凭在下一己之力尚无法参透。”
“何事？”普朗塔先生急切地问。
“是否存在这样一种可能：特雷莫雷尔先生有着极其重大的利害干系，急于在自家宅邸里搜寻出某件东西——一份契约、一封信函，或者某种尺寸极小、被秘密藏匿的绝密文书？”
“是的——这完全可能，”老治安法官回答道。
“但在下必须掌握百分之百的确凿断言。”
普朗塔先生沉思片刻。
“那么好吧，”他接着说道，“我可以断言，百分之百确信，若是特雷莫雷尔夫人暴毙身亡，伯爵必定会把整栋宅邸翻个底朝天，去搜寻某份他明知藏在妻子手中的秘密文件，而那份文件，我本人甚至曾亲眼过目、亲手经手过。”
“那么，”勒科克先生眼中爆发出摄人心魄的光芒，“整出大戏彻底严丝合缝了。

===Original===

On reaching Valfeuillu, I, like you, was struck with the frightful disorder of the rooms. Like you, I thought at first that this disorder was the result of design. I was wrong; a more careful scrutiny has convinced me of it. The assassin, it is true, threw everything into disorder, broke the furniture, hacked the chairs in order to make us think that some furious villains had been there. But amid these acts of premeditated violence I have followed up the involuntary traces of an exact, minute, and I may say patient search. Everything seemed turned topsy-turvy by chance; articles were broken open with the hatchet, which might have been opened with the hands; drawers had been forced which were not shut, and the keys of which were in the locks. Was this folly? No. For really no corner or crevice where a letter might be hid has been neglected. The table and bureau-drawers had been thrown here and there, but the narrow spaces between the drawers had been examined﻿—I saw proofs of it, for I found the imprints of fingers on the dust which lay in these spaces. The books had been thrown pell-mell upon the floor, but every one of them had been handled, and some of them with such violence that the bindings were torn off. We found the mantelshelves in their places, but every one had been lifted up. The chairs were not hacked with a sword, for the mere purpose of ripping the cloth﻿—the seats were thus examined. My conviction of the certainty that there had been a most desperate search, at first roused my suspicions. I said to myself, ‘The villains have been looking for the money which was concealed; therefore they did not belong to the household.’ ”
“But,” observed the doctor, “they might belong to the house, and yet not know the money was hidden; for Guespin﻿—”
“Permit me,” interrupted M. Lecoq, “I will explain myself. On the other hand, I found indications that the assassin must have been closely connected with Madame de Trémorel﻿—her lover, or her husband. These were the ideas that then struck me.”
“And now?”
“Now,” responded the detective, “with the certainty that something besides booty might have been the object of the search, I am not far from thinking that the guilty man is he whose body is being searched for﻿—the Count Hector de Trémorel.”
M. Plantat and Dr. Gendron had divined the name; but neither had as yet dared to utter his suspicions.

===Chinese===

一赶到瓦尔菲伊，”大侦探接着说道，“在下立刻察觉出这案子根本对不上号。多米尼先生咬定凶手是那个园丁，这推论实在太肤浅平庸了。在下认定作案者必定是一位精明强干、受过高等教育、心机深不可测的亡命之徒。然而若是按照在下的推论假说去推敲，我们却不得不面临一个难以自圆其说的致命悖论：那就是这位深谋远虑的凶手精心谋划伪造了现场的一切，却偏偏在最关键的收尾时刻破绽百出，仿佛智商骤降成了个白痴。是谁能做到这一切？谁既能从容谋划出这一整套金蝉脱壳的妙计，又能在案发深夜拥有大把时间在城堡各处自由穿梭？谁能既对城堡的每一处机关暗道了如指掌，又知晓巨款的确切藏匿之所？更关键的是，谁有动机把大案伪造成不仅是一场图财害命，更是一场连男主人自己也一同横遭屠戮的惨案？答案只有一个。在下毫不避讳地宣布，在下深信那个杀害伯爵夫人的真凶，正是官府眼下正下河打捞尸体的那个人——埃克托·德·特雷莫雷尔伯爵本人。”
普朗塔先生与让德龙医生此前早已在心底暗暗猜中了这个名字；然而谁也没有勇气将这骇人听闻的怀疑宣之于口。

===Original===

They awaited this name of Trémorel; and yet, pronounced as it was in the middle of the night, in this great sombre room, by this at least strange personage, it made them shudder with an indescribable fright.
“Observe,” resumed M. Lecoq, “what I say; I believe it to be so. In my eyes, the count’s guilt is only as yet extremely probable. Let us see if we three can reach the certainty of it. You see, gentlemen, the inquest of a crime is nothing more nor less than the solution of a problem. Given the crime, proved, patent, you commence by seeking out all the circumstances, whether serious or superficial; the details and the particulars. When these have been carefully gathered, you classify them, and put them in their order and date. You thus know the victim, the crime, and the circumstances; it remains to find the third term of the problem, that is, x, the unknown quantity﻿—the guilty party. The task is a difficult one, but not so difficult as is imagined. The object is to find a man whose guilt explains all the circumstances, all the details found﻿—all, understand me. Find such a man, and it is probable﻿—and in nine cases out of ten, the probability becomes a reality﻿—that you hold the perpetrator of the crime.”
So clear had been M. Lecoq’s exposition, so logical his argument, that his hearers could not repress an admiring exclamation:
“Very good! Very good!”
“Let us then examine together if the assumed guilt of the Count de Trémorel explains all the circumstances of the crime at Valfeuillu.”
He was about to continue when Dr. Gendron, who sat near the window, rose abruptly.
“There is someone in the garden,” said he.
All approached the window. The weather was glorious, the night very clear, and a large open space lay before the library window; they looked out, but saw no one.
“You are mistaken, Doctor,” said Plantat, resuming his armchair.
M. Lecoq continued:
“Now let us suppose that, under the influence of certain events that we will examine presently, Monsieur de Trémorel had made up his mind to get rid of his wife. The crime once resolved upon, it was clear that the count must have reflected, and sought out the means of committing it with impunity; he must have weighed the circumstances, and estimated the perils of his act.

===Chinese===

他们一直在等待着特雷莫雷尔这个名字被揭晓；然而在这个万籁俱寂的深夜，在这间昏暗阴森的大宅子里，从眼前这位奇特诡谲的刑警口中吐露出来，依然让他们不由自主地打了个寒战，心头涌起一股难以言状的彻骨悚然。
“请二位注意，”勒科克先生接着说道，“在下方才说的是：在下深信事实正是如此。在在下眼里，伯爵的罪责目前依然处于极大概率的范畴。且让我们三人合力推演，看看能否将这推论淬炼成确凿无疑的铁证。二位先生，侦办命案归根结底无非是在求解一道复杂的方程式。已知犯罪事实确凿无疑、昭然若揭，首先要做的便是搜集提炼所有的客观条件，无论轻重缓急；巨细靡遗搜罗所有细节特征。搜集齐备之后，将它们分门别类，按时间节点与逻辑因果串联成链条。如此一来，受害者、罪案本身以及全部客观环境便成了已知项；剩下的便是求解方程式的第三项，也就是未知数x——真正的作案凶手。这项差事看似难如登天，实则并没有常人想象的那么玄妙。核心要义在于寻觅到这么一个嫌疑人，只要假定是他作案，现场遗留的所有疑点、所有细节便能迎刃而解全部得到合理解释——请二位务必听清，是全部细节，无一例外。只要找出了这样一个人，那么极大概率——在十之八九的案子里，概率便直接等同于客观真相——你便稳稳攥住了真正的作案元凶。”
勒科克先生的这番剖析是如此高屋建瓴，逻辑是如此丝丝入扣，以至于两位听众忍不住击节赞叹：
“精彩！太精彩了！”
“那么现在就让我们共同来查验，假定特雷莫雷尔伯爵就是真凶，能否合理解释瓦尔菲伊现场的所有离奇线索。”
他正打算继续往下剖析，坐在靠窗位置的让德龙医生却猛然站起身来。
“花园里有人，”医生低声警示道。
三人立刻快步凑近窗前。夜色澄澈明朗，月朗星稀，藏书室窗外正对着一片开阔平坦的空地；他们极目远眺，空地上空无一人。
“您看走眼了，医生，”普朗塔先生重新坐回扶手椅中说道。
勒科克先生接着往下说道：
“现在让我们大胆假设，在某些不可抗力的变故逼迫下——我们稍后便来详加剖析这些变故——特雷莫雷尔先生下定决心非除掉自己的结发妻子不可。除掉妻子的念头一旦坐实，显而易见，伯爵必定会煞费苦心反复推敲，搜寻能让自己逃脱法网制裁的金蝉脱壳之计；他必定会权衡利弊，精确丈量每一步行动的凶险代价。

===Original===

Let us admit, also, that the events which led him to this extremity were such that he feared to be disturbed, and that he also feared that a search would be made for certain things, even should his wife die a natural death.”
“That is true,” said M. Plantat, nodding his head.
“Monsieur de Trémorel, then, determined to kill his wife, brutally, with a knife, with the idea of so arranging everything, as to make it believed that he too had been assassinated; and he also decided to endeavor to thrust suspicion on an innocent person, or at least, an accomplice infinitely less guilty than he.
“He made up his mind in advance, in adopting this course, to disappear, fly, conceal himself, change his personality; to suppress, in short, Count Hector de Trémorel, and make for himself, under another name, a new position and identity. These hypotheses, easily admitted, suffice to explain the whole series of otherwise inconsistent circumstances. They explain to us in the first place, how it was that on the very night of the murder, there was a large fortune in ready money at Valfeuillu; and this seems to me decisive. Why, when a man receives sums like this, which he proposes to keep by him, he conceals the fact as carefully as possible. Monsieur de Trémorel had not this common prudence. He shows his bundles of banknotes freely, handles them, parades them; the servants see them, almost touch them. He wants everybody to know and repeat that there is a large sum in the house, easy to take, carry off, and conceal. And what time of all times, does he choose for this display? Exactly the moment when he knows, and everyone in the neighborhood knows, that he is going to pass the night at the château, alone with Madame de Trémorel.
“For he is aware that all his servants are invited, on the evening of July 8th to the wedding of the former cook. So well aware of it is he, that he defrays the wedding expenses, and himself names the day. You will perhaps say that it was by chance that this money was sent to Valfeuillu on the very night of the crime. At the worst that might be admitted. But believe me, there was no chance about it, and I will prove it. We will go tomorrow to the count’s banker, and will inquire whether the count did not ask him, by letter or verbally, to send him these funds precisely on July 8th.

===Chinese===

我们同样必须承认，把他逼上这等绝路的变故是如此凶险致命，以至于他深恐案发后官府大张旗鼓彻查，深恐某些见不得光的隐秘被翻箱倒柜搜寻出来，哪怕妻子纯属暴病自然身亡，他也难逃被搜查的噩运。”
“确实如此，”普朗塔先生点头附和道。
“于是，特雷莫雷尔先生断然决定用最残暴利落的手段手刃妻子，动用利刃，并精心布置伪造好一切现场痕迹，好让全天下的人都深信他自己同样在这场浩劫中横遭屠戮、尸骨无存；与此同时，他还处心积虑企图把祸水东引，栽赃嫁祸给某个清白无辜之人，抑或是某个罪不至死的替罪羊。
“在敲定这套方略的刹那，他便早已在心底做好了抹去一切过往痕迹的决断：人间蒸发，畏罪潜逃，隐姓埋名，彻底改头换面；简而言之，就是从肉体和法律意义上彻底抹杀‘埃克托·德·特雷莫雷尔伯爵’这个人，换上一副崭新的面孔和身份，在阴暗角落里开启全新的生活。只要确立了这一逻辑假设，此前看似荒诞不经、自相矛盾的所有离奇细节，瞬间便变得顺理成章、豁然开朗。首先，这一假设合理解释了为何偏偏在案发当夜，瓦尔菲伊宅邸里竟然存放着大笔巨额现钞；而在在下看来，这一点最具决定性意义。试想，一个寻常人若收到如此巨额的现金并打算留在身边，向来唯恐走漏半点风声，必定谨小慎微藏之唯恐不及。特雷莫雷尔先生却反常到了极点。他肆无忌惮地公开展示大捆大捆的钞票，当众把玩摩挲，大肆炫耀；下人们全看在眼里，甚至触手可及。他唯恐天下不知，巴不得所有人都到处传话，声称宅邸里存放着一笔天降巨款，极易盗取、极易携带、极易藏匿。而他究竟挑选了怎样一个荒唐的时辰来大肆炫耀这笔横财呢？偏偏挑中了他明知、且四邻八乡所有人都明知他将与伯爵夫人二人独守空房的这一夜！
“因为他心里雪亮，宅邸里的所有仆役都接到了请帖，将于7月8日傍晚动身前往巴黎参加老厨娘的婚宴。他不仅对此心知肚明，甚至还慷慨解囊全额包办了婚宴的全部开销，并亲自敲定了举办婚宴的黄道吉日。诸位或许会辩解说，这笔巨款恰巧在案发当夜送达瓦尔菲伊纯属巧合。退一万步讲，这种巧合在理论上或许勉强说得过去。但在下敢断言，这绝非巧合，而且在下必定会拿出铁证来。明早咱们只需拜会伯爵开户的银行掌柜，当面查验伯爵究竟是否曾通过亲笔信函或当面嘱托，指名道姓要求银行务必在7月8日这天将巨款如数送达宅邸。

===Original===

Well, if he says yes, if he shows us such a letter, or if he declares that the money was called for in person, you will confess, no doubt, that I have more than a probability in favor of my theory.”
Both his hearers bowed in token of assent.
“So far, then, there is no objection.”
“Not the least,” said M. Plantat.
“My conjectures have also the advantage of shedding light on Guespin’s position. Honestly, his appearance is against him, and justifies his arrest. Was he an accomplice or entirely innocent? We certainly cannot yet decide. But it is a fact that he has fallen into an admirably well-laid trap. The count, in selecting him for his victim, took all care that every doubt possible should weigh upon him. I would wager that Monsieur de Trémorel, who knew this fellow’s history, thought that his antecedents would add probability to the suspicions against him, and would weigh with a terrible weight in the scales of justice. Perhaps, too, he said to himself that Guespin would be sure to prove his innocence in the end, and he only wished to gain time to elude the first search. It is impossible that we can be deceived. We know that the countess died of the first blow, as if thunderstruck. She did not struggle; therefore she could not have torn a piece of cloth off the assassin’s vest. If you admit Guespin’s guilt, you admit that he was idiot enough to put a piece of his vest in his victim’s hand; you admit that he was such a fool as to go and throw this torn and bloody vest into the Seine, from a bridge, in a place where he might know search would be made﻿—and all this, without taking the common precaution of attaching it to a stone to carry it to the bottom. That would be absurd.
“To me, then, this piece of cloth, this smeared vest, indicate at once Guespin’s innocence and the count’s guilt.”
“But,” objected Dr. Gendron, “if Guespin is innocent, why don’t he talk? Why don’t he prove an alibi? How was it he had his purse full of money?”
“Observe,” resumed the detective, “that I don’t say he is innocent; we are still among the probabilities. Can’t you suppose that the count, perfidious enough to set a trap for his servant, was shrewd enough to deprive him of every means of proving an alibi?”
“But you yourself deny the count’s shrewdness.”
“I beg your pardon; please hear me.

===Chinese===

只要银行掌柜点头称是，只要他出示了这封信函，或者证实巨款确实是伯爵亲自登门提取的，二位想必不得不承认，在下的推论假说早已拥有了压倒性的铁证支持。”
两位听众双双颔首，深表赞同。
“既然如此，截至目前，逻辑上可有任何破绽漏洞？”
“毫无破绽，”普朗塔先生赞叹道。
“在下的这套推论，同样顺理成章厘清了盖斯潘身上的不白之冤。平心而论，盖斯潘的个人行迹与前科劣迹确实对他极其不利，官府将他缉拿归案完全顺理成章。他究竟是从犯还是彻头彻尾的冤大头？眼下我们尚不能轻下定论。但有一点毋庸置疑，那就是他落入了一个极其精巧老辣的连环陷阱之中。伯爵在选中他充当替死鬼时，处心积虑把所有足以置人于死地的致命疑点全往他身上引。在下敢打赌，特雷莫雷尔先生深知这个仆人的底细，料定盖斯潘的前科劣迹会无限放大司法官的怀疑，在审判的天平上成为压垮他的千钧重石。或许伯爵内心也清楚，盖斯潘早晚能够自证清白，然而伯爵图谋的不过是在案发初期为自己的潜逃争取宝贵的时间差而已。我们绝不可能被眼前的障眼法所蒙蔽。我们早已查明，伯爵夫人是在遭受第一记致命一击的刹那便如同遭遇晴天霹雳般气绝身亡的。她根本没有过任何挣扎反抗；因此她绝不可能从凶手的背心上硬生生撕扯下一截布条。倘若诸位认定盖斯潘是真凶，那就等于承认他是个蠢到会亲手把自家背心的布条塞进死者掌心里的白痴；等于承认他是个蠢到大费周章把这件沾满鲜血的撕裂背心大摇大摆从铁索桥上抛入塞纳河中、且明知官府必会下河搜查的笨蛋——而且在抛尸弃物时，竟然连系上一块石头沉底这等最粗浅的防范都没做。这未免太荒谬绝伦了。
“因此在在下看来，掌心的那截碎布与沉入水底的血背心，恰恰同时雄辩地证明了两点：盖斯潘是清白无辜的，而伯爵才是真正的嗜血元凶。”
“但是，”让德龙医生提出反驳，“如果盖斯潘当真是清白的，他为何负隅顽抗拒不开口？为何拿不出铁证如山的不在场证明？他的皮夹子里怎么会塞得满满当当装满巨款？”
“请医生注意，”大侦探回应道，“在下并未武断认定盖斯潘百分之百无辜；我们依然在概率的迷宫中摸索。您难道就不能假设，心狠手辣到能给自家仆人设下死局的伯爵，其心机手段足以高明到事先剥夺仆人自证清白的一切不在场可能吗？”
“可你刚才还在口口声声嘲弄伯爵手段笨拙呢。”
“请原谅，容在下细细道来。

===Original===

The count’s plan was excellent, and shows a superior kind of perversity; the execution alone was defective. This is because the plan was conceived and perfected in safety, while when the crime had been committed, the murderer, distressed, frightened at his danger, lost his coolness and only half executed his project. But there are other suppositions. It might be asked whether, while Madame de Trémorel was being murdered, Guespin might not have been committing some other crime elsewhere.”
This conjecture seemed so improbable to the doctor that he could not avoid objecting to it. “Oh!” muttered he.
“Don’t forget,” replied Lecoq, “that the field of conjectures has no bounds. Imagine whatever complication of events you may, I am ready to maintain that such a complication has occurred or will present itself. Lieuben, a German lunatic, bet that he would succeed in turning up a pack of cards in the order stated in the written agreement. He turned and turned ten hours per day for twenty years. He had repeated the operation 4,246,028 times, when he succeeded.”
M. Lecoq was about to proceed with another illustration, when M. Plantat interrupted him by a gesture.
“I admit your hypotheses; I think they are more than probable﻿—they are true.”
M. Lecoq, as he spoke, paced up and down between the window and the bookshelves, stopping at emphatic words, like a general who dictates to his aides the plan of the morrow’s battle. To his auditors, he seemed a new man, with serious features, an eye bright with intelligence, his sentences clear and concise﻿—the Lecoq, in short, which the magistrates who have employed his talents, would recognize.
“Now,” he resumed, “hear me. It is ten o’clock at night. No noise without, the road deserted, the village lights extinguished, the château servants away at Paris. The count and countess are alone at Valfeuillu.
“They have gone to their bedroom.
“The countess has seated herself at the table where tea has been served. The count, as he talks with her, paces up and down the chamber.”
“Madame de Trémorel has no ill presentiment; her husband, the past few days, has been more amiable, more attentive than ever. She mistrusts nothing, and so the count can approach her from behind, without her thinking of turning her head.
“When she hears him coming up softly, she imagines that he is going to surprise her with a kiss.

===Chinese===

伯爵的谋划是极其完美的，展现出一种极具天分的犯罪邪恶心智；唯独在执行操作环节露出了破绽。这全是因为这套毒计是在绝对安全的心境下构思完善的，而一旦大案真正犯下，凶手在剧烈的心理震荡与迫在眉睫的危机压迫下，丧失了绝对的冷静理智，以至于在执行中破绽百出。然而世间还存在着另一种推测。我们大可以发问：当特雷莫雷尔夫人在瓦尔菲伊惨遭杀戮的同时，盖斯潘会不会正巧在巴黎的某个阴暗角落里犯下了另一桩见不得光的罪行呢？”
这项推论在医生听来显得是如此荒诞离奇，以至于他忍不住脱口反驳道：“哎呀！”
“切莫忘记，”勒科克先生正色答道，“推演推测的疆域是无边无际的。无论二位想象出怎样不可思议的错综偶合，在下都敢断言，这样的人间巧合在以往确实发生过，在将来也必定会重现人间。德意志有个名叫柳本（Lieuben）的狂人，曾同一帮赌徒打赌，声称自己能把一副洗乱的纸牌完全按照字据上预先写好的顺序逐张翻出来。他每天足足翻牌10个小时，持续了整整20年。在他把整套洗牌翻牌的操作机械重复了4,246,028次之后，他竟然当真创造了奇迹成功了。”
勒科克先生刚打算抛出另一则轶事佐证，普朗塔先生却抬手制止了他。
“我全盘接纳您的假说；依我看，这些推论远不止是概率——它们正是铁打的事实。”
勒科克先生说话间，在窗台与书架之间来回踱步，每逢说到要害之处便霍然驻足，宛如一位大将军在向副官们口授次日决战的阵图。在两位听众眼中，他整个人焕然一新，面容庄严肃穆，双眸闪烁着敏锐智慧的光芒，字字句句如刀砍斧削般干脆利落——简而言之，这正是那些曾领教过他过人手段的司法巨擘们所熟识的王牌侦探勒科克。
“现在，”他接着说道，“请诸位静听。时值深夜10点。屋外万籁俱寂，乡间小道上空无一人，村舍的烛火早已熄灭，城堡里的仆役全在巴黎狂欢。瓦尔菲伊宅邸里唯有伯爵夫妇二人独处。
“他们走进了二楼卧室。
“伯爵夫人安坐在奉上香茗的小茶几旁。伯爵一边同她攀谈闲聊，一边在寝居里悠闲踱步。
“特雷莫雷尔夫人心头毫无半点不祥的预感；在刚刚过去的几天里，她的丈夫表现得比以往任何时候都要温存体贴。她毫无戒备之心，以至于伯爵能够悄然潜行至她身后，她甚至连扭头回望一眼的念头都没有过。
“当她听见身后传来轻轻的脚步声时，她满心以为丈夫是要悄悄上前给她一个惊喜的温存亲吻呢。
“而此时此刻，手握利刃的他——”

===Original===

He, meanwhile, armed with a long dagger, stands beside his wife. He knows where to strike that the wound may be mortal. He chooses the place at a glance; takes aim; strikes a terrible blow﻿—so terrible that the handle of the dagger imprints itself on both sides of the wound. The countess falls without a sound, bruising her forehead on the edge of the table, which is overturned. Is not the position of the terrible wound below the left shoulder thus explained﻿—a wound almost vertical, its direction being from right to left?”
The doctor made a motion of assent.
“And who, besides a woman’s lover or her husband is admitted to her chamber, or can approach her when she is seated without her turning round?”
“That’s clear,” muttered M. Plantat.
“The countess is now dead,” pursued M. Lecoq. “The assassin’s first emotion is one of triumph. He is at last rid of her who was his wife, whom he hated enough to murder her, and to change his happy, splendid, envied existence for a frightful life, henceforth without country, friend, or refuge, proscribed by all nations, tracked by all the police, punishable by the laws of all the world! His second thought is of this letter or paper, this object of small size which he knows to be in his wife’s keeping, which he has demanded a hundred times, which she would not give up to him, and which he must have.”
“Add,” interrupted M. Plantat, “that this paper was one of the motives of the crime.”
“The count thinks he knows where it is. He imagines that he can put his hand on it at once. He is mistaken. He looks into all the drawers and bureaus used by his wife﻿—and finds nothing. He searches every corner, he lifts up the shelves, overturns everything in the chamber﻿—nothing. An idea strikes him. Is this letter under the mantelshelf? By a turn of the arm he lifts it﻿—down the clock tumbles and stops. It is not yet half-past ten.”
“Yes,” murmured the doctor, “the clock betrays that.”
“The count finds nothing under the mantelshelf except the dust, which has retained traces of his fingers. Then he begins to be anxious. Where can this paper be, for which he has risked his life? He grows angry. How search the locked drawers? The keys are on the carpet﻿—I found them among the debris of the tea service﻿—but he does not see them. He must have some implement with which to break open everything. He goes downstairs for a hatchet.

===Chinese===

趁着她微微前倾的刹那，他高高扬起蓄谋已久的手臂，从背后狠狠刺下了致命的一刀！这一刀刺得极深、极狠，直贯要害。受害者甚至连一声哀鸣都未能吐出，便当场气绝身亡，身子猛烈前扑，前额重重砸在了茶几硬角上，连带着将桌上的茶具撞得粉碎。特雷莫雷尔夫人就此毙命。
“大案的第一阶段就此告竣。伯爵眼下成了这栋豪宅唯一的绝对主宰。他深知仆人们要在巴黎狂欢整整一夜，要到翌日破晓时分搭乘头班火车才会折返。他坐拥大把宝贵的光阴。眼下摆在他面前的第一要务，便是搜寻出那份逼得他铤而走险弑妻的绝密文件。他动手翻查死者的衣袋。什么都没有。他搜遍了小闺房，拉开抽屉，翻看首饰盒；依旧空空如也。他开始焦躁不安起来。那份让他赌上了身家性命的致命文书究竟被藏在了何处？他的怒火开始熊熊燃烧。怎么打开那些上了锁的柜子？钥匙散落在地毯上——正是后来在下在茶具碎片中摸出的那一串——然而在惊慌之中他根本视而不见。他必须寻觅一把能把一切陈设砸个稀巴烂的顺手兵刃。于是他举步下楼，直奔杂物间去取斧头。

===Original===

The drunkenness of blood and vengeance is dissipated on the staircase; his terrors begin. All the dark corners are peopled, now, with those spectres which form the cortege of assassins; he is frightened, and hurries on. He soon goes up again, armed with a large hatchet﻿—that found on the second story﻿—and makes the pieces of wood fly about him. He goes about like a maniac, rips up the furniture at hazard; but he pursues a desperate search, the traces of which I have followed, among the debris. Nothing, always nothing! Everything in the room is topsy-turvy; he goes into his cabinet and continues the destruction; the hatchet rises and falls without rest. He breaks his own bureau, since he may find something concealed there of which he is ignorant. This bureau belonged to the first husband﻿—to Sauvresy. He takes out all the books in the library, one by one, shakes them furiously, and throws them about the floor. The infernal paper is undiscoverable. His distress is now too great for him to pursue the search with the least method. His wandering reason no longer guides him. He staggers, without calculation, from one thing to another, fumbling a dozen times in the same drawer, while he completely forgets others just by him. Then he thinks that this paper may have been hid in the stuffing of a chair. He seizes a sword, and to be certain, he slashes up the drawing-room chairs and sofas and those in the other rooms.”
M. Lecoq’s voice, accent, gestures, gave a vivid character to his recital. The hearer might imagine that he saw the crime committed, and was present at the terrible scenes which he described. His companions held their breath, unwilling by a movement to distract his attention.
“At this moment,” pursued he, “the count’s rage and terror were at their height. He had said to himself, when he planned the murder, that he would kill his wife, get possession of the letter, execute his plan quickly, and fly. And now all his projects were baffled! How much time was being lost, when each minute diminished the chances of escape! Then the probability of a thousand dangers which had not occurred to him, entered his mind. What if some friend should suddenly arrive, expecting his hospitality, as had occurred twenty times? What if a passerby on the road should notice a light flying from room to room? Might not one of the servants return?

===Chinese===

鲜血与复仇带来的疯狂嗜血眩晕，在走下楼梯的冷风中渐渐消散；彻骨的恐惧开始反噬他的心智。所有漆黑的墙角与阴影深处，此刻仿佛全挤满了随行在杀人凶手身后的狰狞冤魂恶鬼；他吓坏了，脚步越发慌乱仓皇。很快他又折返上楼，手里多了一把劈柴的大短斧——正是后来在三楼被我们发现的那一把——在狂怒与恐慌中，木屑与碎木块在他身旁四处飞溅。他宛如一个发狂的疯子，见柜劈柜，见箱砸箱；展开了一场绝望透顶的疯狂大搜寻，在下一路追踪着散落现场的满地残骸，将他的搜寻轨迹摸得一清二楚。没有，到处都是一无所获！卧室里的一切全被翻了个底朝天；他快步冲进自己的书房继续肆虐破坏；短斧不知疲倦地一起一落。他连自己的大写字台也劈成了碎片，生怕里面暗藏着连他自己都未曾察觉的夹层暗格。要知道，这张写字台曾属于前任主人——索夫勒齐。他把藏书室里的藏书一本接一本抽出来，疯狂地拼命抖搂，随后狠狠甩在满地的碎木屑中。那份该死的魔鬼文件却宛如石沉大海，杳无踪迹。此时此刻他内心的惶恐绝望已然达到了顶点，彻底丧失了哪怕最起码的条理与章法。错乱的心智再也无法指引理智。他像个无头苍蝇般在房间里跌跌撞撞，在同一个拉开的抽屉里来来回回摸索了10多遍，却把自己身旁近在咫尺的其他柜子忘得一干二净。接着他心头猛然闪过一个念头：那份文件会不会被缝进了座椅的填充棉絮里？于是他拔出一柄利剑，为了图个彻底踏实，他像个疯子一样把客厅里的所有扶手椅、长榻以及其余房间里的坐垫砍得千疮百孔、稀烂不堪。”
勒科克先生的嗓音、语调与手势，赋予了他的叙述一种身临其境的骇人现场感。在座的听众仿佛亲眼目睹了凶案的血腥发生，亲身置身于他所描绘的那幕惨烈绝伦的狂暴梦魇之中。两位同伴屏息凝神，生怕发出半点声响打断他的推理演绎。
“就在这个关头，”大侦探接着说道，“伯爵内心的狂暴与惊恐已然飙升到了巅峰。当他当初谋划这桩杀妻大案时，他曾对自己盘算得天衣无缝：手刃妻子，搜出秘信，利落伪造现场，从容远走高飞。而如今他所有的全盘算计全被彻底粉碎了！白白耗费了多少宝贵的光阴啊，每一分每一秒的流逝，都在急剧吞噬着他逃出生天的侥幸生机！紧接着，无数此前他从未料想过的致命凶险骤然涌上了心头。万一突然有哪个夜行的朋友登门投宿呢，正如以往发生过20次的那样？万一公路上的过路行人瞧见城堡的窗户里彻夜烛火通明、人影乱窜呢？万一有哪个仆人因为身体不适提前搭车折返回来呢？

===Original===

When he is in the drawing-room, he thinks he hears someone ring at the gate; such is his terror, that he lets his candle fall﻿—for I have found the marks of it on the carpet. He hears strange noises, such as never before assailed his ears; he thinks he hears walking in the next room; the floor creaks. Is his wife really dead; will she not suddenly rise up, run to the window, and scream for help? Beset by these terrors, he returns to the bedroom, seizes his dagger, and again strikes the poor countess. But his hand is so unsteady that the wounds are light. You have observed, doctor, that all these wounds take the same direction. They form right angles with the body, proving that the victim was lying down when they were inflicted. Then, in the excess of his frenzy, he strikes the body with his feet, and his heels form the contusions discovered by the autopsy.”
M. Lecoq paused to take breath. He not only narrated the drama, he acted it, adding gesture to word; and each of his phrases made a scene, explained a fact, and dissipated a doubt. Like all true artists who wrap themselves up in the character they represent, the detective really felt something of the sensations which he interpreted, and his expressive face was terrible in its contortions.
“That,” he resumed, “is the first act of the drama. An irresistible prostration succeeds the count’s furious passion. The various circumstances which I am describing to you are to be noticed in nearly all great crimes. The assassin is always seized, after the murder, with a horrible and singular hatred against his victim, and he often mutilates the body. Then comes the period of a prostration so great, of torpor so irresistible, that murderers have been known literally to go to sleep in the blood, that they have been surprised sleeping, and that it was with great difficulty that they were awakened. The count, when he has frightfully disfigured the poor lady, falls into an armchair; indeed, the cloth of one of the chairs has retained some wrinkles, which shows that someone had sat in it. What are then the count’s thoughts? He reflects on the long hours which have elapsed, upon the few hours which remain to him.

===Chinese===

当他潜入客厅时，他甚至幻听到大门外的门铃在尖厉作响；恐惧是如此剧烈，以至于他手里的蜡烛失手摔落在了地上——因为在下在地毯上发现了蜡油滴落的焦痕。他听到了无数此前从未袭击过他耳膜的怪异声响；他仿佛听见隔壁房间里有沉重的脚步声在挪动；地板在嘎吱作响。自己的妻子当真死透了吗；她会不会突然直挺挺从血泊中爬起来，扑向窗户放声尖叫救命？在这重重恶鬼索命般的极度恐惧纠缠下，他重新发疯似地冲回卧室，拔出随身短剑，对着可怜的伯爵夫人疯狂补刺。然而由于他的双手抖得实在太剧烈了，刺出的伤口全都极其浅表微弱。医生，您在验尸时想必留意到了，所有这些补刺的伤痕全呈同一走向。它们同死者的躯体呈垂直直角，铁证如山地证明受害者是在仰面平躺的状态下遭受刺击的。随后，在歇斯底里的狂暴宣泄中，他甚至飞起双脚狠狠践踏尸体，他鞋跟的重击便形成了法医解剖中发现的那些皮下淤血挫伤。”
勒科克先生停下话头喘了一口气。他不仅仅是在讲述这桩大戏，更是在全身心演绎它，举手投足间无不饱含戏剧张力；他的每一个字句都在勾勒一幕场景，都在剖析一项事实，都在驱散一团迷雾。宛如所有将灵魂融入角色的伟大演员一样，大侦探真真切切感受到了自己所刻画的人格的极度煎熬，他极富表现力的面庞在痉挛扭曲中显得格外狰狞可怖。
“那，”他接着说道，“正是这场大戏的第一幕。狂暴的歇斯底里过后，排山倒海的极度虚脱随之笼罩了伯爵的心智。在下向二位描述的这些心理与行为轨迹，在世间几乎所有重大恶性罪案中皆屡见不鲜。杀人犯在行凶得手之后，心头往往会滋生出一股针对受害者的莫名彻骨仇恨，往往伴随着对遗体的疯狂残害。紧随其后的，便是一段极度沉沦的麻木虚脱期，这种生理疲惫是如此难以抗拒，以至于在诸多凶案中，凶手甚至会荒诞地直接瘫倒在血泊中呼呼大睡，以至于官府破门而入时人还在梦乡之中，费尽九牛二虎之力才把犯人从沉睡中摇醒。当伯爵把可怜的夫人残害得面目全非之后，他整个人瘫倒在了一张扶手椅中；事实上，其中一张椅子的布面上至今保留着坐压形成的褶皱，证明确实曾有人在上面瘫坐过。此时此刻伯爵脑子里在琢磨些什么？他在苦苦盘算着已经流逝的大把钟点，以及留给自己的屈指可数的最后时间。

===Original===

He reflects that he has found nothing; that he will hardly have time, before day, to execute his plans for turning suspicion from him, and assure his safety, by creating an impression that he, too, has been murdered. And he must fly at once﻿—fly, without that accursed paper. He summons up his energies, rises, and do you know what he does? He seizes a pair of scissors and cuts off his long, carefully cultivated beard.”
“Ah!” interrupted M. Plantat, “that’s why you examined the portrait so closely.”
M. Lecoq was too intent on following the thread of his deductions to note the interruption.
“This is one of those vulgar details,” pursued he, “whose very insignificance makes them terrible, when they are attended by certain circumstances. Now imagine the Count de Trémorel, pale, covered with his wife’s blood, shaving himself before his glass; rubbing the soap over his face, in that room all topsy-turvy, while three steps off lies the still warm and palpitating body! It was an act of terrible courage, believe me, to look at himself in the glass after a murder﻿—one of which few criminals are capable. The count’s hands, however, trembled so violently that he could scarcely hold his razor, and his face must have been cut several times.”
“What!” said Dr. Gendron, “do you imagine that the count spared the time to shave?”
“I am positively sure of it, pos-i-tive-ly. A towel on which I have found one of those marks which a razor leaves when it is wiped﻿—and one only﻿—has put me on the track of this fact. I looked about, and found a box of razors, one of which had recently been used, for it was still moist; and I have carefully preserved both the towel and the box. And if these proofs are not enough, I will send to Paris for two of my men, who will find, somewhere in the house or the garden, both the count’s beard and the cloth with which he wiped his razor. As to the fact which surprises you, Doctor, it seems to me very natural; more, it is the necessary result of the plan he adopted. Monsieur de Trémorel has always worn his full beard: he cuts it off, and his appearance is so entirely altered, that if he met anyone in his flight, he would not be recognized.”
The doctor was apparently convinced, for he cried:
“It’s clear﻿—it’s evident.”

===Chinese===

他意识到自己一无所获；意识到在天色大亮之前，他恐怕几乎没有足够的时间来完成把罪责转移到旁人头上、并伪造自己同样横遭杀害以确保全身而退的善后计划了。他必须立刻逃之夭夭——哪怕没有搜到那张该死的文件，也必须火速奔逃。他强撑起全副意志，霍然站起身来，二位可知他接下来干了件什么惊人之举吗？他抓起一把剪刀，硬生生剪掉了自己精心蓄养多年的长胡须。”
“啊！”普朗塔先生失声打断道，“这正是在下为何见您在卧室里久久端详伯爵大幅肖像画的缘由了。”
勒科克先生全身心沉浸在推理链条之中，甚至无暇理会老人的插话。
“这属于世俗那些最微不足道的细枝末节之一，”他接着说，“正是其微不足道，在特定环境下才显得如此骇人听闻。诸位不妨在脑海中勾勒一番这样的场景：特雷莫雷尔伯爵，面如死灰，满身沾满妻子的滚烫鲜血，站在镜子前给自己刮胡子；在整间被砸得稀烂的卧室里，在距离自己不过三步之遥、妻子温热尚存且血肉模糊的遗体旁，他在自己脸上涂抹着肥皂泡！请二位相信在下，在刚刚犯下弑妻弥天大罪之后敢于端详镜中的自己，需要何等惊世骇俗的亡命胆魄——世间鲜有杀人犯能够承受得住这等心理折磨。然而伯爵的双手抖得实在太剧烈了，以至于他几乎连剃刀都握不稳，脸上必定被划破了好几道口子。”
“什么！”让德龙医生惊呼道，“你难道当真认定伯爵竟然还有闲心在现场刮脸？”
“在下有绝对的把握，绝-对-把-握。一条被我搜获的毛巾，上面赫然留有一道用剃刀刀刃擦拭刀口留下的皂沫血渍——而且唯有一道——正是它让在下顺藤摸瓜锁定了这一铁证。我在四周翻查，找到了一只剃刀盒，其中一把剃刀刀刃最近刚被动用过，因为上面至今尚残留着水汽潮斑；在下已将那条毛巾和剃刀盒全部作为物证封存。若是这些证据尚不足以服众，在下明早自会从巴黎调遣两名得力干将，他们不出半日便能在宅邸或花园的某个角落里，起获伯爵剪下的胡须以及擦拭剃刀的旧布条。至于令医生大感惊愕的这一举动，在在下看来反倒再顺理成章不过了；甚至可以说是他推行这套潜逃大计的必然选择。特雷莫雷尔先生平生向来蓄着满面连鬓美髯：只要他一剃光胡须，整个人容貌大变，以至于在奔逃途中哪怕迎面撞见熟人，旁人也绝难一眼认出他的真容。”
医生显然被彻底说服了，失声赞叹道：
“太清晰了——简直如白昼般一目了然。”

===Original===

“Once thus disguised, the count hastens to carry out the rest of his plan, to arrange everything to throw the law off the scent, and to make it appear that he, as well as his wife, has been murdered. He hunts up Guespin’s vest, tears it out at the pocket, and puts a piece of it in the countess’s hand. Then taking the body in his arms, crosswise, he goes downstairs. The wounds bleed frightfully﻿—hence the numerous stains discovered all along his path. Reaching the foot of the staircase he is obliged to put the countess down, in order to open the garden-door. This explains the large stain in the vestibule. The count, having opened the door, returns for the body and carries it in his arms as far as the edge of the lawn; there he stops carrying it, and drags it by the shoulders, walking backward, trying thus to create the impression that his own body has been dragged across there and thrown into the Seine. But the wretch forgot two things which betray him to us. He did not reflect that the countess’s skirts, in being dragged along the grass, pressing it down and breaking it for a considerable space, spoiled his trick. Nor did he think that her elegant and well-curved feet, encased in small high-heeled boots, would mould themselves in the damp earth of the lawn, and thus leave against him a proof clearer than the day.”
M. Plantat rose abruptly.
“Ah,” said he, “you said nothing of this before.”
“Nor of several other things, either. But I was before ignorant of some facts which I now know; and as I had reason to suppose that you were better informed than I, I was not sorry to avenge myself for a caution which seemed to me mysterious.”
“Well, you are avenged,” remarked the doctor, smiling.
“On the other side of the lawn,” continued M. Lecoq, “the count again took up the countess’s body. But forgetting the effect of water when it spirts, or﻿—who knows?﻿—disliking to soil himself, instead of throwing her violently in the river, he put her down softly, with great precaution. That’s not all. He wished it to appear that there had been a terrible struggle. What does he do? Stirs up the sand with the end of his foot. And he thinks that will deceive the police!”
“Yes, yes,” muttered Plantat, “exactly so﻿—I saw it.”
“Having got rid of the body, the count returns to the house. Time presses, but he is still anxious to find the paper.

===Chinese===

“易容打扮妥当之后，伯爵便急于实施计划的其余部分，布置一切以蒙蔽司法机关，制造出他本人同妻子一样遭遇谋杀的假象。他翻出了盖斯潘的背心，撕扯掉口袋处的一大块布料，硬塞进了伯爵夫人的掌心里。随后他横抱着妻子的遗体，一步步走下楼梯。死者身上的刀口疯狂向外渗血——这正是我们在沿途地毯与大理石地面上发现大量血斑的由头。来到楼梯底层，为了腾出手来打开通往花园的玻璃大门，他不得不先把伯爵夫人的遗体放倒在地板上。这正好解释了门厅大理石地面上的那一滩巨大血泊。伯爵拉开大门后，重新抱起尸体，一路穿行抱到了草坪边缘；在那里，他不再抱运尸体，而是改用抓着死者双肩、倒退着倒行的方式拖拽尸体，试图借此伪造出他自己的遗体被凶手一路拖过草坪抛入塞纳河的假象。然而这个恶徒却百密一疏遗漏了两处致命破绽，将他彻底出卖给了我们。他万万没想到，伯爵夫人厚重沉实的长裙在被拖过草坪时，将大片草丛压平折断，其宽阔的倒伏面积彻底砸了他的如意算盘。他更没想到，伯爵夫人那双纤细秀美、穿着高跟精致皮靴的双脚，深深踩印在草坪湿润的泥土中，留下了比白昼还要清晰确凿的反向铁证。”
普朗塔先生霍然起身。
“啊，”他说，“这些细节您先前可半个字都没向我透露过呢。”
“还有好几桩要紧事在下先前同样守口如瓶呢。只因在下先前尚未掌握眼下厘清的诸多事实；更何况在下先前料定您老掌握的内情远比在下要深厚得多，因此在下借机小施惩戒，报复一下您先前高深莫测的戒备之心，也算扯平了嘛。”
“好吧，这回算你扯平了，”医生微笑着打趣道。
“穿过草坪来到对岸后，”勒科克先生接着说道，“伯爵再度抱起了夫人的遗体。然而由于忘却了水花四溅的物理定律，抑或是——天知道呢？——出于嫌脏怕弄脏自身衣服的本能，他没有狠命将尸体抛入河心，而是极其小心翼翼、从容不迫地将遗体轻轻平放在泥滩浅水里。这还不是全部。他巴望着伪造出岸边曾发生过一场殊死搏斗的假象。他干了些什么？他用脚尖在沙滩上胡乱踩踏翻捣。他天真地以为这样的小儿科把戏就能把堂堂刑警糊弄过去！”
“没错，没错，”普朗塔喃喃自语道，“一点不错——我当时亲眼瞧见了。”
“料理完尸体之后，伯爵匆忙折返回宅邸。时间万分紧迫，但他心头依然贼心不死，贪恋着那份绝密文件。

===Original===

He hastens to take the last measures to assure his safety. He smears his slippers and handkerchief with blood. He throws his handkerchief and one of his slippers on the sward, and the other slipper into the river. His haste explains the incomplete execution of his manoeuvres. He hurries﻿—and commits blunder after blunder. He does not reflect that his valet will explain about the empty bottles which he puts on the table. He thinks he is turning wine into the five glasses﻿—it is vinegar, which will prove that no one has drunk out of them. He ascends, puts forward the hands of the clock, but forgets to put the hands and the striking bell in harmony. He rumples up the bed, but he does it awkwardly﻿—and it is impossible to reconcile these three facts, the bed crumpled, the clock showing twenty minutes past three, and the countess dressed as if it were midday. He adds as much as he can to the disorder of the room. He smears a sheet with blood; also the bed-curtains and furniture. Then he marks the door with the imprint of a bloody hand, too distinct and precise not to be done designedly. Is there so far a circumstance or detail of the crime, which does not explain the count’s guilt?”
“There’s the hatchet,” answered M. Plantat, “found on the second story, the position of which seemed so strange to you.”
“I am coming to that. There is one point in this mysterious affair, which, thanks to you, is now clear. We know that Madame de Trémorel, known to her husband, possessed and concealed a paper or a letter, which he wanted, and which she obstinately refused to give up in spite of all his entreaties. You have told us that the anxiety﻿—perhaps the necessity﻿—to have this paper, was a powerful motive of the crime. We will not be rash then in supposing that the importance of this paper was immense﻿—entirely beyond an ordinary affair. It must have been, somehow, very damaging to one or the other. To whom? To both, or only the count? Here I am reduced to conjectures. It is certain that it was a menace﻿—capable of being executed at any moment﻿—suspended over the head of him or them concerned by it. Madame de Trémorel surely regarded this paper either as a security, or as a terrible arm which put her husband at her mercy. It was surely to deliver himself from this perpetual menace that the count killed his wife.”

===Chinese===

他急于采取最后的脱身伪装措施。他把自己的拖鞋和手帕统统涂抹上鲜血。他把手帕和其中一只拖鞋随手扔在草地上，把另一只拖鞋扔进了河里。内心的慌乱仓促完美解释了他在执行手法上的粗糙纰漏。他越是急躁——露出的破绽便越多。他压根没料到贴身男仆能一清二楚解释清楚他在餐桌上码放的那几只空酒瓶的来历。他满以为自己往5只酒杯里倒的是陈年葡萄酒——哪知道倒进去的竟然是酸醋，这恰恰铁证如山证明根本没人真正喝过一口。他登上次楼，随手把座钟的指针往前拨，却忘了让打鸣报时的机芯同表盘指针保持步调一致。他把床铺弄乱，动起手来却拙笨不堪——以至于这三项事实根本无法自圆其说：床铺被人故意揉乱、座钟赫然停在3点20分，而伯爵夫人遇害时身上却穿着大白天盛装的大礼服。他尽可能把屋里的陈设破坏得越发狼藉。他抓起被单涂抹上鲜血；床幔和家具上也全蹭上了血迹。接着他在房门上按上了一个血手印，这手印按得过于端正清晰，若非处心积虑故意为之绝无第二种可能。截至目前，大案现场遗留下来的所有蛛丝马迹，哪一项不能用伯爵作案来合理解释？”
“还有那把短斧呢，”普朗塔先生提醒道，“在三楼地板上起获的那把斧头，其奇特的投掷朝向先前可把您难为得够呛呢。”
“在下正要说到要害处呢。在这桩离奇大案中，多亏了您老的指点，眼下终于有一处核心节点彻底水落石出了。我们心知肚明，特雷莫雷尔夫人手中藏有一份绝密文件或信函，伯爵对此一清二楚，急于将其据为己有，而夫人任凭丈夫如何软磨硬泡苦苦哀求，始终执拗拒绝交出。您老方才告诉我们，搜寻——甚至是必须夺取——这份文件的迫切欲望，正是催生这起杀妻大案的根本动因。那么我们不妨大胆推定，这份文件的利害关系必定大得超乎想象——绝非寻常琐事可比。它必定包藏着对他们其中一人、抑或是对双方都足以招致灭顶之灾的致命把柄。究竟是对谁致命？是对双方，还是唯独对伯爵一人？在此在下只能诉诸于合理的推测。但可以断定的是，那必定是一柄随时能够落下的达摩克利斯之剑——时刻悬挂在当事人的头顶之上。特雷莫雷尔夫人必定是将这份文件视作保全自身的免死金牌，或是牢牢将丈夫掌控在股掌之间的致命杀手锏。伯爵正是为了彻底摆脱这柄如影随形、时刻悬在头顶的利刃，才横下心来残杀结发妻子的。”

===Original===

The logic was so clear, the last words brought the evidence out so lucidly and forcibly, that his hearers were struck with admiration. They both cried:
“Very good!”
“Now,” resumed M. Lecoq, “from the various elements which have served to form our conviction, we must conclude that the contents of this letter, if it can be found, will clear away our last doubts, will explain the crime, and will render the assassin’s precautions wholly useless. The count, therefore, must do everything in the world, must attempt the impossible, not to leave this danger behind him. His preparations for flight ended, Hector, in spite of his deadly peril, of the speeding time, of the coming day, instead of flying recommences with more desperation than ever his useless search. Again he goes through all the furniture, the books, the papers﻿—in vain. Then he determines to search the second story, and armed with his hatchet, goes up to it. He has already attacked a bureau, when he hears a cry in the garden. He runs to the window﻿—what does he see? Philippe and old Bertaud are standing on the riverbank under the willows, near the corpse. Can you imagine his immense terror? Now, there’s not a second to lose﻿—he has already delayed too long. The danger is near, terrible. Daylight has come, the crime is discovered, they are coming, he sees himself lost beyond hope. He must fly, fly at once, at the peril of being seen, met, arrested. He throws the hatchet down violently﻿—it cuts the floor. He rushes down, slips the banknotes in his pocket, seizes Guespin’s torn and smeared vest, which he will throw into the river from the bridge, and saves himself by the garden. Forgetting all caution, confused, beside himself, covered with blood, he runs, clears the ditch, and it is he whom old Bertaud sees making for the forest of Mauprévoir, where he intends to arrange the disorder of his clothes. For the moment he is safe. But he leaves behind him this letter, which is, believe me, a formidable witness, which will enlighten justice and will betray his guilt and the perfidy of his projects. For he has not found it, but we will find it; it is necessary for us to have it to defeat Monsieur Domini, and to change our doubts into certainty.”

===Chinese===

这番逻辑推演是如此清晰严密，最后的结论将事实真相揭示得如此透彻有力，令在座的两位听众无不钦佩得五体投地。他们异口同声击节赞道：
“太绝了！”
“现在，”勒科克先生接着说道，“综合这诸多铸就我们内心确信的核心要素，我们必须得出这样一个结论：那封信件所包藏的内情，一旦重见天日，必定能彻底驱散我们心头最后的疑云，合理解释大案的全部始末，并让凶手费尽心机布下的所有伪装彻底沦为徒劳的废纸。因此，伯爵必定会穷尽世间一切手段，哪怕拼上身家性命也绝不敢把这等灭顶之灾遗留在身后。善后潜逃的准备就绪之后，埃克托面对着万劫不复的死局、面对着飞速流逝的光阴、面对着即将破晓的大天亮，不仅没有拔腿远走高飞，反倒以百倍于前的绝望狂躁，重新拉开了徒劳大搜寻的序幕。他再一次把所有的家具、书架、文卷疯狂洗劫了一遍——依旧一无所获。紧接着他咬牙决定搜查三楼，手握短斧冲上了楼。他正挥斧劈向一只橡木大柜，就在此时，花园里冷不丁传来了一声凄厉的惊呼。他惊慌失措地奔向窗前探头张望——他看到了什么？菲利普和老贝尔托正双双伫立在河岸边的柳树下，紧挨着伯爵夫人的遗体！二位能想象得出他此刻内心翻江倒海的极度恐惧吗？此时此刻，连一分一秒的工夫都耽搁不起了——他已经在这里拖延得太久太久了。危险近在咫尺，大难临头。天色已然破晓，大案当场东窗事发，巡捕追兵转瞬即至，他意识到自己已陷入了万劫不复的绝境。他必须逃，必须立刻拔腿奔逃，哪怕冒着被人迎面撞见、当场堵截、当场缉拿的万丈凶险也必须逃之夭夭。他恼羞成怒狠狠把短斧摔掷在地——斧刃深深切入地板。他飞奔下楼，将巨额钞票塞进大衣口袋，一把抓起盖斯潘那件撕裂染血的破背心（他打算在过桥时将其抛入河心），穿过花园仓皇逃命。他把所有的谨慎防范彻底抛到了九霄云外，神智昏乱，失魂落魄，满身沾满鲜血，他狂奔疾走，一步跃过壕沟，而这道仓皇逃窜的身影，恰恰正是老贝尔托今早亲眼目睹一头钻进莫普雷瓦尔森林里去收拾凌乱衣衫的那个幽灵！眼下他暂时逃脱了法网。但他身后遗留下的那封信，请二位务必深信在下，将是一位不容置疑的可怕铁证，它将彻底照亮司法的迷局，将他的滔天罪恶与其毒计的阴险奸诈大白于天下。因为他没有搜寻到它，但我们必定能把它挖出来；我们必须掌握它，才能将多米尼先生彻底击溃，才能将我们心头的推测彻底铸成无可辩驳的钢铁定论。”

## XI / 第十一章
===Original===

A long silence followed the detective’s discourse. Perhaps his hearers were casting about for objections. At last Dr. Gendron spoke:
“I don’t see Guespin’s part in all this.”
“Nor I, very clearly,” answered M. Lecoq. “And here I ought to confess to you not only the strength, but the weakness also, of the theory I have adopted. By this method, which consists of reconstructing the crime before discovering the criminal, I can be neither right nor wrong by halves. Either all my inferences are correct, or not one of them is. It’s all, or nothing. If I am right, Guespin has not been mixed up with this crime, at least directly; for there isn’t a single circumstance which suggests outside aid. If, on the other hand, I am wrong﻿—”
M. Lecoq paused. He seemed to have heard some unexpected noise in the garden.
“But I am not wrong. I have still another charge against the count, of which I haven’t spoken, but which seems to be conclusive.”
“Oh,” cried the doctor, “what now?”
“Two certainties are better than one, and I always doubt. When I was left alone a moment with François, the valet, I asked him if he knew exactly the number of the count’s shoes; he said yes, and took me to a closet where the shoes are kept. A pair of boots, with green Russia leather tops, which François was sure the count had put on the previous morning, was missing. I looked for them carefully everywhere, but could not find them. Again, the blue cravat with white stripes which the count wore on the 8th, had also disappeared.”
“There,” cried M. Plantat, “that is indisputable proof that your supposition about the slippers and handkerchief was right.”
“I think that the facts are sufficiently established to enable us to go forward. Let’s now consider the events which must have decided﻿—”
M. Lecoq again stopped, and seemed to be listening. All of a sudden, without a word he jumped on the windowsill and from thence into the garden, with the bound of a cat which pounces on a mouse. The noise of a fall, a stifled cry, an oath, were heard, and then a stamping as if a struggle were going on. The doctor and M. Plantat hastened to the window. Day was breaking, the trees shivered in the fresh wind of the early morning﻿—objects were vaguely visible without distinct forms across the white mist which hangs, on summer nights, over the valley of the Seine.

===Chinese===

密探的一番剖析之后，书房里陷入了良久的沉默。或许听客们正在极力推敲破绽与疑点。让德龙医生终于打破了沉寂：
“我在这整桩案子里，实在瞧不出盖斯潘究竟扮演了什么角色。”
“我也瞧不太分明，”勒科克先生答道，“说到这里，我倒该向二位坦白，我所采取的这一推导之法不仅有其长处，也同样存在致命的软肋。这种在查明真凶之前先复原犯罪全貌的推演，结果绝无半对半错的可能。要么我全盘皆对，要么全盘皆错，非此即彼，全无中庸之途。假使我是对的，盖斯潘便绝未直接卷入这桩罪行，因为全案没有任何一处痕迹暗示有外力相助。反之，假使我错了——”
勒科克先生猛地打住了话头。他似乎听到了花园里传来一阵异样的声响。
“但我绝不会错。我手里还握有一条针对伯爵的铁证，方才尚未提及，但这足以成为定案的关键。”
“哦？”医生惊呼道，“那又是什么？”
“双重确凿总好过孤证，而我向来抱有疑心。方才我同男仆弗朗索瓦独处了片刻，便询问他是否确切知晓伯爵所穿鞋履的尺码。他答称知晓，并领我前去存放鞋履的壁橱。一双带绿色俄罗斯皮革靴帮的高筒靴不翼而飞，弗朗索瓦断言，伯爵前一天清晨出门时穿的正是那一双。我翻遍各处仔细搜寻，始终不见踪影。此外，伯爵在8日当天佩戴的那条蓝白条纹领带，也同样失踪了。”
“着啊！”普朗塔先生喊道，“这恰恰是不容辩驳的铁证，足以证实你对拖鞋和手帕的推测分毫不差！”
“我认为事实已然昭然若揭，足够支持我们继续往下推进了。现在，让我们来审视究竟是何等变故促使了——”
勒科克先生再次戛然而止，神情专注地侧耳倾听。电光石火间，他连一句话也没多说，如同一只扑向猎物的灵猫，纵身跃上窗台，径直跳入了花园。紧接着，窗外传来了重重跌落的闷响、一声压抑的惊呼、一句恶狠狠的咒骂，随即便是一阵扭打撕扯的沉重踏地声。医生和普朗塔先生急忙冲到窗前。天色已然破晓，树影在清晨的凉风中微微颤动——在夏夜笼罩着塞纳河谷的茫茫白雾中，一切景物都模糊难辨，辨不出清晰轮廓。

===Original===

In the middle of the lawn, at rapid intervals, they heard the blunt noise of a clinched fist striking a living body, and saw two men, or rather two phantoms, furiously swinging their arms. Presently the two shapes formed but one, then they separated, again to unite; one of the two fell, rose at once, and fell again.
“Don’t disturb yourselves,” cried M. Lecoq’s voice. “I’ve got the rogue.”
The shadow of the detective, which was upright, bent over, and the conflict was recommenced. The shadow stretched on the ground defended itself with the dangerous strength of despair; his body formed a large brown spot in the middle of the lawn, and his legs, kicking furiously, convulsively stretched and contracted. Then there was a moment when the lookers-on could not make out which was the detective. They rose again and struggled; suddenly a cry of pain escaped, with a ferocious oath.
“Ah, wretch!”
And almost immediately a loud shout rent the air, and the detective’s mocking tones were heard:
“There he is! I’ve persuaded him to pay his respects to us﻿—light me up a little.”
The doctor and his host hastened to the lamp; their zeal caused a delay, and at the moment that the doctor raised the lamp, the door was rudely pushed open.
“I beg to present to you,” said M. Lecoq, “Master Robelot, bonesetter of Orcival, herborist by prudence, and poisoner by vocation.”
The stupefaction of the others was such that neither could speak.
It was really the bonesetter, working his jaws nervously. His adversary had thrown him down by the famous knee-stroke which is the last resort of the worst prowlers about the Parisian barriers. But it was not so much Robelot’s presence which surprised M. Plantat and his friend. Their stupor was caused by the detective’s appearance; who, with his wrist of steel﻿—as rigid as handcuffs﻿—held the doctor’s ex-assistant, and pushed him forward. The voice was certainly Lecoq’s; there was his costume, his big-knotted cravat, his yellow-haired watch-chain﻿—still it was no longer Lecoq. He was blond, with highly cultivated whiskers, when he jumped out the window; he returned, brown, with a smooth face.

===Chinese===

在草坪中央，一阵阵短促而沉闷的重拳击肉声接连传来，他们依稀看见两个人影——不如说是两具幽灵——正疯狂地挥舞着双臂。须臾间，两具身影扭打成一团，接着分开，旋即再度纠缠在一处；其中一人仰面跌倒，又瞬间弹起，紧接着再次被重重摔翻在地。
“二位不必惊慌！”勒科克先生的声音喊道，“我抓着这恶棍了。”
挺立着的密探身影俯下身去，厮杀再度爆发。瘫倒在地的黑影带着绝望的凶险蛮力负隅顽抗；他的身躯在草坪中央化为一团巨大的深褐色暗影，双腿狂乱蹬踹，剧烈地蜷缩又猛蹬开来。有一瞬间，窗前的旁观者甚至分不清哪一道才是密探的身影。两人再度翻滚爬起、扭打搏斗；突然间，伴随着一声凶暴的咒骂，一声凄厉的痛呼脱口而出。
“啊，你这杂种！”
几乎与此同时，一声洪亮的呼喊划破长空，传来了密探嘲弄的语调：
“老实了吧！我好说歹说才请动他来给咱们请安——劳驾给我照个亮。”
医生和屋主人赶忙去端油灯；忙乱中反倒耽搁了片刻，就在医生高高擎起油灯的一刹那，房门被人粗暴地撞开了。
“容我向二位引见，”勒科克先生说道，“奥尔西瓦尔的正骨匠罗伯洛老爷，平日谨小慎微卖点草药，本行却是精研鸩毒的行家里手。”
在场二人目瞪口呆，惊骇得一句话也说不出来。
被押进来的当真是那正骨匠，他那两腮正神经质地剧烈抽搐。他的对手使出了巴黎城关一带亡命狂徒压箱底的顶膝杀招，方才将他彻底掀翻在地。然而，令普朗塔先生和好友震惊的，倒还不仅仅是罗伯洛的现身。真正让他们陷入呆滞的，是这位密探的赫然真容：那只如手铐般冰冷坚硬的铁腕死死钳制着医生的这位昔日助手，推着他大步向前。声音无疑是勒科克的，身上穿的也是他的行头、他那打着大结的领带、他那系着黄发的怀表链——然而眼前的人却绝不再是先前的勒科克了。从窗口跃出去时，他还是个蓄着精心打理的金黄色颊须的男人；可如今从正门迈回来的，却是一个面色微黑、面庞光洁的男子。

===Original===

The man who had jumped out was a middle-aged person, with an expressive face which was in turn idiotic and intelligent; the man who returned by the door was a fine young fellow of thirty-five, with a beaming eye and a sensitive lip; a splendid head of curly black hair, brought out vividly the pallor of his complexion, and the firm outline of his head and face. A wound appeared on his neck, just below the chin.
“Monsieur Lecoq!” cried M. Plantat, recovering his voice.
“Himself,” answered the detective, “and this time the true Lecoq.” Turning to Robelot, he slapped him on the shoulder and added:
“Go on, you.”
Robelot fell upon a sofa, but the detective continued to hold him fast.
“Yes,” he continued, “this rascal has robbed me of my blond locks. Thanks to him and in spite of myself, you see me as I am, with the head the Creator gave me, and which is really my own.” He gave a careless gesture, half angry, half good-humored. “I am the true Lecoq; and to tell the truth, only three persons besides yourselves really know him﻿—two trusted friends, and one who is infinitely less so﻿—she of whom I spoke a while ago.”
The eyes of the other two met as if to question each other, and M. Lecoq continued:
“What can a fellow do? All is not rose color in my trade. We run such dangers, in protecting society, as should entitle us to the esteem, if not the affection of our fellow-men: Why, I am condemned to death, at this moment, by seven of the most dangerous criminals in France. I have caught them, you see, and they have sworn﻿—they are men of their word, too﻿—that I should only die by their hands. Where are these wretches? Four at Cayenne, one at Brest; I’ve had news of them. But the other two? I’ve lost their track. Who knows whether one of them hasn’t followed me here, and whether tomorrow, at the turning of some obscure road, I shall not get six inches of cold steel in my stomach?”
He smiled sadly.
“And no reward,” pursued he, “for the perils which we brave. If I should fall tomorrow, they would take up my body, carry it to my house, and that would be the end.” The detective’s tone had become bitter, the irritation of his voice betrayed his rancor. “My precautions happily are taken. While I am performing my duties, I suspect everything, and when I am on my guard I fear no one.

===Chinese===

跳出窗去的分明是个中年人，生着一张神情生动、时而呆滞时而精明的面孔；而从门口走回来的，却是一位年约35岁的英俊青年，双目炯炯有神，唇线分明；一头浓密卷曲的乌黑秀发，更衬托出他面色的苍白以及头面部轮廓的坚毅刚健。就在他的下颌正下方，颈项处赫然现出一道伤口。
“勒科克先生！”普朗塔先生回过神来惊呼道。
“正是鄙人，”密探应道，“而且这回可是如假包换的真正勒科克。”他转向罗伯洛，重重拍了拍对方的肩膀，又补了一句：
“老实点，进去。”
罗伯洛一头跌坐在沙发上，但密探依然死死钳制着他不放。
“不错，”他继续说道，“这个恶棍扯掉了我那头金发。多亏了他，我才身不由己地让二位见到了我的庐山真面目——造物主赐予我的、完完全全属于我自己的这颗脑袋。”他漫不经心地摆了摆手，神情半是恼怒半是戏谑。“我才是真正的勒科克；说句老实话，除了二位之外，世上当真认得他这副真面目的不过三个人——两位推心置腹的挚友，还有一个算不上什么朋友的人，也就是我刚才提起过的那位女子。”
另外两人的目光交汇了一瞬，似在面面相觑，勒科克先生接着说道：
“做我们这行的又能如何？我这门差事可绝非处处都是花团锦簇。为了守护世道安宁，我们所冒的凶险，哪怕换不来同胞的亲近爱戴，至少也该博得几分敬意吧。可眼下，全法国有7位穷凶极恶的重刑犯正对我下了必杀令。你们瞧，他们都是被我亲手拿获的，这帮家伙发过毒誓——而且全都是说到做到的亡命徒——我定要死在他们的刀下。这些恶棍如今都在何处？4个在卡宴服苦役，1个关在布雷斯特；这几个的下落我尚能掌握。可另外两个呢？我早已断了他们的踪迹。天晓得他们当中会不会有人一路尾随我到了这里，又天晓得明天在哪个偏僻狭窄的路口拐角，我的腹中会不会冷不丁挨上6寸冰冷的利刃？”
他凄凉地笑了笑。
“而我们出生入死，却换不来任何抚恤或荣光，”他继续说道，“假使我明日横尸街头，他们顶多抬走我的遗体运回寒舍，一切便就此草草收场。”密探的语调变得尖刻起来，声音里的激愤泄露了他心头的积怨。“所幸我处处提防。办案执行公务之时，我对周遭的一切皆心存戒备；只要我防备在身，便无惧世间任何人。

===Original===

But there are days when one is tired of being on his guard, and would like to be able to turn a street corner without looking for a dagger. On such days I again become myself; I take off my false beard, throw down my mask, and my real self emerges from the hundred disguises which I assume in turn. I have been a detective fifteen years, and no one at the prefecture knows either my true face or the color of my hair.”
Master Robelot, ill at ease on his lounge, attempted to move.
“Ah, look out!” cried M. Lecoq, suddenly changing his tone. “Now get up here, and tell us what you were about in the garden?”
“But you are wounded!” exclaimed Plantat, observing stains of blood on M. Lecoq’s shirt.
“Oh, that’s nothing﻿—only a scratch that this fellow gave me with a big cutlass he had.”
M. Plantat insisted on examining the wound, and was not satisfied until the doctor declared it to be a very slight one.
“Come, Master Robelot,” said the old man, “what were you doing here?”
The bonesetter did not reply.
“Take care,” insisted M. Plantat, “your silence will confirm us in the idea that you came with the worst designs.”
But it was in vain that M. Plantat wasted his persuasive eloquence. Robelot shut himself up in a ferocious and dogged silence. M. Gendron, hoping, not without reason, that he might have some influence over his former assistant, spoke:
“Answer us; what did you come for?”
Robelot made an effort; it was painful, with his broken jaw, to speak.
“I came to rob; I confess it.”
“To rob﻿—what?”
“I don’t know.”
“But you didn’t scale a wall and risk the jail without a definite object?”
“Well, then, I wanted﻿—”
He stopped.
“What? Go on.”
“To get some rare flowers in the conservatory.”
“With your cutlass, hey?” said M. Lecoq. Robelot gave him a terrible look; the detective continued:
“You needn’t look at me that way﻿—you don’t scare me. And don’t talk like a fool, either. If you think we are duller than you, you are mistaken﻿—I warn you of it.”
“I wanted the flowerpots,” stammered the man.
“Oh, come now,” cried M. Lecoq, shrugging his shoulders, “don’t repeat such nonsense. You, a man that buys large estates for cash, steal flowerpots! Tell that to somebody else. You’ve been turned over tonight, my boy, like an old glove. You’ve let out in spite of yourself a secret that tormented you furiously, and you came here to get it back again.

===Chinese===

然而总有那么几天，一个人也会厌倦这种时时枕戈待旦的日子，渴望在街角拐弯时不必战战兢兢地提防暗处刺来的匕首。在这样的日子里，我便变回我自己；我摘掉假胡须，扯下面具，从我轮番扮演的百副伪装中脱胎而出，恢复真身。我在警局当密探已有15年，整座警察厅里根本没人见过我的真实长相，也没人知晓我头发的本色。”
罗伯洛老爷斜倚在长榻上如坐针毡，试图挪动身子。
“哈，老实点！”勒科克先生猝然变了腔调喝道，“现在给我站直了，交代交代你在花园里究竟打算干什么？”
“可是你负伤了！”普朗塔瞧见勒科克先生衬衫上的斑斑血迹，惊呼起来。
“嗨，不值一提——不过是这厮拿手里一把大砍刀给我划破了点皮。”
普朗塔先生执意要察看伤势，直到医生断言这不过是点皮外轻伤，他才稍稍放下心来。
“说吧，罗伯洛老爷，”老人开口道，“你黑灯瞎火到这儿来干什么？”
正骨匠一声不吭。
“仔细掂量掂量，”普朗塔先生紧逼道，“你的沉默只会让我们更加认定你来者不善，心怀最险恶的勾当。”
然而普朗塔先生苦口婆心的劝说终究徒劳无功。罗伯洛闭口不言，陷入了一副凶顽而死硬的沉默中。让德龙先生料想自己在这位昔日助手面前多少还有几分威严，便开口喝问道：
“回答我们，你来这儿究竟想干什么？”
罗伯洛挣扎着动了动嘴唇；下颌遭受重创，开口说话令他剧痛难忍。
“我是来偷东西的；我招认。”
“偷东西——偷什么？”
“我不知道。”
“若是没有确凿的目标，你犯得着翻墙涉险、甘冒吃牢饭的罪名？”
“那……我是想……”
他咽住了。
“想怎样？说下去。”
“想去温室里弄几株名贵的花木。”
“手里还拎着大砍刀，对吧？”勒科克先生冷笑道。罗伯洛狠狠剜了他一眼；密探接着说道：
“你用不着拿这副凶相瞪我——吓不着我。你也别在这儿满嘴胡话当傻子糊弄人。假使你以为我们比你蠢，那你就大错特错了——我奉劝你省省吧。”
“我……我是想偷几个花盆，”那汉子结结巴巴地嘟囔道。
“得了，得了吧，”勒科克先生耸耸肩斥道，“别再翻来覆去胡扯这套鬼话了。你一个随手就能豪掷现款买下大宗地产的财主，会跑来偷花盆？去把这套鬼话哄三岁小儿去吧。我的好兄弟，你今晚就跟一只被翻了个底朝天的旧手套一样，早已被我看穿了。你身不由己吐露了一个把你折磨得发狂的大秘密，眼下跑来无非是想杀人灭口，把这个秘密重新咽回肚子里。

===Original===

You thought that perhaps Monsieur Plantat had not told it to anybody, and you wanted to prevent him from speaking again forever.”
Robelot made a sign of protesting.
“Shut up now,” said M. Lecoq. “And your cutlass?”
While this conversation was going on, M. Plantat reflected.
“Perhaps,” he murmured, “I’ve spoken too soon.”
“Why so?” asked M. Lecoq. “I wanted a palpable proof for Monsieur Domini; we’ll give him this rascal, and if he isn’t satisfied, he’s difficult to please.”
“But what shall we do with him?”
“Shut him up somewhere in the house; if necessary, I’ll tie him up.”
“Here’s a dark closet.”
“Is it secure?”
“There are thick walls on three sides of it, and the fourth is closed with a double door; no openings, no windows, nothing.”
“Just the place.”
M. Plantat opened the closet, a black-looking hole, damp, narrow, and full of old books and papers.
“There,” said M. Lecoq to his prisoner, “in here you’ll be like a little king,” and he pushed him into the closet. Robelot did not resist, but he asked for some water and a light. They gave him a bottle of water and a glass.
“As for a light,” said M. Lecoq, “you may dispense with it. You’ll be playing us some dirty trick.”
M. Plantat, having shut the closet-door, took the detective’s hand.
“Monsieur,” said he, earnestly, “you have probably just saved my life at the peril of your own; I will not thank you. The day will come, I trust, when I may﻿—”
The detective interrupted him with a gesture.
“You know how I constantly expose myself,” said he, “once more or less does not matter much. Besides, it does not always serve a man to save his life.” He was pensive a moment, then added: “You will thank me after awhile, when I have gained other titles to your gratitude.”
M. Gendron also cordially shook the detective’s hand, saying:
“Permit me to express my admiration of you. I had no idea what the resources of such a man as you were. You got here this morning without information, without details, and by the mere scrutiny of the scene of the crime, by the sole force of reasoning, have found the criminal: more, you have proved to us that the criminal could be no other than he whom you have named.”
M. Lecoq bowed modestly. These praises evidently pleased him greatly.
“Still,” he answered, “I am not yet quite satisfied. The guilt of the Count de Trémorel is of course abundantly clear to me. But what motives urged him?

===Chinese===

你心想普朗塔先生兴许尚未把底细告知他人，便赶来让他永远闭嘴，再也开不了口。”
罗伯洛作势想要抗辩。
“给我闭嘴，”勒科克先生喝道，“那你手里的砍刀又算怎么回事？”
这番问话之际，普朗塔先生陷入了沉思。
“兴许，”他低声呢喃道，“是我把话说得太早了。”
“何出此言？”勒科克先生问道，“我原本就打算给多米尼先生奉上一件铁证；把这恶棍扭送给他，他要是还不满意，那可就当真鸡蛋里挑骨头了。”
“可眼下该拿他怎么办？”
“先关在宅子里哪个背风隐秘的角落；有必要的话，我亲自把他捆结实。”
“这儿有间暗室密柜。”
“结实稳当吗？”
“三面都是厚实的实心墙，第四面是加固的双层门；没有通气孔，没有窗户，什么都没有。”
“再合适不过了。”
普朗塔先生打开了密室门，里面黑黢黢的，潮湿逼仄，塞满了泛黄的旧书和公文废纸。
“去吧，”勒科克先生对囚犯说道，“待在里面你快活得赛过小皇帝，”说着便将他推搡进了密室。罗伯洛没有反抗，只是讨要了些清水和一盏灯。他们递给他一瓶清水和一个玻璃杯。
“至于灯嘛，”勒科克先生冷冷道，“你就省省吧。天晓得你会借着火光耍出什么阴招来。”
普朗塔先生紧紧合上暗室的门，握住了密探的手。
“先生，”他神情凝重而诚挚地说道，“你方才冒着舍生取义的凶险救了我的性命；我就不多言谢了。我相信总有一天，我能够——”
密探抬手打断了他。
“你自知晓我是个成天把脑袋别在裤腰带上的人，”他说道，“多涉险一次少涉险一次，倒也没什么大不了。再者说，救人一命往往也未必当真就是积德行善。”他沉吟了片刻，随即又道：“待到日后我挣得更多让您感恩戴德的由头，您再来谢我也不迟。”
让德龙先生也热忱地握住了密探的手，赞道：
“请容我向你致以崇高的敬意。我以往竟丝毫不知像你这样的人物拥有何等通天手段。你今晨赶赴此地时全无先验情报，全无半点案情细枝末节，仅仅凭借对凶案现场的勘验、凭借纯粹的逻辑推理，就锁定了真凶；更神异的是，你向我们彻底证明了，罪魁祸首非你所指认的那个人莫属。”
勒科克先生谦恭地躬身致意。这番赞赏显然令他受用至极。
“然而，”他答道，“我心头依然未能释怀。特雷莫雷尔伯爵的罪责在我眼中自然已确凿无疑。但究竟是何等动机驱使他走上了这条绝路？

===Original===

How was he led to this terrible impulse to kill his wife, and make it appear that he, too, had been murdered?”
“Might we not conclude,” remarked the doctor, “that, disgusted with Madame de Trémorel, he has got rid of her to rejoin another woman, adored by him to madness?”
M. Lecoq shook his head.
“People don’t kill their wives for the sole reason that they are tired of them and love others. They quit their wives, live with the new loves﻿—that’s all. That happens every day, and neither the law nor public opinion condemns such people with great severity.”
“But it was the wife who had the fortune.”
“That wasn’t the case here. I have been posting myself up. M. de Trémorel had a hundred thousand crowns, the remains of a colossal fortune saved by his friend Sauvresy; and his wife by the marriage contract made over a half million to him. A man can live in ease anywhere on eight hundred thousand francs. Besides, the count was master of all the funds of the estate. He could sell, buy, realize, borrow, deposit, and draw funds at will.”
The doctor had nothing to reply. M. Lecoq went on, speaking with a certain hesitation, while his eyes interrogated M. Plantat.
“We must find the reasons of this murder, and the motives of the assassin’s terrible resolution﻿—in the past. Some crime so indissolubly linked the count and countess, that only the death of one of them could free the other. I suspected this crime the first thing this morning, and have seen it all the way through; and the man that we have just shut up in there﻿—Robelot﻿—who wanted to murder Monsieur Plantat, was either the agent or the accomplice of this crime.”
The doctor had not been present at the various episodes which, during the day at Valfeuillu and in the evening at the mayor’s, had established a tacit understanding between Plantat and Lecoq. He needed all the shrewdness he possessed to fill up the gaps and understand the hidden meanings of the conversation to which he had been listening for two hours. M. Lecoq’s last words shed a ray of light upon it all, and the doctor cried, “Sauvresy!”
“Yes﻿—Sauvresy,” answered M. Lecoq. “And the paper which the murderer hunted for so eagerly, for which he neglected his safety and risked his life, must contain the certain proof of the crime.”
M. Plantat, despite the most significant looks and the direct provocation to make an explanation, was silent.

===Chinese===

究竟是什么迫使他产生了如此骇人听闻的冲动，非要杀害发妻，并处心积虑制造自己也同样惨遭谋害的假象呢？”
“我们难道不能推断，”医生插话道，“是他厌弃了特雷莫雷尔夫人，转而狂热地迷恋上了别的女人？”
“显而易见，”勒科克先生说道，“这桩案子的根由底下必然藏着女人。”
“那么，”医生说道，“伯爵杀了妻子，带着新欢远走高飞逃往异国他乡——仅此而已。这种事情屡见不鲜，无论是法律还是世俗舆论，对此类劣迹的惩处向来都不算严苛。”
“可家财全在妻子名下啊。”
“本案并非如此。我早已把底细摸了个一清二楚。特雷莫雷尔先生手里握有100000克朗，那是他挚友索夫勒齐为他保住的万贯家财所余下的残数；而他的妻子在缔结婚约时，直接过户给他500000法郎。手握800000法郎，一个人在天底下任何角落都能过上优裕无忧的日子。更何况，伯爵执掌着庄园所有的资财大权。他可以随心所欲地变卖、购置、变现、举债、储蓄以及提取资金。”
医生无言以对。勒科克先生继续说了下去，语调中带着几分踌躇迟疑，目光则探询地注视着普朗塔先生。
“我们必须从往昔的岁月中，去探寻这桩谋杀的原委，探寻凶手做出这般可怕决断的动机。必定是某种罪恶将伯爵与伯爵夫人牢不可破地绑在了一起，以至于唯有一方的死亡才能让另一方彻底解脱。今晨一早我便对这桩隐秘罪行有所怀疑，并且在随后的调查中处处印证了这一点；而我们刚刚关进去的那个家伙——罗伯洛——那个企图谋害普朗塔先生的人，要么是这桩罪行的操刀走狗，要么就是同谋从犯。”
白天在瓦尔菲伊庄园以及傍晚在镇长公馆里所发生的种种变故，在普朗塔和勒科克之间建立起了一种心照不宣的默契，而医生当时并不在场。他必须穷尽自己所有的机敏睿智，才能填补当中的空白，听懂自己已聆听了整整两个小时的这番密谈背后的弦外之音。勒科克先生最后的这番话犹如一道闪电照亮了一切，医生脱口惊呼道：“索夫勒齐！”
“正是——索夫勒齐，”勒科克先生答道，“而凶手如此如饥似渴搜寻的那张纸片、那张让他连自身安危都弃之不顾、冒着杀头之罪也要挖出来的纸片，必定藏着这桩罪恶无可辩驳的确凿铁证。”
尽管迎着二人饱含深意的目光与直截了当的探询逼问，普朗塔先生却依然保持着沉默。

===Original===

He seemed a hundred leagues off in his thoughts, and his eyes, wandering in space, seemed to follow forgotten episodes in the mists of the past. M. Lecoq, after a brief pause, decided to strike a bold blow.
“What a past that must have been,” exclaimed he, “which could drive a young, rich, happy man like Hector de Trémorel to plan in cool blood such a crime, to resign himself to disappear after it, to cease to exist, as it were to lose all at once his personality, his position, his honor and his name! What a past must be that which drives a young girl of twenty to suicide!”
M. Plantat started up, pale, more moved than he had yet appeared.
“Ah,” cried he, in an altered voice, “you don’t believe what you say! Laurence never knew about it, never!”
The doctor, who was narrowly watching the detective, thought he saw a faint smile light up his mobile features. The old justice of the peace went on, now calmly and with dignity, in a somewhat haughty tone:
“You didn’t need tricks or subterfuge, Monsieur Lecoq, to induce me to tell what I know. I have evinced enough esteem and confidence in you to deprive you of the right to arm yourself against me with the sad secret which you have surprised.”
M. Lecoq, despite his cool-headedness, was disconcerted.
“Yes,” pursued M. Plantat, “your astonishing genius for penetrating dramas like this has led you to the truth. But you do not know all, and even now I would hold my tongue, had not the reasons which compelled me to be silent ceased to exist.”
He opened a secret drawer in an old oaken desk near the fireplace and took out a large paper package, which he laid on the table.
“For four years,” he resumed, “I have followed, day by day﻿—I might say, hour by hour﻿—the various phases of the dreadful drama which ended in blood last night at Valfeuillu. At first, the curiosity of an old retired attorney prompted me. Later, I hoped to save the life and honor of one very dear to me. Why did I say nothing of my discoveries? That, my friends, is the secret of my conscience﻿—it does not reproach me. Besides, I shut my eyes to the evidence even up to yesterday; I needed the brutal testimony of this deed!”
Day had come. The frightened blackbirds flew whistling by. The pavement resounded with the wooden shoes of the workmen going fieldward. No noise troubled the sad stillness of the library, unless it were the rustling of the leaves which M.

===Chinese===

他的思绪似乎早已飘到了百里开外，漫无目的游离的目光，仿佛在追随着往事迷雾中那些被尘封淡忘的过往片段。片刻停顿之后，勒科克先生决定孤注一掷击其要害。
“那究竟是怎样一段不可告人的往事啊，”他感慨道，“竟能逼得像埃克托·德·特雷莫雷尔这样年轻富有、养尊处优的贵介公子，处心积虑、冷血无情地策划出如此惊天罪案；逼得他甘愿在事后人间蒸发、就此隐姓埋名，一下子彻底葬送自己的身份、地位、声誉与姓名！那又该是怎样一段往事，竟会逼得一位年仅20岁的妙龄姑娘走上自绝的绝路！”
普朗塔先生浑身猛然一震，霍然站起身来，面色惨白，神情之激动胜过以往任何时刻。
“啊！”他变了声调厉声喊道，“你自己都绝不相信这套鬼话！洛朗斯对此事从来一无所知，绝不知情！”
目不转睛紧盯着密探的医生，隐约捕捉到密探那张变幻莫测的脸上掠过了一抹若有若无的浅笑。老治安法官稳了稳心神，神态恢复了从容与尊严，以一种颇为傲然的口吻继续说道：
“勒科克先生，你何必动用机巧手段或试探伎俩来诱使我说出实情。我对你展现出了足够的敬重与推心置腹，你本不该拿你意外窥破的这份令人心碎的秘密，作为对付我的武器。”
尽管勒科克向来沉着冷静，此刻也不由得面露尴尬窘态。
“不错，”普朗塔先生接着说道，“你洞悉此类疑难大案的惊世奇才，确实引导你逼近了真相。但你并未掌握全部内情，若非迫使我守口如瓶的由头如今已荡然无存，我即便到了此刻也绝不会多吐露半个字。”
他拉开壁炉旁一张老旧橡木书桌的暗屉，取出一个厚厚的纸包，搁在了桌上。
“整整4年以来，”他接着说道，“我日复一日——甚至可以说时时刻刻——都在追踪着昨夜在瓦尔菲伊庄园以血腥惨剧收场的这出可怕悲剧的每一个起承转合。起初，这不过是一个退休老诉讼代理人的好奇心作祟。后来，我一心渴望挽救一位于我而言重于泰山之人的性命与清白。我为何对自己的诸多发现秘而不宣？我的朋友们，这是深藏在我良知深处的隐秘——我的良心对此问心无愧。更何况，直到昨天之前，我一直都在对眼前确凿的证据视而不见；我需要昨夜这桩惨绝人寰的罪行作为最无可辩驳的当头棒喝！”
大天已然亮透。受惊的乌鸫鸣叫着从窗外掠过。石板路上传来下田劳作的工人脚下木屐清脆的响声。没有任何声响打破书斋凄凉的沉寂，除非是普朗塔先生……

===Original===

Plantat was turning over, or now and then a groan from Robelot.
“Before commencing,” said the old man, “I ought to consider your weariness; we have been up twenty-four hours﻿—”
But the others protested that they did not need repose. The fever of curiosity had chased away their exhaustion. They were at last to know the key of the mystery.
“Very well,” said their host, “listen to me.”

===Chinese===

……翻动纸页的沙沙声，或是罗伯洛时不时发出的一声呻吟。
“在开讲之前，”老人说道，“我理应体谅二位的疲乏；我们已经整整熬了24个小时——”
但另外两人极力分辩，声称自己根本无须歇息。狂热的好奇心早已将倦意一扫而空。他们终于即将知晓这桩谜案的解密钥匙了。
“很好，”主人说道，“那么请听我细说。”

## XII / 第十二章
===Original===

The Count Hector de Trémorel, at twenty-six, was the model and ideal of the polished man of the world, proper to our age; a man useless alike to himself and to others, harmful even, seeming to have been placed on earth expressly to play at the expense of all. Young, noble, elegant, rich by millions, endowed with vigorous health, this last descendant of a great family squandered most foolishly and ignobly both his youth and his patrimony. He acquired by excesses of all kinds a wide and unenviable celebrity. People talked of his stables, his carriages, his servants, his furniture, his dogs, his favorite loves. His cast-off horses still took prizes, and a jade distinguished by his notice was eagerly sought by the young bloods of the town. Do not think, however, that he was naturally vicious; he had a warm heart, and even generous emotions at twenty. Six years of unhealthy pleasures had spoiled him to the marrow. Foolishly vain, he was ready to do anything to maintain his notoriety. He had the bold and determined egotism of one who has never had to think of anyone but himself, and has never suffered. Intoxicated by the flatteries of the so-called friends who drew his money from him, he admired himself, mistaking his brutal cynicism for wit, and his lofty disdain of all morality and his idiotic scepticism, for character. He was also feeble; he had caprices, but never a will; feeble as a child, a woman, a girl. His biography was to be found in the petty journals of the day, which retailed his sayings﻿—or what he might have said; his least actions and gestures were reported.
One night when he was supping at the Café de Paris, he threw all the plates out the window. It cost him twenty thousand francs. Bravo! One morning gossiping Paris learned with stupefaction that he had eloped to Italy with the wife of X﻿—﻿—, the banker, a lady nineteen years married. He fought a duel, and killed his man. The week after, he was wounded in another. He was a hero! On one occasion he went to Baden, where he broke the bank. Another time, after playing sixty hours, he managed to lose one hundred and twenty thousand francs﻿—won by a Russian prince.
He was one of those men whom success intoxicates, who long for applause, but who care not for what they are applauded. Count Hector was more than ravished by the noise he made in the world.

===Chinese===
