“你猜我还干了什么好事？”索夫勒齐继续兴致勃勃地表功道，“料想你眼下身边大概连换洗衣物都缺，我特意命人装了整整三四只大衣箱塞满了你的行头华服，走铁路托运发了过来，眼下咱们府上的仆人正赶去提货呢。”
埃克托心头也隐隐觉得索夫勒齐这番殷勤周到有些过了界，觉得对方俨然将自己当成了一个毫无远见的无知稚童来照料。当着一位贵妇人的面，口口声声宣扬自己连换洗衣裳都捉襟见肘，这严重刺伤了他的自尊心。

===Original===

He forgot that he had found it a very simple thing in the morning to ask his friend for some linen.
Just then a noise was heard in the vestibule. Doubtless the trunks had come. Bertha went out to give the necessary orders.
“Quick!” cried Sauvresy. “Now that we are alone, here are your trinkets. I had some trouble in getting them. They are suspicious at the pawnbroker’s. I think they began to suspect that I was one of a band of thieves.”
“You didn’t mention my name, did you?”
“That would have been useless. My notary was with me, fortunately. One never knows how useful one’s notary may be. Don’t you think society is unjust toward notaries?”
Trémorel thought his friend talked very lightly about a serious matter, and this flippancy vexed him.
“To finish up, I paid a visit to Miss Jenny. She has been abed since last evening, and her chambermaid told me she had not ceased sobbing bitterly ever since your departure.”
“Had she seen no one?”
“Nobody at all. She really thought you dead, and when I told her you were here with me, alive and well, I thought she would go mad for joy. Do you know, Hector, she’s really pretty.”
“Yes﻿—not bad.”
“And a very good little body, I imagine. She told me some very touching things. I would wager, my friend, that she don’t care so much for your money as she does for yourself.”
Hector smiled superciliously.
“In short, she was anxious to follow me, to see and speak to you. I had to swear with terrible oaths that she should see you tomorrow, before she would let me go; not at Paris, as you said you would never go there, but at Corbeil.”
“Ah, as for that﻿—”
“She will be at the station tomorrow at twelve. We will go down together, and I will take the train for Paris. You can get into the Corbeil train, and breakfast with Miss Jenny at the hotel of the Belle Image.”
Hector began to offer an objection. Sauvresy stopped him with a gesture.
“Not a word,” said he. “Here is my wife.”

===Chinese===

他全然忘记了，就在当天清晨，自己向好友开口讨要几件内衣还觉得是件再天经地义不过的寻常小事。
恰在此时，门厅前传来了阵阵响动。毫无疑问定是那些衣箱送到了。贝尔塔款款步出大厅前去张罗吩咐。
“快！”索夫勒齐急忙喊道，“趁着眼下屋里只有咱们两个，这是你的金银首饰。为了把这些物件赎回来，我可费了老鼻子劲。典当行那帮家伙疑心重得很。我瞧他们差点把我当成什么盗窃团伙的同案犯了。”
“你没向他们报出我的名号吧？”
“报了也是白搭。所幸当时我的公证人就跟在身边。一个人永远料想不到自己的公证人能派上多大的用场。你难道不觉得世人对公证人这门行当向来太苛刻不公了吗？”
特雷莫雷尔觉得好友把如此严肃的大事说得轻浮戏谑，这种轻佻的做派让他暗自光火。
“最后，我还顺道登门探望了珍妮小姐。她自打昨晚起便卧床不起，她的贴身女佣告知我，自打你抽身离去之后，她便一刻不停地哭得死去活来。”
“她见过旁人没有？”
“一个外人也没见过。她当真以为你早已不在人世了，当我告知她你眼下同我待在一块儿、平平安安活得好好的时，我瞧她高兴得简直要发狂了。埃克托，你可知晓，她生得当真俊俏极了。”
“嗯——马马虎虎吧。”
“而且依我瞧，她还是个心地极善良的小人儿。她同我吐露了不少肺腑之言，句句催人泪下。我的朋友，我敢同你打赌，她图的可绝非你的万贯家财，而是你这个人。”
埃克托傲慢地冷笑了一声。
“总而言之，她急不可耐想要尾随我同来，非要亲眼见你一面同你说说话不可。我指天画地发了毒誓，答应明儿一定安排你们相见，她才肯放我脱身；地点不在巴黎，既然你口口声声说绝不再踏足巴黎半步，那咱们就定在科尔贝伊。”
“啊，若是定在那儿——”
“明儿中午12点整，她准时在火车站守候。咱们俩一道下山赶赴车站，我乘车去往巴黎，你转乘去往科尔贝伊的班车，跟珍妮小姐在靓影旅馆（Belle Image）一道共进早午餐。”
埃克托刚欲开口推托反对。索夫勒齐抬手打断了他。
“别声张，”他低声道，“我内人过来了。”

## XIX / 第十九章
===Original===

The Count de Trémorel did not anticipate that the respite which Bertha begged would last long. Sauvresy had seemed better during the last week. He got up every day, and commenced to go about the house; he even received numerous visits from the neighbors; without apparent fatigue. But alas, the master of Valfeuillu was only the shadow of himself. His friends would never have recognized in that emaciated form and white face, and burning, haggard eye, the robust young man with red lips and beaming visage whom they remembered. He had suffered so! He did not wish to die before avenging himself on the wretches who had filched his happiness and his life. But what punishment should he inflict? This fixed idea burning in his brain, gave his look a fiery eagerness. Ordinarily, there are three modes in which a betrayed husband may avenge himself. He has the right, and it is almost a duty﻿—to deliver the guilty ones up to the law, which is on his side. He may adroitly watch them, surprise them and kill them. There is a law which does not absolve, but excuses him, in this. Lastly, he may affect a stolid indifference, laugh the first and loudest at his misfortune, drive his wife from his roof, and leave her to starve. But what poor, wretched methods of vengeance. Give up his wife to the law? Would not that be to offer his name, honor, and life to public ridicule? To put himself at the mercy of a lawyer, who would drag him through the mire. They do not defend the erring wife, they attack her husband. And what satisfaction would he get? Bertha and Trémorel would be condemned to a year’s imprisonment, perhaps eighteen months, possibly two years. It seemed to him simpler to kill them. He might go in, fire a revolver at them, and they would not have time to comprehend it, for their agony would be but for a moment; and then? Then, he must become a prisoner, submit to a trial, invoke the judge’s mercy, and risk conviction. As to turning his wife out of doors, that was to hand her over quietly to Hector. He imagined them leaving Valfeuillu, hand in hand, happy and smiling, and laughing in his face. At this thought he had a fit of cold rage; his self-esteem adding the sharpest pains to the wounds in his heart. None of these vulgar methods could satisfy him. He longed for some revenge unheard-of, strange, monstrous, as his tortures were.

===Chinese===

特雷莫雷尔伯爵断未料到，贝尔塔苦苦哀求争取的缓刑竟能维系多久。在过去的一周里，索夫勒齐的病情似乎有所好转。他每天都能下床走动，开始在宅院里信步踱步；他甚至接待了不少乡邻的频频探视，并未显露出明显的疲态。然而可悲的是，瓦尔菲伊庄园的男主人如今不过是一具形销骨立的躯壳。昔日的亲朋好友倘若见到这具骨瘦嶙峋的身躯、惨白的面容，以及那双燃烧着狂热与憔悴的眸子，绝不可能认出这竟是记忆中那位唇红齿白、容光焕发的健硕青年。他饱受了何等非人的煎熬！在亲手惩戒那两个窃取了他幸福与生命的恶徒之前，他绝不甘心咽下最后一口气。然而究竟该施加何等惩罚？这一顽念如烈火般灼烧着他的脑髓，令他的目光闪烁着狼一般的狂热渴望。寻常而言，一个遭到背叛的丈夫大抵有三种复仇的途径。他有权——甚至几乎是一桩天职——将奸夫淫妇扭送法办，毕竟法律完全站在他这一边。他也可以暗中窥伺跟踪，抓奸在床当场将二人击毙，法律对此即便不予免刑，也定会予以减轻体谅。最后，他还可以故作麻木不仁，抢在所有人前头第一个大声嘲笑自己的倒霉遭遇，将弃妇逐出家门，任由她冻饿而死。然而这些复仇手段何其卑微蹩脚！将发妻扭送官府？那岂不是将自己的姓氏、门楣与声誉拱手奉送给世人尽情嘲弄？岂不是将自己置于那些能言善辩的诉讼律师的摆布之下，任由对方在法庭泥淖中践踏玷污自己的尊严？他们绝不会为失足的淫妇辩护，反倒会对无辜的丈夫反咬一口。而他又能讨回什么公道？贝尔塔与特雷莫雷尔顶多被判处1年监禁，也许18个月，至多2年。在他看来，索性亲手杀了他们反倒更痛快利落。他大可以推门而入，抬手一枪将他们轰毙，而他们甚至连死因都来不及领会，因为他们的临终痛苦不过转瞬即逝；可之后呢？之后他便沦为阶下囚，接受法庭公审，摇尾乞怜法官的恩赦，还要冒着被定罪服刑的凶险。至于把妻子逐出家门，无异于顺水推舟将她拱手奉送给埃克托。他仿佛亲眼瞧见他们携手并肩离开瓦尔菲伊，喜笑颜开，甚至当面肆无忌惮地嘲弄他的无能。一念及此，一股冰冷的狂怒狠狠撕扯着他的心扉；高傲的自尊心在滴血的心头创口上又狠狠撒了一把盐。这些庸俗下作的报复手段，无一能平息他胸中的滔天怨气。他渴望一场前所未闻、诡异离奇、残暴可怖得足以匹敌他所受酷刑的惊天复仇。

===Original===

Then he thought of all the horrible tales he had read, seeking one to his purpose; he had a right to be particular, and he was determined to wait until he was satisfied. There was only one thing that could balk his progress﻿—Jenny’s letter. What had become of it? Had he lost it in the woods? He had looked for it everywhere, and could not find it.
He accustomed himself, however, to feign, finding a sort of fierce pleasure in the constraint. He learned to assume a countenance which completely hid his thoughts. He submitted to his wife’s caresses without an apparent shudder; and shook Hector by the hand as heartily as ever. In the evening, when they were gathered about the drawing-room table, he was the gayest of the three. He built a hundred air-castles, pictured a hundred pleasure-parties, when he was able to go abroad again. Hector rejoiced at his returning health.
“Clement is getting on finely,” said he to Bertha, one evening.
She understood only too well what he meant.
“Always thinking of Laurence?”
“Did you not permit me to hope?”
“I asked you to wait, Hector, and you have done well not to be in a hurry. I know a young girl who would bring you, not one, but three millions as dowry.”
This was a painful surprise. He really had no thoughts for anyone but Laurence, and now a new obstacle presented itself.
“And who is that?”
She leaned over, and whispered tremblingly in his ear:
“I am Clement’s sole heiress; perhaps he’ll die; I might be a widow tomorrow.”
Hector was petrified.
“But Sauvresy, thank God! is getting well fast.”
Bertha fixed her large, clear eyes upon him, and with frightful calmness said:
“What do you know about it?”
Trémorel dared not ask what these strange words meant. He was one of those men who shun explanations, and who, rather than put themselves on their guard in time, permit themselves to be drawn on by circumstances; soft and feeble beings, who deliberately bandage their eyes so as not to see the danger which threatens them, and who prefer the sloth of doubt, and acts of uncertainty to a definite and open position, which they have not the courage to face.
Besides, Hector experienced a childish satisfaction in seeing Bertha’s distress, though he feared and detested her. He conceived a great opinion of his own value and merit, when he saw the persistency and desperation with which she insisted on keeping her hold on him.
“Poor woman!”

===Chinese===

于是他搜肠刮肚回忆起平生所读过的所有骇人听闻的志怪传奇，一心想搜寻出一则契合自己心意的复仇蓝本；他有足够的资格挑三拣四，并铁了心要耐心守候直至心满意足。唯有一桩隐忧可能会阻碍他的全盘复仇大计——那便是珍妮的那封密信。那封信究竟落到了何处？难道是他遗失在密林深处了？他翻遍各处仔细搜寻，却始终无影无踪。
然而他逐渐学会了虚与委蛇，甚至在这份压抑伪装中体会到了一种残忍的快感。他学会了摆出一副将内心思绪捂得严严实实的面孔。他表面上毫无战栗地忍受着发妻的温存爱抚，也一如既往热忱地同埃克托握手言欢。每逢夜幕降临，三人围坐在客厅桌案旁时，他反倒成了其中谈笑最欢畅的那一个。他天马行空地勾勒出上百座空中楼阁，描摹着一旦自己康复远行后要张罗的上百场游猎欢聚。埃克托暗自为他的康复转机感到庆幸。
“克莱芒的身子骨硬朗多了，”一天傍晚他对贝尔塔说道。
贝尔塔对他的弦外之音心领神会。
“你满脑子依然只记挂着洛朗斯？”
“难道不是你亲口允准我抱有这份希望的吗？”
“我叫你安心等待，埃克托，而你没有操之过急，这很明智。我知晓有一位年轻姑娘能带给你不是100万，而是整整300万的丰厚嫁妆。”
这番话令他如遭重击、惊骇莫名。他心里分明只有洛朗斯一人，如今却半路杀出个崭新的阻碍。
“那姑娘究竟是谁？”
贝尔塔俯下身子，在他耳畔战栗着低语道：
“我是克莱芒唯一的法定继承人；他也许随时会咽气；我明天一早兴许就成了寡妇。”
埃克托当场惊呆了。
“可是索夫勒齐托上帝的福，身子骨正在飞速好转啊。”
贝尔塔将一双澄澈蔚蓝的大眼睛死死钉在他脸上，以一种令人毛骨悚然的平静口吻吐露道：
“他的底细，你又能知道多少？”
特雷莫雷尔不敢深究这句诡异之语背后的含义。他是那种对挑明真相避之唯恐不及的人，宁可听任命运摆布随波逐流，也绝不肯提前枕戈待旦；这种软弱怯懦的庸碌之徒，往往故意蒙蔽自己的双眼以避开扑面而来的凶险，宁可沉湎于疑窦的怠惰与举棋不定的混沌中，也绝不敢挺起胸膛去直面清晰明确的现实处境。
不仅如此，尽管埃克托对贝尔塔心怀畏惧与厌恶，但瞧见她陷入这般疯狂的煎熬苦楚，他心底竟涌起了一股孩童般的病态窃喜。每当瞧见她这般死乞白赖、绝望而执拗地紧抓着他不放，他便对自己非凡的魅力与身价产生了无限的陶醉与自得。
“可怜的女人！”

===Original===

thought he. “In her grief at losing me, and seeing me another’s, she has begun to wish for her husband’s death!”
Such was the torpor of his moral sense that he did not see the vileness of Bertha’s and his own thoughts.
Meanwhile Sauvresy’s state was not reassuring for Hector’s hopes and plans. On the very day when he had this conversation with Bertha, her husband was forced to take to his bed again. This relapse took place after he had drank a glass of quinine and water, which he had been accustomed to take just before supper; only, this time, the symptoms changed entirely, as if one malady had yielded to another of a very different kind. He complained of a pricking in his skin, of vertigo, of convulsive twitches which contracted and twisted his limbs, especially his arms. He cried out with excruciating neuralgic pains in the face. He was seized with a violent, persistent, tenacious craving for pepper, which nothing could assuage. He was sleepless, and morphine in large doses failed to bring him slumber; while he felt an intense chill within him, as if the body’s temperature were gradually diminishing. Delirium had completely disappeared, and the sick man retained perfectly the clearness of his mind. Sauvresy bore up wonderfully under his pains, and seemed to take a new interest in the business of his estates. He was constantly in consultation with bailiffs and agents, and shut himself up for days together with notaries and attorneys. Then, saying that he must have distractions, he received all his friends, and when no one called, he sent for some acquaintance to come and chat with him in order to forget his illness. He gave no hint of what he was doing and thinking, and Bertha was devoured by anxiety. She often watched for her husband’s agent, when, after a conference of several hours, he came out of his room; and making herself as sweet and fascinating as possible, she used all her cunning to find out something which would enlighten her as to what he was about. But no one could, or at least would, satisfy her curiosity; all gave evasive replies, as if Sauvresy had cautioned them, or as if there were nothing to tell.
No complaints were heard from Sauvresy.

===Chinese===

他暗自寻思：“她在即将失去我、眼睁睁看着我投向他人怀抱的巨大悲痛中，竟开始诅咒起自己丈夫的性命了！”
他的道德感知已然麻木迟钝到了这般田地，竟丝毫察觉不出贝尔塔与他自身的这番念头究竟有多么卑劣肮脏。
与此时，索夫勒齐的病情急转直下，彻底击碎了埃克托的如意算盘。就在他同贝尔塔发生这番密谈的当天，索夫勒齐便不得不再次卧床不起。这场复发发生在他喝下一杯金鸡纳霜水之后——这是他往常在晚餐正餐前雷打不动的习惯；然而这一次，所有的病症彻底变了样，仿佛旧疾已愈，转而被另一种截然不同的凶险恶疾所取代。他抱怨浑身皮肤有针刺般的奇痒与灼痛，头晕目眩，四肢阵阵剧烈抽搐扭曲，尤以双臂为甚。他因面部撕心裂肺的神经痛而凄厉惨叫。他心头涌起了一股暴烈、执拗、挥之不去的对胡椒的狂热渴求，无论吃下多少都无法平息那股干渴。他整夜辗转反侧无法合眼，哪怕大剂量的吗啡也无法催生半点睡意；与此同时，他只觉体内骨髓深处透出一股冰封刺骨的严寒，仿佛周身躯体的体温正一点一滴逐渐熄灭。然而谵妄高热却已荡然无存，病人的神智异乎寻常地清醒机敏。索夫勒齐以惊人的毅力硬扛着这般剧痛，甚至对名下田庄产业的打理表现出了一种全新的狂热兴致。他频频召见管事巡丁与代理人详谈，一连数日同公证人与诉讼代理人闭门密谋。随后，他又宣称自己需要排遣苦闷，大开门扉款待所有前来看望的故旧；若是无人登门，他便命人专程请几位熟人前来陪他闲聊，以忘却病痛折磨。对于自己的所思所为他秘而不宣，贝尔塔整个人被焦灼的疑云撕咬吞噬。每逢丈夫名下的代理人在密谈数小时迈出卧房之际，她总是暗中埋伏截住对方；她极尽所能摆出一副最甜美动人、风情万种的姿态，使出浑身解数试图套出一星半点内情，好弄明白丈夫究竟在暗中捣鼓什么名堂。然而没有一个人能够——或者说愿意——满足她的好奇心；所有人的答复皆含糊其辞、闪烁其词，仿佛早已得到过索夫勒齐的严词叮嘱，又仿佛当真无事可禀。
索夫勒齐口中听不到半句怨言。

===Original===

He talked constantly of Bertha and Hector; he wished all the world to know their devotion to him; he called them his “guardian angels,” and blessed Heaven that had given him such a wife and such a friend. Sauvresy’s illness now became so serious that Trémorel began to despair; he became alarmed; what position would his friend’s death leave him in? Bertha, having become a widow, would be implacable. He resolved to find out her inmost thoughts at the first opportunity; she anticipated him, and saved him the trouble of broaching the subject. One afternoon, when they were alone, M. Plantat being in attendance at the sick man’s bedside, Bertha commenced.
“I want some advice, Hector, and you alone can give it to me. How can I find out whether Clement, within the past day or two, has not changed his will in regard to me?”
“His will?”
“Yes, I’ve already told you that by a will of which I myself have a copy, Sauvresy has left me his whole fortune. I fear that he may perhaps revoke it.”
“What an idea!”
“Ah, I have reasons for my apprehensions. What are all these agents and attorneys doing at Valfeuillu? A stroke of this man’s pen may ruin me. Don’t you see that he can deprive me of his millions, and reduce me to my dowry of fifty thousand francs?”
“But he will not do it; he loves you﻿—”
“Are you sure of it? I’ve told you, there are three millions; I must have this fortune﻿—not for myself, but for you; I want it, I *must* have it! But how can I find out﻿—how? how?”
Hector was very indignant. It was to this end, then, that his delays had conducted him! She thought that she had a right now to dispose of him in spite of himself, and, as it were, to purchase him. And he could not, dared not, say anything!
“We must be patient,” said he, “and wait﻿—”
“Wait﻿—for what? Till he’s dead?”
“Don’t speak so.”
“Why not?” Bertha went up to him, and in a low voice, muttered:
“He has only a week to live; and see here﻿—”
She drew a little vial from her pocket, and held it up to him.
“That is what convinces me that I am not mistaken.”
Hector became livid, and could not stifle a cry of horror. He comprehended all now﻿—he saw how it was that Bertha had been so easily subdued, why she had refrained from speaking of Laurence, her strange words, her calm confidence.
“Poison!” stammered he, confounded.
“Yes, poison.”
“You have not used it?”

===Chinese===

他逢人便口口声声念叨着贝尔塔与埃克托；他巴不得全天下都知道二人对他照料得何等忠贞赤诚；他管他们叫自己的“守护天使”，指天叩谢上苍竟赐予他这般贤惠的发妻与这等患难与共的挚友。索夫勒齐的病势如今沉重到了让特雷莫雷尔心惊胆战的地步；他心头警铃大作：一旦挚友撒手人寰，自己将沦落到何等万劫不复的境地？贝尔塔一旦熬成寡妇，必将步步紧逼、绝不手软。他决意寻找机会探明她的真实心意；而贝尔塔却抢先一步找上了门，省去了他开口挑明的麻烦。某天午后趁着四下无人，普朗塔先生正在病榻前当值侍奉，贝尔塔径直开了口。
“埃克托，我需要你的拿主意，而唯有你能替我参详。我究竟该如何探明克莱芒在过去一两天里，有没有悄悄修改他那份涉及我的遗嘱？”
“他的遗嘱？”
“不错，我早就告知过你，依照一份由我亲自收存副本的遗嘱，索夫勒齐死后全部家财皆由我继承。可我眼下唯恐他暗中推翻这桩遗嘱。”
“你怎么会产生这种荒唐念头！”
“哼，我的忧虑并非空穴来风。那些账房代理人和诉讼代理人成天赖在瓦尔菲伊究竟在干些什么？那家伙大笔一挥，就能让我死无葬身之地。你难道瞧不出，他随时能剥夺原本属于我的数百万家财，把我打回原形、只留给我区区50000法郎的微薄嫁妆吗？”
“但他绝不会这么干的；他深爱着你——”
“你当真敢打包票？我早同你讲过，那可是整整300万巨款；我必须把这笔财富攥在掌心里——不是为了我自己，而是为了你；我想要它，我必须得到它！可我究竟该怎么探听虚实——究竟该怎么做？”
埃克托心头涌起了一股强烈的反感。原来自己的瞻前顾后与一再拖延，最终竟将局势推向了这般田地！这个女人自命如今有权强行摆布他，甚至俨然成了出资收买他的金主！而他偏偏理亏词穷，连半句重话都不敢吐露！
“我们必须沉住气，”他劝解道，“安心等待——”
“等待——等什么？等他两脚一蹬咽气吗？”
“别把话说得这么难听。”
“为什么不能说？”贝尔塔凑到他身前，压低了嗓门恶狠狠地耳语道：
“他顶多只有1个星期的活头了；瞧瞧这个——”
她从衣兜里掏出一只精巧的玻璃小药瓶，高高举到他眼前。
“正是这玩意儿让我深信，自己的推断绝不会错。”
埃克托面色骤然惨白如死尸，喉头忍不住滚出一声惊恐的倒抽凉气。他此刻终于恍然大悟——他终于明白贝尔塔为何会这般轻易隐忍妥协，为何绝口不再提洛朗斯，为何吐露那些诡谲莫测的怪话，为何流露出那般胸有成竹的从容镇定。
“毒药！”他浑身颤抖、语无伦次地结巴道。
“没错，正是鸩毒。”
“你还没动过手吧？”

===Original===

She fixed a hard, stern look upon him﻿—the look which had subdued his will, against which he had struggled in vain﻿—and in a calm voice, emphasizing each word, answered:
“I have used it.”
The count was, indeed, a dangerous man, unscrupulous, not recoiling from any wickedness when his passions were to be indulged, capable of everything; but this horrible crime awoke in him all that remained of honest energy.
“Well,” he cried, in disgust, “you will not use it again!”
He hastened toward the door, shuddering; she stopped him.
“Reflect before you act,” said she, coldly. “I will betray the fact of your relations with me; who will then believe that you are not my accomplice?”
He saw the force of this terrible menace, coming from Bertha.
“Come,” said she, ironically, “speak﻿—betray me if you choose. Whatever happens, for happiness or misery, we shall no longer be separated; our destinies will be the same.”
Hector fell heavily into a chair, more overwhelmed than if he had been struck with a hammer. He held his bursting forehead between his hands; he saw himself shut up in an infernal circle, without outlet.
“I am lost!” he stammered, without knowing what he said, “I am lost!”
He was to be pitied; his face was terribly haggard, great drops of perspiration stood at the roots of his hair, his eyes wandered as if he were insane. Bertha shook him rudely by the arm, for his cowardice exasperated her.
“You are afraid,” she said. “You are trembling! Lost? You would not say so, if you loved me as I do you. Will you be lost because I am to be your wife, because we shall be free to love in the face of all the world? Lost! Then you have no idea of what I have endured? You don’t know, then, that I am tired of suffering, fearing, feigning.”
“Such a crime!”
She burst out with a laugh that made him shudder.
“You ought to have said so,” said she, with a look full of contempt, “the day you won me from Sauvresy﻿—the day that you stole the wife of this friend who saved your life. Do you think that was a less horrid crime? You knew as well as I did how much my husband loved me, and that he would have preferred to die, rather than lose me thus.”
“But he knows nothing, suspects nothing of it.”
“You are mistaken; Sauvresy knows all.”
“Impossible!”
“All, I tell you﻿—and he has known all since that day when he came home so late from hunting.

===Chinese===

她将两道冰冷残忍的目光死死定格在他脸上——正是那曾狠狠碾碎他的意志、令他徒劳挣扎却无法抗拒的可怕目光——以一种极其平静、字字千钧的口吻答道：
“我已经下了毒。”
伯爵诚然是个凶险狡诈、毫无底线的恶徒，每逢欲壑难填之际哪怕伤天害理也绝不手软，什么丧尽天良的勾当都能干得出来；然而面对这桩骇人听闻的弑夫暴行，他体内残存的那点良善血性终究被彻底惊醒了。
“你疯了！”他满脸厌恶地失声惊呼，“你绝不准再动它半下！”
他浑身战栗着转身奔向房门；贝尔塔却一把拦住了他。
“在动手之前先掂量清楚，”她冷冷吐露道，“我大可以当场把你我通奸苟且的底细全盘捅出去；到那时，天下间有谁会相信你不是我的谋杀同谋？”
他瞬间领教了贝尔塔这句恶毒威胁的分量。
“来啊，”她冷嘲热讽道，“去揭发啊——只要你乐意，大可以把我卖了。无论招来什么祸福，无论上天堂还是下地狱，你休想再甩掉我独善其身；咱们俩生生世世绑在了一条绳上。”
埃克托颓然跌坐在椅子上，整个人如遭重锤轰顶。他用双手死死抱住快要炸裂的额头；他惊恐地发现自己已深陷一座毫无出路的修罗地狱。
“我彻底完了！”他神智昏乱地喃喃自语，“我全完了！”
他此刻的模样着实可怜；一张面庞惨白如纸、形容枯槁，豆大的冷汗在发际线处涔涔滚落，一双眼珠子狂乱游移，宛如癫狂的疯汉。贝尔塔粗暴地一把拽住他的胳膊拼命摇晃，他的这副懦夫做派令她出离愤怒。
“你怕了，”她啐道，“你在浑身打哆嗦！全完了？倘若你爱我哪怕有我爱你的半分深，你绝不会吐出这等窝囊话！难道做我的丈夫，难道咱们能堂堂正正站在大庭广众之下长相厮守，反倒叫你全完了？全完了！这么说你根本体会不到我所饱受的无尽煎熬？难道你不知道，我已经受够了内心的折磨、提心吊胆与虚与委蛇吗？”
“这可是杀头的灭门大罪啊！”
她喉头迸发出一阵令他毛骨悚然的惨笑。
“当天你从索夫勒齐身边把我勾引到手的时候，”她满眼鄙夷地嘲讽道，“当天你霸占这位救了你性命的生死之交的发妻的时候，你本该把这套大道理搬出来！难道你觉得那桩勾当就不算伤天害理的罪恶？你同我一样心知肚明我丈夫有多么宠爱我，他宁可去死，也绝不甘心遭受这等奇耻大辱。”
“可是他根本一无所知，丝毫未曾起疑。”
“你大错特错了；索夫勒齐全都知道。”
“绝不可能！”
“全都知道，我实话告诉你——自打他那天下帖子打猎迟迟未归的那天起，他就已经洞若观火了。

===Original===

Don’t you remember that I noticed his strange look, and said to you that my husband suspected something? You shrugged your shoulders. Do you forget the steps in the vestibule the night I went to your room? He had been spying on us. Well, do you want a more certain proof? Look at this letter, which I found, crumpled up and wet, in one of his vest pockets.”
She showed him the letter which Sauvresy had forcibly taken from Jenny, and he recognized it well.
“It is a fatality,” said he, overwhelmed. “But we can separate and break off with each other. Bertha, I can go away.”
“It’s too late. Believe me, Hector, we are today defending our lives. Ah, you don’t know Clement! You don’t know what the fury of a man like him can be, when he sees that his confidence has been outrageously abused, and his trust vilely betrayed. If he has said nothing to me, and has not let us see any traces of his implacable anger, it is because he is meditating some frightful vengeance.”
This was only too probable, and Hector saw it clearly.
“What shall we do?” he asked, in a hoarse voice; he was almost speechless.
“Find out what change he has made in his will.”
“But how?”
“I don’t know yet. I came to ask your advice, and I find you more cowardly than a woman. Let me act, then; don’t do anything yourself; I will do all.”
He essayed an objection.
“Enough,” said she. “He must not ruin us after all﻿—I will see﻿—I will think.”
Someone below called her. She went down, leaving Hector overcome with despair.
That evening, during which Bertha seemed happy and smiling, his face finally betrayed so distinctly the traces of his anguish, that Sauvresy tenderly asked him if he were not ill?
“You exhaust yourself tending on me, my good Hector,” said he. “How can I ever repay your devotion?”
Trémorel had not the strength to reply.
“And that man knows all,” thought he. “What courage! What fate can he be reserving for us?”
The scene which was passing before Hector’s eyes made his flesh creep. Every time that Bertha gave her husband his medicine, she took a hairpin from her tresses, and plunged it into the little vial which she had shown him, taking up thus some small, white grains, which she dissolved in the potions prescribed by the doctor.
It might be supposed that Trémorel, enslaved by his horrid position, and harassed by increasing terror, would renounce forever his proposed marriage with Laurence.

===Chinese===

难道你忘了吗，那天我便警觉到他异样的眼神，提醒过你我丈夫起了疑心？可你当时不过耸了耸肩膀。难道你忘了他听见门厅里的沉重脚步声？那天深夜我偷偷溜进你的卧房，他在暗中窥伺跟踪咱们！怎么，你想要铁证如山吗？睁大眼睛瞧瞧这封信吧，这是我从他马甲口袋里翻出来的一张揉成一团、被雨水浸透的残信！”
她亮出了那封索夫勒齐当年硬生生从珍妮手中夺下的信笺，埃克托一眼便辨认了出来。
“这是命中注定的劫数，”他面如死灰、瘫软如泥道，“但咱们完全可以就此一刀两断、各奔前程。贝尔塔，我大可以远走高飞。”
“太迟了。信我一句，埃克托，咱们如今是在捍卫自己的项上人头！啊，你根本不了解克莱芒！你根本料想不到，像他那样的人物一旦察觉到自己的肺腑深情遭到肆意践踏、赤诚信任遭到卑劣出卖，胸中的怒火会凶残暴烈到何等境地！假使他表面上对我只字不提，甚至不露半点不共戴天的狂怒痕迹，那纯粹是因为他正在暗中酝酿一场骇人听闻的血腥复仇！”
这番话不仅合情合理，甚至近在眼前，埃克托看得一清二楚。
“那咱们究竟该怎么办？”他嗓音嘶哑粗粝地问道，几乎连话都说不利索了。
“查明他在遗嘱上究竟动了什么手脚。”
“可怎么查？”
“我眼下还拿不准。我原本是来讨个主意，谁知你比娘们还要怯懦胆小。既然如此，那就由我全权接手；你什么都别插手，一切全包在我身上。”
他刚欲开口抗辩。
“够了，”她厉声喝断道，“他休想在最后关头毁了咱们——我会想方设法摸清底细的。”
楼下传来了呼唤她的声响。她转身步下楼梯，留下埃克托形单影只地瘫在原地，任由绝望吞噬。
当天夜晚，贝尔塔在席间表现得容光焕发、笑靥如花，而埃克托面庞上那股难以掩饰的凄苦绝望，终于被索夫勒齐敏锐地捕捉到了，他满含关切地温言询问伯爵是否抱恙。
“你为了伺候照料我，把身子骨都给拖垮了，我的好埃克托，”索夫勒齐动情地感慨道，“这份厚恩我究竟该如何报答才好？”
特雷莫雷尔连吐露半个字的力气都没有了。
“这个男人分明什么都知晓，”他暗自心惊胆战，“何等骇人的隐忍！他究竟在暗中给咱们备下了何等惨烈的下场？”
眼前上演的这一幕让埃克托浑身寒毛倒竖。每逢贝尔塔给丈夫端药之际，她总是悄无声息地从发髻上拔下一支发簪，探入那只方才向他展示过的精巧小药瓶里，挑出几粒微细的白色粉末，溶解在医生开出的药剂汤汁中。
常人或许以为，特雷莫雷尔身陷这等绝境，在与日俱增的恐惧折磨下，理当彻底断绝迎娶洛朗斯为妻的痴心妄想。

===Original===

Not so. He clung to that project more desperately than ever. Bertha’s threats, the great obstacles now intervening, his anguish, crime, only augmented the violence of his love for her, and fed the flame of his ambition to secure her as his wife. A small and flickering ray of hope which lighted the darkness of his despair, consoled and revived him, and made the present more easy to bear. He said to himself that Bertha could not be thinking of marrying him the day after her husband’s death. Months, a whole year must pass, and thus he would gain time; then some day he would declare his will. What would she have to say? Would she divulge the crime, and try to hold him as her accomplice? Who would believe her? How could she prove that he, who loved and had married another woman, had any interest in Sauvresy’s death? People don’t kill their friends for the mere pleasure of it. Would she provoke the law to exhume her husband? She was now in a position, thought he, wherein she could, or would not exercise her reason. Later on, she would reflect, and then she would be arrested by the probability of those dangers, the certainty of which did not now terrify her.
He did not wish that she should ever be his wife at any price. He would have detested her had she possessed millions; he hated her now that she was poor, ruined, reduced to her own narrow means. And that she was so, there was no doubt, Sauvresy indeed knew all. He was content to wait; he knew that Laurence loved him enough to wait for him one, or three years, if necessary. He already had such absolute power over her, that she did not try to combat the thoughts of him, which gently forced themselves on her, penetrated to her soul, and filled her mind and heart. Hector said to himself that in the interest of his designs, perhaps it was well that Bertha was acting as she did. He forced himself to stifle his conscience in trying to prove that he was not guilty. Who thought of this crime? Bertha. Who was executing it? She alone. He could only be reproached with moral complicity in it, a complicity involuntary, forced upon him, imposed somehow by the care for his own life. Sometimes, however, a bitter remorse seized him.

===Chinese===

事实却绝非如此。他反倒比以往任何时刻都更加死心塌地、疯狂地抓着这根救命稻草不放。贝尔塔的诛心恐吓、横亘眼前的重重万丈险阻、胸中的锥心剧痛与弥天大罪，非但未能熄灭他对洛朗斯的炽热狂恋，反倒像烈火烹油般助长了他非要迎娶她过门的熊熊野心。在绝望的无边黑暗中，这一缕摇曳微弱的希望之光，宽慰并唤醒了他残存的意志，让残酷的当下变得稍稍容易挨过一些。他暗自宽慰自己：贝尔塔绝不可能在丈夫咽气的翌日就急着同他完婚。数月、甚至整整1年时光必将悄然流逝，这就为他赢得了宝贵的周旋喘息之机；待到有朝一日，他便能堂堂正正挑明自己的意志。到那时她又能怎样？难道她敢自曝罪行，企图将他作为同谋拉下水吗？全天下谁会信她的一面之词？她又凭什么证明，一个移情别恋、已然迎娶了另一位佳人的男子，会有谋害索夫勒齐的杀人动机？世人绝不会纯粹为了寻开心而去戕害挚友。难道她有胆量逼迫法庭开棺验尸？他心想，贝尔塔眼下正处于理智全无、任性妄为的疯癫状态。待到日后她恢复清醒，必将被那些横陈眼前的致命凶险所震慑勒马——而眼下她不过是被狂乱冲昏了头脑，浑然不觉畏惧罢了。
无论如何，他绝不肯在任何代价下迎娶她为妻。即便她坐拥几百万家财，他也只会对她深恶痛绝；而如今她沦为了赤贫无依的穷妇，名下产业荡然无存，他更是对她恨之入骨。毫无疑问，索夫勒齐确实早已看穿了一切。埃克托甘心等待；他深知洛朗斯对他的爱意坚如磐石，哪怕等待1年甚至3年也在所不惜。他对洛朗斯早已树立起了无与伦比的绝对掌控，以至于她根本不曾抗拒对他的眷恋思念——那份柔情悄无声息地渗透进她的灵魂，占据了她的整颗心扉与全部神思。埃克托在心底暗自盘算：从自己图谋的大局着眼，贝尔塔这般行径未尝不是一件幸事。他极力麻痹掐灭自己的良知，拼命向自己证明自己绝非罪魁祸首。究竟是谁策划了这桩弑夫谋杀？是贝尔塔。是谁在亲手执行鸩毒？唯独她一人。世人充其量只能在道德层面上指摘他是被动卷入的从犯——一种不由自主、身不由己、在保全自身性命的胁迫下无可奈何的隐忍苟同。然而在某些深夜时刻，一股彻骨的悔恨仍会如毒蛇般狠狠噬咬他的心扉。

===Original===

He could have understood a sudden, violent, rapid murder; could have explained to himself a knife-stroke; but this slow death, given drop by drop, horribly sweetened by tenderness, veiled under kisses, appeared to him unspeakably hideous. He was mortally afraid of Bertha, as of a reptile, and when she embraced him he shuddered from head to foot.
She was so calm, so engaging, so natural; her voice had the same soft and caressing tones, that he could not forget it. She plunged her hairpin into the fatal vial without ceasing her conversation, and he did not surprise her in any shrinking or shuddering, nor even a trembling of the eyelids. She must have been made of brass. Yet he thought that she was not cautious enough; and that she put herself in danger of discovery; and he told her of these fears, and how she made him tremble every moment.
“Have confidence in me,” she answered. “I want to succeed﻿—I am prudent.”
“But you may be suspected.”
“By whom?”
“Eh! How do I know? Everyone﻿—the servants, the doctor.”
“No danger. And suppose they did suspect?”
“They would make examinations, Bertha; they would make a minute scrutiny.”
She gave a smile of the most perfect security.
“They might examine and experiment as much as they pleased, they would find nothing. Do you think I am such a fool as to use arsenic?”
“For Heaven’s sake, hush!”
“I have procured one of those poisons which are as yet unknown, and which defy all analysis; one of which many doctors﻿—and learned ones, too﻿—could not even tell the symptoms!”
“But where did you get this﻿—this﻿—”
He dared not say, “poison.”
“Who gave you *that*?” resumed he.
“What matters it? I have taken care that he who gave it to me should run the same danger as myself, and he knows it. There’s nothing to fear from that quarter. I’ve paid him enough to smother all his regrets.”
An objection came to his lips; he wanted to say, “It’s too slow;” but he had not the courage, though she read his thought in his eyes.
“It is slow, because that suits me,” said she. “Before all, I must know about the will﻿—and that I am trying to find out.”

===Chinese===

他原本尚能理解那种猝不及防、暴烈迅疾的血腥屠戮；哪怕心口挨上一记白刀子进红刀子出，他也自认能给出合理的解释；然而这种温水煮青蛙般一点一滴施加的缓慢凌迟，这种被包裹在脉脉温存与如胶似漆的亲吻之下的甜腻鸩杀，在他看来简直令人作呕、丑陋至极。他畏惧贝尔塔如畏蛇蝎，每逢她投怀送抱温存拥抱之际，他全身上下的每一寸肌肤都会从头到脚泛起一阵毛骨悚然的战栗。
她是那般从容、那般楚楚动人、那般自然得体；她的嗓音永远带着那股柔情似水的呢喃软语，教他永世难忘。她一边同丈夫谈笑风生，一边若无其事地将发簪探入那只绝命小瓶中，而他竟从未能在她脸上捕捉到哪怕一丝一毫的心虚瑟缩、战栗，甚至连眼皮都不曾眨动一下。这个女人的心肠必定是黄铜浇铸的。然而他总觉得她行事过于鲁莽大胆，随时有招致东窗事发的凶险；他向她坦陈了自己的惶恐，告知她她的一举一动无时无刻不在令自己提心吊胆。
“对我放一百个心，”她答道，“我一心只求成事——我谨慎得很。”
“可你随时会引人疑窦。”
“被谁疑心？”
“嗨！我哪能说得准？全府的下人、甚至前来看诊的大夫！”
“绝无可能。退一万步讲，就算他们起疑又如何？”
“他们会暗中查验，贝尔塔；他们会展开翻箱倒柜的细致勘验。”
她脸上绽放出一抹笃定安泰的迷人浅笑。
“任凭他们如何验看查访，也休想抓到半点把柄。你难道以为我是个蠢到会去动用砒霜的笨蛋吗？”
“求求你老天在上，快闭嘴吧！”
“我弄到的是一种当世尚未被医学界破译的绝密异毒，能彻底逃过一切理化检验；哪怕是城里最有学问的名医泰斗，也休想辨认出它的中毒病征！”
“可你究竟是从何处弄来这……这……”
他甚至不敢从嘴里吐出“毒药”两个字。
“究竟是谁给你的这玩意儿？”他定了定神追问道。
“这又有何相干？我早做好了周密防备，让那个把药卖给我的人担负着同我一样的杀头凶险，他自己心里比谁都清楚。在这方面绝无后顾之忧。我付给他的封口银子，足够将他所有的良心悔恨堵得严严实实。”
一句反驳涌到了埃克托的唇边；他本想脱口而出：“这药效发作得太慢了；”但他终究没有这股勇气，尽管贝尔塔早已从他的眼神中看穿了他的心意。
“之所以发作得慢，纯粹是因为这正合我意，”她说道，“在做断决之前，我必须先摸清遗嘱的底细——而这正是我眼下极力探寻的关键。”

===Original===

She occupied herself constantly about this will, and during the long hours that she passed at Sauvresy’s bedside, she gradually, with the greatest craft and delicacy, led her husband’s mind in the direction of his last testament, with such success that he himself mentioned the subject which so absorbed Bertha.
He said that he did not comprehend why people did not always have their worldly affairs in order, and their wishes fully written down, in case of accident. What difference did it make whether one were ill or well? At these words Bertha attempted to stop him. Such ideas, she said, pained her too much. She even shed real tears, which fell down her cheeks and made her more beautiful and irresistible than before; real tears which moistened her handkerchief.
“You dear silly creature,” said Sauvresy, “do you think that makes one die?”
“No; but I do not wish it.”
“But, dear, have we been any the less happy because, on the day after our marriage, I made a will bequeathing you all my fortune? And, stop; you have a copy of it, haven’t you? If you were kind, you would go and fetch it for me.”
She became very red, then very pale. Why did he ask for this copy? Did he want to tear it up? A sudden thought reassured her; people do not tear up a document which can be cancelled by a scratch of the pen on another sheet of paper. Still, she hesitated a moment.
“I don’t know where it can be.”
“But I do. It is in the left-hand drawer of the glass cupboard; come, please me by getting it.”
While she was gone, Sauvresy said to Hector:
“Poor girl! Poor dear Bertha! If I died, she never would survive me!”
Trémorel thought of nothing to reply; his anxiety was intense and visible.
“And this man,” thought he, “suspects something! No; it is not possible.”
Bertha returned.
“I have found it,” said she.
“Give it to me.”
He took the copy of his will, and read it with evident satisfaction, nodding his head at certain passages in which he referred to his love for his wife. When he had finished reading, he said:
“Now give me a pen and some ink.”
Hector and Bertha reminded him that it would fatigue him to write; but he insisted. The two guilty ones, seated at the foot of the bed and out of Sauvresy’s sight, exchanged looks of alarm. What was he going to write? But he speedily finished it.
“Take this,” said he to Trémorel, “and read aloud what I have just added.”

===Chinese===

她满脑子时时刻刻都在盘算着这份遗嘱，在索夫勒齐病榻前侍奉度过的漫长岁月里，她步步为营、极尽机巧与温存，润物细无声地将丈夫的思绪引向了身后立嘱之事，手段之高超，竟最终引得索夫勒齐主动挑起了这桩令贝尔塔魂牵梦萦的话题。
索夫勒齐感慨道，他实在想不通世人为何总不能提前将身后的钱财账目料理停当，防患于未然白纸黑字将心愿立下。一个人究竟是抱恙在身还是康健无虞，又有什么分别呢？听闻此言，贝尔塔故作悲切地上前劝阻。她声称这些不吉利的念头刺痛了她的心扉。她甚至当场滚落出晶莹剔透的滚滚清泪，泪珠顺着娇靥滑落，反倒让她显得比以往更加风华绝代、楚楚动人；那可是浸湿了手帕的货真价实的泪花。
“你这个痴心的小傻瓜，”索夫勒齐动情地抚慰道，“难道你以为立下遗嘱人就会咽气吗？”
“不；但我绝不许你胡思乱想。”
“可是亲爱的，难道成婚翌日我立下遗嘱将全部家财赠予你之后，咱们俩的日子就少快活了半分吗？对了，你手里收存着一份副本，对吧？倘若你心疼我，就去替我取来瞧瞧。”
她面色骤然涨得通红，旋即又转为惨白。他为何索要这份副本？难道他打算当面将其撕碎作废？冷不丁一个念头宽慰了她：一个人倘若当真要推翻遗嘱，只需在另一张白纸上随手勾勒数笔便能令前纸作废，根本犯不着多此一举去撕毁副本。然而她仍旧迟疑了片刻。
“我一时记不清搁在何处了。”
“可我记得。就在玻璃大立柜左侧的抽屉里；乖，去替我取来，权当宽宽我的心。”
趁着她离席取物的工夫，索夫勒齐转向埃克托感叹道：
“可怜的姑娘！可怜可爱的贝尔塔！倘若我当真撒手西归，她必定也绝不肯独活于世！”
特雷莫雷尔脑海中一片空白，竟搜寻不出半句对答；他浑身上下散发出的焦灼与惶恐已然溢于言表。
“可这个男人，”他暗自心惊，“分明早已起疑！不，绝无可能。”
贝尔塔折返了回来。
“我找到了，”她说道。
“递给我。”
他接过遗嘱副本，带着显而易见的心满意足逐字审阅，读到某些详述对妻子忠贞爱意的深情字句时，还频频点头称许。阅毕，他开口吩咐道：
“去替我取笔墨来。”
埃克托与贝尔塔慌忙从旁劝阻，声称动笔写字会耗损他的元气精力；然而他却执意坚持。两位心怀鬼胎的罪人并肩端坐在床尾、避开索夫勒齐视线的阴暗死角里，神情惊恐地面面相觑。他究竟打算在上面添写什么？然而索夫勒齐很快便落下了笔。
“拿着，”他对特雷莫雷尔吩咐道，“把我刚添上去的这几行字，当众朗声念出来。”

===Original===

Hector complied with his friend’s request, with trembling voice:

> “This day, being sound in mind, though much suffering, I declare that I do not wish to change a line of this will. Never have I loved my wife more﻿—never have I so much desired to leave her the heiress of all I possess, should I die before her.
> “Clement Sauvresy.”

===Chinese===

埃克托依从好友的嘱托，用颤抖不已的嗓音念诵道：

> “今日，余虽饱受病痛折磨，然神智明晰如常，特此声明：余对本遗嘱字句绝无更易之念。余对发妻之深情爱笃胜过以往任何时刻——倘若余先其一步撒手人寰，余渴望将其立为余名下全副身家唯一继承人之夙愿，亦从未如此决绝坚定。
> “克莱芒·索夫勒齐。”

===Original===

Mistress of herself as Bertha was, she succeeded in concealing the unspeakable satisfaction with which she was filled. All her wishes were accomplished, and yet she was able to veil her delight under an apparent sadness.
“Of what good is this?” said she, with a sigh.
She said this, but half an hour afterward, when she was alone with Hector, she gave herself up to the extravagance of her delight.
“Nothing more to fear,” exclaimed she. “Nothing! Now we shall have liberty, fortune, love, pleasure, life! Why, Hector, we shall have at least three millions; you see, I’ve got this will myself, and I shall keep it. No more agents or notaries shall be admitted into this house henceforth. Now I must hasten!”
The count certainly felt a satisfaction in knowing her to be rich, for he could much more easily get rid of a millionaire widow than of a poor penniless woman. Sauvresy’s conduct thus calmed many sharp anxieties. Her restless gayety, however, her confident security, seemed monstrous to Hector. He would have wished for more solemnity in the execution of the crime; he thought that he ought at least to calm Bertha’s delirium.
“You will think more than once of Sauvresy,” said he, in a graver tone.
She answered with a “prrr,” and added vivaciously:
“Of him? when and why? Oh, his memory will not weigh on me very heavily. I trust that we shall be able to live still at Valfeuillu, for the place pleases me; but we must also have a house at Paris﻿—or we will buy yours back again. What happiness, Hector!”
The mere prospect of this anticipated felicity so shocked Hector, that his better self for the moment got the mastery; he essayed to move Bertha.
“For the last time,” said he, “I implore you to renounce this terrible, dangerous project. You see that you were mistaken﻿—that Sauvresy suspects nothing, but loves you as well as ever.”
The expression of Bertha’s face suddenly changed; she sat quite still, in a pensive revery.
“Don’t let’s talk any more of that,” said she, at last. “Perhaps I was mistaken. Perhaps he only had doubts﻿—perhaps, although he has discovered something, he hopes to win me back by his goodness. But you see﻿—”
She stopped. Doubtless she did not wish to alarm him.
He was already much alarmed. The next day he went off to Melun without a word; being unable to bear the sight of this agony, and fearing to betray himself.

===Chinese===

尽管贝尔塔极擅掩饰心机，此刻也费了九牛二虎之力才将心头喷涌而出的狂喜彻底强压下去。她的所有欲求悉数如愿以偿，可她依然巧妙地将内心的狂喜包裹在了一副强作愁苦的面具之下。
“这又何苦呢，”她幽幽叹了口气。
她嘴上这般哀叹，可仅仅过了半个小时，当她同埃克托在暗室独处之际，整个人便彻底沉溺于肆无忌惮的癫狂狂喜之中。
“再无后顾之忧了！”她手舞足蹈地尖叫道，“万事大吉！从今往后，自由、财富、爱情、享乐与荣华富贵全归咱们了！埃克托，你瞧，咱们手里至少攥着整整300万巨款！如今遗嘱原件落在了我自个儿手里，我定要将它死死捂住。从今往后，全府上下休想再放进任何账房代理或公证人半步。眼下我必须加紧动手了！”
获悉她即将坐拥泼天巨富，伯爵心头确实闪过了一丝踏实——毕竟摆脱一个腰缠万贯的富孀，远比甩掉一个身无分文的贫妇要容易得多。索夫勒齐此番立嘱，确实替他抹去了诸多锥心的恐慌。然而贝尔塔那股躁动不安的欢欣若狂、那副笃定安泰的张狂姿态，在埃克托眼中却显得灭绝人性、狰狞可怖。他本巴望这场弑夫惨剧的执行能多上几分庄严肃穆；他觉得至少应当给贝尔塔的狂乱浇上一盆冷水。
“有朝一日，你必将不止一次回想起索夫勒齐，”他以一种格外严肃凝重的语调告诫道。
贝尔塔轻蔑地从鼻孔里哼出一声嗤笑，随即伶牙俐齿地反诘道：
“想起他？何时想？又为何要想？哼，他的阴魂绝不可能在我心头压上半点分量。我但愿咱们往后依然能常住瓦尔菲伊，只因这处庄园深得我心；不过咱们在巴黎也必须置办一处体面公馆——要不然就把你原先的那座大宅赎回来。埃克托，那该是何等神仙日子啊！”
这番提前畅想的美满前景令埃克托不寒而栗，良知的微光在这一刻重新占据了上风；他试图打动贝尔塔的心肠。
“最后一次了，”他苦苦哀求道，“我求求你彻底放弃这桩残暴而凶险的勾当吧。你亲眼瞧见了，你先前全猜错了——索夫勒齐根本一无所知，他依然同往昔那般深爱着你。”
贝尔塔脸上的神情骤然一变；她凝神静坐，陷入了一阵深不可测的沉思中。
“别再提这茬了，”良久她才缓缓开口道，“兴许是我多心猜错了。兴许他起初不过略有起疑——兴许即便他察觉到了什么蛛丝马迹，也一心渴望用他的菩萨心肠感化我回头。可你看——”
她咽住了话头。显然她不愿彻底吓破他的胆子。
而埃克托早已吓得魂不附体。翌日清晨，他连声招呼都没打便逃往了默伦；他再也承受不住目睹这场凌迟惨剧的折磨，唯恐当场露出马脚出卖自己。

===Original===

But he left his address, and when she sent word that Sauvresy was always crying out for him, he hastily returned. Her letter was most imprudent and absurd, and made his hair stand on end. He had intended, on his arrival, to reproach her; but it was she who upbraided him.
“Why this flight?”
“I could not stay here﻿—I suffered, trembled, felt as if I were dying.”
“What a coward you are!”
He would have replied, but she put her finger on his mouth, and pointed with her other hand to the door of the next room.
“Sh! Three doctors have been in consultation there for the past hour, and I haven’t been able to hear a word of what they said. Who knows what they are about? I shall not be easy till they go away.”
Bertha’s fears were not without foundation. When Sauvresy had his last relapse, and complained of a severe neuralgia in the face and an irresistible craving for pepper, Dr. R﻿—﻿— had uttered a significant exclamation. It was nothing, perhaps﻿—yet Bertha had heard it, and she thought she surprised a sudden suspicion on the doctor’s part; and this now disturbed her, for she thought that it might be the subject of the consultation. The suspicion, however, if there had ever been any, quickly vanished. The symptoms entirely changed twelve hours later, and the next day the sick man felt pains quite the opposite of those which had previously distressed him. This very inconstancy of the distemper served to puzzle the doctor’s conclusions. Sauvresy, in these latter days, had scarcely suffered at all, he said, and had slept well at night; but he had, at times, strange and often distressing sensations. He was evidently failing hourly; he was dying﻿—everyone perceived it. And now Dr. R﻿—﻿— asked for a consultation, the result of which had not been reached when Trémorel returned.
The drawing-room door at last swung open, and the calm faces of the physicians reassured the poisoner. Their conclusions were that the case was hopeless; everything had been tried and exhausted; no human resources had been neglected; the only hope was in Sauvresy’s strong constitution.
Bertha, colder than marble, motionless, her eyes full of tears, seemed so full of grief on hearing this cruel decision, that all the doctors were touched.
“Is there no hope then? Oh, my God!” cried she, in agonizing tones.
Dr. R﻿—﻿— hardly dared to attempt to comfort her; he answered her questions evasively.

===Chinese===

然而他到底还是留下了行踪住址，当贝尔塔派人传话声称索夫勒齐正哭天抢地一刻不停呼唤他的名字时，他只得急匆匆折返回来。贝尔塔送来的书信不仅鲁莽荒谬至极，更吓得他头皮发麻、寒毛直竖。他原本打定主意一抵达便严词数落她；可未等他开口，反倒是贝尔塔率先对他迎头痛击。
“你临阵脱逃究竟是何居心？”
“我实在待不下去了——我浑身难受、战栗发抖，觉得自己都快要咽气了。”
“你当真是个不折不扣的懦夫！”
他刚欲开口回击，贝尔塔却一把将食指竖在唇边，另一只手指了指隔壁房间的大门。
“嘘！三位名医在里面闭门会诊了整整1个小时了，我愣是没能偷听到半句切实的结论。谁晓得他们究竟在捣鼓什么名堂？在他们滚蛋之前，我这颗心休想落回肚子里。”
贝尔塔的恐慌并非毫无根据。当索夫勒齐上一次旧疾复发、抱怨面部剧烈神经痛以及对胡椒产生无可抗拒的病态狂热时，R医生曾冷不丁发出一声饱含深意的惊叹。那或许根本不值一提——然而贝尔塔却真真切切听在了耳中，她敏锐地捕捉到了大夫眼中闪过的一丝狐疑；而这正是她此刻心神不宁的症结所在，唯恐这成了此次名医会诊的焦点。然而那点疑云即便曾经存在过，也早已烟消云散了。短短12小时之后，病人的症状全盘翻转，翌日病患所遭受的痛苦竟同先前的折磨截然相反。这种变幻莫测的诡谲病情，反倒将大夫们的诊断思路彻底搅成了一团浆糊。据索夫勒齐自述，他在这最近几天里几乎感觉不到痛楚，夜里也睡得安稳香甜；但他周身各处偶尔会泛起一些诡异而凄苦的异样麻木。他的元气显然正在按小时飞速衰竭；他正在步向死亡——全庄园的人都看在眼里。而如今，R医生提议发起了一场联合会诊，当特雷莫雷尔赶回府上时，大夫们尚未得出最终定论。
客厅的大门终于悠悠推开了，几位名医面庞上的泰然从容，让这位投毒凶手彻底吃下了一颗定心丸。大夫们的会诊结论是：回天乏术，药石无灵；所有能动用的手段早已穷尽；人世间的一切医理皆未曾遗漏；唯一的生机全系于索夫勒齐平日里底子硬朗的体魄。
贝尔塔比大理石还要冷酷，木雕泥塑般伫立在原地，泪眼婆娑，在听闻这道残酷的宣判时表现得肝肠寸断，以至于在场的所有名医无不为之动容。
“难道就当真半点指望都没有了吗？天哪，我的老天爷啊！”她发出了撕心裂肺的哀嚎。
R医生甚至不敢上前多加宽慰；面对她的连声追问，只能含糊其辞地好言搪塞。

===Original===

“We must never despair,” said he, “when the invalid is of Sauvresy’s age and constitution; nature often works miracles when least expected.”
The doctor, however, lost no time in taking Hector apart and begging him to prepare the poor, devoted, loving young lady for the terrible blow about to ensue.
“For you see,” added he, “I don’t think Monsieur Sauvresy can live more than two days!”
Bertha, with her ear at the keyhole, had heard the doctor’s prediction; and when Hector returned from conducting the physician to the door, he found her radiant. She rushed into his arms.
“Now” cried she, “the future truly belongs to us. Only one black point obscured our horizon, and it has cleared away. It is for me to realize Doctor R﻿—﻿—’s prediction.” They dined together, as usual, in the dining-room, while one of the chambermaids remained beside the sickbed. Bertha was full of spirits which she could scarcely control. The certainty of success and safety, the assurance of reaching the end, made her imprudently gay. She spoke aloud, even in the presence of the servants, of her approaching liberty. During the evening she was more reckless than ever. If any of the servants should have a suspicion, or a shadow of one she might be discovered and lost. Hector constantly nudged her under the table and frowned at her, to keep her quiet; he felt his blood run cold at her conduct; all in vain. There are times when the armor of hypocrisy becomes so burdensome that one is forced, cost what it may, to throw it off if only for an instant.
While Hector was smoking his cigar, Bertha was more freely pursuing her dream. She was thinking that she could spend the period of her mourning at Valfeuillu, and Hector, for the sake of appearances, would hire a pretty little house somewhere in the suburbs. The worst of it all was that she would be forced to seem to mourn for Sauvresy, as she had pretended to love him during his lifetime. But at last a day would come when, without scandal, she might throw off her mourning clothes, and then they would get married. Where? At Paris or Orcival?
Hector’s thoughts ran in the same channel. He, too, wished to see his friend under the ground to end his own terrors, and to submit to Bertha’s terrible yoke.

===Chinese===

“咱们永远不能轻言放弃，”医生说道，“尤其是病人正当索夫勒齐先生这把年纪、又素来体魄强健的前提下；造物主往往会在山穷水尽之际降下奇迹。”
然而医生转过头来，便急忙将埃克托拉到一旁，恳求他好生给这位苦命、贤良、用情至深的年轻夫人打打预防针，好让她对即将降临的晴天霹雳有个心理准备。
“依我看哪，”医生悄声补充道，“索夫勒齐先生恐怕熬不过2天了！”
贝尔塔耳朵紧贴在门锁的钥匙孔上，早已将大夫的绝命断言听得一清二楚；当埃克托将医生送出大门折返回来时，赫然瞧见她整个人容光焕发、喜不自胜。她一把飞扑进他的怀抱。
“成了！”她忘情地尖叫道，“锦绣前程当真完完全全属于咱们了！横亘在天际线上的唯一一抹阴霾，如今彻底烟消云散了。接下来，就由我来兑现R医生的预言吧。”两人一如往常在餐厅共进晚餐，一名贴身女仆则留在病榻前守候侍奉。贝尔塔周身洋溢着按捺不住的雀跃狂喜。大获全胜与高枕无忧的笃定、终点近在咫尺的狂热，让她表现出了一种极其危险放肆的欢快。她当着仆役们的面，大声畅谈起自己即将重获的自由身。到了夜晚，她的言行更是放浪形骸、肆无忌惮。倘若有哪个仆人稍起疑心、甚至捕捉到一丝蛛丝马迹，她都将彻底暴露、死无葬身之地。埃克托在桌底下频频用脚尖踢碰她，横眉冷对朝她使眼色，试图让她收敛安静；她的癫狂做派让他吓得浑身血液几乎凝固；然而一切皆是徒劳。人世间有时便是如此：伪善的面具一旦背负得过于沉重，哪怕付出天大的代价，一个人也势必在某个瞬间将其狠狠扯下喘上一口气。
趁着埃克托点燃雪茄吞云吐雾之际，贝尔塔更加肆意地追逐起她那荒唐的迷梦。她暗自盘算着守丧期完全可以在瓦尔菲伊庄园度过，而埃克托为了掩人耳目，大可以在近郊某处租下一座精巧别致的小洋房。整桩变故中最让人难熬的，无非是她不得不装模作样替索夫勒齐哭丧服孝，就如同在丈夫生前不得不伪装深爱他一样。然而总有一天，她能在合乎礼法、全无非议的风光体面中褪去丧服，到那时二人便可名正言顺喜结连理。至于完婚的地点，究竟是选在巴黎，还是奥尔西瓦尔呢？
埃克托脑海中的思绪也在同一条沟壑里打转。他也一心巴望着尽早将好友深埋黄土之下，好彻底了结心头的无边惊恐，俯首甘为贝尔塔那道可怕的铁轭所驱使。

## XV / 第十五章
===Original===

On going to bed, that night, the count was less enchanted than ever with the devotion of his friend Sauvresy. There is not a diamond on which a spot cannot be found with a microscope.
“Here he is,” thought he, “abusing his privileges as the saver of my life. Can’t a man do you a service, without continually making you feel it? It seems as though because he prevented me from blowing my brains out, I had somehow become something that belongs to him! He came very near upbraiding me for Jenny’s extravagance. Where will he stop?”
The next day at breakfast he feigned indisposition so as not to eat, and suggested to Sauvresy that he would lose the train.
Bertha, as on the evening before, crouched at the window to see them go away. Her troubles during the past eight-and-forty hours had been so great that she hardly recognized herself. She scarcely dared to reflect or to descend to the depths of her heart. What mysterious power did this man possess, to so violently affect her life? She wished that he would go, never to return, while at the same time she avowed to herself that in going he would carry with him all her thoughts. She struggled under the charm, not knowing whether she ought to rejoice or grieve at the inexpressible emotions which agitated her, being irritated to submit to an influence stronger than her own will.
She decided that today she would go down to the drawing-room. He would not fail﻿—were it only for politeness﻿—to go in there; and then, she thought, by seeing him nearer, talking with him, knowing him better, his influence over her would vanish. Doubtless he would return, and so she watched for him, ready to go down as soon as she saw him approaching. She waited with feverish shudderings, anxiously believing that this first tête-à-tête in her husband’s absence would be decisive. Time passed; it was more than two hours since he had gone out with Sauvresy, and he had not reappeared. Where could he be?
At this moment, Hector was awaiting Jenny at the Corbeil station. The train arrived, and Jenny soon appeared. Her grief, joy, emotion had not made her forget her toilet, and never had she been so rollickingly elegant and pretty. She wore a green dress with a train, a velvet mantle, and the jauntiest little hat in the world.

===Chinese===

当天夜里上床就寝之际，伯爵对自己挚友索夫勒齐的赤诚奉献，感受到的非但不再是感激，反倒是前所未有的心烦意乱。即便世间最纯净无瑕的钻石，在显微镜下也能挑出斑驳的瑕疵。
“你们瞧瞧他，”埃克托暗自寻思，“分明是在滥用救命恩人的特权。难道一个人施了恩惠，就非得时时刻刻让受恩者刻骨铭心地感受到这份压迫吗？仿佛就因为他拦着没让我崩碎脑袋，我整个人就莫名其妙成了他的私有附属品一样！他方才甚至差点就珍妮的奢靡挥霍当面数落我了。他究竟还要得寸进尺到何等地步？”
翌日共进早餐时，他故意装出身体抱恙的模样一口未动，还旁敲侧击提醒索夫勒齐莫要误了赶火车的时辰。
贝尔塔一如前夜那般，悄悄蜷缩在窗前目送着二人远去。在过去的整整48小时里，她胸中所饱受的心神动荡是如此剧烈，以至于连她自己都快认不出自己了。她甚至不敢静下心来反躬自省、不敢探入自己灵魂的深渊。这个男人究竟拥有何等勾魂摄魄的神秘魔力，竟能如此暴烈狂乱地撼动她的整个人生？她一方面巴不得他立刻拔腿远去、此生再莫踏足此地半步，可另一方面却又在心底无可救药地向自己承认：倘若他当真离去，必将带走她所有的神思与魂魄。她在这股无形的魔咒下苦苦挣扎，面对这股撕扯胸膛的莫名心悸，竟不知究竟该喜该悲；而屈从于一股凌驾于自身意志之上的狂暴牵引，更让她心头涌起了一股无名怒火。
她暗自拿定主意，今天一定要下楼去大客厅。哪怕纯粹出于礼节，他也绝不可能不踏入客厅；她心想，只要能离得更近一些端详他、同他搭话、更深入地了解他，他施加在自己身上的无形魔力势必烟消云散。他料想很快就会折返，于是她目不转睛地守候在窗前，随时准备一瞧见他的身影便步下楼梯。她在阵阵发热的战栗中苦苦守候，满心焦灼地认定，趁着丈夫不在府上的这初次独处幽会，必将成为定夺命运的关键时刻。时间飞速流逝；距离他同索夫勒齐出门已过去了整整2个多小时，他却始终没有现身。他究竟能去往何处？
而此时此刻，埃克托正在科尔贝伊火车站等候着珍妮的到来。列车缓缓进站，珍妮的身影很快便映入了眼帘。重重悲痛、狂喜与心潮澎湃，丝毫未能让她疏忽了自己的仪容装扮，她平生似乎从未展现出如此明艳欢脱、风情万种的优雅身姿。她身着一袭拖地翠绿长裙，外披一件天鹅绒斗篷，头顶斜戴着一顶全天下最俏皮别致的小礼帽。

===Original===

As soon as she saw Hector standing near the door, she uttered a cry, pushed the people aside, and rushed into his arms, laughing and crying at the same time. She spoke quite loud, with wild gestures, so that everyone could hear what she said.
“You didn’t kill yourself, after all,” said she. “Oh, how I have suffered; but what happiness I feel today!”
Trémorel struggled with her as he could, trying to calm her enthusiastic exclamations, softly repelling her, charmed and irritated at once, and exasperated at all these eyes rudely fixed on him. For none of the passengers had gone out. They were all there, staring and gazing. Hector and Jenny were surrounded by a circle of curious folks.
“Come along,” said Hector, his patience exhausted. He drew her out of the door, hoping to escape this prying curiosity; but he did not succeed. They were persistently followed. Some of the Corbeil people who were on the top of the omnibus begged the conductor to walk his horses, that this singular couple might not be lost to view, and the horses did not get into a trot until they had disappeared in the hotel.
Sauvresy’s foresight in recommending the place of meeting had thus been disconcerted by Jenny’s sensational arrival. Questions were asked; the hostess was adroitly interrogated, and it was soon known that this person, who waited for eccentric young ladies at the Corbeil station, was an intimate friend of the owner of Valfeuillu. Neither Hector nor Jenny doubted that they formed the general topic of conversation. They breakfasted gayly in the best room at the Belle Image, during which Trémorel recounted a very pretty story about his restoration to life, in which he played a part, the heroism of which was well calculated to redouble the little lady’s admiration. Then Jenny in her turn unfolded her plans for the future, which were, to do her justice, most reasonable. She had resolved more than ever to remain faithful to Hector now that he was ruined, to give up her elegant rooms, sell her furniture, and undertake some honest trade. She had found one of her old friends, who was now an accomplished dressmaker, and who was anxious to obtain a partner who had some money, while she herself furnished the experience. They would purchase an establishment in the Breda quarter, and between them could scarcely fail to prosper.

===Chinese===

刚一瞥见立在门边的埃克托，她便失声发出一声惊呼，一把推开挡路的人群，飞扑进他的怀抱，一边又哭又笑。她扯着嗓门大声呼喊，手舞足蹈，好让在场的所有人都能听清她的满腔肺腑：
“您到底还是没自尽成，”她嚷道，“天哪，这些日子我饱受了何等煎熬；可我今天又是何等快活啊！”
特雷莫雷尔手忙脚乱地应付着她，极力试图平息她那狂热奔放的惊呼，轻手轻脚地将她推开，心头既被她的真情打动又烦躁不堪，更被四周无数双粗暴聚焦在自己身上的直勾勾目光惹得无名火起。只因车厢里的乘客竟没有一个挪步离开，所有人全挤在原地目瞪口呆地看热闹。埃克托与珍妮被这群好事之徒团团围在核心。
“快跟我走，”埃克托耐性耗尽地低喝道。他拉扯着她冲出门外，一心渴望摆脱这群窥探的好奇目光；然而一切徒劳无功，人群亦步亦趋地尾随在后。坐在公共马车站顶座席上的几位科尔贝伊本地看客，甚至央求车夫把马车放慢步速，好不至于跟丢这对离奇古怪的旷世男女，直到二人彻底消失在旅馆大门内，车夫才重新甩响马鞭让马儿小跑起来。
索夫勒齐当初出于体贴挑选这一僻静碰头地点的先见之明，就这样被珍妮这场轰轰烈烈的盛大登场彻底搅黄了。闲言碎语迅速在坊间传开；女掌柜被人旁敲侧击套出了口风，不出多久世人便获悉：在科尔贝伊火车站与这位举止出格的风流女郎私会的贵介公子，乃是瓦尔菲伊庄园主人的莫逆之交。无论是埃克托还是珍妮，都毫不怀疑自己已然沦为了全镇茶余饭后的头条谈资。两人在靓影旅馆（Belle Image）最体面的一间客房里欢畅地共进早午餐，席间特雷莫雷尔编造了一套娓娓动听的死里逃生传奇，将自己在其中的英勇壮举吹得神乎其神，狠狠成倍放大了这位小美人对他的顶礼膜拜。随后珍妮也依次描摹了自己对未来的规划打算，平心而论，她的这番打算可谓务实至极。既然埃克托如今家财散尽，她反倒比以往任何时刻都更铁了心要对他矢志不渝；她打算退掉巴黎的豪奢寓所，变卖陈设家具，转而去干一门正经本分的体面生计。她重新联系上了一位当年交好的旧姐妹，对方如今已是一名手艺拔尖的成衣裁缝，正急切寻觅一位有些许本钱的入股合伙人，由对方出资、她自个儿负责出技术操持业务。她们打算在布雷达街区盘下一处铺面，凭二人的联手打拼，生意断无不兴隆之理。

===Original===

Jenny talked with a pretty, knowing, businesslike air, which made Hector laugh. These projects seemed very comic to him; yet he was touched by this unselfishness on the part of a young and pretty woman, who was willing to work in order to please him.
But, unhappily, they were forced to part. Jenny had gone to Corbeil intending to stay a week; but the count told her this was absolutely impossible. She cried bitterly at first, then got angry, and finally consoled herself with a plan to return on the following Tuesday.
“Goodbye,” said she, embracing Hector, “think of me.” She smilingly added, “I ought to be jealous; for they say your friend’s wife is perhaps the handsomest woman in France. Is it true?”
“Upon my word, I don’t know. I’ve forgotten to look at her.”
Hector told the truth. Although he did not betray it, he was still under the surprise of his chagrin at the failure of his attempt at suicide. He felt the dizziness which follows great moral crises as well as a heavy blow on the head, and which distracts the attention from exterior things. But Jenny’s words, “the handsomest woman in France,” attracted his notice, and he could, that very evening, repair his forgetfulness. When he returned to Valfeuillu, his friend had not returned; Mme. Sauvresy was alone reading, in the brilliantly lighted drawing-room. Hector seated himself opposite her, a little aside, and was thus able to observe her at his ease, while engaging her in conversation. His first impression was an unfavorable one. He found her beauty too sculptural and polished. He sought for imperfections, and finding none, was almost terrified by this lovely, motionless face, these clear, cold eyes. Little by little, however, he accustomed himself to pass the greater part of the afternoon with Bertha, while Sauvresy was away arranging his affairs﻿—selling, negotiating, using his time in cutting down interests and discussing with agents and attorneys. He soon perceived that she listened to him with pleasure, and he judged from this that she was a decidedly superior woman, much better than her husband. He had no wit, but possessed an inexhaustible fund of anecdotes and adventures. He had seen so many things and known so many people that he was as interesting as a chronicle. He had a sort of frothy fervor, not wanting in brilliancy, and a polite cynicism which, at first, surprised one.

===Chinese===

珍妮谈吐间透着一股俏皮精明、精打细算的掌柜气派，逗得埃克托忍俊不禁。这些天真的宏图大计在他眼中显得滑稽无比；然而一位年轻貌美的女子竟甘愿为了取悦他而自食其力出卖劳力，这份毫无私心的赤诚终究打动了他的心弦。
然而无奈的是，二人终究不得不挥泪作别。珍妮原本大包小包赶赴科尔贝伊打算厮守上整整一周；但伯爵直言相告这绝无可能。起初她哭得梨花带雨，旋即又耍起了小性子娇嗔发作，直到最后两人约定下星期二再度重聚，她才破涕为笑重拾宽慰。
“永别了，”她紧紧拥吻着埃克托呢喃道，“千万要时刻挂念着我。”她嫣然一笑打趣道：“我本该大吃飞醋的；因为旁人都传说，您那位好友的内人兴许是全法国最标致的大美人。这传闻当真属实吗？”
“天地良心，这我可真说不上来。我压根就忘了正眼瞧她。”
埃克托说的是实话。尽管表面上不动声色，自尽未遂所带来的沉重懊丧与惊惶至今仍死死盘踞在他的心头。他体验着重大精神崩塌或颅骨遭受钝击后那种常有的眩晕与麻木，这让他对周遭的一切外物皆心不在焉。然而珍妮脱口而出的这句“全法国最标致的大美人”，却猛地勾起了他的兴致，而就在当晚，他便有了弥补这一疏忽的机会。当他折返回瓦尔菲伊庄园时，好友尚未归家；索夫勒齐夫人形单影只地独坐在灯火通明的大客厅里翻阅书卷。埃克托挑了她对面的位置坐下，身子略微偏向一侧，如此一来便能在同她从容攀谈的当口，肆无忌惮地细细端详她。他的第一印象并不尽如人意。他觉得她的美貌过于雕塑般刻板、过于冰冷无瑕。他极力搜寻她身上的瑕疵纰漏，可一无所获，这副精致动人却木然无波的面庞、这双澄澈而冰冷的眸子，甚至让他心头隐隐生出一丝敬畏与骇然。然而日复一日，他逐渐习惯了趁着索夫勒齐外出料理账目事务——变卖资产、商务周旋、将大把时光耗费在砍削高利贷利息以及同账房代理人和诉讼律师唇枪舌剑之际——在客厅里陪伴贝尔塔消磨大半个午后的时光。不出多久他便敏锐地觉察出，她极其享受聆听他的高谈阔论，由此他断定这绝非寻常凡妇俗女，在见识气度上远远胜过她那位庸俗的丈夫。埃克托固然全无真正的深邃机智，胸中却搜刮着取之不尽的风流逸事与奇闻轶事。他阅人无数、历经沧桑红尘，讲起古今掌故来生动得宛如一本摊开的野史秘闻。他谈吐间带着一股泡沫般虚浮的狂热与不乏闪光点的风趣，以及一种初听之下令人错愕不凡的雅致冷嘲。

===Original===

Had Bertha been unimpassioned, she might have judged him at his value; but she had lost her power of insight. She heard him, plunged in a foolish ecstasy, as one hears a traveller who has returned from far and dangerous countries, who has visited peoples of whose language the hearer is ignorant, and lived in the midst of manners and customs incomprehensible to ourselves.
Days, weeks, months passed on, and the Count de Trémorel did not find life at Valfeuillu as dull as he had thought. He insensibly slipped along the gentle slope of material well-being, which leads directly to brutishness. A physical and moral torpor had succeeded the fever of the first days, free from disagreeable sensations, though wanting in excitement. He ate and drank much, and slept twelve round hours. The rest of the time, when he did not talk with Bertha, he wandered in the park, lounged in a rocking-chair, or took a jaunt in the saddle. He even went fishing under the willows at the foot of the garden; and grew fat. His best days were those which he spent at Corbeil with Jenny. He found in her something of his past, and she always quarrelled with him, which woke him up. Besides, she brought him the gossip of Paris and the small talk of the boulevards. She came regularly every week, and her love for Hector, far from diminishing, seemed to grow with each interview. The poor girl’s affairs were in a troubled condition. She had bought her establishment at too high a price, and her partner at the end of the first month decamped, carrying off three thousand francs. She knew nothing about the trade which she had undertaken, and she was robbed without mercy on all sides. She said nothing of these troubles to Hector, but she intended to ask him to come to her assistance. It was the least that he could do.
At first, the visitors to Valfeuillu were somewhat astonished at the constant presence there of a young man of leisure; but they got accustomed to him. Hector assumed a melancholy expression of countenance, such as a man ought to have who had undergone unheard-of misfortunes, and whose life had failed of its promise. He appeared inoffensive; people said:
“The count has a charming simplicity.”
But sometimes, when alone, he had sudden and terrible relapses. “This life cannot last,” thought he; and he was overcome with childish rage when he contrasted the past with the present.

===Chinese===

倘若贝尔塔当时心如止水，她本能够一眼看穿他徒有其表的虚浮身价；然而她早已彻底丧失了明辨秋毫的理智洞察。她沉溺在愚蠢的如痴如醉中，聆听着他的高谈阔论，就如同一名深闺凡人聆听着自极远、极险恶异域归来的探险家，讲述着那些言语不通的蛮荒部族、以及寻常人匪夷所思的风土人情。
数日、数周乃至数月匆匆流逝，特雷莫雷尔伯爵渐渐发觉，瓦尔菲伊庄园的日子根本不像他原先预料的那般索然无味。他在物质优裕的平缓斜坡上悄无声息地滑落，而这条斜坡的尽头，直通肉体的麻木与堕落。最初几天的精神高热退去之后，取而代之的是一种肉体与灵魂上的彻底怠惰——虽无刺激狂欢，倒也无病无灾、惬意安泰。他大口啖肉大碗痛饮，每天雷打不动蒙头大睡整整12个小时。其余的大把时光，只要不陪贝尔塔闲聊，他便漫步在偌大的庄园林苑里，懒洋洋地斜倚在摇椅上发呆，或是翻身上马巡游一番。他甚至还会跑到花园尽头的柳树荫下垂钓度日；整个人渐渐发福丰满起来。他过得最快活的时光，无非是每周前往科尔贝伊同珍妮相聚的日子。他在她身上能重新觅得昔日纸醉金迷的旧影，而她成天同他拌嘴吃醋，倒也能让他昏睡的神经清醒几分。不仅如此，她还能源源不断为他带来巴黎市井的花边绯闻与林荫大道上的街谈巷议。她雷打不动每周前来赴约，而她对埃克托的爱火非但未曾冷却，反倒在每一次耳鬓厮磨中愈燃愈炽。这个可怜姑娘的生计陷入了重重泥淖。她盘下那处铺面的价格被敲了大竹杠，而她的合伙人在第一个月末便卷走了整整3000法郎逃之夭夭。她对自己经办的这门行当一窍不通，周遭所有人都在肆无忌惮地宰割盘剥她。她对埃克托将这些烦心苦楚捂得严严实实，心里却暗自盘算着找个由头向他求援。这原本就是他分内理所应当的关照。
起初，登门造访瓦尔菲伊的宾客们对庄园里常年寄居着这样一位游手好闲的名门公子颇感诧异；然而久而久之人们便习以为常了。埃克托在人前总摆出一副郁郁寡欢的忧郁面孔，俨然一个饱尝旷古未闻的横祸惨剧、半生宏图彻底付诸东流的落魄贵胄。他显得人畜无害，四邻乡绅纷纷交口称赞道：
“伯爵大人身上当真有一种令人如沐春风的纯朴谦逊。”
然而有时形单影只独处之际，他心头仍会冷不丁爆发出一阵阵可怕的心神激荡。“这种日子绝不可能长久，”他暗自咬牙切齿；每当将昔日的风光无限与当下的寄人篱下两相对照，他便被一股孩童般的暴怒所吞噬。

===Original===

How could he shake off this dull existence, and rid himself of these stiffly good people who surrounded him, these friends of Sauvresy? Where should he take refuge? He was not tempted to return to Paris; what could he do there? His house had been sold to an old leather merchant; and he had no money except that which he borrowed of Sauvresy. Yet Sauvresy, to Hector’s mind, was a most uncomfortable, wearisome, implacable friend; he did not understand halfway measures in desperate situations.
“Your boat is foundering,” he said to Hector; “let us begin by throwing all that is superfluous into the sea. Let us keep nothing of the past; that is dead; we will bury it, and nothing shall recall it. When your situation is relieved, we will see.”
The settlement of Hector’s affairs was very laborious. Creditors sprung up at every step, on every side, and the list of them seemed never to be finished. Some had even come from foreign lands. Several of them had been already paid, but their receipts could not be found, and they were clamorous. Others, whose demands had been refused as exorbitant, threatened to go to law, hoping to frighten Sauvresy into paying. Sauvresy wearied his friend by his incessant activity. Every two or three days he went to Paris, and he attended the sales of the property in Burgundy and Orleans. The count at last detested and hated him; Sauvresy’s happy, cheerful air annoyed him; jealousy stung him. One thought﻿—that a wretched one﻿—consoled him a little. “Sauvresy’s happiness,” said he to himself, “is owing to his imbecility. He thinks his wife dead in love with him, whereas she can’t bear him.”
Bertha had, indeed, permitted Hector to perceive her aversion to her husband. She no longer studied the emotions of her heart; she loved Trémorel, and confessed it to herself. In her eyes he realized the ideal of her dreams. At the same time she was exasperated to see in him no signs of love for her. Her beauty was not, then, irresistible, as she had often been told. He was gallant and courteous to her﻿—nothing more.
“If he loved me,” thought she, “he would tell me so, for he is bold with women and fears no one.”
Then she began to hate the girl, her rival, whom Hector went to meet at Corbeil every week. She wished to see her, to know her. Who could she be? Was she handsome? Hector had been very reticent about Jenny.

===Chinese===

他究竟该如何挣脱这味同嚼蜡的沉闷桎梏，如何摆脱周遭环伺的这群道貌岸然的方正乡绅——索夫勒齐的这帮狐朋狗友？他又能去往何方？他如今不过年方35岁，坐拥每年40000里弗尔的稳定地租进项，而这份厚实的身家，全仰仗索夫勒齐的精明手腕才从沉船的废墟中抢救保全下来。然而在瓦尔菲伊庄园，他充其量不过是个无足轻重的次要角色；他深知自己在世人眼中，不过是一个靠他人恩赐庇护苟活的可怜虫。
贝尔塔的痴心爱慕并未能逃过他的眼睛；只因随着时光流逝，她的爱火已然澎湃到了近乎无法自持的地步。他虽然自命不凡，却从未生出过跨越雷池、行差踏错的念头。他并非受道德良知的羁绊——那种无聊的说教早被他抛到了九霄云外；但他生性畏惧麻烦，甚至厌恶惹火烧身。他心知肚明，一旦招惹上贝尔塔这等心气高傲、性情刚烈的深闺怨妇，必将招致无穷无尽的纠缠与惨烈的决裂，到那时整个瓦尔菲伊都将化作修罗场。他宁可去招惹十个珍妮，也绝不愿同这位索夫勒齐夫人沾染上半点私情。他甚至刻意保持着冷若冰霜的克制距离，试图以此熄灭她心头的痴心妄想。
然而他的疏离冷漠，反倒像烈火烹油般助长了贝尔塔内心的狂热。对一个平生任性妄为、从未遭遇过忤逆的女人而言，抗拒便是最致命的诱饵。她将埃克托的克制视作对她的轻蔑与挑衅。她开始歇斯底里地嫉恨起那个每周引得埃克托前往科尔贝伊私会的神秘情妇。她迫切渴望亲眼瞧一瞧那个女人，探清她的底细。那究竟是个怎样的狐媚子？当真生得倾国倾城吗？而埃克托对珍妮的底细向来守口如瓶。

===Original===

He evaded all questions about her, not sorry to let Bertha’s imagination work on his mysterious visits.
The day at last came when she could no longer resist the intensity of her curiosity. She put on the simplest of her toilets, in black, threw a thick veil over her head, and hastened to the Corbeil station at the hour that she thought the unknown girl would present herself there. She took a seat on a bench in the rear of the waiting-room. She had not long to wait. She soon perceived the count and a young girl coming along the avenue, which she could see from where she sat. They were arm in arm, and seemed to be in a very happy mood. They passed within a few steps of her, and as they walked very slowly, she was able to scrutinize Jenny at her ease. She saw that she was pretty, but that was all. Having seen that which she wished, and become satisfied that Jenny was not to be feared (which showed her inexperience) Bertha directed her steps homeward. But she chose her time of departure awkwardly; for as she was passing along behind the cabs, which concealed her, Hector came out of the station. They crossed each other’s paths at the gate, and their eyes met. Did he recognize her? His face expressed great surprise, yet he did not bow to her. “Yes, he recognized me,” thought Bertha, as she returned home by the river-road; and surprised, almost terrified by her boldness, she asked herself whether she ought to rejoice or mourn over this meeting. What would be its result? Hector cautiously followed her at a little distance. He was greatly astonished. His vanity, always on the watch, had already apprised him of what was passing in Bertha’s heart, but, though modesty was no fault of his, he was far from guessing that she was so much enamoured of him as to take such a step.
“She loves me!” he repeated to himself, as he went along. “She loves me!”
He did not yet know what to do. Should he fly? Should he still appear the same in his conduct toward her, pretending not to have seen her? He ought to fly that very evening, without hesitation, without turning his head; to fly as if the house were about to tumble about his head. This was his first thought. It was quickly stifled under the explosion of the base passions which fermented in him. Ah, Sauvresy had saved him when he was dying!

===Chinese===

他巧妙避开了所有涉及情妇的追问，甚至颇为得意地任由贝尔塔的妒火在那些神秘莫测的幽会遐想中狂乱滋长。
终于有一天，贝尔塔再也按捺不住内心抓心挠肝的强烈好奇。她换上了最素雅不起眼的一身黑色衣裙，头上蒙了一面厚实的黑纱面罩，算准了那位神秘女郎抵达火车站的班次，急匆匆赶赴科尔贝伊车站。她在候车大厅最偏僻的一张长椅上坐了下来。她并未苦等多久。很快，顺着座席正对的林荫大道，她远远瞥见了伯爵正同一名年轻女子并肩挽臂漫步而来。二人显得欢快惬意极了。他们从距离她不过几步之遥的眼前踱步而过，由于步伐极慢，贝尔塔得以从容不迫地将珍妮上下细细审视了个遍。她瞧出对方确实生得有几分姿色，但也仅此而已。既然得偿所愿探清了虚实，深信这个浅薄的小娘们根本不足为虑（这恰恰暴露了她深闺见识的幼稚与无知），贝尔塔便转身迈步踏上了归途。然而她挑拣的离去时机却蹩脚至极；正当她借着一排马车的遮挡试图悄然溜走之际，埃克托恰好步出了车站。两人在大门口迎面撞了个正着，目光猝不及防地交织在一处。他究竟认出她来了没有？伯爵脸上分明流露出巨大的错愕，可他并未向她欠身致意。“不错，他必定认出我来了，”贝尔塔沿着沿河大道折返庄园，暗自心慌意乱；她对自己这番胆大妄为的鲁莽举动感到震惊甚至骇然，不知究竟该为这场邂逅暗自窃喜还是悔恨莫及。这番撞破究竟会招致何等结局？埃克托则保持着一段不远不近的距离，悄无声息地尾随在她身后。他内心同样掀起了惊涛骇浪。他那时刻警觉的虚荣心，早就洞悉了贝尔塔心底翻涌的春情，然而尽管他向来狂妄自大从不妄自菲薄，却也断没想到这个女人竟对自己迷恋到了甘愿冒此奇险的地步。
“她爱上我了！”他一路疾行，在心头反复回荡着这个念头，“她彻底爱上我了！”
他一时竟不知该如何应对。是该拔腿远逃？还是该若无其事地继续维系以往的客套周旋、假装压根没在车站撞见她？照理说，他理当就在今晚收拾行装拔腿远遁，毫不迟疑，头也不回；逃得就如同整栋宅院即将大厦倾塌一般决绝。这原本是他脑海中蹦出的第一个念头。然而这个清醒的念头，转瞬间便被他体内沉渣泛起的卑劣欲念彻底扑灭撕碎了。啊，索夫勒齐当初在他命悬一线之际挽救了他的性命！

===Original===

Sauvresy, after saving him, had welcomed him, opened to him his heart, purse, house; at this very moment he was making untiring efforts to restore his fortunes. Men like Trémorel can only receive such services as outrages. Had not his sojourn at Valfeuillu been a continual suffering? Was not his self-conceit tortured from morning till night? He might count the days by their humiliations. What! Must he always submit to﻿—if he was not grateful for﻿—the superiority of a man whom he had always been wont to treat as his inferior?
“Besides,” thought he, judging his friend by himself, “he only acts thus from pride and ostentation. What am I at his house, but a living witness of his generosity and devotion? He seems to live for me﻿—it’s Trémorel here and Trémorel there! He triumphs over my misfortunes, and makes his conduct a glory and title to the public admiration.”
He could not forgive his friend for being so rich, so happy, so highly respected, for having known how to regulate his life, while he had exhausted his own fortune at thirty. And should he not seize so good an opportunity to avenge himself for the favors which overwhelmed him?
“Have I run after his wife?” said he to himself, trying to impose silence on his conscience. “She comes to me of her own will, herself, without the least temptation from me. I should be a fool if I repelled her.”
Conceit has irresistible arguments. Hector, when he entered the house, had made up his mind. He did not fly. Yet he had the excuse neither of passion nor of temptation; he did not love her, and his infamy was deliberate, coldly premeditated. Between her and him a chain more solid than mutual attraction was riveted; their common hatred of Sauvresy. They owed too much to him. His hand had held both from degradation.
The first hours of their mutual understanding were spent in angry words, rather than the cooings of love. They perceived too clearly the disgrace of their conduct not to try to reassure each other against their remorse. They tried to prove to each other that Sauvresy was ridiculous and odious; as if they were absolved by his deficiencies, if deficiencies he had. If indeed trustfulness is foolishness, Sauvresy was indeed a fool, because he could be deceived under his own eyes, in his own house, because he had perfect faith in his wife and his friend.

===Chinese===

索夫勒齐不仅救了他的命，更待他如手足，对他敞开了胸怀、钱袋与家门；就在此时此刻，老友仍在不知疲倦地四处奔走，替他重整家业。然而在特雷莫雷尔这种恶徒眼中，旁人施加的一切天大恩惠，最终无一不被他扭曲视作对自己的莫大侮辱。他在瓦尔菲伊庄园寄人篱下的日子，难道不是一场漫长无休的无形凌迟吗？他的虚荣心难道不是从早到晚都在饱受严刑拷打吗？他简直可以用所受的屈辱来丈量度过的每一个日日夜夜。凭什么！难道他竟要永远仰人鼻息、俯首帖耳地屈从于一个平生向来被他踩在脚底视作下等庸才之人的所谓优越气度吗？
“不仅如此，”他以己度人、用自己肮脏的小人之心揣度起好友来，“他这番做派纯粹是为了满足虚荣与炫耀。我留在他府上算什么，不过是他标榜自身仗义疏财与舍己为人的活道具罢了！他成天仿佛全是为了我而活——张口特雷莫雷尔闭口特雷莫雷尔！他分明是在我的落魄悲剧上耀武扬威，拿对我的施舍当作博取天下人交口称赞的勋章与资本！”
他无法原谅自己的朋友竟如此富有、如此幸福美满、如此受人爱戴敬重；无法原谅对方懂得如何精明地打理人生，而自己却在30岁时便将万贯家财挥霍殆尽。如今横陈眼前有这等天赐良机，他难道不该借此狠狠报复一下这些压得他喘不过气来的所谓恩惠吗？
“难道是我主动勾引他老婆的吗？”他扪心自问，拼命掐灭良知的微弱抗议，“分明是她自己死乞白赖投怀送抱，甚至全无半点我的引诱撩拨。我要是将她拒之门外，岂不成了天下第一号大傻瓜。”
膨胀的虚荣心总有令人无法抗拒的诡辩逻辑。迈入大门的那一刻，埃克托心中已拿定了主意。他没有逃。然而他既无干柴烈火的情欲借口，亦无猝不及防的诱惑托辞；他根本不爱她，他的卑劣行径乃是一场蓄谋已久、冷血深思熟虑的背信弃义。在他们二人之间，焊接起了一条比寻常两情相悦更加坚不可摧的黑暗纽带——那便是对索夫勒齐共同的刻骨仇恨。他们欠他的实在太多太多了。正是那双手，曾将他们二人从万劫不复的泥淖中死死托起。
他们暗通款曲、达成心照不宣默契的最初几个小时，并未沉浸在情意绵绵的温存私语中，反倒充斥着咬牙切齿的刻毒恶语。他们太清醒地意识到自己行径的禽兽不如与奇耻大辱，以至于必须靠互相打气来抵御良知的严酷鞭笞。他们极力向彼此证明索夫勒齐是何等滑稽可憎、面目可厌；仿佛只要证明了受害者的荒唐可笑，他们犯下的滔天大罪便能得以免罪开脱。倘若胸无城府的赤诚坦荡当真算作痴傻，那么索夫勒齐确实是个十足的傻瓜——只因他竟在自己的眼皮底下、在自己的卧榻之侧被人肆意蒙蔽羞辱，只因他对自己的发妻与至交怀有不掺杂质的绝对信任。

===Original===

He suspected nothing, and every day he rejoiced that he had been able to keep Trémorel by him. He often repeated to his wife:
“I am too happy.”
Bertha employed all her art to encourage these joyous illusions. She who had before been so capricious, so nervous, wilful, became little by little submissive to the degree of an angelic softness. The future of her love depended on her husband, and she spared no pains to prevent the slightest suspicion from ruffling his calm confidence. Such was their prudence that no one in the house suspected their state. And yet Bertha was not happy. Her love did not yield her the joys she had expected. She hoped to be transported to the clouds, and she remained on the earth, hampered by all the miserable ties of a life of lies and deceit.
Perhaps she perceived that she was Hector’s revenge on her husband, and that he only loved in her the dishonored wife of an envied friend. And to crown all, she was jealous. For several months she tried to persuade Trémorel to break with Jenny. He always had the same reply, which, though it might be prudent, was irritating.
“Jenny is our security﻿—you must think of that.”
The fact was, however, that he was trying to devise some means of getting rid of Jenny. It was a difficult matter. The poor girl, having fallen into comparative poverty, became more and more tenacious of Hector’s affection. She often gave him trouble by telling him that he was no longer the same, that he was changed; she was sad, and wept, and had red eyes.
One evening, in a fit of anger, she menaced him with a singular threat.
“You love another,” she said. “I know it, for I have proofs of it. Take care! If you ever leave me, my anger will fall on her head, and I will not have any mercy on her.”
The count foolishly attached no importance to these words; they only hastened the separation.
“She is getting very troublesome,” thought he. “If some day I shouldn’t go when she was expecting me, she might come up to Valfeuillu, and make a wretched scandal.”
He armed himself with all his courage, which was assisted by Bertha’s tears and entreaties, and started for Corbeil resolved to break off with Jenny. He took every precaution in declaring his intentions, giving the best reasons for his decision that he could think of.
“We must be careful, you know, Jenny,” said he, “and cease to meet for a while.

===Chinese===

他对周遭的一切全无半点疑心，甚至每天都在暗自庆幸自己有能耐将特雷莫雷尔长久留在身边。他频频对妻子感叹道：
“我实在太幸福了。”
贝尔塔使出浑身解数去迎合纵容这些欢快的幻象。昔日那个乖戾刁蛮、神经质且任性妄为的怨妇，竟一步步蜕变成了一个百依百顺、宛如天使般温存柔顺的贤妻。她这份偷情苟合的未来全系于丈夫一身，她不惜耗费一切心力，谨防哪怕一丝一毫的嫌隙疑云吹皱丈夫心头这池平静笃定的深水。二人的行事防范是如此周密谨慎，以至于全府上下竟无一人觉察出他们暗通款曲的勾当。然而贝尔塔却丝毫品尝不到幸福。这份偷来的欢愉并未能赐予她魂牵梦萦的极乐狂喜。她原本巴望着一跃飞升至九霄云外，可双脚却依旧沉重地陷在泥淖里，被整套谎言与欺瞒编织而成的卑劣锁链捆绑得寸步难行。
或许她逐渐敏锐地觉察出，自己不过是埃克托报复她丈夫的一件称手兵刃，埃克托在她身上迷恋的，不过是一位受尽世人艳羡的挚友被玷污的名门发妻罢了。更要命的是，她打心眼里疯狂地吃醋。一连数月，她软磨硬泡逼迫特雷莫雷尔彻底斩断同珍妮的私情。而他每一次给出的答复虽然冠冕堂皇、谨慎周全，却每一次都让她火冒三丈：
“珍妮是咱们俩最好的防空洞与挡箭牌——你必须替大局着想。”
然而实际上，埃克托自己也在绞尽脑汁琢磨着彻底甩掉珍妮的妥善脱身之策。这着实是桩棘手的难事。这个可怜姑娘沦入相对赤贫的窘境之后，反倒对埃克托的眷恋依附得愈发绝望而执拗。她常常泪眼婆娑地找他哭诉，数落他不再同往昔一样体贴、数落他彻底变了心；她整日郁郁寡欢，动辄哭成泪人，一双眼睛哭得红肿不堪。
某天傍晚在盛怒之下，她甚至冷不丁对他发出了恶狠狠的诛心恐吓：
“你心里分明有了别的小妖精，”她咬牙啐道，“我心知肚明，我手里握着铁证！你给我小心点！倘若你胆敢抛弃我，我的滔天怒火定会倾泻在她的脑袋上，我绝不会对她施舍半点慈悲怜悯！”
伯爵极其愚蠢地全然没把这番狠话放在心上；这反而加速了他快刀斩乱麻的决心。
“这小娘们越来越不知天高地厚了，”他暗自寻思，“哪天我要是爽约没去赴会，她指不定真敢一头杀上瓦尔菲伊庄园，闹出一桩不可收拾的丑闻来。”
在贝尔塔的眼泪与苦苦哀求的催化下，他终于横下一条心，鼓起毕生的勇气赶赴科尔贝伊，决意彻底同一刀两断。在挑明意图之际他做足了铺垫防范，搬出了自己所能搜肠刮肚想到的所有冠冕堂皇的托辞：
“咱们往后必须格外小心提防了，珍妮，你知晓的，”他说道，“咱们得暂时彻底断绝往来。

===Original===

I am ruined, you know, and the only thing that can save me is marriage.”
Hector had prepared himself for an explosion of fury, piercing cries, hysterics, fainting-fits. To his great surprise, Jenny did not answer a word. She became as white as her collar, her ruddy lips blanched, her eyes stared.
“So,” said she, with her teeth tightly shut to contain herself, “so you are going to get married?”
“Alas, I must,” he answered with a hypocritical sigh. “You know that lately I have only been able to get money for you by borrowing from my friend; his purse will not be at my service forever.”
Jenny took Hector by the hand, and led him to the window. There, looking intently at him, as if her gaze could frighten the truth out of him, she said, slowly:
“It is really true, is it, that you are going to leave me to get married?”
Hector disengaged one of his hands, and placed it on his heart.
“I swear it on my honor,” said he.
“I ought to believe you, then.”
Jenny returned to the middle of the room. Standing erect before the mirror, she put on her hat, quietly disposing its ribbons as if nothing had occurred. When she was ready to go, she went up to Trémorel. “For the last time,” said she, in a tone which she forced to be firm, and which belied her tearful, glistening eyes. “For the last time, Hector, are we really to part?”
“We must.”
Jenny made a gesture which Trémorel did not see; her face had a malicious expression; her lips parted to utter some sarcastic response; but she recovered herself almost immediately.
“I am going, Hector,” said she, after a moment’s reflection; “If you are really leaving me to get married, you shall never hear of me again.”
“Why, Jenny, I hope I shall still remain your friend.”
“Well, only if you abandon me for another reason, remember what I tell you; you will be a dead man, and she, a lost woman.”
She opened the door; he tried to take her hand; she repulsed him.
“Adieu!”
Hector ran to the window to assure himself of her departure. She was ascending the avenue leading to the station.
“Well, that’s over,” thought he, with a sigh of relief. “Jenny was a good girl.”

===Chinese===

我如今彻底破产了，你也是知道的，全天下唯一能将我从深渊中拯救出来的生路，唯有联姻完婚。”
埃克托早已做好了迎接一场雷霆震怒的万全准备——尖声嘶嚎、撒泼发疯、歇斯底里、昏厥倒地。然而大出他意料的是，珍妮竟连半个字都没吐露。她的面庞刹那间变得同衣领一般惨白无血，原本红润的双唇褪尽了血色，一双眼珠子直勾勾地凸瞪着。
“这么说来，”她死死咬紧牙关强压着体内翻江倒海的狂澜，从齿缝间挤出话来，“这么说来，你当真是要去迎娶别人了？”
“唉，造化弄人，我别无选择，”他长吁短叹、摆出一副虚伪透顶的悲切模样答道，“你自知晓，最近这些日子我接济你的银两，全是我低声下气从好友手里拆借来的；他的钱袋子绝不可能一辈子任由我索取无度。”
珍妮一把攥住埃克托的手腕，将他径直拽到了窗前。在那儿，她将目光如利刃般死死钉在他脸上，仿佛要用这审视的烈火逼出他灵魂深处的所有真话，一字一顿、极其缓慢地吐露道：
“这当真是大实话吗？你果真是为了迎娶名门闺秀才要抛弃我？”
埃克托抽出一只手，郑重其事地覆在自己的心口上。
“我向我的名誉发誓，”他信誓旦旦道。
“既然如此，那我便信你一次。”
珍妮款款折步走回了厅房正中央。她挺直腰板伫立在穿衣镜前，戴上了自己的小礼帽，从容不迫地整理着帽带，神情自若得仿佛什么变故都不曾发生过一样。收拾停当准备拔腿之际，她径直迈步走到特雷莫雷尔身前。“最后一次了，”她强压着嗓音力求坚决地说道，可那泛红含泪的泪眼却无情出卖了她内心的碎裂，“最后一次了，埃克托，咱们俩当真就此恩断义绝了吗？”
“非如此不可。”
珍妮下意识地做了一个手势，特雷莫雷尔并未能捕捉到；她整张面庞上掠过了一抹狠辣恶毒的神采；双唇微启似欲吐露某句尖酸刻薄的嘲讽；可她转瞬间便将情绪彻底平复了下去。
“我这就走，埃克托，”片刻沉吟之后她缓缓说道，“假使你当真是为了联姻娶妻才抛弃我，你此生再不会听到关于我的半点风声。”
“嗨，珍妮，我巴望着咱们往后依旧能做挚友。”
“哼，但倘若你是为了别的由头抛弃我，你给我牢牢记住我今天撂下的这句话：你必将横尸街头，而那个狐媚子，必将万劫不复！”
她猛地拉开房门；他试图上前握住她的柔荑，却被她一把粗暴地推开了。
“永别了！”
埃克托慌忙快步冲到窗前，目送她的离去以确保她当真彻底走远。只见她正快步穿过通往火车站的林荫大道。
“呼，总算是彻底了结了，”他如释重负地长舒了一口气，“平心而论，珍妮当真算是个好姑娘。”

## XVI / 第十六章
===Original===

The count told half a truth when he spoke to Jenny of his marriage. Sauvresy and he had discussed the subject, and if the matter was not as ripe as he had represented, there was at least some prospect of such an event. Sauvresy had proposed it in his anxiety to complete his work of restoring Hector to fortune and society.
One evening, about a month before the events just narrated, he had led Hector into the library, saying:
“Give me your ear for a quarter of an hour, and don’t answer me hastily. What I am going to propose to you deserves serious reflection.”
“Well, I can be serious when it is necessary.”
“Let’s begin with your debts. Their payment is not yet completed, but enough has been done to enable us to foresee the end. It is certain that you will have, after all debts are paid, from three to four hundred thousand francs.”
Hector had never, in his wildest hopes, expected such success.
“Why, I’m going to be rich,” exclaimed he joyously.
“No, not rich, but quite above want. There is, too, a mode in which you can regain your lost position.”
“A mode? what?”
Sauvresy paused a moment, and looked steadily at his friend.
“You must marry,” said he at last.
This seemed to surprise Hector, but not disagreeably.
“I, marry? It’s easier to give that advice than to follow it.”
“Pardon me﻿—you ought to know that I do not speak rashly. What would you say to a young girl of good family, pretty, well brought up, so charming that, excepting my own wife, I know of no one more attractive, and who would bring with her a dowry of a million?”
“Ah, my friend, I should say that I adore her! And do you know such an angel?”
“Yes, and you too, for the angel is Mademoiselle Laurence Courtois.”
Hector’s radiant face overclouded at this name, and he made a discouraged gesture.
“Never,” said he. “That stiff and obstinate old merchant, Monsieur Courtois, would never consent to give his daughter to a man who has been fool enough to waste his fortune.”
Sauvresy shrugged his shoulders.
“Now, there’s what it is to have eyes, and not see. Know that this Courtois, whom you think so obstinate, is really the most romantic of men, and an ambitious old fellow to boot. It would seem to him a grand good speculation to give his daughter to the Count Hector de Trémorel, cousin of the Duke of Samblemeuse, the relative of the Commarins, even though you hadn’t a sou.

===Chinese===

伯爵向珍妮提及自己即将完婚时，倒也并非全盘撒谎。索夫勒齐与他确实切磋商讨过这桩亲事，而且即便事情并未进展到他吹嘘的那般板上钉钉，至少也展现出了极大的成事眉目。索夫勒齐之所以热心张罗做媒，全因他一心渴望帮人帮到底，助埃克托彻底重振家业、重返上流社会。
就在上述变故发生前约摸一个月的一个傍晚，他将埃克托引进了藏书室，郑重其事地说道：
“腾出一刻钟来听我细说，且莫急着回绝我。我接下来要向你提议的大事，值得你深思熟虑。”
“行啊，正经关头我向来不含糊。”
“咱们先从你的债务说起。虽说外债尚未彻底清偿完毕，但眼下料理的进度已足够让咱们预见胜局。有一点是确凿无疑的：待到所有债务悉数结清之后，你名下至少还能保住300000到400000法郎的现钱。”
埃克托哪怕在最荒诞的狂想中，也断未料到竟能取得如此辉煌的大胜。
“天哪，这么说我又成富翁了，”他喜出望外地失声嚷道。
“算不上大富大贵，但绝对足以摆脱衣食之忧了。不仅如此，眼下还有一条捷径能让你彻底重夺昔日风光的尊贵地位。”
“捷径？什么捷径？”
索夫勒齐停顿了片刻，目光沉静笃定地注视着老友。
“你必须成家立业，迎娶名门之女，”良久他才缓缓吐露道。
这番提议令埃克托颇感错愕，却丝毫未引起他的反感。
“我？结婚？出主意容易，践行起来可就难如登天了。”
“非也——你自该知晓我向来从不信口开河。假使有一位名门闺秀，不仅容貌娇美、教养极佳，其楚楚动人之姿除了我自个儿的发妻之外，我平生所见再无任何人能出其右，并且过门时还能带来整整100万法郎的巨额嫁妆，你意下如何？”
“啊，我的朋友，那我定要将她奉若神明去顶礼膜拜！难道你当真认得这等天仙？”
“自然认得，而且你也认得，因为这位天仙正是洛朗斯·库尔图瓦小姐。”
一听到这个芳名，埃克托方才还容光焕发的面庞刹那间阴云密布，他颓丧地摆了摆手。
“绝无可能，”他叹道，“库尔图瓦先生是个顽固不化的老古董暴发商贾，他无论如何也绝不会把自个儿的掌上明珠下嫁给一个败光了万贯家财的浪荡蠢货。”
索夫勒齐耸了耸肩膀。
“瞧瞧，这就叫有眼无珠不识真佛。你有所不知，这位被你当成死顽固的库尔图瓦老汉，骨子里实则是全天下最异想天开的浪漫主义者，而且还是个野心勃勃、一心钻营的老财主。哪怕你身上连半个苏都不剩，能把亲闺女许配给桑布勒默斯公爵的表亲、科马兰家族的远亲——特雷莫雷尔伯爵埃克托先生，在他看来简直是一桩光宗耀祖、稳赚不赔的泼天大买卖。

===Original===

What wouldn’t he give to have the delicious pleasure of saying, Monsieur the Count, my son-in-law; or my daughter, Madame the Countess Hector! And you aren’t ruined, you know, you are going to have an income of twenty thousand francs, and perhaps enough more to raise your capital to a million.”
Hector was silent. He had thought his life ended, and now, all of a sudden, a splendid perspective unrolled itself before him. He might then rid himself of the patronizing protection of his friend; he would be free, rich, would have a better wife, as he thought, than Bertha; his house would outshine Sauvresy’s. The thought of Bertha crossed his mind, and it occurred to him that he might thus escape a lover who although beautiful and loving was proud and bold, and whose domineering temper began to be burdensome to him.
“I may say,” said he, seriously to his friend, “that I have always thought Monsieur Courtois an excellent and honorable man, and Mademoiselle Laurence seems to me so accomplished a young lady, that a man might be happy in marrying her even without a dowry.”
“So much the better, my dear Hector, so much the better. But you know, the first thing is to engage Laurence’s affections; her father adores her, and would not, I am sure, give her to a man whom she herself had not chosen.”
“Don’t disturb yourself,” answered Hector, with a gesture of triumph, “she will love me.”
The next day he took occasion to encounter M. Courtois, who invited him to dinner. The count employed all his practised seductions on Laurence, which were so brilliant and able that they were well fitted to surprise and dazzle a young girl. It was not long before the count was the hero of the mayor’s household. Nothing formal had been said, nor any direct allusion or overture made; yet M. Courtois was sure that Hector would some day ask his daughter’s hand, and that he should freely answer, “yes;” while he thought it certain that Laurence would not say “no.”
Bertha suspected nothing; she was now very much worried about Jenny, and saw nothing else. Sauvresy, after spending an evening with the count at the mayor’s, during which Hector had not once quitted the whist-table, decided to speak to his wife of the proposed marriage, which he thought would give her an agreeable surprise. At his first words, she grew pale.

===Chinese===

单是为了能享受到人前风光自称‘我的乘龙快婿伯爵先生’、或者‘小女埃克托伯爵夫人’的那份飘飘欲仙的虚荣快感，他还有什么代价舍不得掏？更何况你如今根本没有彻底倾家荡产，你的地租进项每年足有20000法郎，稍加运筹便能将本钱壮大到100万法郎。”
埃克托陷入了沉默。他原以为自己的一生早已走到了穷途末路，而如今，一道璀璨夺目的壮丽图景竟猝不及防地在他眼前徐徐展开。如此一来，他便能彻底挣脱挚友那高高在上的恩庇枷锁；他将重获自由、重获万贯家财，还将拥有一位在他看来远比贝尔塔更贤良温婉的发妻；他的门楣宅第将比索夫勒齐的瓦尔菲伊庄园更加风光显赫。贝尔塔的倩影在他脑海中一闪而过，他蓦地意识到，这恰恰是摆脱这位情妇的天赐良机——贝尔塔虽美艳多情，性情却过于狂傲决绝，其专横跋扈的控制欲已然让他日益感到窒息沉重。
“平心而论，”他郑重其事地对好友说道，“我向来认定库尔图瓦先生是一位端方正直的长者，而洛朗斯小姐在我眼中更是不可多得的贤淑闺秀，哪怕分文嫁妆不带，能迎娶此等佳人也堪称人生至福。”
“那可太好不过了，我亲爱的埃克托，再好不过了！但你千万记住，首要之务是俘获洛朗斯的芳心；她父亲对她宠爱备至，我敢打包票，倘若不是闺女亲手挑中的如意郎君，老汉绝不肯强行婚配。”
“放一百个心吧，”埃克托带着胜券在握的得意姿态应道，“她定会爱上我的。”
翌日他特意创造机会在街头同库尔图瓦先生偶遇，老汉随即盛情邀约他共进晚餐。伯爵在洛朗斯面前使出了浑身解数，将平日欢场上磨砺出的那套风流倜傥、妙趣横生的求爱手段施展得淋漓尽致，这套华彩手段足以让任何涉世未深的年轻姑娘目眩神迷、芳心大乱。不出多久，伯爵便成了镇长府上的座上宾与大红人。尽管双方尚未正式挑明，亦未曾做出任何明确的提亲暗示，但库尔图瓦先生心里已笃定埃克托迟早会登门求亲，而自己定会欣然应允；同时老汉也深信，洛朗斯绝不可能说出半个“不”字。
而贝尔塔起初对此全无察觉；她当时正为珍妮的私会抓心挠肝，根本无暇旁顾。某天傍晚索夫勒齐陪同伯爵赴镇长府上做客折返后——席间埃克托整晚黏在惠斯特纸牌桌旁未曾挪步——索夫勒齐决意将这桩精心筹备的锦绣良缘告知发妻，料想这必定能给妻子带来一份天大的惊喜。然而刚吐露了三言两语，贝尔塔的面色便刷地惨白如纸。

===Original===

Her emotion was so great that, seeing she would betray herself, she hastily retired to her boudoir. Sauvresy, quietly seated in one of the bedroom armchairs, continued to expatiate on the advantages of such a marriage﻿—raising his voice, so that Bertha might hear him in the neighboring room.
“Do you know,” said he, “that our friend has an income of sixty thousand crowns? We’ll find an estate for him near by, and then we shall see him and his wife every day. They will be very pleasant society for us in the autumn months. Hector is a fine fellow, and you’ve often told me how charming Laurence is.”
Bertha did not reply. This unexpected blow was so terrible that she could not think clearly, and her brain whirled.
“You don’t say anything,” pursued Sauvresy. “Don’t you approve of my project? I thought you’d be enchanted with it.”
