She saw that if she were silent any longer, her husband would go in and find her sunk upon a chair, and would guess all. She made an effort and said, in a strangled voice, without attaching any sense to her words:
“Yes, yes; it is a capital idea.”
“How you say that! Do you see any objections?”
She was trying to find some objection, but could not.
“I have a little fear of Laurence’s future,” said she at last.
“Bah! Why?”
“I only say what I’ve heard you say. You told me that Monsieur Trémorel has been a libertine, a gambler, a prodigal﻿—”
“All the more reason for trusting him. His past follies guarantee his future prudence. He has received a lesson which he will not forget. Besides, he will love his wife.”
“How do you know?”
“Parbleu, he loves her already.”
“Who told you so?”
“Himself.”
And Sauvresy began to laugh about Hector’s passion, which he said was becoming quite pastoral.
“Would you believe,” said he, laughing, “that he thinks our worthy Courtois a man of wit? Ah, what spectacles these lovers look through! He spends two or three hours every day with the mayor. What do you suppose he does there?”
Bertha, by great effort, succeeded in dissembling her grief; she reappeared with a smiling face. She went and came, apparently calm, though suffering the bitterest anguish a woman can endure. And she could not run to Hector, and ask him if it were true!
For Sauvresy must be deceiving her. Why? She knew not. No matter. She felt her hatred of him increasing to disgust; for she excused and pardoned her lover, and she blamed her husband alone.

===Chinese===

她胸中翻涌的心潮澎湃是如此骇人，眼见自己即将当场失态败露，她慌忙抽身疾步躲进了闺房。索夫勒齐悠闲地斜倚在卧室的安乐椅上，依旧滔滔不绝地铺陈着这桩联姻的无尽益处——他特意拔高了嗓门，好让隔壁闺房里的贝尔塔听得真切：
“你可知晓，”他高声嚷道，“咱们这位好友往后每年将坐拥60000克朗的丰厚进项！咱们在近旁替他物色一处体面庄园，到时候咱们两家每天都能走动相见。一到金秋时节，有他们夫妇相伴，咱们的日子该有多快活！埃克托是个万里挑一的好小伙，而你也曾屡屡同我夸赞洛朗斯是何等温婉动人。”
贝尔塔一声不吭。这记从天而降的晴天霹雳是如此残酷致命，打得她脑海中一片空白，整颗头颅都在剧烈眩晕。
“你怎么不吭声啊，”索夫勒齐接着追问道，“难道你不赞同我这桩谋划？我还以为你定会高兴得手舞足蹈呢。”
贝尔塔意识到，自己若是再僵持沉默下去，丈夫定会推门进来撞见自己瘫软在椅子上的狼狈丑态，从而将底细猜个八九不离十。她拼尽残存的力气，用近乎窒息的嘶哑嗓音挤出话来，连自己吐露了些什么都浑然不知：
“赞成，赞成；当真是个绝妙的主意。”
“瞧你这话说的，腔调怎么这般古怪！难道你瞧出了什么不妥？”
她极力搜肠刮肚试图找寻推托阻拦的由头，可脑海中一片干涸。
“我……我只是有些担忧洛朗斯往后的终身前程，”良久她才挤出一句。
“嗨！何出此言？”
“我不过是拾人牙慧罢了。你从前亲口告知过我，说特雷莫雷尔先生早年放浪形骸、是个嗜赌如命的浪荡纨绔——”
“这恰恰正是对他深信不疑的理由！以往的荒唐教训，是他日后谨言慎行的最好保证。他已经狠狠栽过大跟头，绝不会重蹈覆辙。更何况，他定会全心全意珍爱自己的发妻。”
“你又从何得知？”
“见鬼，他眼下就已经爱上她了！”
“是谁对你说的？”
“是他自个儿亲口招认的。”
说着，索夫勒齐忍不住哈哈大笑起来，打趣着埃克托如今这股宛如田园牧歌般纯情的求爱狂热。
“你敢信吗，”他忍俊不禁道，“他如今竟把咱们那位老实巴交的库尔图瓦老汉捧成了满腹经纶的当世奇才！哎，情场上的男女戴的都是些什么怪眼光啊！他每天雷打不动要在镇长府上泡上两三个小时。你想想，他泡在那儿究竟能干些什么？”
贝尔塔拼尽毕生的定力，终于将内心的撕心裂肺死死强压了下去；她重新迈出闺房时，脸上已然挂上了一副笑靥如花的从容面具。她若无其事地在厅房里穿梭忙碌，表面波澜不惊，胸中却承受着一个女人所能饱尝的最苦涩绝望的酷刑。而她偏偏无法立刻飞奔到埃克托身前，当面揪住他逼问这究竟是不是真的！
索夫勒齐必定是在信口雌黄骗她。可为何要骗？她摸不清底细。但这全然不重要了。她心头对丈夫的厌恶与嫌弃刹那间激增到了令人作呕的地步；只因她毫无原则地原谅宽恕了自己的情郎，将所有的滔天怨恨，一股脑全扣在了无辜丈夫的头上。

===Original===

Whose idea was this marriage? His. Who had awakened Hector’s hopes, and encouraged them? He, always he. While he had been harmless, she had been able to pardon him for having married her; she had compelled herself to bear him, to feign a love quite foreign to her heart. But now he became hateful; should she submit to his interference in a matter which was life or death to her?
She did not close her eyes all night; she had one of those horrible nights in which crimes are conceived. She did not find herself alone with Hector until after breakfast the next day, in the billiard-hall.
“Is it true?” she asked.
The expression of her face was so menacing that he quailed before it. He stammered:
“True﻿—what?”
“Your marriage.”
He was silent at first, asking himself whether he should tell the truth or equivocate. At last, irritated by Bertha’s imperious tone, he replied:
“Yes.”
She was thunderstruck at this response. Till then, she had a glimmer of hope. She thought that he would at least try to reassure her, to deceive her. There are times when a falsehood is the highest homage. But no﻿—he avowed it. She was speechless; words failed her.
Trémorel began to tell her the motives which prompted his conduct. He could not live forever at Valfeuillu. What could he, with his habits and tastes, do with a few thousand crowns a year? He was thirty; he must, now or never, think of the future. M. Courtois would give his daughter a million, and at his death there would be a great deal more. Should he let this chance slip? He cared little for Laurence, it was the dowry he wanted. He took no pains to conceal his meanness; he rather gloried in it, speaking of the marriage as simply a bargain, in which he gave his name and title in exchange for riches. Bertha stopped him with a look full of contempt.
“Spare yourself,” said she. “You love Laurence.”
He would have protested; he really disliked her.
“Enough,” resumed Bertha. “Another woman would have reproached you; I simply tell you that this marriage shall not be; I do not wish it. Believe me, give it up frankly, don’t force me to act.”
She retired, shutting the door violently; Hector was furious.
“How she treats me!” said he to himself. “Just as a queen would speak to a serf. Ah, she don’t want me to marry Laurence!” His coolness returned, and with it serious reflections.

===Chinese===

这场联姻究竟是谁出的馊主意？是索夫勒齐！究竟是谁点燃了埃克托心头的非分之想、又推波助澜鼓励他去求爱？是他，永远是他！只要丈夫此前尚算人畜无害，她原本还能勉为其难容忍他，甚至在他面前伪装出一副与其内心毫无瓜葛的脉脉深情。可如今这个男人却变得面目可憎；难道在关乎自己生死存亡的终身大事上，她竟要任由他肆意横加干涉吗？
那一整夜她未曾合眼；她度过了一个罪恶在黑暗中悄然滋生的可怕长夜。直到翌日早午餐之后，她才终于在台球厅里觅得了同埃克托单独相处的机会。
“这全都是真的吗？”她开门见山逼问道。
她脸上的凶相是如此狰狞可怖，以至于埃克托被吓得当场瑟缩退却。他结结巴巴地支吾道：
“什么……什么真的？”
“你完婚的事。”
他起初陷入了沉默，暗自权衡究竟是该全盘招认还是闪烁其词。最终，在贝尔塔那副居高临下的颐指气使激怒之下，他冷冷吐露道：
“是真的。”
这个回答犹如五雷轰顶，将她当场震碎。直到这一刻之前，她心头尚残存着最后一丝侥幸的希望。她原以为埃克托至少会竭力抚慰她、甚至巧言令色欺瞒她。在某些时刻，谎言反倒是对一个女人最崇高的敬意。可他没有——他竟直截了当地招认了。她目瞪口呆，喉咙里连半个字都吐不出来。
特雷莫雷尔开始滔滔不绝地为自己的行止辩白脱罪。他不可能一辈子寄人篱下赖在瓦尔菲伊庄园。依照他的做派与排场，每年几千克朗的微薄进项能顶什么用？他如今已是年过30的人了；机不可失时不再来，他必须为自己的后半生未雨绸缪。库尔图瓦先生会陪送闺女整整100万法郎的嫁妆，老汉百年之后留下的遗产更是数不胜数。难道他要眼睁睁放过这等改天换命的良机？他宣称自己根本不在乎洛朗斯，他图谋的唯独是那笔泼天嫁妆。他甚至懒得遮掩自己的下作卑劣，反倒以此为荣，大言不惭地将婚姻吹嘘成一场再公平不过的交易——用自己的名门头衔去置换对方的万贯家私。贝尔塔用一道饱含嫌弃与鄙夷的寒光硬生生掐断了他的大放厥词。
“省省你的唾沫吧，”她啐道，“你分明是真心爱上了洛朗斯。”
他刚欲开口抗辩否认；他是真的厌恶她这副嘴脸。
“够了，”贝尔塔冷冷喝断道，“换做寻常妇人早就对你哭天抢地数落不休了；可我只把话撂在这儿：这门亲事休想办成；我不准你结！信我一句，老老实实给我断了这个念想，别逼我动手。”
说完她猛地摔门而去，留下埃克托在原地暴跳如雷。
“她竟敢这般待我！”他暗自咬牙切齿，“简直就像个女王在训斥底下的农奴奴隶。哈，她竟敢不准我迎娶洛朗斯！”然而随着理智逐渐回笼，严酷的现实思量重新占据了上风。

===Original===

If he insisted on marrying, would not Bertha carry out her threats? Evidently; for he knew well that she was one of those women who shrink from nothing, whom no consideration could arrest. He guessed what she would do, from what she had said in a quarrel with him about Jenny. She had told him, “I will confess everything to Sauvresy, and we will be the more bound together by shame than by all the ceremonies of the church.”
This was surely the mode she would adopt to break a marriage which was so hateful to her; and Trémorel trembled at the idea of Sauvresy knowing all.
“What would he do,” thought he, “if Bertha told him? He would kill me offhand﻿—that’s what I would do in his place. Suppose he didn’t; I should have to fight a duel with him, and if I killed him, quit the country. Whatever would happen, my marriage is irrevocably broken, and Bertha seems to be on my hands for all time.”
He saw no possible way out of the horrible situation in which he had put himself.
“I must wait,” thought he.
And he waited, going secretly to the mayor’s, for he really loved Laurence. He waited, devoured by anxiety, struggling between Sauvresy’s urgency and Bertha’s threats. How he detested this woman who held him, whose will weighed so heavily on him! Nothing could curb her ferocious obstinacy. She had one fixed idea. He had thought to conciliate her by dismissing Jenny. It was a mistake. When he said to her:
“Bertha, I shall never see Jenny again.”
She answered, ironically:
“Mademoiselle Courtois will be very grateful to you!”
That evening, while Sauvresy was crossing the courtyard, he saw a beggar at the gate, making signs to him.
“What do you want, my good man?”
The beggar looked around to see that no one was listening.
“I have brought you a note,” said he, rapidly, and in a low tone. “I was told to give it, only to you, and to ask you to read it when you are alone.”
He mysteriously slipped a note, carefully sealed, into Sauvresy’s hand.
“It comes from pretty girl,” added he, winking.
Sauvresy, turning his back to the house, opened it and read:

> “Sir﻿—You will do a great favor to a poor and unhappy girl, if you will come tomorrow to the Belle Image, at Corbeil, where you will be awaited all day.
> “Your humble servant,
> “Jenny F﻿—﻿—.”

There was also a postscript.

> “Please, sir, don’t say a word of this to the Count de Trémorel.”

===Chinese===

倘若他执意完婚，贝尔塔当真会将她的威胁付诸实施吗？显而易见；只因他心知肚明，这个女人属于那种丧心病狂、什么勾当都豁得出来的亡命徒，世俗间的一切利害皆阻挡不了她的决绝。回想起先前就珍妮之事大吵大闹时贝尔塔脱口而出的狠话，他便能猜透她的手段。她曾恶狠狠地撂下话：“我会把一切私情全盘向索夫勒齐坦白，到那时，焊死咱们俩的将是无尽的奇耻大辱，这份锁链比教会所有的神圣婚仪还要牢不可破千百倍！”
毫无疑问，这必定是她用来彻底砸碎这桩令她恨之入骨的婚姻的雷霆手段；而一想到索夫勒齐洞悉全貌的惨烈下场，特雷莫雷尔便吓得浑身战栗。
“一旦贝尔塔全招了，”他暗自心惊胆战，“索夫勒齐会如何处置？他会当场拔枪将我崩毙——换做我是他，我也定会痛下杀手。就算他不杀我，我也必须同他拔剑决斗，一旦失手斩杀了他，我便只得沦为杀人逃犯亡命天涯。无论结局如何，这门亲事必将彻底化为泡影，而贝尔塔这块甩不掉的膏药，势必一辈子死死粘在我的手上。”
面对自己一手酿成的这出万劫不复的死局，他横看竖看也找寻不出半条脱身的活路。
“只能暂且按兵不动了，”他心中盘算道。
于是他只得表面上拖延僵持，暗地里则频频偷偷摸摸跑去镇长府上献殷勤，只因他确实不可救药地迷恋上了洛朗斯。他度日如年，饱受着焦灼的撕咬煎熬，在索夫勒齐的热切催促与贝尔塔的凶残恐吓之间左支右绌、苦苦挣扎。他是何等痛恨这个死死钳制着他的女人，这个以钢铁般的蛮横意志沉沉压在他头顶的恶妇！没有任何规劝能驯服她那凶顽死硬的固执。她整个人彻底被这个心魔死死攫住了。他原以为把珍妮扫地出门能稍稍安抚她的心绪。事实证明这纯属大错特错。当他对她表功道：
“贝尔塔，我此生再不会见珍妮半面了。”
她却冷嘲热讽地回敬道：
“那库尔图瓦小姐可真得对你感恩戴德呢！”
当天傍晚，正当索夫勒齐穿过庄园庭院之际，赫然瞧见栅栏大门外有一名乞丐正鬼鬼祟祟地朝他频频招手示意。
“你想要什么，我的好乡亲？”
乞丐警惕地四下张望了一番，确认隔墙无耳。
“我给您送了封信来，”那人压低了嗓门语速飞快地说道，“吩咐我的人千叮咛万嘱咐，务必亲手交到您一个人手里，还求您避开旁人独处时再拆阅。”
他神秘兮兮地将一封封缄得整整齐齐的字条，悄悄塞进了索夫勒齐的掌心。
“这可是位大美人托送的，”那汉子挤眉弄眼地补充了一句。
索夫勒齐背对着庄园主楼的大门，拆开字条审阅起来：

> “老爷尊前：倘若您明日肯屈尊驾临科尔贝伊的靓影旅馆，您便是挽救了一位命苦绝望的可怜姑娘；小女子将整日恭候您的大驾。
> “您卑微的仆人，
> “珍妮·凡……”

字条下端还附有一行附言：

> “千万求求老爷，对此事切莫向特雷莫雷尔伯爵透露半字。”

===Original===

“Ah ha,” thought Sauvresy, “there’s some trouble about Hector, that’s bad for the marriage.”
“I was told, sir,” said the beggar, “there would be an answer.”
“Say that I will come,” answered Sauvresy, throwing him a franc piece.

===Chinese===

“哎呀呀，”索夫勒齐心中暗自琢磨，“埃克托这小子看来又惹下风流债了，这可对成亲不利。”
“那位姑娘交代了，老爷，”乞丐讨好地说道，“问有没有回话。”
“告知她我明儿准时赴约，”索夫勒齐答道，顺手朝他抛过去一枚1法郎硬币。

## XVII / 第十七章
===Original===

The next day was cold and damp. A fog, so thick that one could not discern objects ten steps off, hung over the earth. Sauvresy, after breakfast, took his gun and whistled to his dogs.
“I’m going to take a turn in Mauprévoir wood,” said he.
“A queer idea,” remarked Hector, “for you won’t see the end of your gun-barrel in the woods.”
“No matter, if I see some pheasants.”
This was only a pretext, for Sauvresy, on leaving Valfeuillu, took the direct road to Corbeil, and half an hour later, faithful to his promise, he entered the Belle Image tavern.
Jenny was waiting for him in the large room which had always been reserved for her since she became a regular customer of the house. Her eyes were red with recent tears; she was very pale, and her marble color showed that she had not slept. Her breakfast lay untouched on the table near the fireplace, where a bright fire was burning. When Sauvresy came in, she rose to meet him, and took him by the hand with a friendly motion.
“Thank you for coming,” said she. “Ah, you are very good.”
Jenny was only a girl, and Sauvresy detested girls; but her grief was so sincere and seemed so deep, that he was touched.
“You are suffering, Madame?” asked he.
“Oh, yes, very much.”
Her tears choked her, and she concealed her face in her handkerchief.
“I guessed right,” thought Sauvresy. “Hector has deserted her. Now I must smooth the wound, and yet make future meetings between them impossible.”
He took the weeping Jenny’s hand, and softly pulled away the handkerchief.
“Have courage,” said he.
She lifted her tearful eyes to him, and said:
“You know, then?”
“I know nothing, for, as you asked me, I have said nothing to Trémorel; but I can imagine what the trouble is.”
“He will not see me any more,” murmured Jenny. “He has deserted me.”
Sauvresy summoned up all his eloquence. The moment to be persuasive and paternal had come. He drew a chair up to Jenny’s, and sat down.
“Come, my child,” pursued he, “be resigned. People are not always young, you know. A time comes when the voice of reason must be heard. Hector does not desert you, but he sees the necessity of assuring his future, and placing his life on a domestic foundation; he feels the need of a home.”
Jenny stopped crying. Nature took the upper hand, and her tears were dried by the fire of anger which took possession of her.

===Chinese===

翌日清晨阴冷潮湿。大地上弥漫着浓重大雾，浓烈得甚至十步开外便辨不清人影。用过早饭后，索夫勒齐拎起猎枪，朝猎犬吹了一声口哨。
“我打算去莫普雷瓦尔密林转上一圈，”他说道。
“这主意可真够奇特，”埃克托打趣道，“进了树林子，你恐怕连自己的猎枪枪管都瞧不见呢。”
“无妨，只要能撞见几只野鸡就成。”
这不过是个托辞，索夫勒齐迈出瓦尔菲伊庄园后，便径直踏上了通往科尔贝伊的近道官道，半个小时后，他信守诺言迈入了靓影旅馆。
珍妮一直在那间自打她成为常客起便专为她保留的宽敞上房里恭候着。她的双眼因方才痛哭过而红肿不堪；面色惨白如大理石，显露出整夜未合眼的憔悴。壁炉里烈火熊熊燃烧，靠近炉火的桌案上，摆着一口未动的早点。一见索夫勒齐推门而入，她霍然起身相迎，满怀感激与亲昵地一把拉住了他的手。
“多谢您肯大驾光临，”她说道，“啊，您当真是个大善人。”
珍妮不过是个风尘女子，索夫勒齐平生向来厌恶轻浮的下等女人；然而眼前这姑娘的悲恸是如此真切、如此痛彻心扉，以至于他心头也不由得为之一软。
“您饱受折磨了，夫人？”他温言询问道。
“呜呜，是的，痛苦极了。”
泪水瞬间堵住了她的喉咙，她用手帕死死捂住面庞泣不成声。
“我果然料中了，”索夫勒齐暗自寻思，“埃克托果然狠心抛弃了她。眼下我必须既抚平她的创伤，又彻底断绝他们日后再相会的念想。”
他握住哭泣中珍妮的手，轻轻将手帕从她脸上拉开。
“坚强起来，”他柔声劝慰道。
她抬起泪眼汪汪的眸子凝视着他，抽泣道：
“这么说，您全都知晓了？”
“我什么都不知晓，依着你的嘱托，我对特雷莫雷尔只字未提；但我完全能猜透症结所在。”
“他再也不愿见我了，”珍妮喃喃哭诉道，“他彻底抛弃了我。”
索夫勒齐调动起毕生的口才。此刻正是施展循循善诱、父兄般开导规劝的绝佳时机。他拉过一把椅子在珍妮身旁坐下。
“好啦，我的好孩子，”他接着劝解道，“认命吧。世人不可能永远长生不老、永远风华正茂。人生总有一个时刻，必须静心倾听理智的召唤。埃克托绝非故意弃你于不顾，而是他清醒地意识到必须给自个儿的后半生谋一条稳固的出路，将生活安顿在齐整的家室基石之上；他需要一个名正言顺的家。”
珍妮止住了啼哭。骨子里的天性猛然反弹，胸中骤然腾起的熊熊怒火，瞬间将眼角的泪水烤得干干净净。

===Original===

She rose, overturning her chair, and walked restlessly up and down the room.
“Do you believe that?” said she. “Do you believe that Hector troubles himself about his future? I see you don’t know his character. He dream of a home, or a family? He never has and never will think of anything but himself. If he had any heart, would he have gone to live with you as he has? He had two arms to gain his bread and mine. I was ashamed to ask money of him, knowing that what he gave me came from you.”
“But he is my friend, my dear child.”
“Would you do as he has done?”
Sauvresy did not know what to say; he was embarrassed by the logic of this daughter of the people, judging her lover rudely, but justly.
“Ah, I know him, I do,” continued Jenny, growing more excited as her mind reverted to the past. “He has only deceived me once﻿—the morning he came and told me he was going to kill himself. I was stupid enough to think him dead, and to cry about it. He, kill himself? Why, he’s too much of a coward to hurt himself! Yes, I love him, but I don’t esteem him. That’s our fate, you see, only to love the men we despise.”
Jenny talked loud, gesticulating, and every now and then thumping the table with her fist so that the bottles and glasses jingled. Sauvresy was somewhat fearful lest the hotel people should hear her; they knew him, and had seen him come in. He began to be sorry that he had come, and tried to calm the girl.
“But Hector is not deserting you,” repeated he. “He will assure you a good position.”
“Humph! I should laugh at such a thing! Have I any need of *him*? As long as I have ten fingers and good eyes, I shall not be at the mercy of any man. He made me change my name, and wanted to accustom me to luxury! And now there is neither a Miss Jenny, nor riches, but there is a Pélagie, who proposes to get her fifty sous a day, without much trouble.”
“No,” said Sauvresy, “you will not need﻿—”
“What? To work? But I like work; I am not a do-nothing. I will go back to my old life. I used to breakfast on a sou’s worth of biscuit and a sou’s worth of potatoes, and was well and happy. On Sundays, I dined at the Turk for thirty sous. I laughed more then in one afternoon, than in all the years I have known Trémorel.”
She no longer cried, nor was she angry; she was laughing. She was thinking of her old breakfasts, and her feasts at the Turk.
Sauvresy was stupefied.

===Chinese===

她霍然起身，撞翻了身后的椅子，焦躁不安地在房中来回踱步。
“您当真相信这套鬼话？”她尖声质问，“您当真相信埃克托会为他自个儿的未来深谋远虑？依我瞧，您根本摸不透他的心肠！他做梦渴望成家立业、生儿育女？他平生从前、往后，自始至终除了他自个儿之外，绝不可能记挂天地间的任何旁人！他倘若有一星半点良心，又怎会像如今这般厚颜无耻地赖在您府上吃闲饭？他长着一双手臂，原本能够自食其力赚出他自个儿同我的嚼谷口粮。我向他伸手要银子都觉得害臊脸红，只因我心知肚明他递给我的每一分钱，全是从您手里讨来的！”
“但他是我同甘共苦的手足挚友啊，我的好姑娘。”
“换做是您，您会像他这般行事吗？”
索夫勒齐一时语塞；这位出身民间的下层女子粗粝却字字诛心的逻辑剖析，直击埃克托的灵魂死穴，令他尴尬得无言以对。
“哈，我太了解他了，我比谁都懂！”珍妮一边回溯往事，神情越发亢奋激动，“他平生唯独欺瞒过我一次——就是那天大清早跑来告知我他要去崩碎脑袋。我当时简直蠢到家了，竟当真以为他会横尸街头，还为他哭得死去活来！他？去自裁？他比天底下任何叫花子都要怕死，连碰破点皮都吓得浑身哆嗦！不错，我深爱着他，但我打心眼里鄙夷他。这便是我们这等苦命女人的宿命：偏偏只会无可救药地爱上那些令我们作呕鄙薄的人渣！”
珍妮嗓门越来越高，手舞足蹈，时不时握紧粉拳狠狠砸在桌案上，震得杯盘碗盏丁当乱响。索夫勒齐暗暗心惊胆战，生怕旅馆上下人等听见这番泼辣吵嚷；店里的人全都认得他，方才也亲眼见他迈入房门。他心头不由得暗自懊悔自己此番孟浪赴约，极力安抚眼前的姑娘。
“可是埃克托并未弃你于不顾啊，”他反复劝解道，“他会为你安排一份体面安稳的生活。”
“哼！谁稀罕他的施舍，去他的吧！难道我少了他就会饿死不成？只要老娘手里还有十根手指头、一双招子没瞎，我就绝不受任何男人的窝囊气！他逼着我改名换姓，非要让我学着过锦衣玉食的日子！可如今既没有什么珍妮小姐，也没有什么荣华富贵了，站在这里的是佩拉吉，老娘轻轻松松每天就能赚上50个苏的硬通货！”
“不，”索夫勒齐劝道，“你本无须如此——”
“无须什么？自食其力出卖劳力？可我乐意干活，我可不是好吃懒做的寄生虫！我要重回我从前的穷日子。从前早饭花上1个苏买两块干饼、花上1个苏买两只洋芋，我吃得既饱足又快活。一到礼拜天，花上30个苏就能在土耳其人餐馆美美吃上一顿大餐。当年我在一下午里哈哈大笑的快活劲儿，比我结识特雷莫雷尔这几年加起来的乐子还要多得多！”
她不再痛哭，甚至也不再怒火中烧；她竟破涕为笑、吃吃地笑出了声。她满脑子回味着从前那些散碎清贫的早茶与下等小馆子里的牙祭。

===Original===

He had no idea of this Parisian nature, detestable and excellent, emotional to excess, nervous, full of transitions, which laughs and cries, caresses and strikes in the same minute, which a passing idea whirls a hundred leagues from the present moment.
“So,” said Jenny, more calmly, “I snap my fingers at Hector,”﻿—she had just said exactly the contrary, and had forgotten it﻿—“I don’t care for him, but I will not let him leave me in this way. It shan’t be said that he left me for another. I won’t have it.”
Jenny was one of those women who do not reason, but who feel; with whom it is folly to argue, for their fixed idea is impregnable to the most victorious arguments. Sauvresy asked himself why she had asked him to come, and said to himself that the part he had intended to play would be a difficult one. But he was patient.
“I see, my child,” he commenced, “that you haven’t understood or even heard me. I told you that Hector was intending to marry.”
“He!” answered Jenny, with an ironical gesture. “He get married.”
She reflected a moment, and added:
“If it were true, though﻿—”
“I tell you it is so.”
“No,” cried Jenny, “no, that can’t be possible. He loves another, I am sure of it, for I have proofs.”
Sauvresy smiled; this irritated her.
“What does this letter mean,” cried she warmly, “which I found in his pocket, six months ago? It isn’t signed to be sure, but it must have come from a woman.”
“A letter?”
“Yes, one that destroys all doubts. Perhaps you ask, why I did not speak to him about it? Ah, you see, I did not dare. I loved him. I was afraid if I said anything, and it was true he loved another, I should lose him. And so I resigned myself to humiliation, I concealed myself to weep, for I said to myself, he will come back to me. Poor fool!”
“Well, but what will you do?”
“Me? I don’t know﻿—anything. I didn’t say anything about the letter, but I kept it; it is my weapon﻿—I will make use of it. When I want to, I shall find out who she is, and then﻿—”
“You will compel Trémorel, who is kindly disposed toward you, to use violence.”
“He? What can he do to me? Why, I will follow him like his shadow﻿—I will cry out everywhere the name of this other. Will he have me put in St. Lazare prison? I will invent the most dreadful calumnies against him. They will not believe me at first; later, part of it will be believed.

===Chinese===

索夫勒齐对这种巴黎底层女郎的复杂天性毫无概念——既可恶又令人敬佩，情感过剩到了极点，神经质，喜怒无常瞬息万变，能在同一分钟里前一刻哭天抢地后一刻狂笑不止，上一秒温存爱抚下一秒拔刀相向，脑海中随便闪过的一个念头就能将她从当下抽离甩出百里开外。
“所以，”珍妮心平气和了几分说道，“我对他埃克托嗤之以鼻，”——她方才分明口口声声说了截然相反的话，转头便忘了个一干二净——“我压根不在乎他，但我绝不准他就这么一脚把我踹开。全天下休想议论说是他为了别的小妖精抛弃了我。老娘绝不答应！”
珍妮属于那种全凭本能直觉行事、绝不讲半点道理的女人；同这种人讲事实摆道理纯属对牛弹琴，因为她们死死认准的牛角尖，在世间最无可辩驳的钢铁逻辑面前也固若金汤。索夫勒齐暗自琢磨她究竟为何非要请他前来，深知自己打算扮演的体面和事佬角色必定棘手万分。但他依旧耐着性子。
“我瞧得出来，我的好孩子，”他重新开口道，“你根本没听懂、或者压根没听进我的话。我方才分明同你讲过，埃克托是打算名正言顺迎娶正头娘子。”
“他？”珍妮冷嘲热讽地做了个滑稽的手势，“他成亲完婚？”
她沉吟了片刻，随即补充道：
“哪怕这真是实话——”
“我千真万确告知你这就是事实。”
“不！”珍妮尖叫起来，“不，绝无可能！他心里分明有了别的小妖精，我敢打包票，因为我手里攥着铁证！”
索夫勒齐微微一笑；这抹笑容瞬间将她彻底激怒了。
“那这封信又算怎么回事！”她火冒三丈地嘶吼道，“这是我6个月前从他的大衣口袋里翻出来的！信上固然没有落款署名，但绝对出自一个女人的手笔！”
“一封信？”
“没错，一封足以粉碎一切疑窦的绝密信！您兴许要问，我当初为何不拿着信跟他当面对质？嗨，您自知晓，我当时哪敢啊！我深爱着他。我唯恐一旦挑明，证实他当真移情别恋，我便会彻底失去他。所以我只得忍气吞声打落牙齿往肚里吞，躲在暗处哭成泪人，只因我暗自欺骗自己：他玩腻了迟早会折回到我身边。可怜的蠢货！”
“那……那你如今究竟打算怎样？”
“我？我不知道——我什么都豁得出来！我从前没提过这封信，但我一直死死收存着；这是我的杀手锏——我定要让它见血！只要我愿意，我随时能查出那个贱人究竟是谁，到那时——”
“你这般蛮横纠缠，只会逼得原本对你心存愧疚关照的特雷莫雷尔对你诉诸暴力。”
“他？他能奈我何？哼，我要像冤魂索命一样如影随形尾随他——我要满世界扯着嗓门大喊那个贱人的名字！难道他敢串通官府把我关进圣拉扎尔监狱吗？我要编造出全天下最骇人听闻的恶毒谣言去毁他的名声！起初世人或许半信半疑；可日子久了，总会有人信以为真！

===Original===

I have nothing to fear﻿—I have no parents, no friends, nobody on earth who cares for me. That’s what it is to raise girls from the gutter. I have fallen so low that I defy him to push me lower. So, if you are his friend, sir, advise him to come back to me.”
Sauvresy was really alarmed; he saw clearly how real and earnest Jenny’s menaces were. There are persecutions against which the law is powerless. But he dissimulated his alarm under the blandest air he could assume.
“Hear me, my child,” said he. “If I give you my word of honor to tell you the truth, you’ll believe me, won’t you?”
She hesitated a moment, and said:
“Yes, you are honorable; I will believe you.”
“Then, I swear to you that Trémorel hopes to marry a young girl who is immensely rich, whose dowry will secure his future.”
“He tells you so; he wants you to believe it.”
“Why should he? Since he came to Valfeuillu, he could have had no other affair than this with you. He lives in my house, as if he were my brother, between my wife and myself, and I could tell you how he spends his time every hour of every day as well as what I do myself.”
Jenny opened her mouth to reply, but a sudden reflection froze the words on her lips. She remained silent and blushed violently, looking at Sauvresy with an indefinable expression. He did not observe this, being inspired by a restless though aimless curiosity. This proof, which Jenny talked about, worried him.
“Suppose,” said he, “you should show me this letter.”
She seemed to feel at these words an electric shock.
“To you?” she said, shuddering. “Never!”
If, when one is sleeping, the thunder rolls and the storm bursts, it often happens that the sleep is not troubled; then suddenly, at a certain moment, the imperceptible flutter of a passing insect’s wing awakens one.
Jenny’s shudder was like such a fluttering to Sauvresy. The sinister light of doubt struck on his soul. Now his confidence, his happiness, his repose, were gone forever. He rose with a flashing eye and trembling lips.
“Give me the letter,” said he, in an imperious tone. Jenny recoiled with terror. She tried to conceal her agitation, to smile, to turn the matter into a joke.
“Not today,” said she. “Another time; you are too curious.”
But Sauvresy’s anger was terrible; he became as purple as if he had had a stroke of apoplexy, and he repeated, in a choking voice:
“The letter, I demand the letter.”

===Chinese===

我光脚的不怕穿鞋的——我没有父母双亲，没有朋党故旧，全天下根本没人疼我怜我！这就是把姑娘从烂泥坑里拉扯出来的下场！我已经烂到骨子里了，我倒要瞧瞧他还能怎么把我往更深的泥坑里踩！所以，先生，假使您当真是他的挚友，奉劝他老老实实滚回到老娘身边来！”
索夫勒齐这回当真被吓到了；他清清楚楚领教了珍妮这些鱼死网破的诛心威胁有多么凶险暴烈。世间某些下作恶毒的纠缠迫害，往往连国法条律都束手无策。然而他竭力将内心的惊慌掩饰在一副最和颜悦色的宽厚神情之下。
“听我说，我的好姑娘，”他说道，“倘若我以我的名誉起誓向你坦陈实情，你总肯相信我了吧？”
珍妮犹豫了片刻，答道：
“成，您是个端方君子；我信您。”
“既然如此，那我向上帝发誓：特雷莫雷尔一心渴望迎娶的是一位家财万贯的名门闺秀，对方的巨额嫁妆将保全他的锦绣后半生。”
“那是他哄骗您的；他巴不得您也信这套鬼话。”
“他何苦骗我？自从他迁入瓦尔菲伊庄园以来，除了同你幽会之外，绝不可能在暗中沾染任何旁的女色。他同我同榻而居、宛如同胞手足，夹在我发妻同我二人之间，我甚至能将他每天每时每刻的行踪消遣，同我自个儿的底细一样倒背如流。”
珍妮刚欲张口反驳，冷不丁脑海中闪过的一道雷电，瞬间将唇边的话语冻结成了寒冰。她骤然陷入了死一般的沉寂，整张脸蛋涨得血红，用一种难以言喻、诡谲莫测的惊恐神情死死盯着索夫勒齐。索夫勒齐并未能洞察这道异样的神色，他纯粹被一股焦躁却盲目的好奇心所驱使。珍妮方才口口声声念叨的所谓铁证，在他心头激起了挥之不去的疑云。
“这样吧，”他开口道，“何不把那封信拿出来容我过目一番？”
这句轻描淡写的话，却让珍妮整个人如遭雷击。
“给您看？”她浑身剧烈战栗着失声尖叫道，“绝不！”
当一个人在沉睡之中，哪怕天边雷声轰鸣、暴雨倾盆，往往也惊扰不了甜美的梦乡；然而冷不丁在某个刹那，一只飞虫掠过耳畔极其微弱的振翅声，却能猛地将人从酣睡中惊醒。
珍妮方才这一记毛骨悚然的战栗，在索夫勒齐心中便恰似这声致命的振翅！怀疑的狰狞阴火瞬间劈入了他的灵魂深处。在这一刹那，他毕生的绝对信任、所有的幸福美满、心灵的宁静安泰，彻底灰飞烟灭。他霍然起身，双眸喷火，嘴唇剧烈颤抖。
“把信交给我！”他以一种不容置疑的严苛威权厉声喝道。珍妮吓得步步倒退。她极力掩饰内心的惊涛骇浪，勉强挤出一丝惨笑，试图将话题打个哈哈敷衍过去：
“今天可不行，”她强颜欢笑道，“改天吧；您也太爱打听闲事了。”
然而索夫勒齐的怒火已然暴烈如火山喷发；他的面庞涨得发紫，宛如骤发中风偏瘫，用近乎窒息的嘶吼一字一顿咆哮道：
“信！把信给我交出来！”

===Original===

“Impossible,” said Jenny. “Because,” she added, struck with an idea, “I haven’t got it here.”
“Where is it?”
“At my room, in Paris.”
“Come, then, let us go there.”
She saw that she was caught; and she could find no more excuses, quick-witted as she was. She might, however, easily have followed Sauvresy, put his suspicions to sleep with her gayety, and when once in the Paris streets, might have eluded him and fled. But she did not think of that. It occurred to her that she might have time to reach the door, open it, and rush downstairs. She started to do so. Sauvresy caught her at a bound, shut the door, and said, in a low, hoarse voice:
“Wretched girl! Do you wish me to strike you?”
He pushed her into a chair, returned to the door, double locked it, and put the keys in his pocket. “Now,” said he, returning to the girl, “the letter.”
Jenny had never been so terrified in her life. This man’s rage made her tremble; she saw that he was beside himself, that she was completely at his mercy; yet she still resisted him.
“You have hurt me very much,” said she, crying, “but I have done you no harm.”
He grasped her hands in his, and bending over her, repeated:
“For the last time, the letter; give it to me, or I will take it by force.”
It would have been folly to resist longer. “Leave me alone,” said she. “You shall have it.”
He released her, remaining, however, close by her side, while she searched in all her pockets. Her hair had been loosened in the struggle, her collar was torn, she was tired, her teeth chattered, but her eyes shone with a bold resolution.
“Wait﻿—here it is﻿—no. It’s odd﻿—I am sure I’ve got it though﻿—I had it a minute ago﻿—”
And, suddenly, with a rapid gesture, she put the letter, rolled into a ball, into her mouth, and tried to swallow it. But Sauvresy as quickly grasped her by the throat, and she was forced to disgorge it.
He had the letter at last. His hands trembled so that he could scarcely open it.
It was, indeed, Bertha’s writing.
Sauvresy tottered with a horrible sensation of dizziness; he could not see clearly; there was a red cloud before his eyes; his legs gave way under him, he staggered, and his hands stretched out for a support. Jenny, somewhat recovered, hastened to give him help; but her touch made him shudder, and he repulsed her. What had happened he could not tell. Ah, he wished to read this letter and could not.

===Chinese===

“交不出来，”珍妮咬牙道，“只因——”她脑中灵光一闪，慌忙编造道：“我压根没带在身上。”
“信在何处？”
“在巴黎我寓所的房里。”
“那好，咱们立刻动身去取。”
珍妮心知肚明自己彻底露馅了；饶是她向来机敏过人，此刻也搜刮不出半句托辞。照理说，她本可以顺水推舟尾随索夫勒齐前往巴黎，路上用欢声笑语打消他的戒备，待到迈入巴黎错综复杂的街巷之后，随时能拔腿隐入人海逃之夭夭。然而狂乱之中她全然没有料到这一层。她脑海中唯一的念头，是趁机猛扑到门前，拉开房门逃向楼下。她拔腿欲逃。索夫勒齐一个虎扑便将她拦腰截住，狠狠撞上了房门，用一种低沉、沙哑而令人胆寒的嗓音咆哮道：
“你这贱人！难道非逼我动手打你不可吗？”
他一把将她狠狠搡进椅子里，反身扑向房门，咔哒两声拧上了双重保险锁，将钥匙顺手揣进了衣兜。“现在，”他转过身逼向可怜的姑娘，“把信给我。”
珍妮平生从未品尝过如此毁天灭地的恐惧。这个男人浑身散发出的狂暴怒火令她抖如筛糠；她看清了他已然陷入了癫狂，深知自己已彻底沦为砧板上的鱼肉；然而她仍在拼死抗拒。
“你弄疼我了，”她抽抽搭搭地哭喊道，“我又没招你惹你！”
他铁钳般的大手死死钳制住她的双腕，俯下身去，咬牙切齿地重复道：
“最后一次警告你，把信交出来；交给我，否则我就动手硬抢了！”
再负隅顽抗纯属自取灭亡。“放开我，”她哭喊道，“我给你就是了！”
索夫勒齐松开了铁掌，却依然如恶煞般寸步不离紧贴在她身侧，注视着她在全身上下的衣兜里翻箱倒柜。在方才的厮打中，她的秀发早已披散散乱，衣领被生生撕裂，整个人气喘吁吁，上下牙关剧烈打战，然而那双黑眸深处却燃烧着孤注一掷的决绝凶光。
“等等——在这儿——不对。真见鬼——我分明带在身上了——刚才还在兜里的——”
电光石火间，只见她手臂猛地一扬，将揉成一团小纸丸的信笺狠狠塞进了嘴里，拼命想要咽下肚去！然而索夫勒齐的大手比闪电还要迅疾，一把死死扼住了她的咽喉，剧烈的窒息逼得她不得不狼狈地将纸丸吐了出来。
他终于将那封信攥在了掌心。他的双手颤抖得如此厉害，以至于连展开纸页的力气都快丧失了。
上面的笔迹，赫然正是贝尔塔的墨宝！
索夫勒齐整个人剧烈摇晃起来，周遭天旋地转，一阵可怕的极度眩晕狠狠砸中了他；他眼前一片模糊，视界里被一片猩红如血的迷雾所笼罩；双腿彻底脱力酸软，他摇摇晃晃踉跄跌撞，双手胡乱在虚空中抓挠以求支撑。稍稍回过神来的珍妮慌忙上前试图搀扶；然而她的指尖刚触碰到他的那一瞬间，他整个人触电般战栗起来，猛地一把将她推翻在地。究竟发生了什么，他脑海中一片混沌。啊，他迫切渴望阅读这封信，可双眼却无论如何也聚不起焦来！

===Original===

He went to the table, turned out and drank two large glasses of water one after another. The cold draught restored him, his blood resumed its natural course, and he could see. The note was short, and this was what he read:
“Don’t go tomorrow to Petit-Bourg; or rather, return before breakfast. He has just told me that he must go to Melun, and that he should return late. A whole day!”
“He”﻿—that was himself. This other lover of Hector’s was Bertha, his wife. For a moment he saw nothing but that; all thought was crushed within him. His temples beat furiously, he heard a dreadful buzzing in his ears, it seemed to him as if the earth were about to swallow him up. He fell into a chair; from purple he became ashy white. Great tears trickled down his cheeks.
Jenny understood the miserable meanness of her conduct when she saw this great grief, this silent despair, this man with a broken heart. Was she not the cause of all? She had guessed who the writer of the note was. She thought when she asked Sauvresy to come to her, that she could tell him all, and thus avenge herself at once upon Hector and her rival. Then, on seeing this man refusing to comprehend her hints, she had been full of pity for him. She had said to herself that he would be the one who would be most cruelly punished; and then she had recoiled﻿—but too late﻿—and he had snatched the secret from her.
She approached Sauvresy and tried to take his hands; he still repulsed her.
“Let me alone,” said he.
“Pardon me, sir﻿—I am a wretch, I am horrified at myself.”
He rose suddenly; he was gradually coming to himself.
“What do you want?”
“That letter﻿—I guessed﻿—”
He burst into a loud, bitter, discordant laugh, and replied:
“God forgive me! Why, my dear, did you dare to suspect my wife?”
While Jenny was muttering confused excuses, he drew out his pocketbook and took from it all the money it contained﻿—some seven or eight hundred francs﻿—which he put on the table.
“Take this, from Hector,” said he, “he will not permit you to suffer for anything; but, believe me, you had best let him get married.”
Then he mechanically took up his gun, opened the door, and went out. His dogs leaped upon him to caress him; he kicked them off. Where was he going? What was he going to do?

===Chinese===

他挪步挪到桌案前，倒了满满两大杯凉水，接连一饮而尽。刺骨的冰凉激醒了他的神智，血液重新恢复了平缓的周流，双眼终于重新看清了字迹。短笺极短，映入眼帘的赫然是这几行字：
“明儿切莫去往小堡；或者无论如何，务必在早饭前赶回来。他方才告知我他必须去一趟默伦，要很晚才能折返。整整一整天呢！”
“他”——指的正是索夫勒齐他自个儿！埃克托私通苟合的另一个情妇，正是贝尔塔，是他明媒正娶的结发妻子！有那么一瞬间，他整个人的脑海被这个残酷的真相彻底填平挤碎；所有的思维感知全被碾成了齑粉。他的双侧太阳穴狂乱地突突暴跳，双耳中轰鸣着骇人的巨响，仿佛脚下的大地即将裂开深渊将他活活吞噬。他瘫软跌坐在椅子上；面庞由先前的酱紫瞬间褪成了惨白如纸的死灰之色。大颗大颗滚烫的泪珠，顺着面颊肆无忌惮地滂沱滑落。
瞧见这摧心剖肝的滔天悲恸、这无声胜有声的万丈绝望、这个彻底心碎成灰的可怜男人，珍妮终于良心发现，深深领教了自己行径的卑鄙与残忍。难道这毁灭性的一切，不正是她一手造成的吗？她其实早就猜透了这封短笺究竟出自何人之手。当初修书恳请索夫勒齐赴会时，她本巴望着将一切真相全盘托出，好借刀杀人一举报复埃克托同她的情敌。然而当亲眼瞧见这位君子对她的百般明示暗示充耳不闻、甚至以圣贤之心度小人之腹时，她心头不由得涌起了无尽的怜悯与悔恨。她暗自告诫自己：一旦真相大白，遭受最残酷凌迟惩罚的，反倒是这位无辜的大善人！她原本想要悬崖勒马临阵退缩——然而一切为时已晚——他终究用蛮力将这份绝密硬生生夺了过去。
她挪步凑近索夫勒齐，试图去握他的双手；然而他再一次狠狠将她推开。
“离我远点，”他喃喃道。
“求您宽恕我吧，老爷——我是个该遭天谴的恶妇，连我自己都厌恶我自己！”
索夫勒齐霍然站起身来；他的神智正在一点一滴艰难地回拢。
“你到底想怎样？”
“那封信……我其实猜到了……”
他喉头蓦地迸发出一阵洪亮、尖刻而歇斯底里的凄厉狂笑，厉声回敬道：
“老天在上！怎么，我的好姑娘，难道你竟敢疑心到我家夫人的头上？”
趁着珍妮语无伦次、结结巴巴地吐露着错乱的歉词之际，他猛地掏出钱夹，将里面所有的现钞一股脑倾倒一空——足足有七八百法郎——啪的一声拍在桌案上。
“拿着吧，这是埃克托给你的，”他说道，“他绝不会让你挨饿受冻；不过信我一句，你最好还是由着他去成家立业吧。”
说完，他近乎本能地抓起猎枪，拉开房门，踉跄着迈步走了出去。守在门外的猎犬们欢快地摇着尾巴扑上前向主人献媚；他狠狠飞起一脚将它们踹翻在一旁。他究竟要奔向何方？他又究竟打算做出何等惊天的决断？

## XVIII / 第十八章
===Original===

A small, fine, chilly rain had succeeded the morning fog; but Sauvresy did not perceive it. He went across the fields with his head bare, wandering at hazard, without aim or discretion. He talked aloud as he went, stopping ever and anon, then resuming his course. The peasants who met him﻿—they all knew him﻿—turned to look at him after having saluted him, asking themselves whether the master of Valfeuillu had not gone mad. Unhappily he was not mad. Overwhelmed by an unheard-of, unlooked-for catastrophe, his brain had been for a moment paralyzed. But one by one he collected his scattered ideas and acquired the faculty of thinking and of suffering. Each one of his reflections increased his mortal anguish. Yes, Bertha and Hector had deceived, had dishonored him. She, beloved to idolatry; he, his best and oldest friend, a wretch that he had snatched from misery, who owed him everything. And it was in his house, under his own roof, that this infamy had taken place. They had taken advantage of his noble trust, had made a dupe of him. The frightful discovery not only embittered the future, but also the past. He longed to blot out of his life these years passed with Bertha, with whom, but the night before, he had recalled these “happiest years of his life.” The memory of his former happiness filled his soul with disgust. But how had this been done? When? How was it he had seen nothing of it? And now things came into his mind which should have warned him had he not been blind. He recalled certain looks of Bertha, certain tones of voice, which were an avowal. At times, he tried to doubt. There are misfortunes so great that to be believed there must be more than evidence.
“It is not possible!” muttered he.
Seating himself upon a prostrate tree in the midst of Mauprévoir forest, he studied the fatal letter for the tenth time within four hours.
“It proves all,” said he, “and it proves nothing.”
And he read once more.
“Do not go tomorrow to Petit-Bourg﻿—”
Well, had he not again and again, in his idiotic confidence, said to Hector:
“I shall be away tomorrow, stay here and keep Bertha company.”
This sentence, then, had no positive signification. But why add:
“Or rather, return before breakfast.”
This was what betrayed fear, that is, the fault. To go away and return again anon, was to be cautious, to avoid suspicion. Then, why “he,” instead of, “Clement?”

===Chinese===

清晨的大雾散去，取而代之的是一阵凄风苦雨、冰冷刺骨的毛毛细雨；然而索夫勒齐对此浑然不觉。他光着脑袋横穿田野，信马由缰、漫无目的地在泥泞中游荡。他一边走一边自言自语，时不时猝然停下脚步，旋即又拔腿疾行。沿途撞见他的乡里农夫们——大家全都认得他——在向他鞠躬致意后，无不驻足扭头回望，暗自琢磨瓦尔菲伊庄园的男主人莫非是失心疯发作了。然而可悲的是，他神智清醒得很，根本没有疯。被这场旷古未闻、从天而降的灭顶横祸当头痛击，他的大脑曾陷入了短暂的麻木瘫痪。可随着思绪一点一滴慢慢回拢，他重新恢复了思考与承受痛苦的能力。脑海中泛起的每一个念头，都在成倍加剧着他那濒死的万丈煎熬。是的，贝尔塔和埃克托欺骗了他、践踏羞辱了他的尊严！那个被他奉若神明、宠若至宝的发妻；那个他最年深日久、从赤贫泥淖中一把拉扯出来的至交手足——一个欠了他天大恩情的人渣！而这场丧尽天良的丑恶勾当，偏偏就发生在他的深宅大院里，在他的眼皮底下！他们无情利用了他那光明磊落的绝对信任，将他玩弄于股掌之间。这场骇人听闻的撞破，不仅将他余生的希望彻底撕成粉碎，更彻底玷污毒化了他全部的过往回忆。他恨不得将自己同贝尔塔共同度过的那些岁月从生命中生生剜去——就在前天夜里，他还曾同她依偎着追忆那些“一生中最幸福的时光”。对往昔幸福的追忆，此刻在他灵魂深处激起了令人作呕的反胃。这一切究竟是何时开始的？又是如何得手的？为何自己平生竟瞎了眼、对此丝毫未曾察觉？此时此刻，过往无数本该敲响警钟的细枝末节如潮水般涌上心头。他回想起贝尔塔当年的某些异样眼神、某些意味深长的语调腔调——那分明就是私通的无声供状！在某些瞬间，他甚至极力试图怀疑这一切不过是幻觉。世间有些灾祸是如此沉重灭顶，以至于仅仅凭借眼见为实，都让人难以置信。
“绝不可能！”他喃喃自语道。
在莫普雷瓦尔密林深处一根横卧的倒木上坐下，他在短短4个小时内第10次掏出那封致命的短笺细细端详。
“它能证明一切，”他自言自语道，“可它又什么都证明不了。”
他再一次逐字审读：
“明儿切莫去往小堡——”
哼，难道在他自个儿那愚蠢至极的绝对信任下，他不曾一次又一次对埃克托叮嘱过：
“我明儿出门不在府上，你留在家里好生陪陪贝尔塔。”
这么看来，单凭这一句话并没有板上钉钉的确凿杀伤力。可她为何偏要画蛇添足加上一句：
“或者无论如何，务必在早饭前赶回来。”
这恰恰出卖了内心的恐慌，出卖了私通的罪证！去而复返、急匆匆赶回，正是为了掩人耳目，为了避开旁人的嫌疑。不仅如此，信里为何称呼“他”，而不是“克莱芒”？

===Original===

This word was striking. “He”﻿—that is, the dear one, or else, the master that one hates. There is no medium﻿—’tis the husband, or the lover. “He,” is never an indifferent person. A husband is lost when his wife, in speaking of him, says, “He.”
But when had Bertha written these few lines? Doubtless some evening after they had retired to their room. He had said to her, “I’m going tomorrow to Melun,” and then she had hastily scratched off this note and given it, in a book, to Hector.
Alas! the edifice of his happiness, which had seemed to him strong enough to defy every tempest of life, had crumbled, and he stood there lost in the midst of its debris. No more happiness, joys, hopes﻿—nothing! All his plans for the future rested on Bertha; her name was mingled in his every dream, she was at once the future and the dream. He had so loved her that she had become something of himself, that he could not imagine himself without her. Bertha lost to him, he saw no direction in life to take, he had no further reason for living. He perceived this so vividly that the idea of suicide came to him. He had his gun, powder and balls; his death would be attributed to a hunting accident, and all would be over.
Oh, but the guilty ones!
They would doubtless go on in their infamous comedy﻿—would seem to mourn for him, while really their hearts would bound with joy. No more husband, no more hypocrisies or terrors. His will giving his fortune to Bertha, they would be rich. They would sell everything, and would depart rejoicing to some distant clime. As to his memory, poor man, it would amuse them to think of him as the cheated and despised husband.
“Never!” cried he, drunk with fury, “never! I must kill myself, but first, I must avenge my dishonor!”
But he tried in vain to imagine a punishment cruel or terrible enough. What chastisement could expiate the horrible tortures which he endured? He said to himself that, in order to assure his vengeance, he must wait﻿—and he swore that he would wait. He would feign the same stolid confidence, and resigned himself to see and hear everything.
“My hypocrisy will equal theirs,” thought he.
Indeed a cautious duplicity was necessary. Bertha was most cunning, and at the first suspicion would fly with her lover. Hector had already﻿—thanks to him﻿—several hundred thousand francs.

===Chinese===

这个字眼犹如一道惊雷。“他”——要么指代心中挚爱的情郎，要么指代自己切齿痛恨的专横主子。绝无任何中庸的余地——非此即彼，要么是丈夫，要么是姘头。“他”绝不可能指代一个无关痛痒的局外旁人。当一个妻子在私底下背着丈夫提起对方只用“他”来指代时，这个丈夫便已彻底名存实亡了。
然而贝尔塔究竟是何时写下这几行绝命小字的？毫无疑问必定是在某个二人回房歇息的深夜。他曾对她说，“我明儿打算去一趟默伦，”于是她便急匆匆草草划下这几行密札，夹在一本书里暗中递交给了埃克托。
唉！他原本坚信足以傲立世间、抵御人世间一切狂风骤雨的幸福大厦，转瞬间轰然崩塌，只留他形单影只地迷失在满地凄凉的残垣断壁之中。再无幸福，再无欢愉，再无期冀——一切荡然无存！他对未来的全盘构想皆建立在贝尔塔一身；她的名字融汇在他每一个魂牵梦萦的憧憬里，她既是他的未来，更是他的全部美梦。他曾是如此深爱着她，以至于她已然化作了他肉体与灵魂的一部分，他甚至无法想象没有她的日子该如何存活。一旦贝尔塔从生命中被彻底剥夺，他在滚滚红尘中便再也找寻不出任何前行的方向，在这世间再无半点苟活的由头。这一绝望的认知是如此清晰刺骨，以至于自尽的念头刹那间劈入了他的脑海。他手里握着猎枪、弹药与铅弹；他的暴毙完全会被当成一场打猎走火的意外事故，一切就此尘归尘土归土。
啊，可那两个恶徒呢！
他们毫无疑问会继续粉墨登场、将这出下作无耻的活丑剧演将下去——他们表面上会为他披麻戴孝、哭天抢地，内心深处却必将因狂喜而雀跃沸腾。再无碍手碍脚的丈夫，再无遮人耳目的伪善与惶恐。他的遗嘱早已将全副身家悉数赠予贝尔塔，他们转眼便成了坐拥巨资的泼天富豪。他们会变卖所有的庄园地产，喜笑颜开远走高飞逃往异国他乡长相厮守。至于他索夫勒齐身后留下的名声，可怜的苦主啊，在他们茶余饭后充其量不过是个被戴了绿帽子还遭人嗤之以鼻的受骗窝囊废罢了！
“绝不！”他因滔天狂怒而宛如酗酒癫狂般嘶声咆哮道，“绝不能便宜了他们！我死不足惜，但在赴死之前，我必将亲手洗雪这奇耻大辱！”
然而他搜肠刮肚，却无论如何也想不出一种足够残酷、足够骇人听闻的惩戒极刑。天地间究竟何等惩罚，才足以抵偿他此刻正在承受的撕心裂肺的万丈酷刑？他暗自告诫自己：为了确保这场复仇万无一失，他必须隐忍等待——他指天发誓定要死死忍下去。他要继续装出一副木讷纯良、全无芥蒂的盲目信任，咬紧牙关去直视、去聆听眼前上演的一切丑态。
“我的伪善心机，定要同他们的奸狡并驾齐驱！”他心中暗自发狠。
事实确实如此，此刻唯有老谋深算的极度隐忍方能成事。贝尔塔奸猾似鬼，稍起一丝风吹草动便会携同情夫远走高飞。多亏了他索夫勒齐的一手操持，埃克托名下如今早已截留了数十万法郎的丰厚家私。

===Original===

The idea that they might escape his vengeance gave him energy and a clear head.
It was only then that he thought of the flight of time, the rain falling in torrents, and the state of his clothes.
“Bah!” thought he, “I will make up some story to account for myself.”
He was only a league from Valfeuillu, but he was an hour and a half reaching home. He was broken, exhausted; he felt chilled to the marrow of his bones. But when he entered the gate, he had succeeded in assuming his usual expression, and the gayety which so well hinted his perfect trustfulness. He had been waited for, but in spite of his resolutions, he could not sit at table between this man and woman, his two most cruel enemies. He said that he had taken cold, and would go to bed. Bertha insisted in vain that he should take at least a bowl of broth and a glass of claret.
“Really,” said he, “I don’t feel well.”
When he had retired, Bertha said:
“Did you notice, Hector?”
“What?”
“Something unusual has happened to him.”
“Very likely, after being all day in the rain.”
“No. His eye had a look I never saw before.”
“He seemed to be very cheerful, as he always is.”
“Hector, my husband suspects!”
“He? Ah, my poor good friend has too much confidence in us to think of being jealous.”
“You deceive yourself, Hector; he did not embrace me when he came in, and it is the first time since our marriage.”
Thus, at the very first, he had made a blunder. He knew it well; but it was beyond his power to embrace Bertha at that moment; and he was suffering more than he thought he should. When his wife and his friend ascended to his room, after dinner, they found him shivering under the sheets, red, his forehead burning, his throat dry, and his eyes shining with an unusual brilliancy. A fever soon came on, attended by delirium. A doctor was called, who at first said he would not answer for him. The next day he was worse. From this time both Hector and Bertha conceived for him the most tender devotion. Did they think they should thus in some sort expiate their crime? It is doubtful. More likely they tried to impose on the people about them; everyone was anxious for Sauvresy. They never deserted him for a moment, passing the night by turns near his bed. And it was painful to watch over him; a furious delirium never left him.

===Chinese===

一想到那两个恶徒极可能从他的复仇铁网中脱逃，一股冰冷的决绝反倒赐予了他钢铁般的旺盛精力与无比清醒的头脑。
直到此时此刻，他才蓦然意识到光阴的飞逝、落在身上的凄风冷雨、以及冻透骨髓的阵阵寒颤。他看了一眼怀表；指针已然划过了下午4点。他已经在冰冷的荒野密林里跌跌撞撞游荡了整整7个多小时。他迈步踏上了归途，步伐坚毅稳健。在行将踏入瓦尔菲伊庄园门槛的前一刹那，他重新振作了心神，强迫自己摆出往日那副开朗舒朗的面容。他穿过前厅，将猎枪递给迎上前来的仆人，推门步入了客厅。贝尔塔与埃克托正围坐在桌案旁低声闲聊。两人神色泰然自若，全无半点心虚之相。
“哎呀！”索夫勒齐刚一进门，贝尔塔便娇嗔着迎上前道，“你总算舍得回来了！我们还以为你出了什么意外呢。”
“我玩得太尽兴了，在林子里追逐几只野鸡，不知不觉就迷了路。”
他正欲再寒暄几句，喉头冷不丁被一阵狂暴的剧烈咳嗽生生堵住，整个人剧烈颤抖起来。他浑身冷热交加，骨节咯咯作响。
“我着凉了，”他喘息着从齿缝间挤出话来。
贝尔塔与特雷莫雷尔惊慌失色地将他搀扶到安乐椅上，命人快马加鞭赶往科尔贝伊去延请大夫。当医生风尘仆仆赶到之际，索夫勒齐早已陷入了一阵凶险的高热谵妄之中。大夫诊断为急性胸膜肺炎，病情凶险万分。在接下来的八天八夜里，病人的生死悬于一线。全府上下人心惶惶，贝尔塔和埃克托表现得无微不至、寸步不离，二人轮流在病榻前通宵达旦地守候侍奉。而守护在他榻前着实是一桩撕心裂肺的酷刑折磨；一场狂暴绝伦的高热狂乱自始至终未曾离开过他的躯壳。

===Original===

Several times force had to be used to keep him on the bed; he tried to throw himself out of the window. The third day he had a strange fancy; he did not wish to stay in his chamber. He kept crying out:
“Carry me away from here, carry me away from here.”
The doctor advised that he should be humored; so a bed was made up for him in a little room on the ground-floor, overlooking the garden. His wanderings did not betray anything of his suspicions; perhaps the firm will was able even to control the delirium. The fever finally yielded on the ninth day. His breathing became calmer, and he slept. When he awoke, reason had returned. That was a frightful moment. He had, so to speak, to take up the burden of his misery. At first he thought it the memory of a horrid nightmare; but no. He had not dreamed. He recalled the Belle Image, Jenny, the forest, the letter. What had become of the letter? Then, having the vague impression of a serious illness, he asked himself if he had said anything to betray the source of his misery. This anxiety prevented his making the slightest movement, and he opened his eyes softly and cautiously. It was eleven at night, and all the servants had gone to bed. Hector and Bertha alone were keeping watch; he was reading a paper, she was crocheting. Sauvresy saw by their placid countenances that he had betrayed nothing. He moved slightly; Bertha at once arose and came to him.
“How are you, dear Clement?” asked she, kissing him fondly on the forehead.
“I am no longer in pain.”
“You see the result of being careless.”
“How many days have I been sick?”
“Eight days.”
“Why was I brought here?”
“Because you wished it.”
Trémorel had approached the bedside.
“You refused to stay upstairs,” said he, “you were ungovernable till we had you brought here.”
“But don’t tire yourself,” resumed Hector. “Go to sleep again, and you will be well by tomorrow. And good night, for I am going to bed now, and shall return and wake your wife at four o’clock.”
He went out, and Bertha, having given Sauvresy something to drink, returned to her seat.
“What a friend Trémorel is,” murmured she. Sauvresy did not answer this terribly ironical exclamation. He shut his eyes, pretended to sleep, and thought of the letter. What had he done with it? He remembered that he had carefully folded it and put it in the right-hand pocket of his vest. He must have this letter.

===Chinese===

有好几回，下人们不得不合力使出蛮力死死按住他，以免他从病榻上翻滚下来；他甚至几次三番试图跳窗寻死。到了第3天，他冷不丁生出了一桩怪癖执念；他无论如何也不肯继续留在二楼的卧室里。他扯着嗓门一刻不停地嘶声哀嚎：
“把我抬走，快把我从这间屋子里抬走！”
医生建议顺从病患的意愿；于是下人们在底楼一间俯瞰后花园的小厢房里，替他收拾安置了一张病榻。在整场昏睡胡话中，他的谵妄之词竟奇迹般未曾泄露出半点关于奸情的疑云；或许是他那钢铁般的坚毅意志，甚至在意识模糊的狂乱深渊中也能死死勒住舌头的缰绳。直到第9天，高烧终于开始退去。他的呼吸渐渐恢复了平稳，安然陷入了沉睡。当他终于睁开双眼之际，理智与清醒已然重新回到了躯体。那真是一场毛骨悚然的惨烈时刻。他不得不重新肩负起自己那生不如死的无边苦楚。起初他还自欺欺人地以为这不过是一场荒诞不经的噩梦；然而残酷的现实瞬间将他打醒。他没有做梦。他清楚地忆起了靓影旅馆、珍妮、密林、以及那封短笺。那封信究竟落在何处了？随即，感知到自己大病初愈的虚弱躯壳，他暗自惊恐地扪心自问：在方才那场昏乱的高热谵妄中，自己是否曾失口吐露过只言片语出卖了心头的秘密？这份惶恐令他甚至不敢挪动半下身躯，只是极其小心翼翼地微微掀开眼帘。此刻已是深夜11点整，府上的奴仆佣人早已悉数歇息。唯独埃克托与贝尔塔二人依旧在房中守夜照料；他正就着灯光翻阅报纸，她则在一旁静静飞针走线钩织花边。索夫勒齐从二人平静笃定的神色中敏锐地断定，自己在高烧中未曾泄露半点天机。他身子微微挪动了一下；贝尔塔立刻察觉并放下针线款款走到榻前。
“你觉得怎么样了，亲爱的克莱芒？”她俯下身子，深情款款地在丈夫额头印上了一吻柔声询问道。
“我身上不痛了。”
“瞧你，这便是粗心大意不爱惜身子的下场。”
“我病倒几天了？”
“整整8天了。”
“为何要把我抬到这间屋子里来？”
“这全是你自个儿闹着要换的呀。”
特雷莫雷尔也挪步凑到了病榻边。
“你当时死活不肯留在楼上，”他说道，“大吵大闹非要下人把你抬到这儿来才肯罢休。”
“不过你眼下可莫要耗神多说话，”埃克托接着劝道，“好生合眼再睡上一觉，明儿一早准能彻底痊愈。那就道声晚安吧，我眼下也该回房安歇了，清晨4点我准时下来换你内人去睡。”
说完他迈步走了出去，贝尔塔给索夫勒齐端喂了些温水，便折返回原处坐下。
“特雷莫雷尔当真算得上一位打着灯笼都难找的生死至交啊，”她喃喃感叹道。面对这句极度讽刺刻毒的由衷赞美，索夫勒齐一声未吭。他闭上双眼假寐，心头却飞速盘算着那封信的下落。他究竟把信弄到哪儿去了？他依稀记起自己当时将信小心翼翼折叠齐整，塞进了马甲右侧的口袋里。他必须抢先把这封信找回来。

===Original===

It would balk his vengeance, should it fall into his wife’s hands; and this might happen at any moment. It was a miracle that his valet had not put it on the mantel, as he was accustomed to do with the things which he found in his master’s pockets. He was reflecting on some means of getting it, of the possibility of going up to his bedroom, where his vest ought to be, when Bertha got up softly. She came to the bed and whispered gently:
“Clement, Clement!”
He did not open his eyes, and she, persuaded that he was sleeping, though very lightly, stole out of the room, holding her breath as she went.
“Oh, the wretch!” muttered Sauvresy, “she is going to *him*!”
At the same time the necessity of recovering the letter occurred to him more vividly than ever.
“I can get to my room,” thought he, “without being seen, by the garden and backstairs. She thinks I’m asleep; I shall get back and abed before she returns.”
Then, without asking himself whether he were not too feeble, or what danger there might be in exposing himself to the cold, he got up, threw a gown around him, put on his slippers and went toward the door.
“If anyone sees me, I will feign delirium,” said he to himself.
The vestibule lamp was out and he found some difficulty in opening the door; finally, he descended into the garden. It was intensely cold, and snow had fallen. The wind shook the limbs of the trees crusted with ice. The front of the house was sombre. One window only was lighted﻿—that of Trémorel’s room; that was lighted brilliantly, by a lamp and a great blazing fire. The shadow of a man﻿—of Hector﻿—rested on the muslin curtains; the shape was distinct. He was near the window, and his forehead was pressed against the panes. Sauvresy instinctively stopped to look at his friend, who was so at home in his house, and who, in exchange for the most brotherly hospitality, had brought dishonor, despair and death.
Hector made a sudden movement, and turned around as if he was surprised by an unwonted noise. What was it? Sauvresy only knew too well. Another shadow appeared on the curtain﻿—that of Bertha. And he had forced himself to doubt till now! Now proofs had come without his seeking. What had brought her to that room, at that hour? She seemed to be talking excitedly.

===Chinese===

假使这封信不慎落入发妻的手中，他的全盘复仇大计便将彻底化为泡影；而这等大祸随时随地都可能降临。男仆在替他更衣清理时，居然没有依照惯例将从主子兜里掏出的物什整整齐齐摆在壁炉台上，这简直是个奇迹。正当他绞尽脑汁思忖着该如何弄回信笺、琢磨着自己是否有力气悄悄爬回二楼找寻那件遗留在卧房的马甲之际，贝尔塔悄无声息地从椅上站起身来。她蹑手蹑脚挪步挪到床榻边，极轻柔地低语唤道：
“克莱芒，克莱芒！”
索夫勒齐合着双眼毫无反应，贝尔塔深信他已然沉睡，便屏气敛息、轻手轻脚地溜出了房门。
“啊，你这荡妇贱人！”索夫勒齐在齿缝间暗自咬牙切齿地咆哮道，“她这是要去找他苟合去了！”
与此同时，抢回密信的紧迫感比以往任何时刻都更暴烈地撕扯着他的心扉。
“我可以神不知鬼不觉爬回我的卧室，”他暗自盘算，“穿过后花园走后楼梯，谁也瞧不见。她以为我睡熟了；我定能在她折返之前拿回密信乖乖躺回被窝。”
于是，他全然顾不上自己大病初愈的虚弱体魄，更顾不上冒着严寒外出会招致何等致命的伤风危险，翻身下床，随手披上一件晨袍，趿拉上拖鞋，径直朝着房门挪步走去。
“万一撞见旁人，我就继续装疯卖傻装谵妄，”他心中暗自发狠道。
门厅里的壁灯早已熄灭，他在黑暗中颇费了一番周折才拨开门闩；终于，他迈步走入了后花园。外面天寒地冻、呵气成冰，天地间落了一层皑皑白雪。呼啸的寒风剧烈摇曳着结满冰凌的森森树枝。整栋公馆的正面黑黢黢一片。唯独一扇窗户依然灯火通明——那正是特雷莫雷尔的卧室之窗；在油灯的照耀与壁炉熊熊炉火的映衬下，整扇窗户亮如白昼。一道男子的黑色剪影——埃克托的身影——清晰地投射在薄纱窗帘上，轮廓分明得一览无余。他正伫立在窗前，额头紧紧抵在玻璃窗格上。索夫勒齐下意识地驻足凝视着他的这位“挚友”——这个在他府上宾至如归、却用灭顶的耻辱、绝望与死亡来报答同胞手足般盛情款待的恶魔。
埃克托蓦地动弹了一下身子，警惕地猛然回过头来，仿佛被门外某种异样的响动所惊动。究竟是何声响？索夫勒齐心里比谁都更清楚。只见薄纱窗帘上，悄然浮现出了第二道窈窕的黑影——正是贝尔塔的身姿！直到方才这一刻之前，索夫勒齐甚至仍在强迫自己心存侥幸！而如今，铁证如山，根本无须他去苦苦追寻便赤裸裸呈现在了眼前。在这样的深夜时分，究竟是何等由头驱使她溜进这间卧房？只见她在窗帘后手舞足蹈，情绪显得激动异常。

===Original===

He thought he could hear that full, sonorous voice, now as clear as metal, now soft and caressing, which had made all the chords of passion vibrate in him. He once more saw those beautiful eyes which had reigned so despotically over his heart, and whose expressions he knew so well. But what was she doing? Doubtless she had gone to ask Hector something, which he refused her, and she was pleading with him; Sauvresy saw that she was supplicating, by her motions; he knew the gesture well. She lifted her clasped hands as high as her forehead, bent her head, half shut her eyes. What languor had been in her voice when she used to say:
“Say, dear Clement, you will, will you not?”
And now she was using the same blandishments on another. Sauvresy was obliged to support himself against a tree. Hector was evidently refusing what she wished; then she shook her finger menacingly, and tossed her head angrily, as if she were saying:
“You won’t? You shall see, then.”
And then she returned to her supplications.
“Ah,” thought Sauvresy, “*he* can resist her prayers; *I* never had such courage. He can preserve his coolness, his will, when she looks at him; I never said no to her; rather, I never waited for her to ask anything of me; I have passed my life in watching her lightest fancies, to gratify them. Perhaps that is what has ruined me!”
Hector was obstinate, and Bertha was roused little by little; she must be angry. She recoiled, holding out her arms, her head thrown back; she was threatening him. At last he was conquered; he nodded, “Yes.” Then she flung herself upon him, and the two shadows were confounded in a long embrace.
Sauvresy could not repress an agonized cry, which was lost amid the noises of the night. He had asked for certainty; here it was. The truth, indisputable, evident, was clear to him. He had to seek for nothing more, now, except for the means to punish surely and terribly. Bertha and Hector were talking amicably. Sauvresy saw that she was about to go downstairs, and that he could not now go for the letter. He went in hurriedly, forgetting, in his fear of being discovered, to lock the garden door.

===Chinese===

他仿佛亲耳听到了那道圆润嘹亮的熟悉嗓音——时而如金石击节般清脆，时而如春风拂面般温存软语——当年正是这道嗓音，曾一次次拨动他体内所有的深情琴弦。他眼前仿佛再次浮现出了那双曾对他心扉施加专横暴政的美丽眼眸，那眼波流转间的每一抹神态他都了如指掌。然而她此刻究竟在做些什么？毫无疑问，她定是溜进房来向埃克托乞求某桩心愿，而埃克托断然回绝了她，她正极尽所能苦苦哀求；索夫勒齐从她动作的起承转合间清清楚楚瞧出她正在乞怜，这套姿态他太熟悉了！她将紧扣的双掌高高举到额前，螓首低垂，双眸微阖。当年每逢她娇嗔呢喃：
“好不好嘛，亲爱的克莱芒，你就依了我吧，好不好？”她的嗓音该是何等楚楚可怜、柔肠百转啊！
而如今，她竟在用这套原封不动的狐媚手段，去讨好侍奉另一个男人！索夫勒齐只觉天旋地转，不得不伸手死死扶住身旁的一棵大树。埃克托显然铁了心不肯答应；只见贝尔塔气急败坏地晃动着食指厉声恐吓，愤怒地猛甩着脑袋，仿佛在恶狠狠地咒骂：
“你不答应？那就走着瞧！”
然而紧接着，她又放软了身段，重新开始了苦苦哀求。
“哈，”索夫勒齐暗自悲怆自嘲，“那个男人竟能硬起心肠抗拒她的乞求；而我平生却从未有过这等铁石心肠！每逢她拿这双眼眸凝视着我，他仍能保持冷酷沉着与钢铁意志；而我平生却从未对她说出过半个‘不’字；甚至我从未等她开口索要，大半辈子都在鞍前马后盯着她最微不足道的心思去竭力迎合满足。或许正是这份纵容，彻底将我送上了断头台！”
埃克托冥顽不灵死不松口，贝尔塔体内的怒火渐渐被挑拨到了极致；她显然动了真怒。她身子向后倒退了两步，张开双臂，仰面仰头，咬牙切齿地向他发出最后通牒。最终埃克托被彻底降服了；他无可奈何地点了点头表示应允。转瞬间，贝尔塔便如饿虎扑食般飞扑进他的怀抱，两道黑影在窗帘后紧紧交融纠缠在了一处，化作了一记漫长销魂的深情热吻。
索夫勒齐喉头忍不住滚出一声撕心裂肺的凄厉悲鸣，然而这声绝望的痛呼瞬间便被呼啸凄厉的夜风吞噬得无影无踪。他平生一心渴求确凿的铁证；如今铁证赫然呈现在眼前！无可辩驳、昭然若揭的血淋淋真相，在他眼前彻底大白。从今往后，他再也无须去搜寻任何证据了，他余生唯一的使命，便是筹谋出一种足以斩草除根、残暴至极的复仇杀招。窗帘后的二人开始和颜悦色地温存私语。索夫勒齐瞧见贝尔塔已然准备转身下楼，深知自己眼下绝无可能再去二楼搜寻密信了。他慌慌张张疾步折返回底楼房间，由于唯恐当场败露行踪，惊慌失措之中竟浑然忘记了将后花园的大门重新反锁拴紧。

===Original===

He did not perceive that he had been standing with naked feet in the snow, till he had returned to his bedroom again; he saw some flakes on his slippers, and they were damp; quickly he threw them under the bed, and jumped in between the clothes, and pretended to be asleep.
It was time, for Bertha soon came in. She went to the bed, and thinking that he had not woke up, returned to her embroidery by the fire. Trémorel also soon reappeared; he had forgotten to take his paper, and had come back for it. He seemed uneasy.
“Have you been out tonight, Madame?” asked he, in a low voice.
“No.”
“Have all the servants gone to bed?”
“I suppose so; but why do you ask?”
“Since I have been upstairs, somebody has gone out into the garden, and come back again.”
Bertha looked at him with a troubled glance.
“Are you sure of what you say?”
“Certainly. Snow is falling, and whoever went out brought some back on his shoes. This has melted in the vestibule﻿—”
Mme. Sauvresy seized the lamp, and interrupting Hector, said:
“Come.”
Trémorel was right. Here and there on the vestibule pavement were little puddles.
“Perhaps this water has been here some time,” suggested Bertha.
“No. It was not there an hour ago, I could swear. Besides, see, here is a little snow that has not melted yet.”
“It must have been one of the servants.”
Hector went to the door and examined it.
“I do not think so,” said he. “A servant would have shut the bolts; here they are, drawn back. Yet I myself shut the door tonight, and distinctly recollect fastening the bolts.”
“It’s very strange!”
“And all the more so, look you, because the traces of the water do not go much beyond the drawing-room door.”
They remained silent, and exchanged anxious looks. The same terrible thought occurred to them both.
“If it were he?”
But why should he have gone into the garden? It could not have been to spy on them.
They did not think of the window.
“It couldn’t have been Clement,” said Bertha, at last. “He was asleep when I went back, and he is in a calm and deep slumber now.”
Sauvresy, stretched upon his bed, heard what his enemies were saying. He cursed his imprudence.
“Suppose,” thought he, “they should think of looking at my gown and slippers!”
Happily this simple idea did not occur to them; after reassuring each other as well as they were able, they separated; but each heart carried an anxious doubt.

===Chinese===

直到重新折返回卧室之际，他才蓦然惊觉自己方才竟一直赤着脚板踩在皑皑白雪之中；他瞥见自己的拖鞋上沾染着尚未融化的雪片，鞋底早已湿透；他眼疾手快一把将拖鞋踢进了床底下，飞身钻入被窝盖紧被褥，装出一副鼾声如雷的酣睡模样。
好险，只因贝尔塔转瞬便推门迈了进来。她径直挪步走到床榻边，认定丈夫未曾醒转，便重新折返回壁炉旁拾起刺绣飞针走线。不多时，特雷莫雷尔竟也重新踱步现身；他声称自己方才忘记拿走报纸，特意折返回来取。他神色间透着一股掩饰不住的狐疑与焦躁。
“夫人，今晚您曾出过门吗？”他压低了嗓门悄声询问道。
“没有啊。”
“府上的下人们全都歇下了吗？”
“料想都睡下了；可你为何这般发问？”
“自从我方才上楼之后，有人曾偷偷溜进过花园，随后又折返回来了。”
贝尔塔满脸疑云、神色不安地凝视着他。
“你当真敢打包票？”
“千真万确。外头正下着大雪，无论刚才出门的是哪个家伙，鞋底上的泥雪全带回了门厅。如今泥雪全在门厅石板地上融化了——”
索夫勒齐夫人一把抓起油灯，打断了埃克托的话头：
“快跟我来瞧瞧。”
特雷莫雷尔说得一点没错。前厅的石板地上，斑斑驳驳横陈着一处处水洼泥水。
“兴许这摊水早就存在了吧，”贝尔塔强自辩解道。
“绝不可能。我敢指天发誓，一个小时前这里分明干干净净。更何况你瞧，这儿甚至还残存着一小撮未曾融化干净的残雪呢。”
“那必定是哪个粗心大意的仆人踩出来的。”
埃克托挪步踱到大门前仔细勘验。
“我看绝非下人所为，”他断言道，“要是仆役进出，必定会顺手把门闩反锁插紧；可你瞧这儿，双重保险门闩分明全被拨开了。然而今晚临睡前是我亲手合上的大门，我记得清清楚楚当时把门闩插得严严实实。”
“这可当真古怪透顶了！”
“更诡谲的是，你仔细瞧，这串水渍足迹延伸到大客厅门前便戛然而止了。”
两人陷入了死一般的沉寂，神情惶恐地面面相觑。同一个骇人听闻的可怕念头，瞬间同时劈入了二人的脑海：
“倘若……倘若是他干的呢？”
可他平白无故跑到冰天雪地的花园里去干什么？总不可能是为了暗中窥伺跟踪他们俩吧。
他们全然没想到那扇灯火通明的窗户。
“绝不可能是克莱芒，”良久贝尔塔才打破沉默断言道，“方才我回房时他睡得正香，眼下更是陷入了深沉酣畅的沉睡呢。”
躺在病榻上的索夫勒齐将仇人们的这番对话听得一字不落。他暗自痛骂自己的粗心大意。
“倘若他们脑子灵光一闪，”他暗自心惊，“跑来查验我的晨袍与拖鞋，那可就全露馅了！”
所幸仇人们脑子里并未跳出这个念头；在彼此勉强好言抚慰了一番之后，二人各自散去；然而每一个人的心头，都沉甸甸压上了一层挥之不去的焦灼疑云。

===Original===

Sauvresy on that night had a terrible crisis in his illness. Delirium, succeeding this ray of reason, renewed its possession of his brain. The next morning Dr. R﻿—﻿— pronounced him in more danger than ever; and sent a despatch to Paris, saying that he would be detained at Valfeuillu three or four days. The distemper redoubled in violence; very contradictory symptoms appeared. Each day brought some new phase of it, which confounded the foresight of the doctors. Every time that Sauvresy had a moment of reason, the scene at the window recurred to him, and drove him to madness again.
On that terrible night when he had gone out into the snow, he had not been mistaken; Bertha was really begging something of Hector. This was it:
M. Courtois, the mayor, had invited Hector to accompany himself and his family on an excursion to Fontainebleau on the following day. Hector had cordially accepted the invitation. Bertha could not bear the idea of his spending the day in Laurence’s company, and begged him not to go. She told him there were plenty of excuses to relieve him from his promise; for instance, he might urge that it would not be seemly for him to go when his friend lay dangerously ill. At first he positively refused to grant her prayer, but by her supplications and menaces she persuaded him, and she did not go downstairs until he had sworn that he would write to M. Courtois that very evening declining the invitation. He kept his word, but he was disgusted by her tyrannical behavior. He was tired of forever sacrificing his wishes and his liberty, so that he could plan nothing, say or promise nothing without consulting this jealous woman, who would scarcely let him wander out of her sight. The chain became heavier and heavier to bear, and he began to see that sooner or later it must be wrenched apart. He had never loved either Bertha or Jenny, or anyone, probably; but he now loved the mayor’s daughter. Her dowry of a million had at first dazzled him, but little by little he had been subdued by Laurence’s charms of mind and person. He, the dissipated rake, was seduced by such grave and naive innocence, such frankness and beauty; he would have married Laurence had she been poor﻿—as Sauvresy married Bertha. But he feared Bertha too much to brave her suddenly, and so he waited.

===Chinese===

那天夜里，索夫勒齐的病情遭遇了一场骇人听闻的凶险反复。短暂重拾的一丝理智之光退去之后，狂暴的谵妄再度彻底占据了他的大脑。翌日清晨，R医生宣称他的病情比以往任何时候都要命悬一线；大夫急忙向巴黎发去了急电，声称自己势必在瓦尔菲伊庄园滞留三四天之久。病魔的肆虐变本加厉；种种自相矛盾的凶险症状接踵而至。每一天都会演变出某种全新的病征，彻底打碎大夫们的所有预判。每逢索夫勒齐偶有一丝神智清醒的片刻，昨夜窗前那一幕幕丑态便如恶魔般扑入脑海，将他再度逼入癫狂的无边深渊。
在那个他赤脚踏雪的可怕深夜，他的直觉丝毫没有看错：贝尔塔当时确实是在对埃克托苦苦哀求。事情的原委实则如下：
镇长库尔图瓦先生盛情邀约埃克托于次日同他们全家一道，前往枫丹白露踏青游猎。埃克托欣然应允赴约。贝尔塔无论如何也忍受不了情郎同洛朗斯整日厮守在一处的折磨，便苦苦央求他切莫赴会。她为他罗列了无数个推托毁约的冠冕堂皇托辞；譬如他完全可以推说自己的生死至交如今正卧床不起命悬一线，自己若是在此关头外出游山玩水未免不合体统。起初埃克托铁石心肠断然回绝，但在她的连声哀求与恶毒恐吓威逼之下，他终究不得不妥协退让，直到他当面立誓答应就在当晚修书一封送呈库尔图瓦先生婉拒邀约，贝尔塔才肯心满意足地下楼离开。他确实信守了承诺，但他心头对这个女人专横跋扈的霸道做派恶心到了极点。他早已厌倦了一再牺牲自己的心愿与自由身，以至于任何打算、言语或诺言，若不提前请示这个几乎一刻不准他脱离视线的醋坛子便休想践行。这道无形的枷锁变得越来越沉重窒息，他敏锐地意识到，早晚有一天自己非得将这道铁轭彻底砸碎不可。他平生或许从未真正爱过贝尔塔，亦未曾爱过珍妮，甚至从未爱过这世间的任何人；然而此时此刻，他却真心实意迷恋上了镇长的千金。那笔100万法郎的丰厚嫁妆起初确实晃花了他的双眼，然而随着时日推移，他已然完完全全被洛朗斯的心智教养与倾国倾城之姿所彻底征服。这位平日在风月场上历经沧桑的花花浪子，彻底拜倒在了这般娴静天真、落落大方与纯良无瑕的石榴裙下；哪怕洛朗斯是个一穷二白的贫家女，他也甘愿八抬大轿迎娶过门——就如同当年索夫勒齐迎娶贝尔塔一样。但他对贝尔塔的凶残狠辣忌惮至深，不敢猝然同她撕破脸皮，于是只得暂且虚与委蛇、静待良机。

===Original===

The next day after the quarrel about Fontainebleau, he declared that he was indisposed, attributed it to the want of exercise, and took to the saddle for several hours every day afterward. But he did not go far; only to the mayor’s. Bertha at first did not perceive anything suspicious in Trémorel’s rides; it reassured her to see him go off on his horse. After some days, however, she thought she saw in him a certain feeling of satisfaction concealed under the semblance of fatigue. She began to have doubts, and these increased every time he went out; all sorts of conjectures worried her while he was away. Where did he go? Probably to see Laurence, whom she feared and detested. The suspicion soon became a certainty with her. One evening Hector appeared, carrying in his buttonhole a flower which Laurence herself had put there, and which he had forgotten to take out. Bertha took it gently, examined it, smelt it, and, compelling herself to smile:
“Why,” said she, “what a pretty flower!”
“So I thought,” answered Hector, carelessly, “though I don’t know what it is called.”
“Would it be bold to ask who gave it to you?”
“Not at all. It’s a present from our good Plantat.”
All Orcival knew that M. Plantat, a monomaniac on flowers, never gave them away to anyone except Mme. Laurence. Hector’s evasion was an unhappy one, and Bertha was not deceived.
“You promised me, Hector,” said she, “not to see Laurence any more, and to give up this marriage.”
He tried to reply.
“Let me speak,” she continued, “and explain yourself afterward. You have broken your word﻿—you are deceiving my confidence! But I tell you, you shall not marry her!” Then, without awaiting his reply, she overwhelmed him with reproaches. Why had he come here at all? She was happy in her home before she knew him. She did not love Sauvresy, it was true; but she esteemed him, and he was good to her. Ignorant of the happiness of true love, she did not desire it. But he had come, and she could not resist his fascination. And now, after having engaged her affection, he was going to desert her, to marry another! Trémorel listened to her, perfectly amazed at her audacity. What! She dared to pretend that it was he who had abused her innocence, when, on the contrary, he had sometimes been astonished at her persistency!

===Chinese===

就枫丹白露之行大吵一架的翌日，他便借口身体抱恙，推托是久未舒展筋骨所致，从那天起便每天翻身上马在近郊巡游数个小时。然而他从未走远；每次不过是径直奔赴镇长府上。贝尔塔起初对特雷莫雷尔的频繁骑马并未起疑；瞧见他策马远去反倒让她暗自心安。然而几天过后，她敏锐地从他满脸故作疲惫的表象之下，捕捉到了一丝难以掩饰的愉悦与餍足。疑云开始在她心头滋生，并且随着他的每一次策马出门而愈演愈烈；每逢他外出之际，各色各样的胡思乱想疯狂撕咬折磨着她的心神。他究竟去了何处？十有八九是跑去私会那个令她既忌惮又恨之入骨的洛朗斯了。这份疑云很快便得到了血淋淋的印证。某天傍晚埃克托推门归来，胸前的衣纽孔里赫然插着一朵鲜花，那是洛朗斯亲手替他别上的，而他粗心大意竟忘了摘下。贝尔塔动作轻柔地取下鲜花，细细端详、凑在鼻尖嗅了嗅，强颜欢笑道：
“哎呀，”她说道，“好漂亮的一朵花啊！”
“我也觉得挺标致，”埃克托漫不经心地应道，“不过我倒叫不上它的名号。”
“冒昧打听一句，是谁赏给你的？”
“这有什么不好说的，咱们那位好心的普朗塔先生送的。”
整个奥尔西瓦尔镇谁人不知，普朗塔先生是个视花如命的花痴老头，平生培育出的名贵鲜花除了赠予洛朗斯小姐之外，绝不肯赏给天地间的任何旁人。埃克托这番拙劣的托辞瞬间露了馅，贝尔塔丝毫没有被蒙蔽过去。
“埃克托，你分明答应过我，”她冷冷吐露道，“绝不再去见洛朗斯半面，彻底斩断这门亲事。”
他刚欲开口狡辩。
“让我把话说完，”贝尔塔接着说道，“等我说完了你再去编你的谎话。你背信弃义——你肆意践踏了我对你的全部信任！但我明明白白告诉你，你休想迎娶她过门！”说完，根本不容他分辩，滔天诛心的数落便如狂风暴雨般倾泻而来。他当初究竟为何要踏足这座庄园？在结识他之前，她在自个儿的家里过得何等安稳美满！她不爱索夫勒齐，这确实是大实话；但她打心眼里敬重他，而索夫勒齐待她更是好得没话说。她原本对真正的两情相悦一无所知，倒也从未奢求过这份极乐。然而偏偏他横插一脚闯了进来，令她彻底沦陷在他那勾魂摄魄的魅力之下。而如今，在偷走了她的一颗芳心之后，他竟妄图将她一脚踹开、转而去迎娶别的女人！特雷莫雷尔目瞪口呆地聆听着这番狂轰滥炸，对这个女人的厚颜无耻惊得说不出话来。什么！她竟有脸倒打一耙、声称是他玷污摧残了她那无辜的清白，而事实上分明是她当初不知羞耻地死乞白赖投怀送抱、好几次甚至连他自己都对她的不知廉耻感到叹为观止！

===Original===

Such was the depth of her corruption, as it seemed to him, that he wondered whether he were her first or her twentieth lover. And she had so led him on, and had so forcibly made him feel the intensity of her will, that he had been fain still to submit to this despotism. But he had now determined to resist on the first opportunity; and he resisted.
“Well, yes,” said he, frankly, “I did deceive you; I have no fortune﻿—this marriage will give me one; I shall get married.” He went on to say that he loved Laurence less than ever, but that he coveted her money more and more every day. “To prove this,” he pursued, “if you will find me tomorrow a girl who has twelve hundred thousand francs instead of a million, I will marry her in preference to Mademoiselle Courtois.”
She had never suspected he had so much courage. She had so long moulded him like soft wax, and this unexpected conduct disconcerted her. She was indignant, but at the same time she felt that unhealthy satisfaction that some women feel, when they meet a master who subdues them; and she admired Trémorel more than ever before. This time, he had taken a tone which conquered her; she despised him enough to think him quite capable of marrying for money. When he had done, she said:
“It’s really so, then; you only care for the million of dowry?”
“I’ve sworn it to you a hundred times.”
“Truly now, don’t you love Laurence?”
“I have never loved her, and never shall.” He thought that he would thus secure his peace until the wedding-day; once married, he cared not what would happen. What cared he for Sauvresy? Life is only a succession of broken friendships. What is a friend, after all? One who can and ought to serve you. Ability consists in breaking with people, when they cease to be useful to you.
Bertha reflected.
“Hear me, Hector,” said she at last. “I cannot calmly resign myself to the sacrifice which you demand. Let me have but a few days, to accustom myself to this dreadful blow. You owe me as much﻿—let Clement get well, first.”
He did not expect to see her so gentle and subdued; who would have looked for such concessions, so easily obtained? The idea of a snare did not occur to him. In his delight he betrayed how he rejoiced in his liberty, which ought to have undeceived Bertha; but she did not perceive it. He grasped her hand, and cried:
“Ah, you are very good﻿—you really love me.”

===Chinese===

在这个女人深不可测的道德沦丧面前，他甚至忍不住暗自琢磨自己究竟算她的第一个入幕之宾，还是第20个奸夫姘头！当初正是她一步步将他引诱下水，又用她那近乎狂暴的蛮横意志逼得他不得不俯首帖耳屈从于她的暴政。然而如今他已下定决心只要逮着机会便予以坚决反击；这一回他终于彻底爆发了。
“不错，正如你所见，”他直截了当摊牌道，“我确实欺瞒了你；我如今身无分文——而这桩联姻能赐予我泼天家资；我非结不可。”他接着大言不惭地宣称，自己对洛朗斯根本谈不上有半点真情，但他对那笔万贯家财的贪欲却与日俱增。“为了向你证明这一点，”他冷酷地补充道，“倘若你明儿一早能替我物色一个坐拥120万法郎而非100万的富家千金，我定会毫不犹豫甩掉库尔图瓦小姐转而迎娶她。”
贝尔塔断没想到他竟有如此血性胆魄。她向来习惯了像揉软蜡一样肆意摆弄他，这番突如其来的强硬反抗彻底将她打了个措手不及。她既怒火中烧，可与此同时，某些女性在遭遇到能将自身彻底征服降服的铁血主子时常常萌生出的病态快感，再度在她心头油然而生；她反倒比以往任何时刻都更加崇拜迷恋特雷莫雷尔。这一回，他的强硬姿态彻底击溃了她；她骨子里本就鄙薄他，认定他绝对是那种利欲熏心为了钱财什么都干得出来的下作之徒。待他说完，她开口问道：
“这么说来全都是大实话了；你眼皮底下当真唯独盯着那100万法郎的嫁妆？”
“我对你发过上百次毒誓了。”
“当真扪心自问，你丝毫都不爱洛朗斯？”
“我平生从未爱过她，往后也绝不会爱。”埃克托心想借此托辞至少能换来婚期之前的安宁太平；一旦大婚礼成，天地间无论发生何等风波他都概不认账了。至于索夫勒齐的死活于他何干？人生在世不过是一场接一场恩断义绝的旧交割席罢了。所谓朋友究竟算个什么东西？无非是可以利用、理当为自己效力的垫脚石罢了。真正的智谋，在于一旦对方丧失了利用价值，便能毫不犹豫将对方一脚踹开。
贝尔塔陷入了沉思。
“听我说，埃克托，”良久她才缓缓开口道，“你逼我做出的这番割肉退让，我一时半刻实在无法平心静气地认命咽下。宽限我几天时日吧，好让我慢慢适应这记晴天霹雳。这是你欠我的——无论如何，至少先等克莱芒的身子骨彻底康复痊愈再说。”
埃克托断没想到她竟能表现得这般温顺克制；天下间哪能有这等唾手可得的轻易退让？他丝毫未曾嗅出其中暗藏的阴险杀机。在一阵狂喜中，他甚至得意忘形地流露出了重获自由身的雀跃欢欣，这本该彻底点醒贝尔塔；然而贝尔塔对此全无察觉。他一把紧紧攥住她的柔荑，动情地高呼道：
“啊，你当真是个好女人——你是真心实意深爱着我的。”

## XX / 第二十章
===Original===

Time passed. Hector and Bertha repaired to Sauvresy’s room; he was asleep. They noiselessly took chairs beside the fire, as usual, and the maid retired. In order that the sick man might not be disturbed by the light of the lamp, curtains had been hung so that, when lying down, he could not see the fireplace and mantel. In order to see these, he must have raised himself on his pillow and leaned forward on his right arm. But now he was asleep, breathing painfully, feverish, and shuddering convulsively. Bertha and Hector did not speak; the solemn and sinister silence was only broken by the ticking of the clock, or by the leaves of the book which Hector was reading. Ten o’clock struck; soon after Sauvresy moved, turned over, and awoke. Bertha was at his side in an instant; she saw that his eyes were open.
“Do you feel a little better, dear Clement?” she asked.
“Neither better nor worse.”
“Do you want anything?”
“I am thirsty.”
Hector, who had raised his eyes when his friend spoke, suddenly resumed his reading.
Bertha, standing by the mantel, began to prepare with great care Dr. R﻿—﻿—’s last prescription; when it was ready, she took out the fatal little vial as usual, and thrust one of her hairpins into it.
She had not time to draw it out before she felt a light touch upon her shoulder. A shudder shook her from head to foot; she suddenly turned and uttered a loud scream, a cry of terror and horror.
“Oh!”
The hand which had touched her was her husband’s. While she was busied with the poison at the mantel, Sauvresy had softly raised himself; more softly still, he had pulled the curtain aside, and had stretched out his arm and touched her. His eyes glittered with hate and anger.
Bertha’s cry was answered by another dull cry, or rather groan; Trémorel had seen and comprehended all; he was overwhelmed.
“All is discovered!” Their eyes spoke these three words to each other. They saw them everywhere, written in letters of fire. There was a moment of stupor, of silence so profound that Hector heard his temples beat. Sauvresy had got back under the bedclothes again. He laughed loudly, wildly, just as a skeleton might have laughed whose jaws and teeth rattled together.
But Bertha was not one of those persons who are overcome by a single blow, terrible as it might be. She trembled like a leaf; her legs staggered; but her mind was already at work seeking a subterfuge.

===Chinese===

光阴悄然流逝。埃克托与贝尔塔来到了索夫勒齐的卧房；他已然沉沉入睡。两人一如既往轻手轻脚地在壁炉旁拉过椅子坐下，女仆便躬身退了出去。为了让病患不受灯光的晃眼打扰，房中特意挂起了厚厚的帷幔，如此一来，他平躺在榻上时便瞧不见壁炉与壁炉台。若要瞧见这些，他必须直挺起上半身斜倚在枕头上，用右臂支撑着身子探出头来。然而此刻他正在昏睡，呼吸粗重而艰难，面带高热，浑身还不时伴随着阵阵痉挛的战栗。贝尔塔与埃克托二人皆闭口不言；唯有座钟沉闷单调的滴答声、或是埃克托手中书页翻动的沙沙声，打破这肃穆而凶险的死寂。座钟敲响了10下；不多时，索夫勒齐动弹了一下身子，翻了个身，醒了过来。贝尔塔刹那间便飘然移步到了榻前；她瞧见他的眼帘已经张开。
“你觉得稍稍好些了吗，亲爱的克莱芒？”她温言询问道。
“不好也不坏。”
“你想要点什么吗？”
“我口渴。”
方才老友开口时埃克托曾抬眼瞧了一下，此刻便重新埋头就着灯光翻书。
贝尔塔伫立在壁炉台旁，格外细致耐心地调配起R医生开出的最后一剂药水；调配停当之后，她一如既往掏出了那只致命的小药瓶，从发髻上拔下一支发簪探了进去。
未等她将发簪从药瓶中抽出，她猛地感觉到一只冰凉的手掌轻轻搭在了自己的香肩上。一阵剧烈彻骨的战栗瞬间从头顶狠狠扫到脚后跟；她猝然回过身来，喉头爆发出了一声凄厉绝伦的尖叫——一声充斥着魂飞魄散与极度惊骇的绝望惨嚎：
“啊！”
搭在她肩头的那只手，正是她丈夫的手掌！正当她在壁炉台前全神贯注摆弄鸩毒之际，索夫勒齐已然悄无声息地撑起了身子；他更加悄无声息地掀开了帷幔，伸直了手臂，轻按在了她的肩头。他的双眸中喷涌着仇恨与狂怒的熊熊烈火。
回应贝尔塔尖叫的，是另一声沉闷的惊呼，不如说是一记濒死的呻吟；特雷莫雷尔眼睁睁目睹并领会了眼前的一切，整个人如遭五雷轰顶瘫软在地。
“全败露了！”二人的目光在虚空中交汇，无声地吐露着这字字万钧的绝望判词。这几个大字仿佛化作了通红的烙铁，在这间屋子的每一处角落熊熊燃烧。死一般的呆滞与沉寂瞬间笼罩了全场，万籁俱寂中，埃克托甚至能清晰听到自己双侧太阳穴狂乱的突突跳动声。索夫勒齐重新缩回了被窝里。他狂乱而放肆地放声大笑起来，笑声凄厉刺耳，恰似一具下颌骨与惨白牙齿剧烈咯咯撞击的森森白骨在狂笑。
然而贝尔塔绝非寻常轻易被击垮的凡妇俗女，哪怕这记重击是如此致命凶残。她虽然浑身抖如风中落叶、双腿直打哆嗦，可那颗机敏奸猾的头脑却已然开始飞速运转，竭力搜寻着金蝉脱壳的遁词。

===Original===

What had Sauvresy seen﻿—anything? What did he know? For even had he seen the vial, this might be explained. It could only have been by simple chance that he had touched her at the moment when she was using the poison. All these thoughts flashed across her mind in a moment, as rapid as lightning shooting between the clouds. And then she dared to approach the bed, and, with a frightfully constrained smile, to say:
“How you frightened me then!”
He looked at her a moment, which seemed to her an age﻿—and simply replied:
“I understand it.”
There was no longer any uncertainty. Bertha saw only too well in her husband’s eyes that he knew something. But what﻿—how much? She nerved herself to go on:
“Are you still suffering?”
“No.”
“Then why did you get up?”
He raised himself upon his pillow, and with a sudden strength, he continued:
“I got up to tell you that I have had enough of these tortures, that I have reached the limits of human energy, that I cannot endure one day longer the agony of seeing myself put to death slowly, drop by drop, by the hands of my wife and my best friend!”
He stopped. Hector and Bertha were thunderstruck. “I wanted to tell you also, that I have had enough of your cruel caution, and that I suffer. Ah, don’t you see that I suffer horribly? Hurry, cut short my agony! Kill me, and kill me at a blow﻿—poisoners!”
At the last word, the Count de Trémorel sprang up as if he had moved by a spring, his eyes haggard, his arms stretched out. Sauvresy, seeing this, quickly slipped his hand under the pillow, pulled out a revolver, and pointed the barrel at Hector, crying out:
“Don’t advance a step!”
He thought that Trémorel, seeing that they were discovered, was going to rush upon him and strangle him; but he was mistaken. It seemed to Hector as though he were losing his mind. He fell down as heavily as if he were a log. Bertha was more self-possessed; she tried to resist the torpor of terror which she felt coming on.
“You are worse, my Clement,” said she. “This is that dreadful fever which frightens me so. Delirium﻿—”
“Have I really been delirious?” interrupted he, with a surprised air.
“Alas, yes, dear, that is what haunts you, and fills your poor sick head with horrid visions.”
He looked at her curiously. He was really stupefied by this boldness, which constantly grew more bold.
“What!

===Chinese===

索夫勒齐究竟瞧见了什么——他当真全瞧见了吗？他究竟掌握了多少底细？即便他方才当真瞥见了药瓶，这一切也完全能编造出合情合理的托辞。他在她动用鸩毒的刹那冷不丁触碰到她，兴许纯粹不过是瞎猫碰上死耗子的机缘巧合罢了。这千头万绪在短短一瞬间劈入她的脑海，迅疾如划破浓云的连环闪电。紧接着，她竟大着胆子挪步重新凑近床榻，脸上挤出一抹僵硬扭曲到了极点的惨笑，娇嗔道：
“哎呀，你方才可真把人家给吓死了！”
索夫勒齐凝视了她片刻——在这漫长得宛如一个世纪的死寂对视之后——他简短冷酷地吐露道：
“我全明白。”
心头的侥幸彻底化为了泡影。贝尔塔从丈夫的这双眼眸深处看清了一切：他分明什么都知晓了。可究竟知晓了多少——深浅几何？她硬着头皮强撑着问下去：
“你身上还在受罪难受吗？”
“不痛了。”
“那你方才为何要硬撑着爬起来？”
他在枕头上重新支起身子，体内猛然爆发出一股骇人的蛮力，厉声宣判道：
“我爬起来是要当面告知你们：这般非人的凌迟折磨我已经受够了！我已经彻底耗尽了血肉之躯所能忍受的极限！我再也无法多忍耐一天这般眼睁睁看着自己被发妻同至交好友联手、一点一滴一勺接一勺缓慢鸩杀的无边地狱！
他打住了话头。埃克托与贝尔塔被这番话轰击得呆若木鸡。“我还想当面告知你们，你们那些小心翼翼的残忍防范我已经受够了，我痛啊！难道你们当真瞧不出我此刻正在饱受生不如死的剧痛吗？痛快点，抓紧给我做个了断！杀了我吧，索性一枪将我轰毙——你们这两个弑夫投毒的狗男女！”
听到这最后的诛心怒骂，特雷莫雷尔伯爵宛如被按动了弹簧机栝般猛地一跃而起，双目凸瞪，双臂僵直前伸。索夫勒齐眼疾手快，一把将手掌探入枕头底下，霍然拔出一柄左轮手枪，黑洞洞的枪口直直顶在埃克托的脑门前，厉声咆哮道：
“敢上前迈出半步试试！”
索夫勒齐还当特雷莫雷尔眼见丑事败露，打算狗急跳墙猛扑上来将他活活掐死；然而他猜错了。埃克托此刻只觉天旋地转、神智彻底崩塌。他烂泥般重重瘫倒在地，宛如一截毫无知觉的死木头。贝尔塔的心智倒还稍微清醒坚韧几分；她极力抵御着周身泛起的恐惧麻木。
“你病情又加重了，我的克莱芒，”她试图做最后的垂死挣扎，“这全是那场该死的高烧折磨的，真把人吓死了。谵妄高热——”
“难道我方才当真是在满嘴胡吣发谵妄吗？”他以一种故作惊愕的腔调打断道。
“唉，千真万确啊亲爱的，正是恶疾在作祟，把你那可怜受罪的头颅塞满了这些毛骨悚然的荒诞幻象。”
他用一种打量怪物的眼神饶有兴致地审视着她。面对这个女人步步升级、愈发不可一世的滔天厚颜无耻，他当真感到叹为观止。
“什么！

===Original===

you think that we, who are so dear to you, your friends, I, your﻿—”
Her husband’s implacable look forced her to stop, and the words expired on her lips.
“Enough of these lies, Bertha,” resumed Sauvresy, “they are useless. No, I have not been dreaming, nor have I been delirious. The poison is only too real, and I could tell you what it is without your taking it out of your pocket.”
She recoiled as if she had seen her husband’s hand stretched out to snatch the blue vial.
“I guessed it and recognized it at the very first; for you have chosen one of those poisons which, it is true, leave scarcely any trace of themselves, but the symptoms of which are not deceptive. Do you remember the day when I complained of a morbid taste for pepper? The next day I was certain of it, and I was not the only one. Doctor R﻿—﻿—, too, had a suspicion.”
Bertha tried to stammer something; her husband interrupted her.
“People ought to try their poisons,” pursued he, in an ironical tone, “before they use them. Didn’t you understand yours, or what its effects were? Why, your poison gives intolerable neuralgia, sleeplessness, and you saw me without surprise, sleeping soundly all night long! I complained of a devouring fire within me, while your poison freezes the blood and the entrails, and yet you are not astonished. You see all the symptoms change and disappear, and that does not enlighten you. You are fools, then. Now see what I had to do to divert Doctor R﻿—﻿—’s suspicions. I hid the real pains which your poison caused, and complained of imaginary, ridiculous ones. I described sensations just the opposite of those which I felt. You were lost, then﻿—and I saved you.”
Bertha’s malignant energy staggered beneath so many successive blows. She wondered whether she were not going mad; had she heard aright? Was it really true that her husband had perceived that he was being poisoned, and yet said nothing; nay, that he had even deceived the doctor? Why? What was his purpose?
Sauvresy paused several minutes, and then went on:
“I have held my tongue and so saved you, because the sacrifice of my life had already been made. Yes, I had been fatally wounded in the heart on the day that I learned that you were faithless to me.”
He spoke of his death without apparent emotion; but at the words, “You were faithless to me,” his voice faltered and trembled.
“I would not, could not believe it at first.

===Chinese===

难道你当真以为我们这些待你如珠如宝的亲人、你的至交朋友，我，你的结发——”
她丈夫那道冰冷残酷、不掺杂丝毫人类温情的目光硬生生掐住了她的喉咙，所有的谎言瞬间死在了唇边。
“别再满嘴跑舌头撒谎了，贝尔塔，”索夫勒齐冷冷打断道，“这一切徒劳无功。不，我既没有胡思乱想，也没有半点谵妄高热。那瓶鸩毒真实得不能再真实了，哪怕你不用掏出口袋，我也能倒背如流报出它的名堂来。”
贝尔塔触电般步步倒退，仿佛瞧见丈夫那只干枯的大手正凌空抓向她藏在兜里的蓝色小药瓶。
“从一开始我便猜透并辨认出了它的底细；只因你挑选的这味奇毒，固然几乎不留任何理化痕迹，但它的中毒病征却根本骗不过行家里手。难道你忘了那天我冷不丁抱怨对胡椒产生了不可遏制的病态狂热？到了翌日我便彻底铁证如山，而且起疑的可绝非唯独我一人。连R医生也早已察觉到了蛛丝马迹。”
贝尔塔刚欲结结巴巴开口分辩，丈夫冷酷的声音便再度将她碾碎。
“世人下毒杀人，”他带着极度刻毒的冷嘲继续嘲弄道，“在操刀之前好歹得先弄明白药性！难道你对自己手里攥着的毒药究竟有何等发作药效一无所知吗？见鬼，你这味鸩毒会引发撕心裂肺的剧烈面部神经痛、导致彻夜无眠，而你瞧见我一连数夜睡得香甜无比，竟丝毫不起疑心！我成天大声叫嚷体内如烈火烹油般干渴灼痛，而你手里的鸩毒分明只会将腑脏与周身血液冻结成冰，可你竟丝毫不觉蹊跷！你眼睁睁瞧着所有的中毒症状全盘翻转消散，脑子里竟没有亮起半点警钟！你们俩简直是愚不可及的蠢才！现在睁开眼瞧瞧吧，为了替你们打消R医生的怀疑，我暗中替你们做了多少掩护！我硬生生将毒发带来的真切剧痛死死咽回肚里，转而满嘴胡吣叫嚷一些子虚乌有、滑稽荒唐的怪病。我向大夫描摹的每一个病征，全与我体内遭受的真实酷刑背道而驰！你们原本早就死无葬身之地了——是我亲手拯救了你们！”
在接二连三的雷霆重击之下，贝尔塔那一身阴鸷恶毒的强硬气焰终于被彻底打得土崩瓦解。她甚至忍不住暗自怀疑自己莫非是在噩梦中发了疯；自己这双耳朵听到的当真是人话吗？难道她的丈夫当真早就洞悉了全盘鸩杀图谋，却始终守口如瓶，甚至反过来替真凶在名医面前打掩护？这究竟是为何？他布下的究竟是何等通天的阴险杀局？
索夫勒齐停顿了良久，方才一字一顿继续说了下去：
“我之所以咬紧牙关守口如瓶替你们遮掩，全因早在你们下毒之前，我的这条性命便早已在万念俱灰中舍弃了。不错，早在获悉你背夫偷汉、背信弃义的那一天起，我的心头便已挨下了致命的绝命一刀。”
提及死亡，他的语气平静得令人心悸；然而当吐露到“你背夫偷汉、背信弃义”这几个字眼时，他的嗓音却无法自持地剧烈颤抖哽咽起来。
“起初，我无论如何也不愿相信、不敢相信。

===Original===

I doubted the evidence of my senses, rather than doubt you. But I was forced to believe at last. I was no longer anything in my house but a laughingstock. But I was in your way. You and your lover needed more room and liberty. You were tired of constraint and hypocrisy. Then it was that, believing that my death would make you free and rich, you brought in poison to rid yourselves of me.”
Bertha had at least the heroism of crime. All was discovered; well, she threw down the mask. She tried to defend her accomplice, who lay unconscious in a chair.
“It is I that have done it all,” cried she. “He is innocent.”
Sauvresy turned pale with rage.
“Ah, really,” said he, “my friend Hector is innocent! It wasn’t he, then, who, to pay me up﻿—not for his life, for he was too cowardly to kill himself; but for his honor, which he owes to me﻿—took my wife from me? Wretch! I hold out my hand to him when he is drowning, I welcome him like a brother, and in return, he desolates my hearth!﻿ ﻿… And you knew what you were doing, my friend Hector﻿—for I told you a hundred times that my wife was my all here below, my present and my future, my dream and happiness and hope and very life! You knew that for me to lose her was to die. But if you had loved her﻿—no, it was not that you loved her; you hated me. Envy devoured you, and you could not tell me to my face, ‘You are too happy.’ Then, like a coward, you dishonored me in the dark. Bertha was only the instrument of your rancor; and she weighs upon you today﻿—you despise and fear her. My friend, Hector, you have been in this house the vile lackey who thinks to avenge his baseness by spitting upon the meats which he puts on his master’s table!”
The count only responded by a shudder. The dying man’s terrible words fell more cruelly on his conscience than blows upon his cheek.
“See, Bertha,” continued Sauvresy, “that’s the man whom you have preferred to me, and for whom you have betrayed me. You never loved me﻿—I see it now﻿—your heart was never mine. And I﻿—I loved you so! From the day I first saw you, you were my only thought; as if your heart had beaten in place of mine. Everything about you was dear and precious to me; I adored your whims, caprices, even your faults. There was nothing I would not do for a smile from you, so that you would say to me, Thank you, between two kisses.

===Chinese===

我宁可怀疑我自身的所有耳闻目睹，也绝不肯去怀疑你分毫。然而残酷的铁证终究逼得我不得不信。在自个儿的深宅大院里，我早已沦为了全天下最滑稽可鄙的绿头王八与笑柄。然而我终究成了碍手碍脚的眼中钉。你同你的姘头渴望更宽敞的天地与肆无忌惮的自由。你们厌倦了提心吊胆的遮遮掩掩与虚与委蛇。正是在这一刻，认定只要我两脚一蹬咽气便能坐拥泼天家产与海阔天空，你们便端出了这瓶鸩毒，急不可耐地要将我置于死地！”
贝尔塔骨子里至少还残留着几分亡命恶徒的决绝悍勇。既然全盘败露，索性将脸皮面具撕个粉碎。她试图挺身而出为瘫倒在椅子里人事不省的同谋揽罪脱罪：
“这一切全是我一个人干的，”她嘶声尖叫道，“埃克托他是无辜的！”
索夫勒齐因狂怒而面色刷地惨白如纸。
“哈，真是感人至深啊！”他咬牙切齿地冷笑道，“我的好兄弟埃克托竟是无辜的！这么说来，当初为了向我报恩——不是报答救命之恩，因为他贪生怕死压根不敢自裁；而是报答我替他抢救保全下来的名誉恩情——抢占了我明媒正娶的发妻的人，竟不是他？猪狗不如的下作畜生！他在万丈深渊里溺水垂死之际，是我向他伸出了援手；我如待亲兄弟般将他迎入家门，而他回敬给我的，却是踏碎我的门楣祖产、将我的厅堂化作废墟！……而且你心知肚明自己究竟在干些什么勾当，我的好兄弟埃克托——只因我对你念叨过上百次：我内人是我在这世间的全部寄托，是我的现在与未来，是我的美梦、欢愉、期冀与身家性命！你心里比谁都更清楚失去她无异于要了我的命！假使你当真是出于干柴烈火的情欲深爱着她倒也罢了——可你根本不爱她，你纯粹是恨我！滔天的妒火将你从骨子里彻底烧空了，你不敢当面冲我叫嚣‘你过得太幸福了’，于是便像个阴沟里的卑鄙懦夫一样，在暗地里狠狠玷污羞辱我！贝尔塔充其量不过是你发泄私怨的一件复仇凶器；而这件凶器如今已成了沉重的累赘——你打心眼里嫌恶鄙薄她、畏惧她！我的好兄弟埃克托，你在这座庄园里，不过是一个妄图通过往端上主子餐桌的珍馐佳肴里吐口水来报复自身卑贱下作的恶毒家奴罢了！”
伯爵除了周身如触电般战栗之外，连半个字都挤不出来。垂死之人吐露出的这字字诛心的判词，比大嘴巴狠狠抽打在他面颊上还要残酷百倍地鞭笞着他的灵魂。
“睁大双眼瞧瞧吧，贝尔塔，”索夫勒齐转向发妻继续说道，“这便是你弃我于不顾、宁可背叛我也非要攀附的所谓如意情郎！你平生从未深爱过我——如今我算是彻底看透了——你的心房里从未有过我的一席之地。可我呢——我是何等掏心掏肺地深爱着你啊！从初见你的那一天起，你便是我在这滚滚红尘里的唯一神思；仿佛你的心跳早已取代了我的心房。你身上的一切于我而言皆是无价的瑰宝；我宠溺你的任性、骄纵、乃至你身上的所有缺点。单是为了博得你的一抹浅笑，好让你在温存亲吻的间隙对我说上一声‘多谢’，我赴汤蹈火在所不辞！

===Original===

You don’t know that for years after our marriage it was my delight to wake up first so as to gaze upon you as you lay asleep, to admire and touch your lovely hair, lying dishevelled across the pillow. Bertha!”
He softened at the remembrance of these past joys, which would not come again. He forgot their presence, the infamous treachery, the poison; that he was about to die, murdered by this beloved wife; and his eyes filled with tears, his voice choked.
Bertha, more motionless and pallid than marble, listened to him breathlessly.
“It is true, then,” continued the sick man, “that these lovely eyes conceal a soul of filth! Ah, who would not have been deceived, as I was? Bertha, what did you dream of when you were sleeping in my arms? Trémorel came, and you thought you saw in him the ideal of your dreams. You admired the precocious wrinkles which betrayed an exhausted life, like the fatal seal which marks the fallen archangel’s forehead. Your love, without thought of mine, rushed toward him, though he did not think of you. You went to evil as if it were your nature. And yet I thought you more immaculate than the Alpine snows. You did not even have a struggle with yourself; you betrayed no confusion which would reveal your first fault to me. You brought me your forehead soiled with *his* kisses without blushing.”
Weariness overcame his energies; his voice became little by little feebler and less distinct.
“You had your happiness in your hands, Bertha, and you carelessly destroyed it, as the child breaks the toy of whose value he is ignorant. What did you expect from this wretch for whom you had the frightful courage to kill me, with a kiss upon your lips, slowly, hour by hour? You thought you loved him, but disgust ought to have come at last. Look at him, and judge between us. See which is the man﻿—I, extended on this bed where I shall soon die, or he shivering there in a corner. You have the energy of crime, but he has only the baseness of it.
